要知道，梅特兰是个天生的滑稽戏子，专爱制造恶作剧；也许是想披着那张险些要了他命的老熊的皮吓我一跳，报个假惊，他一收拾完那张皮，就把它往身上一裹，纯粹为了顽皮取闹，在他值更的时辰悄悄爬上了甲板。熊皮的头，加上雾，想必挡得他没能看见我正在瞄准。
这场悲剧让我病了好几个星期。我看出命运之手已落在我身上。等我下床时，可怜的梅特兰已躺在我们近旁那座骆驼形大冰丘后面的冰里了。
到1月底，我们漂到了北纬80度55分；正是在那时，克拉克当着威尔逊的面问我，愿不愿补可怜的梅特兰的缺，做春季冲刺的第四人。当我说『好，我愿意』时，大卫·威尔逊厌恶地啐了一口。过了一分钟他又叹道：『唉，可怜的梅特兰……』还*啧啧*地倒抽了两口气。
天知道，我当时真有一股冲动，想当场扑上去掐住他的喉咙掐死他：可我忍住了。
离出发冲刺只剩不到一个月了，全船上下鼓足干劲干起来：给狗量身材、做挽具和海豹皮鞋，检修雪橇和皮划艇，把每一盎司多余的重量都削减掉。可我们终究命中注定那一年没能成行。2月20日前后，冰开始挤压，船身承受着骇人的压力。我们不得不拢起双手成喇叭状对着彼此的耳朵喊话，因为整个冰原大陆到处在轰隆、爆裂、雷鸣般地可怕隆起。我们时刻提心吊胆，怕*博雷尔*号被碾成碎柴，只得动手搬出给养，把雪橇、皮划艇、狗和一切物件安置到随时可以弃船逃生的位置。这情形持续了五天，还伴随着一场北面来的暴风，到2月底，又把我们往南赶回了北纬79度40分。克拉克自然放弃了那年夏天进发北极的念头。
紧接着，我们有了一个惊人的发现：我们的驯鹿苔存量不知怎么少得离谱。二副伊根（Egan）挨了责备；可那也无济于事：糟糕的事实依然摆在那里。

===Original===

Clark was advised to kill one or two of the deer, but he pig-headedly refused: and by the beginning of summer they were all dead.
Well, our northward drift recommenced. Toward the middle of February we saw a mirage of the coming sun above the horizon; there were flights of Arctic petrels and snow-buntings; and spring was with us. In an ice-pack of big hummocks and narrow lanes we made good progress all the summer.
When the last of the deer died, my heart sank; and when the dogs killed two of their number, and a bear crushed a third, I was fully expecting what actually came; it was this: Clark announced that he could now take only two companions with him in the spring: and they were Wilson and Mew. So once more I saw David Wilson’s pleased smile of malice.
We settled into our second winter-quarters. Again came December, and all our drear sunless gloom, made worse by the fact that the windmill would not work, leaving us without the electric light.
Ah me, none but those who have felt it could dream of one half the mental depression of that long Arctic night; how the soul takes on the hue of the world; and without and within is nothing but gloom, gloom, and the reign of the Power of Darkness.
Not one of us but was in a melancholic, dismal and dire mood; and on the 13th December Lamburn, the engineer, stabbed Cartwright, the old harpooner, in the arm.
Three days before Christmas a bear came close to the ship, and then turned tail. Mew, Wilson, I and Meredith (a general hand) set out in pursuit. After a pretty long chase we lost him, and then scattered different ways. It was very dim, and after yet an hour’s search, I was returning weary and disgusted to the ship, when I saw some shadow like a bear sailing away on my left, and at the same time sighted a man﻿—I did not know whom﻿—running like a handicapped ghost some little distance to the right. So I shouted out:
“There he is﻿—come on! This way!”
The man quickly joined me, but as soon as ever he recognised me, stopped dead. The devil must have suddenly got into him, for he said:
“No, thanks, Jeffson: alone with you I am in danger of my life.﻿ ﻿…”
It was Wilson. And I, too, forgetting at once all about the bear, stopped and faced him.
“I see,” said I. “But, Wilson, you are going to explain to me *now* what you mean, you hear? What *do* you mean, Wilson?”

===Chinese===

有人劝克拉克宰掉一两头驯鹿，他固执己见不肯：结果到夏初，驯鹿全死光了。
嗯，我们的北向漂流又开始了。2月中旬，我们看见即将归来的太阳悬在海平线上方的蜃景；成群的北极海燕和雪鹀飞来；春天到了我们身边。整个夏天，我们在大冰丘与窄水道交错的浮冰群里进展顺利。
最后一头驯鹿死时，我的心沉了下去；后来狗群咬死了自己队伍里的两条，又有一条被熊压死，我便完全料到了随之而来的事：克拉克宣布，春季冲刺他只能带两名同伴了：人选是威尔逊和缪。于是我又一次看到了大卫·威尔逊那快意的恶笑。
我们安顿进第二个越冬地。12月又来了，带着那全然无日的阴郁，更糟的是风车转不动，我们连电灯也没有了。
唉，没有亲历过的人，做梦也想不到那漫长极夜里精神抑郁的一半滋味；灵魂是怎样染上了世界的颜色啊；身外身内，只有阴郁、阴郁，和黑暗权柄的统治。
我们人人情绪阴沉、凄惨、恶劣；12月13日，轮机长兰伯恩用刀捅了老鱼叉手卡特赖特（Cartwright）的胳膊。
圣诞节前三天，一头熊来到船近旁，随即掉头跑了。缪、威尔逊、我，还有梅雷迪思（一名杂役水手）出发追击。追了老半天把它追丢了，我们便各奔一方。天色极暗，又搜寻了一个钟头之后，我正又累又败兴地往回走，忽然看见左边有个熊似的影子飘然而去，同时又瞥见一个人影——认不清是谁——像个瘸腿的鬼，在右边不远处跑着。于是我喊道：
『在那儿——快来！这边！』
那人很快奔到我身边，可一认出是我，立刻死死站住了。准是魔鬼突然附了他，因为他竟说：
『不，多谢了，杰夫森：跟你单独在一起，我有性命之忧。……』
是威尔逊。而我呢，霎时间把熊忘得一干二净，也站住面对着他。
『我明白了，』我说，『可是威尔逊，你*现在*就得给我讲清楚你这话什么意思，听见没有？你到底是什么意思，威尔逊？』

===Original===

“What I say,” he answered deliberately, eyeing me up and down: “alone with you I am in danger of my life. Just as poor Maitland was, and just as poor Peters was. Certainly, you are a deadly beast.”
Fury leapt, my God, in my heart. Black as the tenebrous Arctic night was my soul.
“Do you mean,” said I, “that I want to put you out of the way in order to go in your place to the Pole? Is that your meaning, man?”
“That’s about my meaning, Jeffson,” says he: “you are a deadly beast, you know.”
“Stop!” I said, with blazing eye. “I am going to kill *you*, Wilson﻿—as sure as God lives: but I want to hear first. Who *told* you that I killed Peters?”
“Your lover killed him﻿—with *your* collusion. Why, I heard you, man, in your beastly sleep, calling the whole thing out. And I was pretty sure of it before, only I had no proofs. By God, I should enjoy putting a bullet into you, Jeffson!”
“You wrong me﻿—you, you wrong me!” I shrieked, my eyes staring with ravenous lust for his blood; “and now I am going to pay you well for it. *Look out, you!*”
I aimed my gun for his heart, and I touched the trigger. He held up his left hand.
“Stop,” he said, “stop.” (He was one of the coolest of men ordinarily.) “There is no gallows on the *Boreal*, but Clark could easily rig one for you. I want to kill you, too, because there are no criminal courts up here, and it would be doing a good action for my country. But not here﻿—not now. Listen to me﻿—don’t shoot. Later we can meet, when all is ready, so that no one may be the wiser, and fight it all out.”
As he spoke I let the gun drop. It was better so. I knew that he was much the best shot on the ship, and I an indifferent one: but I did not care, I did not care, if I was killed.
It is a dim, inclement land, God knows: and the spirit of darkness and distraction is there.
Twenty hours later we met behind the great saddle-shaped hummock, some six miles to the S. E. of the ship. We had set out at different times, so that no one might suspect. And each brought a ship’s-lantern.
Wilson had dug an ice-grave near the hummock, leaving at its edge a heap of brash-ice and snow to fill it. We stood separated by an interval of perhaps seventy yards, the grave between us, each with a lantern at his feet.
Even so we were mere shadowy apparitions one to the other. The air glowered very drearily, and present in my inmost soul were the frills of cold.

===Chinese===

『就是我说的这个意思，』他从容不迫地答道，上下打量着我，『跟你单独在一起，我就有性命之忧。正如可怜的梅特兰，正如可怜的彼得斯。千真万确，你是头害人的畜生。』
狂怒，我的天，在我心头腾起。我的灵魂黑得如同幽暗的极夜。
『你是说，』我说，『我要除掉你好顶替你去北极？你是这个意思吗，嗯？』
『差不多就是这个意思，杰夫森，』他说，『你是头害人的畜生，你知道的。』
『住口！』我说，眼中喷着火。『我要杀的*是*你，威尔逊——上帝在上，这准定无疑：可我要先听明白。是谁*告诉*你我害死了彼得斯？』
『你的情人害死了他——还有*你*的串通。嗐，我亲耳听见你这家伙在可憎的睡梦里把整件事喊了出来。我早先就十拿九稳，只是没有证据。天哪，往你身上打进一颗子弹，我才痛快呢，杰夫森！』
『你冤枉我——你，你冤枉我！』我尖声叫道，眼睛瞪着，闪着嗜血的凶光；『如今我要你好生为此付出代价。*你给我当心！*』
我把枪对准他的心口，手指搭上扳机。他举起了左手。
『住手，』他说，『住手。』（他平日是个最沉得住气的人。）『*博雷尔*号上没有绞架，可克拉克可以轻而易举地给你搭一座。我也想杀你，因为这里没有刑事法庭，宰了你是为我的国家做件好事。但不是在这儿——不是现在。听我说——先别开枪。往后我们可以再碰头，待一切安排妥当，神不知鬼不觉，把这笔账痛痛快快算清。』
他说着，我垂下了枪。这样更好。我知道他是全船枪法最好的人，而我的枪法稀松平常：可我不在乎，哪怕被杀的是我，我也不在乎。
那是一片昏暗、严酷的土地，天知道：黑暗与迷乱的灵就住在那里。
二十小时后，我们在船东南约六英里那座马鞍形大冰丘后面碰了头。我们分头出发，免得惹人起疑。两人各提了一盏船上用的灯。
威尔逊在冰丘旁挖了一座冰坟，坟边留了一堆碎冰和雪好填坟。我们相隔约七十码站定，坟在我们中间，各人脚边放着一盏灯。
即便这样，我们彼此也不过是影子似的幽灵。空中阴沉郁怒，而我灵魂最深处，寒气的绦边森然沁入。

===Original===

A chill moon, a mere abstraction of light, seemed to hang far outside the universe. The temperature was at 55° below zero, so that we had on wind-clothes over our anoraks, and heavy foot-bandages under our Lap boots. Nothing but a weird morgue seemed the world, haunted with despondent madness; and exactly like that world about us were the minds of us two poor men, full of macabre, bleak, and funereal feelings.
Between us yawned an early grave for one or other of our bodies.
I heard Wilson cry out:
“Are you ready, Jeffson?”
“Aye, Wilson!” cried I.
“*Then here goes!*” cries he.
Even as he spoke, he fired. Surely, the man was in deadly earnest to kill me.
But his shot passed harmlessly by me: as indeed was only likely: we were mere shadows one to the other.
I fired perhaps ten seconds later than he: but in those ten seconds he stood perfectly revealed to me in clear, lavender light.
An Arctic fireball had traversed the sky, showering abroad, a sulphurous glamour over the snow-landscape. Before the intenser blue of its momentary shine had passed away, I saw Wilson stagger forward, and drop. And him and his lantern I buried deep there under the rubble ice.

===Chinese===

一轮寒月，简直只是光的一个抽象，仿佛远远悬在宇宙之外。气温零下55度，我们在皮袄外面罩了防风衣，拉普兰靴子里裹了厚厚的脚布。这世界俨然是一座阴森的停尸房，游荡着绝望的疯狂；我们两个可怜人的心绪，恰同我们周围的这个世界一样，充满了阴惨、荒寒和送葬般的情感。
我们两人之间，张着一张早夭的坟，等着收殓你我的肉身之一。
我听见威尔逊喊道：
『准备好了吗，杰夫森？』
『好了，威尔逊！』我喊道。
『*那这就来了！*』他喊道。
话音未落，他已开枪。这人杀我，实在是动了真格。
可他的子弹无害地擦我而过：这也本在情理之中：我们彼此不过是两团影子。
我比他晚了大约十秒开枪：可就在那十秒里，他在一片清澈的淡紫色光里，被我看得纤毫毕现。
一颗北极火流星划过长空，向雪原四面泼洒下硫磺般的辉光。趁着它那一瞬更炽的蓝光尚未消逝，我看见威尔逊踉跄前扑，倒了下去。我把他和他的灯，深深埋在了那片碎冰底下。

===Original===

On the 13th March, nearly three months later, Clark, Mew and I left the *Boreal* in latitude 85° 15′.
We had with us thirty-two dogs, three sledges, three kayaks, human provisions for 112 days, and dog provisions for 40. Being now about 340 miles from the Pole, we hoped to reach it in 43 days, then, turning south, and feeding living dogs with dead, make either Franz Josef Land or Spitzbergen, at which latter place we should very likely come up with a whaler.
Well, during the first days, progress was very slow, the ice being rough and laney, and the dogs behaving most badly, stopping dead at every difficulty, and leaping over the traces. Clark had had the excellent idea of attaching a gold-beater’s-skin balloon, with a lifting power of 35 pounds, to each sledge, and we had with us a supply of zinc and sulphuric-acid to repair the hydrogen-waste from the bags; but on the third day Mew overfilled and burst his balloon, and I and Clark had to cut ours loose in order to equalise weights, for we could neither leave him behind, turn back to the ship, nor mend the bag. So it happened that at the end of the fourth day out, we had made only nineteen miles, and could still from a hummock discern afar the leaning masts of the old *Boreal*. Clark led on ski, captaining a sledge with 400 lbs. of instruments, ammunition, pemmican, aleuronate bread; Mew followed, his sledge containing provisions only; and last came I, with a mixed freight. But on the third day Clark had an attack of snow-blindness, and Mew took his place.
Pretty soon our sufferings commenced, and they were bitter enough. The sun, though constantly visible day and night, gave no heat. Our sleeping-bags (Clark and Mew slept together in one, I in another) were soaking wet all the night, being thawed by our warmth; and our fingers, under wrappings of senne-grass and wolf-skin, were always bleeding. Sometimes our frail bamboo-cane kayaks, lying across the sledges, would crash perilously against an ice-ridge﻿—and they were our one hope of reaching land. But the dogs were the great difficulty: we lost six mortal hours a day in harnessing and tending them. On the twelfth day Clark took a single-altitude observation, and found that we were only in latitude 86° 45′; but the next day we passed beyond the furthest point yet reached by man, viz. 86° 53′, attained by the *Nix* explorers four years previously.

===Chinese===

三月十三日，将近三个月之后，克拉克（Clark）、缪和我离开「北极号」，时在北纬 85 度 15 分。
我们带了三十二条狗、三架雪橇、三只皮筏，112 天的人粮和 40 天的狗粮。此时距极点约 340 英里，我们指望 43 天内抵达，然后折向南行，拿死狗喂活狗，奔法兰士约瑟夫地或斯匹次卑尔根二者之一；若到后一地，我们极可能赶上一条捕鲸船。
唉，起初几天，行进非常缓慢：冰面又崎岖又多裂缝，狗的表现又极坏，一遇障碍便死死站住，或从缰绳里蹿跳出去。克拉克原有个极妙的主意：给每架雪橇系一只金箔匠皮膜的气球（升力三十五磅），我们还带了一批锌和硫酸，用来补充皮囊里氢的损耗；可第三天，缪把他的气球充过了头，爆了；我和克拉克只得把我们的一并割去，以均衡重量——因为我们既不能把他撇下，也不能折回船去，皮囊又补不好。于是出发后的第四天过完，我们才走了十九英里，从一座冰丘上还依稀望得见老「北极号」倾侧的桅杆。克拉克踏着滑雪板领路，掌着一架装四百磅仪器、弹药、干肉饼、面筋粉面包的雪橇；缪跟在后面，他的雪橇只装粮食；我殿后，载着一份混装的货。可第三天克拉克雪盲发作，缪接替了他的位置。
不多久我们的苦楚便开始了，而且苦得够呛。太阳虽昼夜常在，却不给一点热。我们的睡袋（克拉克和缪合睡一袋，我独睡一袋）整夜湿透，被我们的体温暖化着；我们的手指，裹在塞内草和狼皮里，总是淌血。有时我们脆弱的竹架皮筏横搁在雪橇上，撞上冰脊，岌岌可危——而它们是我们登得陆地的唯一指望。但最大的难题是狗：每天套橇和照料它们，要耗掉我们足足六个钟头。第十二天，克拉克测了一次太阳高度，发现我们才到北纬 86 度 45 分；可次日，我们便越过了人类至今所达的最远点，即「尼克斯号」探险者四年前所抵的 86 度 53 分。

===Original===

Our one secret thought now was food, food﻿—our daylong lust for the eating-time. Mew suffered from “Arctic thirst.”

===Chinese===

我们如今唯一秘藏的念头便是食物，食物——我们整天的贪欲，就是盼着开饭的时辰。缪则受着「极地渴」的煎熬。

===Original===

Under these conditions, man becomes in a few days, not a savage only, but a mere beast, hardly a grade above the bear and walrus. Ah, the ice! A long and sordid nightmare was that, God knows.
On we pressed, crawling our little way across the Vast, upon whose hoar silence, from Eternity until then, Bootes only, and that Great Bear, had watched.

===Chinese===

在这样的境况下，人用不了几天，便不但成了野人，简直成了畜生，比熊和海象高不出一个等级。啊，那冰原！天知道，那是一场漫长而龌龊的噩梦。
我们继续向前，在浩茫之上爬行短短的一程；那片灰白的沉寂，自亘古直至彼时，只有牧夫座，和那大熊座，曾加以注视。

===Original===

After the eleventh day our rate of march improved: all lanes disappeared, and ridges became much less frequent. By the fifteenth day I was leaving behind the ice-grave of David Wilson at the rate of ten to thirteen miles a day.
Yet, as it were, his arm reached out and touched me, even there.
His disappearance had been explained by a hundred different guesses on the ship﻿—all plausible enough. I had no idea that anyone connected me in any way with his death.
But on our twenty-second day of march, 140 miles from our goal, he caused a conflagration of rage and hate to break out among us three.
It was at the end of a march, when our stomachs were hollow, our frames ready to drop, and our mood ravenous and inflamed. One of Mew’s dogs was sick: it was necessary to kill it: he asked me to do it.
“Oh,” said I, “you kill your own dog, of course.”
“Well, I don’t know,” he replied, catching fire at once, “you ought to be used to killing, Jeffson.”
“How do you mean, Mew?” said I with a mad start, for madness and the flames of Hell were instant and uppermost in us all: “you mean because my profession﻿—”
“Profession! damn it, no,” he snarled like a dog: “go and dig up David Wilson﻿—I dare say you know where to find him﻿—and he will tell you my meaning, right enough.”
I rushed at once to Clark, who was stooping among the dogs, unharnessing: and savagely pushing his shoulder, I exclaimed:
“That beast accuses me of murdering David Wilson!”
“Well?” said Clark.
“I’d split his skull as clean﻿—!”
“Go away, Adam Jeffson, and let me be!” snarled Clark.
“Is that all you’ve got to say about it, then﻿—you?”
“To the devil with you, man, say I, and let me be!” cried he: “you know your own conscience best, I suppose.”
Before this insult I stood with grinding teeth, but impotent. However, from that moment a deeper mood of brooding malice occupied my spirit. Indeed the humour of us all was one of dangerous, even murderous, fierceness. In that pursuit of riches into that region of cold, we had become almost like the beasts that perish.

===Chinese===

第十一天之后，我们行进的速度加快了：裂缝尽数消失，冰脊也大为稀少。到第十五天，我正以每天十到十三英里的速度，把大卫·威尔逊的冰坟抛在身后。
然而，仿佛他的手臂仍然伸出来，在那里触碰我。
他的失踪，船上早用一百种猜测解释过了——个个都头头是道。我全然想不到，有谁会把他的死同我牵上什么干系。
可是在我们徒行的第二十二天，离目标一百四十英里处，他却叫一场狂怒与仇恨的大火，在我们三人中间烧了起来。
那是在一天行军结束时，我们肚子空空，骨架子快要散了，心绪凶狠而暴躁。缪的一条狗病了：必须宰掉：他叫我动手。
「哦，」我说，「你自己的狗，当然你自己宰。」
「唔，那可说不准，」他答，火气一下就蹿了上来，「宰人你该是惯了的，杰夫森。」
「你什么意思，缪？」我说，狂性猛地一惊而起——那时疯狂和地狱之火，在我们每个人身上都是说来就来、占了上风的：「你是说，因为我的行当——」
「行当！见鬼，不是，」他像狗似的龇出这句：「去把大卫·威尔逊刨出来——他的去处想必你是认得的——他会把我的意思讲给你，讲得再明白不过。」
我立刻冲到克拉克跟前，他正弓身在狗队里卸套；我狠狠推他的肩膀，噴道：
「那畜生指控我谋杀了大卫·威尔逊！」
「哦？」克拉克说。
「我恨不得劈开他的脑壳——干净利落——！」
「走开，亚当·杰夫森，别缠我！」克拉克咆哮。
「这就完了？——你，就这个态度？」
「见你的鬼去，伙计，我说，别缠我！」他叫道：「你自己的良心，想必你自己最清楚。」
对着这般侮辱，我站着，牙关咬得咯咯响，却无能为力。然而从那一刻起，一种更阴沉的、郁郁的恶意盘踞了我的心。的确，我们三人的心绪都已是一种危险的、几乎要杀人的凶横。在那追逐财富、深入这片寒冷之地的途中，我们差不多快变成那必将灭亡的畜类了。

===Original===

On the 10th April we passed the 89th parallel of latitude, and though sick to death, both in spirit and body, pressed still on. Like the lower animals, we were stricken now with dumbness, and hardly once in a week spoke a word one to the other, but in selfish brutishness on through a real hell of cold we moved. It is a cursed region﻿—beyond doubt cursed﻿—not meant to be penetrated by man: and rapid and awful was the degeneration of our souls. As for me, never could I have conceived that savagery so heinous could brood in a human bosom as now I felt it brood in mine. If men could enter into a country specially set apart for the habitation of devils, and there become possessed of evil, as we were so would they be.

===Chinese===

4月10日，我们越过了北纬89度，虽然身心都已病得要死，仍一步一步不停地向前压去。我们已如低等畜生一般中了喑哑之毒，一个星期也难得彼此说上一句话，只顾各自畜生般自私地穿行于一座真正的冰寒地狱。那是一片受诅咒的地域——毫无疑问受了诅咒——本就不该容人闯入：而我们灵魂的堕落，又快又可怕。至于我，若非亲身感到它此刻在我胸中盘踞，我决计想象不到人的胸怀竟能容纳何等凶残的野性。倘若人当真能闯进一片专为魔鬼居住而设的地方，并在那里被邪祟附体，那么我们此刻的模样，便是他们的下场。

===Original===

As we advanced, the ice every day became smoother; so that, from four miles a day, our rate increased to fifteen, and finally (as the sledges lightened) to twenty.
It was now that we began to encounter a succession of strange-looking objects lying scattered over the ice, whose number continually increased as we proceeded. They had the appearance of rocks, or pieces of iron, incrusted with glass-fragments of various colours, and they were of every size. Their incrustations we soon determined to be diamonds, and other precious stones. On our first twenty-mile day Mew picked up a diamond-crystal as large as a child’s foot, and such objects soon became common. We thus found the riches which we sought, beyond all dream; but as the bear and the walrus find them: for ourselves we had lost; and it was a loss of riches barren as ashes, for all those millions we would not have given an ounce of fish-meal. Clark grumbled something about their being meteor-stones, whose ferruginous substance had been lured by the magnetic Pole, and kept from frictional burning in their fall by the frigidity of the air: and they quickly ceased to interest our sluggish minds, except in so far as they obstructed our way.

===Chinese===

我们向前行进，冰面一天天平滑起来；于是我们的速度从每天四英里增到十五英里，末了（随着雪橇减重）增到二十英里。
正是在这时，我们开始接连遇见一些模样古怪的东西，散落在冰面上，而且越往前走越多。它们看去像岩石，或像一块块铁，外面结着五颜六色的玻璃质碎屑，大小不一。那些结晶皮壳，我们很快便认定是钻石和别的宝石。在我们第一个日行二十英里的日子，缪捻起一块钻石晶体，足有小孩的脚那么大，此后这类东西很快便司空见惯。就这样，我们寻到了梦寐所不能及的财富；可那发现，就像熊和海象觅得它们一样：于我们自身，它们便是失去；那是一种贫瘠如灰烬的财富——纵有那千百万之数，我们也绝不肯拿一盎司鱼粉去换。克拉克咕哝了几句，说它们是陨石，其铁质物被磁极所吸引，坠落时又因空气严寒而未曾摩撚烧毁；很快它们便不再引起我们迟钝的心思的兴趣，除非挡了道。

===Original===

We had all along had good weather: till, suddenly, on the morning of the 13th April, we were overtaken by a tempest from the S. W., of such mighty and solemn volume that the heart quailed beneath it. It lasted in its full power only an hour, but during that time snatched two of our sledges long distances, and compelled us to lie face-downward. We had travelled all the sunlit night, and were gasping with fatigue; so as soon as the wind allowed us to huddle together our scattered things, we crawled into the sleeping-bags, and instantly slept.
We knew that the ice was in awful upheaval around us; we heard, as our eyelids sweetly closed, the slow booming of distant guns, and brittle cracklings of artillery. This may have been a result of the tempest stirring up the ocean beneath the ice. Whatever it was, we did not care: we slept deep.
We were within ten miles of the Pole.

===Chinese===

我们一路天气都好：不料4月13日清晨，西南骤起一场暴风，声势浩大而森严，风声底下，人心都发颤。它全力的肆虐只持续了一个钟头，可在那一个钟头里，它把我们的两架雪橇掳出老远，逼得我们匍匐在地不敢抬头。我们赶了一整夜阳光普照的路，正累得直喘；于是风势刚容我们把四散的东西归拢到一处，我们就爬进睡袋，倒头便睡熟了。
我们知道四周的冰正在可怖地隆起；眼皮甜蜜地合上时，我们还听见远处炮声般的低沉轰鸣，和火炮般的脆响。这也许是暴风搅动了冰下海洋的缘故。管它是什么呢，我们全不在意：我们睡得沉极了。
我们离北极只剩十英里。

===Original===

In my sleep it was as though someone suddenly shook my shoulder with urgent Up! up! It was neither Clark nor Mew, but a dream merely: for Clark and Mew, when I started up, I saw lying still in their sleeping-bag.
I suppose it must have been about noon. I sat staring a minute, and my first numb thought was somehow this: that the Countess Clodagh had prayed me “Be first”﻿—for her. Wondrous little now cared I for the Countess Clodagh in her far unreal world of warmth﻿—precious little for the fortune which she coveted: millions on millions of fortunes lay unregarded around me. But that thought, Be first! was deeply suggested in my brain, as if whispered there. Instinctively, brutishly, as the Gadarean swine rushed down a steep place, I, rubbing my daft eyes, arose.
The first thing which my mind opened to perceive was that, while the tempest was less strong, the ice was now in extraordinary agitation. I looked abroad upon a vast plain, stretched out to a circular, but waving horizon, and varied by many hillocks, boulders, and sparkling meteor-stones that everywhere tinselled the blinding white, some big as houses, most small as limbs. And this great plain was now rearranging itself in a widespread drama of havoc, withdrawing in ravines like mutual backing curtsies, then surging to clap together in passionate mountain-peaks, else jostling like the Symplegades, fluent and inconstant as billows of the sea, grinding itself, piling itself, pouring itself in cataracts of powdered ice, while here and there I saw the meteor-stones leap spasmodically, in dusts and heaps, like geysers or spurting froths in a steamer’s wake, a tremendous uproar, meantime, filling all the air. As I stood, I plunged and staggered, and I found the dogs sprawling, with whimperings, on the heaving floor.
I did not care. Instinctively, daftly, brutishly, I harnessed ten of them to my sledge; put on Canadian snowshoes: and was away northward﻿—alone.
The sun shone with a clear, benign, but heatless shining: a ghostly, remote, yet quite limpid light, which seemed designed for the lighting of other planets and systems, and to strike here by happy chance. A great wind from the S. W., meantime, sent thin snow-sweepings flying northward past me.

===Chinese===

睡梦中，仿佛有人猛地摇我的肩膀，急切地催：起来！起来！那不是克拉克，也不是缪，只是一场梦：因为我惊跳起来时，看见克拉克和缪还静静躺在他们的睡袋里。
我估摸那时必是正午前后。我坐着瞪了一分钟，我那麻木的头一个念头，不知怎么竟是这个：克洛达赫伯爵夫人曾祈求我「争第一」——为了她。如今我对那位身处遥远而不真实的温暖世界里的克洛达赫伯爵夫人，真是满不在乎了——对她觊觎的那份家财，更是视若草芥：千万份的家财，正无人理睬地散在我四周。可是那个念头，争第一！却深深印进我脑子里，仿佛有人在那里耳语。我出于本能、出于痴狂、出于兽性，就像格拉森的猪群冲下陡坡一般，揉着我昏花的眼睛，起身了。
我的心智张开眼后见到的头一件事，便是：暴风虽已减弱，冰面却骚动得异乎寻常。我放眼四望，一片浩大的平原，铺展到一圈起伏不定的环形天际线，其间点缀着无数丘阜、巨砾，还有闪亮的陨石，到处给那炫目的白镀上锡箔似的银光，有的大如房屋，多数小如肢体。而这片大平原，此刻正在一场铺天盖地的大浩劫里重新排布自己：退成一道道深谷，像彼此退避的屈膝礼；随即涌起，狂热地合拢成座座山峰；又像撞岩那样彼此冲撞，流变无常如海上的波涛；磨碾着自己，堆积着自己，把自己倾泻成一道道冰粉的瀑布；同时我到处看见陨石痉挛似的跃起，扬成尘、堆成堆，像间歇泉，又像轮船尾流里喷溅的泡沫；一片震天的喧嚣，同时充塞着整个天空。我站在那里，颠簸踉跄；我发现狗都趴伏在起伏的地面上，呜呜哀鸣。
我毫不在乎。我出于本能、出于痴狂、出于兽性，给其中十条套上我的雪橇；蹬上加拿大雪鞋：便独自往北去了。
太阳明亮而慈祥地照着，却不带一丝热：一种幽异的、邈远的、却又十分澄澈的光，仿佛本是为照亮别的行星、别的星系而设，只是碰巧照到了这里。同时，一股西南来的大风，把薄薄的积雪扫着，从我身边向北飞去。

===Original===

The odometer which I had with me had not yet measured four miles, when I began to notice two things: first that the jewelled meteor-stones were now accumulating beyond all limit, filling my range of vision to the northern horizon with a dazzling glister: in mounds, and parterres, and scattered disconnection they lay, like largesse of autumn leaves, spread out over those Elysian fields and fairy uplands of wealth, trillions of billions, so that I had need to steer my twining way among them. Now, too, I noticed that, but for these stones, all roughness had disappeared, not a trace of the upheaval going on a little further south being here, for the ice lay positively as smooth as a table before me. It is my belief that this stretch of smooth ice has never, never felt one shock, or stir, or throe, and reaches right down to the bottom of the deep.

===Chinese===

我随身的里程计还没量满四英里，我便注意到两件事：第一，那些镶满宝石的陨石，如今堆积得没了限度，把我北面的视野一直填到天际，一片刺目的煌煌：一堆堆、一垄垄、散散落落地躺着，像秋叶的遍赏，铺满那些极乐的田野、财富的仙乡，亿亿兆兆，我得在它们中间绕着弯子择路。这时我也注意到，若没有这些石头，一切崎岖都已消失，稍南边正在进行的掀腾，这里竟无一丝痕迹，因为面前的冰平滑得简直像一张桌子。我相信，这一片平滑的冰，从来不曾、从来不曾受过一次震荡、一次搅动、一阵阵痛，而且一直下到深海的洋底。

===Original===

And now with a wild hilarity I flew. Gradually, a dizziness, a lunacy, had seized upon me, till finally, up-buoyed on air, and dancing mad, I sped, I spun, with grinning teeth that chattered and gibbered, and eyeballs of distraction: for a Fear, too﻿—most cold and dreadful﻿—had its hand of ice upon my heart, I being so alone in that place, face to face with the Ineffable: but still with a giddy levity, and a fatal joy, and a blind hilarity, on I sped, I spun.

===Chinese===

此刻我怀着一种狂野的欢癫飞奔。一种眩晕、一种疯癫渐渐攫住了我，到后来，我竟如浮在空中，狂舞着飞掠、旋转，牙关格格作响，口中咿呀乱语，眼球里满是迷乱：因为还有一种恐惧——最冷最可怕的恐惧——也把它冰凉的手按在我心上，我在这地方如此孤单，与那不可言说者面对面：可我仍旧怀着晕眩的轻浮、致命的欢喜、盲目的欢癫，一路飞掠，一路旋转。

===Original===

The odometer measured nine miles from my start. I was in the immediate neighbourhood of the Pole.
I cannot say when it began, but now I was conscious of a sound in my ears, distinct and near, a steady sound of splashing, or fluttering, resembling the noising of a cascade or brook: and it grew. Forty more steps I took (slide I could not now for the meteorites)﻿—perhaps sixty﻿—perhaps eighty: and now, to my sudden horror, I stood by a circular clean-cut lake.
One minute only, swaying and nodding there, I stood: and then I dropped down flat in swoon.

===Chinese===

里程计量出，离我的出发处九英里。我已到了极点的近旁。
我说不清它是何时开始的，可此刻我已察觉耳中有个声音，清晰而迫近，是一种持续的溅流声，或扑动声，像瀑布或溪流的喧响：而且越来越响。我又走了四十步（此刻陨石遍地，滑不得雪了）——兴许六十步——兴许八十步：蓦然间，我满怀骇异，站在了一个边缘齐整的圆湖旁。
我在那里摇摇晃晃、前俯后仰，只站了一分钟：随即便直挺挺昏倒在地。

===Original===

In a hundred years, I suppose, I should never succeed in analysing *why* I swooned: but my consciousness still retains the impression of that horrid thrill. I saw nothing distinctly, for my whole being reeled and toppled drunken, like a spinning-top in desperate death-struggle at the moment when it flags, and wobbles dissolutely to fall; but the very instant that my eyes met what was before me, I knew, I knew, that here was the Sanctity of Sanctities, the old eternal inner secret of the Life of this Earth, which it was a most burning shame for a man to see. The lake, I fancy, must be a mile across, and in its middle is a pillar of ice, very low and broad; and I had the clear impression, or dream, or notion, that there was a name, or word, graven all round in the ice of the pillar in characters which I could never read; and under the name a long date; and the fluid of the lake seemed to me to be wheeling with a shivering ecstasy, splashing and fluttering, round the pillar, always from west to east, in the direction of the spinning of the earth; and it was borne in upon me﻿—I can’t at all say how﻿—that this fluid was the substance of a living creature; and I had the distinct fancy, as my senses failed, that it was a creature with many dull and anguished eyes, and that, as it wheeled forever round in fluttering lust, it kept its eyes always turned upon the name and the date graven in the pillar. But this must be my madness.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我想，再过一百年，我也未必能分析出自己*为什么*晕厥：可我的意识里至今留着那阵骇人震颤的印象。我什么也没看清，因为我的整个存在都在眩晕、醉倒、摇摇欲坠，像一只濒死的陀螺，在转速已衰、放纵地摇晃着将要倒下的那一刻；可我的目光一触到眼前的东西，我就知道，我就知道，这里是至圣中的至圣，是这大地生命古老而永恒的内在秘密，人见了它是天大的罪过。那湖，我猜，直径总有一英里，湖心立着一根冰柱，很低很粗；我有一个清晰的印象——或者梦境，或者念头——冰柱四周刻着一个名字，或一个字，用的是我永远无法辨认的字符；名字下面是一长串日期；而那湖中的流体，仿佛带着战栗的狂喜绕柱流转，泼溅着、拍扑着，永远是自西向东，顺着地球自转的方向；我心里被灌进了一个确信——我完全说不上是怎么来的——这流体是一个活物的体质；而且，就在我的感官涣散之际，我分明觉得它是一个长着许多只晦暗而痛苦的眼睛的活物，当它永远怀着扑腾的贪欲绕柱流转时，那许多只眼睛始终盯着柱上刻着的名字和日期。可这必定是我的疯癫。……

===Original===

It must have been not less than an hour before a sense of life returned to me; and when the thought stabbed my brain that a long, long time I had lain there in the presence of those gloomy orbs, my spirit seemed to groan and die within me.
In some minutes, however, I had scrambled to my feet, clutched at a dog’s harness, and without one backward glance, was flying from that place.
Halfway to the halting-place, I waited Clark and Mew, being very sick and doddering, and unable to advance. But they did not come.
Later on, when I gathered force to go further, I found that they had perished in the upheaval of the ice. One only of the sledges, half buried, I saw near the spot of our bivouac.

===Chinese===

约莫过了一个钟头，生命的知觉才回到我身上；而当那个念头刺进我的脑子——我竟在那对阴沉沉的眼珠面前躺了那么久、那么久——我的灵魂仿佛在我体内呻吟了一声，便死去了。
不过几分钟，我便已手脚并用地爬起来，攥住一副狗挽具，头也不回一眼，从那地方飞奔而逃。
逃到半路，在往宿营地的中途，我等着克拉克和缪，因为我病得厉害，浑身发抖，再走不动了。可他们没有来。
后来，等我攒起力气再往前走，我才发现他们已丧命于那场冰的掀腾。只有一架雪橇，半埋着，我看见在我们露宿之处的近旁。

===Original===

Alone that same day I began my way southward, and for five days made good progress. On the eighth day I noticed, stretched right across the southeastern horizon, a region of purple vapour which luridly obscured the face of the sun: and day after day I saw it steadily brooding there. But what it could be I did not understand.

===Chinese===

就在那一天，我独自一人踏上了南归的路，头五天进展顺利。第八天，我注意到东南方的地平线上横亘着一片紫色的雾霭，森然地遮蔽了太阳的脸：此后一天又一天，我看着它始终在那里盘踞不去。可它究竟是什么，我并不明白。

===Original===

Well, onward through the desert ice I continued my lonely way, with a baleful shrinking terror in my heart; for very stupendous, alas! is the burden of that Arctic solitude upon one poor human soul.
Sometimes on a halt I have lain and listened long to the hollow silence, recoiling, crushed by it, hoping that at least one of the dogs might whine. I have even crept shivering from the thawed sleeping-bag to flog a dog, so that I might hear a sound.
I had started from the Pole with a well-filled sledge, and the sixteen dogs left alive from the ice-packing which buried my comrades. This was on the evening of the 13th April. I had saved from the wreck of our things most of the whey-powder, pemmican, etc., as well as the theodolite, compass, chronometer, train-oil lamp for cooking, and other implements: I was therefore in no doubt as to my course, and I had provisions for ninety days. But ten days from the start my supply of dog-food failed, and I had to begin to slaughter my only companions, one by one.
Well, in the third week the ice became horribly rough, and with moil and toil enough to wear a bear to death, I did only five miles a day. After the day’s work I would crawl with a dying sigh into the sleeping-bag, clad still in the load of skins which stuck to me a mere filth of grease, to sleep the sleep of a swine, indifferent if I never woke.
Always﻿—day after day﻿—on the southeastern horizon, brooded sullenly that curious stretched-out region of purple vapour, like the smoke of the conflagration of the world. And I noticed that its length constantly reached out and out, and silently grew.

===Chinese===

唉，我便这样穿过荒凉的冰原继续独行，心里怀着一种不祥的、瑟缩的恐怖；因为那极地的孤寂，压在一个可怜的人类灵魂上，那分量，唉！实在大得骇人。
有时中途歇下，我躺着，久久听着那空荡荡的寂静，被它压得退缩、碾碎，巴望至少有一条狗能呜咽一声。我甚至曾打着寒嘇，从暖透了的睡袋里爬出去鞭打一条狗，只为能听见一个声响。
我离开极点时，雪橇装得满满的，还带着从那场埋葬我同件的冰的挤压中活下来的十六条狗。那是四月十三日的傍晚。我从物资的残骸里抢出了大半的乳清粉、干肉饼等等，还有经纬仪、罗盘、天文钟、烧海豹油的炊灯，以及别的器物：因此我对自己的航向毫无疑惑，口粮也还有九十天的。可出发后十天，狗粮告罄，我不得不开始宰杀我仅有的同伴，一条一条来。
唉，到第三个星期，冰面变得可怕地崎岖，那份劳苦足够把一头熊磨死，我一天只走得五英里。一天的活计之后，我总是拖着一口垂死的叹息爬进睡袋，身上还裹着那一身皮衣，黏着我一身油污秽垢，睡猪一般的觉，从此不再醒来也无所谓。
日复一日——东南方的天际上，那一带奇异的、拖长了的紫色蒸汽始终阴沉地盘踞着，像世界大火的烟。我注意到它的长度不断地伸展、伸展，无声地生长着。

===Original===

Once I had a very pleasant dream. I dreamed that I was in a garden﻿—an Arabian paradise﻿—so sweet was the perfume. All the time, however, I had a subconsciousness of the gale which was actually blowing from the S. E. over the ice, and, at the moment when I awoke, was half-wittedly droning to myself; “It is a Garden of Peaches; but I am not really in the garden: I am really on the ice; only, the S. E. storm is wafting to me the aroma of this Garden of Peaches.”
I opened my eyes﻿—I started﻿—I sprang to my feet! For, of all the miracles!﻿—I could not doubt﻿—an actual aroma like peach-blossom was in the algid air about me!
Before I could collect my astonished senses, I began to vomit pretty violently, and at the same time saw some of the dogs, mere skeletons as they were, vomiting, too. For a long time I lay very sick in a kind of daze, and, on rising, found two of the dogs dead, and all very queer. The wind had now changed to the north.
Well, on I staggered, fighting every inch of my deplorably weary way. This odour of peach-blossom, my sickness, and the death of the two dogs, remained a wonder to me.
Two days later, to my extreme mystification (and joy), I came across a bear and its cub lying dead at the foot of a hummock. I could not believe my eyes. There she lay on her right side, a spot of dirty-white in a disordered patch of snow, with one little eye open, and her fierce-looking mouth also; and the cub lay across her haunch, biting into her rough fur. I set to work upon her, and allowed the dogs a glorious feed on the blubber, while I myself had a great banquet on the fresh meat. I had to leave the greater part of the two carcasses, and I can feel again now the hankering reluctance﻿—quite unnecessary, as it turned out﻿—with which I trudged onwards. Again and again I found myself asking: “Now, what could have killed those two bears?”
With brutish stolidness I plodded ever on, almost like a walking machine, sometimes nodding in sleep while I helped the dogs, or manouvred the sledge over an ice-ridge, pushing or pulling. On the 3rd June, a month and a half from my start, I took an observation with the theodolite, and found that I was not yet 400 miles from the Pole, in latitude 84° 50′. It was just as though some Will, some Will, was obstructing and retarding me.

===Chinese===

有一回我做了一个极美的梦。我梦见自己置身一座花园——一座阿拉伯的天堂——花香那样甜。然而自始至终，我潜意识里知道东南方正有一场大风实实在在刮过冰原；醒来那一刻，我还迷迷糊糊地自言自语：『这是一座桃花园；可我并不真在园中：我真的在冰上；只是东南风暴把这座桃花园的香气吹到了我这里。』
我睁开眼睛——我一个激灵——我跳了起来！因为，天底下竟有这样的奇迹！——我无法怀疑——我周围凛冽的空气里，真真切切有一股桃花似的香气！
还没等我把惊愕的神志收拢，我就剧烈地呕吐起来，同时看见几条狗——它们已瘦得只剩骨架——也在呕吐。我昏昏沉沉地病了很久，起身时发现两条狗死了，其余的也都不对劲。风向这时已转成北风。
唉，我继续踉跄前行，在这凄惨疲惫的一路上寸寸苦撑。这桃花的香气、我的呕吐、两条狗的死，在我心里始终是个谜。
两天后，叫我极度困惑（又欢喜）的是，我撞见一头母熊和她的幼崽死在一座冰丘脚下。我简直不敢相信自己的眼睛。她右侧躺着，在一片凌乱的雪地里成了一摊污白，一只小眼睛睁着，那副凶相的嘴也张着；幼崽横卧在她的后胯上，嘴咬进她粗糙的毛里。我动手剥割了她，让狗们饱餐了一顿油脂，我自己也大啖了一顿鲜肉。两具尸身的大部分只好丢弃，此刻我仍能重新感到那种恋恋不舍的牵念——事后证明全无必要——我就是怀着它继续跋涉的。我一遍又一遍地问自己：『到底会是什么东西杀死了这两头熊？』
我怀着畜生般的麻木不断苦走，几乎成了一台走路的机器，有时在帮狗推拉雪橇、翻越冰脊的时候，一边打盹。6月3日，启程已一个半月，我用经纬仪测了一回，发现自己离北极还有将近400英里，在北纬84度50分。那情形，仿佛有一种意志，一种意志，在阻挡我、拖延我。

===Original===

However, the intolerable cold was over, and soon my clothes no longer hung stark on me like armour. Pools began to appear in the ice, and presently, what was worse, my God, long lanes, across which, somehow, I had to get the sledge. But about the same time all fear of starvation passed away: for on the 6th June I came across another dead bear, on the 7th three, and thenceforth, in rapidly growing numbers, I met not bears only, but fulmars, guillemots, snipes, Ross’s gulls, little awks﻿—all, all, lying dead on the ice. And never anywhere a living thing, save me, and the two remaining dogs.
If ever a poor man stood shocked before a mystery, it was I now. I had a big fear on my heart.
On the 2nd July the ice began packing dangerously, and soon another storm broke loose upon me from the S. W. I left off my trek, and put up the silk tent on a five-acre square of ice surrounded by lanes: and *again*﻿—for the second time﻿—as I lay down, I smelled that delightful strange odour of peach-blossom, a mere whiff of it, and presently afterwards was taken sick. However, it passed off this time in a couple of hours.
Now it was all lanes, lanes, alas! yet no open water, and such was the difficulty and woe of my life, that sometimes I would drop flat on the ice, and sob: “Oh, no more, no more, my God: here let me die.” The crossing of a lane might occupy ten or twelve entire hours, and then, on the other side I might find another one opening right before me. Moreover, on the 8th July, one of the dogs, after a feed on blubber, suddenly died; and there was left me only “Reinhardt,” a white-haired Siberian dog, with little pert up-sticking ears, like a cat’s. Him, too, I had to kill on coming to open water.
This did not happen till the 3rd August, nearly four months from the Pole.
I can’t think, my God, that any heart of man ever tholed the appalling nightmare and black abysm of sensations in which, during those four long desert months, I weltered: for though I was as a brute, I had a man’s heart to feel. What I had seen, or dreamed, at the Pole followed and followed me; and if I shut my poor weary eyes to sleep, those others yonder seemed to watch me still with their distraught and gloomy gaze, and in my spinning dark dreams spun that eternal ecstasy of the lake.

===Chinese===

然而，那难耐的严寒总算过去了，不久我的衣服不再像盔甲一样硬邦邦地挂在身上。冰面上开始出现水潭，随即又出现了更糟的东西，我的天，长长的水道，我得想方设法把雪橇弄过去。但大约同一时候，对饥馑的一切恐惧都消失了：因为6月6日我又遇上一头死熊，7日遇上三头，此后，数目急速增多，我遇到的不仅是熊，还有暴风鹱、海鸠、鹬鸟、罗斯鸥、小海雀——全都，全都，死躺在冰上。而四面八方再无一个活物，除了我，和剩下的两条狗。
倘若曾有哪个可怜人惊惶地立在一桩谜面前，那就是此刻的我。我心头压着巨大的恐惧。
7月2日，冰开始危险地挤压，不久又一场风暴从西南朝我狂泄而下。我停下跋涉，在一块五英亩见方、四面临水道的方冰上支起丝绸帐篷：躺下时，*又*——这是第二回——我闻到了那股甜美而奇异的桃花香，只一缕，随后不久便又病了。不过这一回，两个钟头就过去了。
如今到处是水道、水道，唉！却又没有开阔的水面，我生活的艰难与悲苦到了这地步：有时我会扑倒在冰上，呜咽道：『哦，不行了，不行了，我的上帝：就让我死在这里吧。』横渡一条水道可能要花整整十个、十二个钟头，而到了对岸，面前可能又敞开另一条。更有甚者，7月8日，一条狗饱餐一顿油脂后突然死了；我身边只剩『莱因哈特』了，一条白色被毛的西伯利亚狗，长着一对猫似的机灵直竖的小耳朵。待到开阔水面时，连它我也不得不杀了。
那是8月3日的事了，离北极已近四个月。
我想不出，我的上帝，人间哪颗心曾忍受过那四个月荒漠岁月里我所沉沦的可怖噩梦和漆黑深渊般的感受：因为尽管我形同畜生，我还有一颗人的心去感受。我在北极所见——或所梦——的景象一而再、再而三地追着我不放；每当我闭上疲惫可怜的眼睛想睡，那边那些眼睛似乎仍在用它们狂乱阴郁的目光注视着我；而在天旋地转的黑梦里，也转着那片湖永恒的狂喜。

===Original===

However, by the 28th July I knew from the look of the sky, and the absence of freshwater ice, that the sea could not be far; so I set to work, and spent two days in putting to rights the now battered kayak. This done, I had no sooner resumed my way than I sighted far off a streaky haze, which I knew to be the basalt cliffs of Franz Josef Land; and in a craziness of joy I stood there, waving my ski-staff about my head, with the senile cheers of a very old man.
In four days this land was visibly nearer, sheer basaltic cliffs mixed with glacier, forming apparently a great bay, with two small islands in the mid-distance; and at fore-day of the 3rd August I arrived at the definite edge of the pack-ice in moderate weather at about the freezing-point.
I at once, but with great reluctance, shot Reinhardt, and set to work to get the last of the provisions, and the most necessary of the implements, into the kayak, making haste to put out to the toilless luxury of being borne on the water, after all the weary trudge. Within fourteen hours I was coasting, with my little lugsail spread, along the shore-ice of that land. It was midnight of a calm Sabbath, and low on the horizon smoked the drowsing red sun-ball, as my canvas skiff lightly chopped her little way through this silent sea. Silent, silent: for neither snort of walrus, nor yelp of fox, nor cry of startled kittiwake, did I hear: but all was still as the jet-black shadow of the cliffs and glacier on the tranquil sea: and many bodies of dead things strewed the surface of the water.

===Chinese===

不过，到7月28日，我从天色和淡水冰的消失看出，海不会远了；于是我动手花了两天，把那条已破烂的皮划艇修好。刚一上路，我就望见远处一带条纹朦胧的雾霭，我知道那是法兰士约瑟夫地的玄武岩峭壁；我欣喜若狂地站在那里，把滑雪杖抡过头挥舞着，发出老翁似的衰迈欢呼。
四天后，那片陆地明显近了，陡直的玄武岩峭壁夹着冰川，显然围成一个大海湾，中景处有两座小岛；8月3日破晓前，天气温和，气温约在冰点，我到了浮冰群确凿的边缘。
我立刻——却万分不忍地——开枪打死了莱因哈特，然后动手把最后的给养和最必要的器具装进皮划艇，急急下了水，去享受由水承载着的、不需劳碌的奢侈，排遣一路的困顿跋涉。不出十四个小时，我已张起小小的斜挂帆，沿着那片陆地的岸冰轻舟而行。那是一个平静的安息日的子夜，红彤彤的太阳球困倦地冒着烟，低悬在海平线上；我的帆布小艇轻轻犁开这寂静之海，一路款款前行。寂静啊，寂静：因为海象的鼻息、狐狸的吠叫、受惊三趾鸥的鸣声，我一概听不见：万物静得如同峭壁和冰川投在平静海面上的漆黑影子：而许多死去的生灵，零落漂浮在水面上。

===Original===

When I found a little fjord, I went up it to the end where stood a stretch of basalt columns, looking like a shattered temple of Antediluvians; and when my foot at last touched land, I sat down there a long, long time in the rubbly snow, and silently wept. My eyes that night were like a fountain of tears. For the firm land is health and sanity, and dear to the life of man; but I say that the great ungenial ice is a nightmare, and a blasphemy, and a madness, and the realm of the Power of Darkness.

===Chinese===

遇见一处小峡湾，我便顺着它走到尽头，那里立着一段玄武岩石柱，看去像一座倾颓的洪水以前之人的神庙；当我的脚终于踏上陆地，我在碎石雪地里坐下，坐了许久许久，默默地流泪。那晚我的双眼像一眼泪泉。因为坚实的陆地是健康与神志清明，是人类生命之所亲；而我说，那片广大而不育的冰原，是一场噩梦，是一句亵渎，是一种疯狂，是黑暗权势的疆土。

===Original===

I knew that I was at Franz Josef Land, somewhere or other in the neighbourhood of C. Fligely (about 82° N.), and though it was so late, and getting cold, I still had the hope of reaching Spitzbergen that year, by alternately sailing all open water, and dragging the kayak over the slack drift-ice. All the ice which I saw was good flat fjord-ice, and the plan seemed feasible enough; so after coasting about a little, and then three days’ good rest in the tent at the bottom of a ravine of columnar basalt opening upon the shore, I packed some bear and walrus flesh, with what artificial food was left, into the kayak, and I set out early in the morning, coasting the shore-ice with sail and paddle. In the afternoon I managed to climb a little way up an iceberg, and made out that I was in a bay whose terminating headlands were invisible. I accordingly decided to make S. W. by W. to cross it, but, in doing so, I was hardly out of sight of land, when a northern storm overtook me toward midnight; before I could think, the little sail was all but whiffed away, and the kayak upset. I only saved it by the happy chance of being near a floe with an ice-foot, which, projecting under the water, gave me foothold; and I lay on the floe in a mooning state the whole night under the storm, for I was half drowned.
And at once, on recovering myself, I abandoned all thought of whalers and of Europe for that year. Happily, my instruments, etc., had been saved by the kayak-deck when she capsized.

===Chinese===

我知道自己到了法兰士约瑟夫地，就在弗利格利角（约北纬 82 度）这一带的什么地方；虽然时节已晚，天气渐冷，我仍抱着当年就到斯匹次卑尔根的希望，打算交替着：凡开阔水面便行船，遇松散浮冰便拖着皮筏走。我看见的冰全是上好的平坦峡湾冰，这计划似乎颇为可行；于是我沿岸略作侦察，又在一处临海的玄武岩石柱峡谷底上的帐篷里好好歇了三天，然后把一些熊肉、海象肉，连同剩下的人工口粮，装进皮筏，一清早便出发，扬帆打桨沿着岸边的水道而行。下午，我设法爬上一座冰山一小段，看清自己正处在一个海湾里，两端的岬角都望不见。于是我决定取西南偏西的方向横渡；可这样走时，我离陆地还没望不见呢，一场北来的风暴便在午夜前后追上了我；我还来不及想，小帆几乎被一卷而空，皮筏也翻了。只因侥幸挨着一块带冰脚的浮冰，那冰脚在水面下探出来，给了我立足之处，我才把它救住；风暴里，我半身湿透、神志恍惚，在浮冰上躺了整夜。
一缓过来，我立刻打消了当年找到捕鲸船、回欧洲去的念头。所幸船具仪器等等，在皮筏倾覆时都被筏甲挡住，保住了。

===Original===

A hundred yards inland from the shore-rim, in a circular place where there was some moss and soil, I built myself a semi-subterranean Eskimo den for the long Polar night. The spot was quite surrounded by high sloping walls of basalt, except to the west, where they opened in a three-foot cleft to the shore, and the ground was strewn with slabs and boulders of granite and basalt. I found there a dead she-bear, two well-grown cubs, and a fox, the latter having evidently fallen from the cliffs; in three places the snow was quite red, overgrown with a red lichen, which at first I took for blood. I did not even yet feel secure from possible bears, and took care to make my den fairly tight, a work which occupied me nearly four weeks, for I had no tools, save a hatchet, knife, and metal-shod ski-staff. I dug a passage in the ground two feet wide, two deep, and ten long, with perpendicular sides, and at its north end a circular space, twelve feet across, also with perpendicular sides, which I lined with stones; the whole excavation I covered with inch-thick walrus-hide, skinned during a whole bitter week from four of a number that lay about the shore-ice; for ridgepole I used a thin pointed rock which I found near, though, even so, the roof remained nearly flat. This, when it was finished, I stocked well, putting in everything, except the kayak, blubber to serve both for fuel and occasional light, and foods of several sorts, which I procured by merely stretching out the hand. The roof of both circular part and passage was soon buried under snow and ice, and hardly distinguishable from the general level of the white-clad ground. Through the passage, if I passed in or out, I crawled flat, on hands and knees: but that was rare: and in the little round interior, mostly sitting in a cowering attitude, I wintered, harkening to the large and windy ravings of darkling December storms above me.

===Chinese===

离岸缘向内陆一百码，有一处长着苔藓和泥土的圆形空地，我在那里为自己挖了一座半地下的爱斯基摩式洞穴，好度过漫长的极夜。那地方四周尽是玄武岩筑成的高斜壁，唯有西面开着一道三英尺宽的裂缝通向海岸，地面上散落着花岗岩和玄武岩的石板与巨砾。我在那里发现一头死母熊、两只半大的幼崽和一只狐狸，那狐狸显然是从峭壁上坠下来的；有三处雪地红彤彤的，长满了一种红色地衣，起初我还当是血。即便到了这时，我仍担心会不会再有熊来，便着意把洞穴弄得相当严实，这工程占了我将近四个星期，因为我没有工具，只有一把小斧、一把刀和一根包铁的滑雪杖。我在地里挖了一条两英尺宽、两英尺深、十英尺长的甬道，两壁垂直，甬道北端挖出一间十二英尺宽的圆形空间，两壁也垂直，内壁我砌了石头；整个挖掘面上盖了一英寸厚的海象皮——那皮是我花了整整一个艰苦的星期，从岸冰上躺着的一群海象里剥下四张而来；栋梁用的是附近找到的一根细而尖的岩石条，即便如此，顶还是近乎平的。洞一完工，我便把它囤得满满的，除了皮划艇，什么都放了进去：既当柴火又偶尔照明的油脂，还有各样吃食，那些东西我只需伸伸手就能弄到。圆形主室和甬道的顶不久便埋在冰雪之下，与那银装素裹的地面浑然难辨。进出甬道时，我得手脚并用匍匐而行：不过那也很少：就在那小小的圆形内室里，多半缩着身子坐着，我过完了冬，听着头顶上黑暗的十二月风暴那盛大而多风的咆哮。

===Original===

All those months the burden of a thought bowed me; and an unanswered question, like the slow turning of a mechanism, revolved in my gloomy spirit: for everywhere around me lay bears, walruses, foxes, thousands upon thousands of little awks, kittiwakes, snow-owls, eider-ducks, gulls﻿—dead, dead. Almost the only living things which I saw were some walruses on the drift-floes: but very few compared with the number which I expected. It was clear to me that some inconceivable catastrophe had overtaken the island during the summer, destroying all life about it, except some few of the amphibia, cetacea, and crustacea.
On the 5th December, having crept out from the den during a southern storm, I had, for the third time, a distant whiff of that selfsame odour of peach-blossom: but now without any aftereffects.

===Chinese===

那几个月里，一个念头的重担一直压弯着我；一个无人应答的问题，像机括的缓转，在我阴郁的灵魂里回旋不歇：因为我的四周，遍地躺着熊、海象、狐狸，成千上万的侏海雀、三趾鸥、雪鸮、绒鸭、海鸥——都死了，死了。我见着的活物，几乎只有浮冰上的几头海象：但比起我预期的数目来，寥寥无几。我心里明白：夏季里，某场不可思议的大灾曾降临这岛，毁去了它四周的一切生灵，只除了两棲类、鲸类和甲壳类中的少数。
十二月五日，趁一场南来的风暴，我从兽穴里爬出，第三次远远闻到了那同一股桃花的气味：只是这一回，没有留下任何后遗。

===Original===

Well, again came Christmas, the New Year﻿—Spring: and on the 22nd May I set out with a well-stocked kayak. The water was fairly open, and the ice so good, that at one place I could sail the kayak over it, the wind sending me sliding at a fine pace. Being on the west coast of Franz Josef Land, I was in as favourable a situation as possible, and I turned my bow southward with much hope, keeping a good many days just in sight of land. Toward the evening of my third day out I noticed a large flat floe, presenting far-off a singular and lovely sight, for it seemed freighted thick with a profusion of pink and white roses, showing in its clear crystal the empurpled reflection. On getting near I saw that it was covered with millions of Ross’s gulls, all dead, whose pretty rosy bosoms had given it that appearance.
Up to the 29th June I made good progress southward and westward (the weather being mostly excellent), sometimes meeting dead bears, floating away on floes, sometimes dead or living walrus-herds, with troop after troop of dead kittiwakes, glaucus and ivory gulls, skuas, and every kind of Arctic fowl. On that last day﻿—the 29th June﻿—I was about to encamp on a floe soon after midnight, when, happening to look toward the sun, my eye fell, far away south across the ocean of floes, upon something﻿—*the masts of a ship*.
A phantom ship, or a real ship: it was all one; real, I must have instantly felt, it could not be: but at a sight so incredible my heart set to beating in my bosom as though I must surely die, and feebly waving the cane oar about my head, I staggered to my knees, and thence with wry mouth toppled flat.
So overpoweringly sweet was the thought of springing once more, like the beasts of Circe, from a walrus into a man. At this time I was tearing my bear’s-meat just like a bear; I was washing my hands in walrus-blood to produce a glairy sort of pink cleanliness, in place of the black grease which chronically coated them.
Worn as I was, I made little delay to set out for that ship; and I had not travelled over water and ice four hours when, to my indescribable joy, I made out from the top of a steep floe that she was the *Boreal*.

===Chinese===

唉，圣诞节又来了，新年——春天：五月二十二日，我带着装备充足的皮筏出发了。水面相当开阔，冰面又好，有一处我甚至能驾着皮筏在冰上滑行，风推着我以可观的速度溜去。我既在法兰士约瑟夫地的西岸，处境再有利不过，便满怀希望地把船头转向南方，好些天里始终保持着望得见陆地。出发后的第三天傍晚，我注意到一大块平坦的浮冰，远远呈现出一派奇异而可爱的景象：它仿佛密密层层地载满了粉白相间的玫瑰，在它清澈的晶体里映着紫红的倒影。走近了我才看清，那上面卧着千百万只罗氏鸥，全都死了，是它们玫瑰色的美丽胸脯，给了浮冰那副模样。
到六月二十九日，我向南向西的行程都很顺利（天气多半极好），时而遇见死熊随浮冰漂走，时而遇见死了的或还活着的海象群，还有一队接一队死去的三趾鸥、灰鸥、白鸥、贼鸥，以及各种极地飞禽。就在最后那天——六月二十九日——刚过午夜我正要在浮冰上扎营，无意间朝太阳那边一望，目光越过南面茫茫的浮冰之海，落在一样东西上——一支船的桅杆。
幽灵船也好，真船也好：全是一回事；它必是真的，我立刻就该觉出，不可能：可一见这难以置信的景象，我的心在胸膛里狂跳，仿佛必死无疑；我衰弱地挥着手中那支桨，踉跄跪倒，又歪着嘴，直挺挺扑倒在地。
再变回人——像喀耳刻的兽群那样，从一头海象重新跃变为一个人——这念头甜美得令人难以承受。这时节我撕食熊肉活像一头熊；我把手浸在海象血里洗，好换得一种黏糊糊的粉红色的「干净」，以代替常年糊在上面的黑油。
我顾不得疲惫，不多耽搁便朝那船出发了；在水上和冰上走了不到四个钟头，从一块陡峭浮冰的顶上，我认出了她是「北极号」——那份欢喜，无可言表。

===Original===

It seemed most strange that she should be anywhere hereabouts: I could only conclude that she must have forced and drifted her way thus far westward out of the ice-block in which our party had left her, and perhaps now was loitering here in the hope of picking us up on our way to Spitzbergen.
In any case, wild was the haste with which I fought my way to be at her, my gasping mouth all the time drawn back in a rictus of laughter at the anticipation of their gladness to see me, their excitement to hear the grand tidings of the Pole attained. Anon I waved the paddle, though I knew that they could not yet see me, and then I dug deep at the whitish water. What astonished me was her mainsail and foremast square-sail﻿—set that calm morning; and her screws were still, for she moved not at all. The sun was abroad like a cold spirit of light, touching the great ocean-room of floes with dazzling spots, and a tint almost of rose was on the world, as it were of a just-dead bride in her spangles and white array. The *Boreal* was the one little distant jet-black spot in all this purity: and upon her, as though she were Heaven, I paddled, I panted. But she was in a queerish state: by 9 a.m. I could see that. Two of the windmill arms were not there, and half lowered down her starboard beam a boat hung askew; moreover, soon after 10 I could clearly see that her mainsail had a long rent down the middle.
I could not at all make her out. She was not anchored, though a sheet-anchor hung over at the starboard cathead; she was not moored; and two small ice-floes, one on each side, were sluggishly bombarding her bows.
I began now to wave the paddle, battling for my breath, ecstatic, crazy with excitement, each second like a year to me. Very soon I could make out someone at the bows, leaning well over, looking my way. Something put it into my head that it was Sallitt, and I began an impassioned shouting. “Hi! Sallitt! Hallo! Hi!” I called.
I did not see him move: I was still a good way off: but there he stood, leaning steadily over, looking my way.

===Chinese===

她竟在这一带出现，实在蹊跷：我只能断定，她必是从我们一行离开她时所困的那片冰块里，一路破冰、漂流到了这么远的西边，如今也许正在此地逡巡，指望在我们去斯匹次卑尔根的路上接上我们。
无论如何，我拼死向她赶去，那份急切是狂野的；我喘息的嘴一直咧成一副笑的痉挛，想着他们见到我时该多欢喜，听到极点已达的伟大消息时该多兴奋。不多时我便挥起桨来，虽然明知他们还望不见我，随后我便向发白的水里深深刨去。叫我纳闷的是她的主帆和前桅横帆——那个无风的清晨竟张着；她的螺旋桨一动不动，因为她压根儿不动。太阳四照，像一位清冷的光之精灵，给浮冰的大洋铺上炫目的光斑；世界上几乎染着一层玫瑰色，像一位刚刚死去的新娘，浑身亮片、一身白裳。「北极号」是这一切皎洁中唯一一个遥远的漆黑小点：朝着她，仿佛朝着天堂，我打桨，我喘息。可她的情形有些古怪：到上午九点我已看得出来。两只风车翼不见了，右舷舷侧半降着一只挂歪了的小艇；而且十点过后不久，我清楚地看见她的主帆中间裂着一道长口子。
我全然摸不透她。她没有抛锚，虽然右舷锚架上吊着一只大锚；她没有系泊；两块小浮冰，一左一右，正懒洋洋地撞击她的船头。
这时我开始挥动船桨，喘息奋斗，欣喜若狂，兴奋得发疯，每一秒于我都像一年。很快我辨认出船头有人，深深地探出身来，朝我这边望。不知怎么我心里认定那是萨利特，便激昂地喊起来。「喂！萨利特！哈罗！喂！」我喊。
我没看见他动：我离得还远：可他就那么站着，定定地探身，朝我这边望。

===Original===

Between me and the ship now was all navigable water among the floes, and the sight of him so visibly near put into me such a shivering eagerness, that I was nothing else but a madman for the time, sending the kayak flying with venomous digs in quick-repeated spurts, and mixing with the diggings my crazy wavings, and with both the daft shoutings of “Hallo! Hi! Bravo! I have *been to the Pole*!”
Well, vanity, vanity. Nearer still I drew: it was broad morning, going on toward noon: I was half a mile away, I was fifty yards. But on board the *Boreal*, though now they *must* have heard me, seen me, I observed no movement of welcome, but all, all was still as death that still Arctic morning, my God. Only, the ragged sail flapped a little, and﻿—one on each side﻿—two ice-floes sluggishly bombarded the bows, with hollow sounds.
I was certain now that Sallitt it was who looked across the ice: but when the ship swung a little round, I noticed that the direction of his gaze was carried with her movement, he no longer looking my way.
“Why, Sallitt!” I shouted reproachfully: “why, Sallitt, man﻿ ﻿… !” I whined.
But even as I shouted and whined, a perfect wild certainty was in my heart: for an aroma like peach, my God, had been suddenly wafted from the ship upon me, and I must have very well known then that that watchful outlook of Sallitt saw nothing, and on the *Boreal* were dead men all; indeed, very soon I saw one of his eyes looking like a glass eye which has slid askew, and glares distraught. And now again my wretched body failed, and my head dropped forward, where I sat, upon the kayak-deck.

===Chinese===

此刻我与船之间，浮冰间全是可航的水；望见他离得这么真切，一阵战栗的渴望灌进我身体，一时我纯粹就是个疯子，一阵紧似一阵地狠力插桨，皮筏飞掠，插桨之间夹着我疯狂的挥舞，夹着两样并作的痴狂叫喊：「哈罗！喂！好啊！我『到过极点』了！」
唉，虚荣，虚荣。我更近了：已是明晃晃的上午，快到中午：我离半英里，我离五十码。可「北极号」上——这会儿他们必是听见我、望见我了——我却看不到一丝欢迎的动静，一切都是，一切都是死一般的寂静，那个寂静的极地上午，我的上帝。只有破帆微微扑动，还有——一左一右——两块浮冰懒洋洋地撞击着船头，声音空空的。
此刻我确信，隔着冰张望的正是萨利特：可当船身微微转了转，我注意到他目光的方向跟着船转了过去，他已不再朝我这边望了。
「嘆，萨利特！」我责备地喊：「嘆，萨利特，伙计……！」我哀声叫。
可就在我又喊又哀的同时，一种完完全全的、狂野的确信已在我心里：因为一股桃子似的香气，我的上帝，突然从船上向我飘来；那时我必定已经全然明白：萨利特那警戒的了望什么也看不见，「北极号」上尽是死人；果然，很快我便看见他的一只眼睛，像一只滑歪了的玻璃眼，直愣愣地瞪着，没了神采。接着我这具可怜的身体又垮了，我一坐之处，头向前一栽，栽在皮筏的甲板上。

===Original===

Well, after a long time, I lifted myself to look again at that forlorn and wandering craft. There she lay, quiet, tragic, as it were culpable of the dark secret she bore; and Sallitt, who had been such good friends with me, would not cease his stare. I knew quite well why he was there: he had leant over to vomit, and had leant ever since, his forearms pressed on the bulwark-beam, his left knee against the boards, and his left shoulder propped on the cathead. When I came quite near, I saw that with every bump of the two floes against the bows, his face shook in response, and nodded a little; strange to say, he had no covering on his head, and I noted the play of the faint breezes in his uncut hair. After a time I would approach no more, for I was afraid; I did not dare, the silence of the ship seemed so sacred and awful; and till late afternoon I sat there, watching the black and massive hull. Above her waterline emerged all round a half-floating fringe of fresh-green seaweed, proving old neglect; an abortive attempt had apparently been made to lower, or take in, the larch-wood pram, for there she hung by a jammed davit-rope, stern up, bow in the water; the only two arms of the windmill moved this way and that, through some three degrees, with an andante creaking singsong; some washed clothes, tied on the bowsprit rigging to dry, were still there; the iron casing all round the bluff bows was red and rough with rust; at several points the rigging was in considerable tangle; occasionally the boom moved a little with a tortured skirling cadence; and the sail, rotten, I presume, from exposure﻿—for she had certainly encountered no bad weather﻿—gave out anon a heavy languid flap at a rent down the middle. Besides Sallitt, looking out there where he had jammed himself, I saw no one.
By a paddle-stroke now, and another presently, I had closely approached her about four in the afternoon, though my awe of the ship was complicated by that perfume of hers, whose fearful effects I knew. My tentative approach, however, proved to me, when I remained unaffected, that, here and now, whatever danger there had been was past; and finally, by a hanging rope, with a thumping desperation of heart, I clambered up her beam.

===Chinese===

嗯，过了许久，我才直起身来，再看那艘孤寂漂泊的船。她躺在那里，安静而凄惨，仿佛为自身载着的黑暗秘密负疚；而萨利特——他曾与我要好——始终定睛凝望，一刻不停。我很清楚他为什么在那里：他本是俯身要呕吐，此后就一直俯在那里，两前臂压在舷墙梁上，左膝抵着船板，左肩拄在锚架上。等我靠得很近时，我看见两块浮冰每一次撞上船头，他的脸就应声一颤，微微点头；说来奇怪，他头上没有一点遮盖，我看得分明，微风在他未剪的头发里穿来穿去。过了一阵，我再也不敢靠近，因为我怕；我不敢，这船的寂静显得那样神圣而可畏；直到下午很晚，我一直坐在那里，望着那漆黑庞大的船体。她的水线以上，四周浮着半截新鲜翠绿的海藻裙边，可见被搁置已久；有人显然试图放下、或收进那条落叶松木的小平底船，却半途而废，此刻它还吊在一根卡死的吊柱索上，尾朝天，头泡在水里；风车仅存的两片翼板，在三度光景的范围内摆来摆去，发出行板般吱吱呀呀的吟唱；几件洗过的衣裳，系在船首桁索具上晾晒，还原样挂在那里；钝圆船首四周的铁包皮，锈得又红又糙；索具有好几处缠得一塌糊涂；偶尔帆桁带着一阵惨厉的锐鸣挪动一下；而那面帆——想必是风吹日晒朽了，因为她肯定没遇上过恶劣天气——不时从中缝的一道裂口里，发出一声沉重的、没精打采的拍打。除了从挤在那里探望的萨利特，我再看不见一个人。
下午四点光景，我划一桨，停一停，再划一桨，终于贴近了她，虽然我对这船的敬畏，还掺着她身上那股香气的忌惮，它的可怕效力我领教过。然而试探地靠近之后，我见自己安然无恙，便明白此时此地，无论先前有过什么凶险，都已经过去了；最后，攀着一根垂下来的缆绳，我心口砰砰狂跳，绝望地一横，爬上了她的舷侧。

===Original===

They had died, it seemed, very suddenly, for nearly all the twelve were in poses of activity. Egan was in the very act of ascending the companionway; Lamburn was sitting against the chart-room door, apparently cleaning two carbines; Odling at the bottom of the engine-room stair seemed to be drawing on a pair of reindeer komagar; and Cartwright, who was often in liquor, had his arms frozen tight round the neck of Martin, whom he seemed to be kissing, they two lying stark at the foot of the mizzenmast.
Over all﻿—over men, decks, rope-coils﻿—in the cabin, in the engine-room﻿—between skylight leaves﻿—on every shelf, in every cranny﻿—lay a purplish ash or dust, very impalpably fine. And steadily reigning throughout the ship, like the very spirit of death, was that aroma of peach-blossom.

===Chinese===

看样子，他们死得很突然，因为十二个人几乎个个都保持着劳作中的姿势。伊根正攀在舱梯半腰；兰伯恩靠着海图室的门坐着，像是在擦拭两支卡宾枪；奥德林在机舱楼梯脚下，似乎正在登一双驯鹿皮靴；而时常酩酊的卡特赖特，双臂冻得死紧地搂着马丁的脖子，像在亲他，两人直挺挺地躺在后桅脚下。
所有一切之上——人的身上、甲板上、绳圈上——舱里、机舱里——天窗的窗叶之间——每个架上、每个缝隙里——都落着一层发紫的灰烬或尘，细得无形无质。而那股桃花的香气，始终君临全船，一如死亡的精魂本身。

===Original===

Here it had reigned, as I could see from the log-dates, from the rust on the machinery, from the look of the bodies, from a hundred indications, during something over a year. It was, therefore, mainly by the random workings of winds and currents that this fragrant ship of death had been brought hither to me.
And this was the first direct intimation which I had that the Unseen Powers (whoever and whatever they may be), who through the history of the world had been so very, very careful to conceal their Hand from the eyes of men, hardly any longer intended to be at the pains to conceal their Hand from me. It was just as though the *Boreal* had been openly presented to me by a spiritual agency, which, though I could not see it, I could readily apprehend.

===Chinese===

从航海日志的日期、机器上的锈、尸体的模样，从上百种迹象看，这东西在这里统治了一年多一点。可见，这条芳香的死亡之船之所以被带到此处、送到我手上，主要是风与洋流随意驱遣的结果。
而这，是我得到的第一个直接的暗示：那班看不见的权能（不论他们是谁、是什么）——他们在整个世界史上，一向那般、那般小心地在人眼前遮掩他们的手——如今几乎不再打算费心在我面前遮掩他们的手了。这就好比「北极号」是被一个灵界的力量公开送到我面前的；那力量，我虽看不见，却能轻易领会。

===Original===

The dust, though very thin and flighty above-decks, lay thickly deposited below, and after having made a tour of investigation throughout the ship, the first thing which I did was to examine that﻿—though I had tasted nothing all day, and was exhausted to death. I found my own microscope where I had left it in the box in my berth to starboard, though I had to lift up Egan to get at it, and to step over Lamburn to enter the chart-room; but there, toward evening, I sat at the table and bent to see if I could make anything of the dust, while it seemed to me as if all the myriad spirits of men that have sojourned on the earth, and angel and devil, and all Time and all Eternity, hung silent round for my decision; and such an ague had me, that for a long time my wandering fingertips, all ataxic with agitation, eluded every delicate effort which I made, and I could nothing do. Of course, I know that an odour of peach-blossom in the air, resulting in death, could only be associated with some vaporous effluvium of cyanogen, or of hydrocyanic (“prussic”) acid, or of both; and when I at last managed to examine some of the dust under the microscope, I was not therefore surprised to find, among the general mass of purplish ash, a number of bright-yellow particles, which could only be minute crystals of potassic ferrocyanide. What potassic ferrocyanide was doing on board the *Boreal* I did not know, and I had neither the means, nor the force of mind, alas! to dive then further into the mystery; I understood only that by some extraordinary means the air of the region just south of the Polar environ had been impregnated with a vapour which was either cyanogen, or some product of cyanogen; also, that this deadly vapour, which is very soluble, had by now either been dissolved by the sea, or else dispersed into space (probably the latter), leaving only its faint after-perfume; and seeing this, I let my poor abandoned head drop again on the table, and long hours I sat there staring mad, for I had a suspicion, my God, and a fear, in my breast.

===Chinese===

那灰尘在甲板上方虽又薄又轻，下层舱里却积得厚厚的。我把全船巡查一遍之后，头一件事就是来检验这灰——尽管我一整天粒米未进，已累得要死。我在右舷自己铺位的箱子里找到了我留下的显微镜，虽然得先把伊根抬起来才拿得到，又得跨过兰伯恩才进得了海图室；可就在那里，傍晚时分，我坐在桌前俯身去瞧，看能不能从这灰尘里瞧出些名堂；那一刻，我仿佛觉得，曾在世上寄居过的亿万人灵，天使与魔鬼，全部的时间与全部的永恒，都默默围在我四周等我的裁决；我寒战得那么厉害，以致好半天里，我游移的指尖激动得全不听使唤，每一次精细的摆弄都落了空，什么也做不成。当然，我明白，空气中桃花似的香气竟能致人死命，这只能同某种气态的氰气体，或者氢氰酸（即『普鲁士酸』）的气味，或两者的混合相关；待我终于设法把一些灰尘放到显微镜下检验时，我发现，在那一片发紫的灰烬总体中，杂着许多亮黄色的微粒，那只能是亚铁氰化钾的细小结晶——对此我并不惊讶。亚铁氰化钾到*博雷尔*号上来做什么，我不知道；而我当时既没有手段，唉，也没有心气，再往这谜底深处钻了；我只明白了两件事：一是某种异乎寻常的方式，使极区紧南面的空气饱含了一种蒸气，它或是氰，或是氰的某种产物；二是这致命的蒸气极易溶解，如今不是已被海水溶解，便是已消散于太空（多半是后者），只留下一缕淡淡的余香；看清这些，我让我那颗被弃的可怜脑袋又垂到桌上，久久地坐在那里，瞪着眼发怔，因为我心里有一样疑心，我的上帝，和一样恐惧。

===Original===

The *Boreal*, I found, contained sufficient provisions, untouched by the dust, in cases, casks, etc., to last me, probably, fifty years. After two days, when I had partially scrubbed and boiled the filth of fifteen months from my skin, and solaced myself with better food, I overhauled her thoroughly, and spent three more days in oiling and cleaning the engine. Then, all being ready, I dragged my twelve dead and laid them together in two rows on the chart-room floor; and I hoisted for love the poor little kayak which had served me through so many tribulations. At nine in the morning of the 6th July, a week from my first sighting of the *Boreal*, I descended to the engine-room to set out.
The screws, like those of most quite modern ships, were driven by the simple contrivance of a constant stream of liquid air, contained in very powerful tanks, exploding through capillary tubes into non-expansion slide-valve chests, much as in the ordinary way with steam: a motor which gave her, in spite of her bluff hulk, a speed of sixteen knots. It is, therefore, the simplest thing for one man to take these ships round the world, since their movement, or stopping, depend upon nothing but the depressing or raising of a steel handle, provided that one does not get blown to the sky meantime, as liquid air, in spite of its thousand advantages, occasionally blows people. At any rate, I had tanks of air sufficient to last me through twelve years’ voyaging; and there was the ordinary machine on board for making it, with forty tons of coal, in case of need, in the bunkers, and two excellent Belleville boilers: so I was well supplied with motors at least.
The ice here was quite slack, and I do not think I ever saw Arctic weather so bright and gay, the temperature at 41°. I found that I was midway between Franz Josef and Spitzbergen, in latitude 79° 23′ N. and longitude 39° E.; my way was perfectly clear; and something almost like a mournful hopefulness was in me as the engines slid into their clanking turmoil, and those long-silent screws began to churn the Arctic sea. I ran up with alacrity and took my stand at the wheel; and the bows of my eventful Argo turned southward and westward.

===Chinese===

「北极号」上，我发现存粮充足，箱里桶里，尘埃未染，大约够我吃五十年。两天后，我把十五个月的污垢从皮肤上刷洗、煮烫去了一部分，又用较好的饭食犒劳了自己，便把她彻底检修了一遍，又花三天给机器上油、擦洗。然后，一切就绪，我把十二具尸体拖拢，并排成两行，安放在海图室的地板上；又怀着爱意，把那只陪我历经无数磨难的小皮筏吊上船。七月六日上午九点——初次望见「北极号」之后一星期——我下到机舱，准备启航。
她的螺旋桨，同大多数最新式的船一样，靠一个简单的装置驱动：一股持续的液态空气流，盛在极坚固的储罐里，经毛细管爆入不膨胀的滑阀箱，原理大抵同寻常的蒸汽机一样：这马达使她尽管船身圆钝，航速仍达十六节。因此，一个人驾这样的船环游世界，是再简单不过的事：她的行与止，只系于一根钢杆的压下或抬起——只要那人中途不被炸上天，因为液态空气尽管有千般好处，偶尔还是会把人炸上天。无论如何，我船上的空气罐足以供我十二年的航行；船上还有制造液态空气的寻常机器，煤舱里备着应急的四十吨煤，还有两座上好的贝尔维尔锅炉：所以，至少在动力方面，我装备得很充足。
这里的冰相当松散，而且我不记得见过这样明丽欢快的极地天气，气温四十一度。我发现自己正在法兰士约瑟夫地与斯匹次卑尔根之间的中途，北纬 79 度 23 分、东经 39 度；一路畅通无阻；当机器滑入它们铿锵的骚动、那久已沉默的螺旋桨开始搅动极地的海洋时，我心里有一种近乎哀伤的希望。我轻快地跑上去，站到舵轮旁；我那历尽风波的阿尔戈之船，船头转向了南，转向了西。

===Original===

When I needed food or sleep, the ship slept, too: when I awoke, she continued her way.
Sixteen hours a day sometimes I stood sentinel at that wheel, overlooking the varied monotony of the ice-sea, till my knees would give, and I wondered why a wheel at which one might sit was not contrived, rather delicate steering being often required among the floes and bergs. By now, however, I was less weighted with my ball of Polar clothes, and stood almost slim in a Lap greatcoat, a round Siberian fur cap on my head.
At midnight when I threw myself into my old berth, it was just as though the engines, subsided now into silence, were a dead thing, and had a ghost which haunted me; for I heard them still, and yet not them, but the silence of their ghost.
Sometimes I would startle from sleep, horrified to the heart at some sound of exploding iceberg, or bumping floe, noising far through that white mystery of quietude, where the floes and bergs were as floating tombs, and the world a liquid cemetery. Never could I describe the strange Doom’s-day shock with which such a sound would recall me from far depths of chaos to recollection of myself: for oftentimes, both waking and in nightmare, I did not know on which planet I was, nor in which Age, but felt myself adrift in the great gulf of time and space and circumstance, without bottom for my consciousness to stand upon; and the world was all mirage and a new show to me; and the boundaries of dream and waking lost.
Well, the weather was most fair all the time, and the sea like a pond. During the morning of the fifth day, the 11th July, I entered, and went moving down, an extraordinary long avenue of snow-bergs and floes, most regularly placed, half a mile across and miles long, like a Titanic double-procession of statues, or the Ming Tombs, but rising and sinking on the cadenced swell; many towering high, throwing placid shadows on the aisle between; some being of a lucid emerald tint; and three or four pouring down cascades that gave a far and chaunting sound. The sea between was of a strange thick bluishness, almost like raw egg-white; while, as always here, some snow-clouds, white and woolly, floated in the pale sky. Down this avenue, which produced a mysterious impression of Cyclopean cathedrals and odd sequesteredness, I had not passed a mile, when I sighted a black object at the end.

===Chinese===

我要吃要睡时，船也睡；我醒来，她便继续她的路。
有时一天十六个钟头，我站在那舵轮前守望，俯瞰冰海变化着的单调，直到双膝发软；我不明白，为什么没人想出一种可以坐着掌的舵——在浮冰与冰山之间，往往需要相当细密的操纵。不过这时，我那身极地行头的圓球已轻了许多，我几乎算得上修长地站着，披一件拉普人的大衣，头上一顶西伯利亚圆毛帽。
午夜，当我倒进旧铺位，那沉入寂静的机器，便仿佛成了一件死物，却有一个纠缠我的鬼魂：因为我仍听见它们，又不是它们，而是它们鬼魂的寂静。
有时我会从睡中惊起，心胆俱裂，为着某一声冰山爆裂、浮冰碰撞的巨响，远远穿透那白色的静谧之谜——那里的浮冰和冰山是一座座漂浮的坟墓，世界是一座液体的墓园。那种声音把我从混沌深处唤回自我知觉时的末日般的震撼，我永远无法描述：因为常常，醒着和在噩梦里一样，我不知道自己身在哪一颗行星、哪一个纪元，只觉得自己漂流在时间、空间与境遇的大鸿沟里，意识底下没有底；世界于我全是蜃景，是一场新戏；梦与醒的界限失掉了。
唉，天气一直极好，海平得像池塘。第五天，七月十一日的上午，我驶入一条非同寻常的雪岭冰山的长巷，两侧排列得极其规整，巷宽半英里，长数英里，像一场巨人般的双列雕像巡游，又像明陵，只是随着韵律的涌浪起伏；许多高耸入云，把安静的影子投在中间的巷道上；有些通体是明澈的翡翠色；还有三四座泻下瀑布，送来辽远的、吟诵般的声响。中间的海水是一种奇怪的稠蓝，几乎像生蛋清；天上照旧漂着几朵雪白毛茸的云。这条长巷给人一种独眼巨人建造的大教堂一般的神秘印象，一种奇异的幽僻；我还没走出一英里，就望见了尽头一个黑色的东西。

===Original===

I rushed to the shrouds, and very soon made out a whaler.
Again the same panting agitations, mad rage to be at her, at once possessed me; I flew to the indicator, turned the lever to full, then back to give the wheel a spin, then up the mainmast ratlins, waving a long foot-bandage of vadmel tweed picked up at random, and by the time I was within five hundred yards of her, had worked myself to such a pitch, that I was again shouting that futile madness: “Hullo! Hi! Bravo! *I have been to the Pole!*”
And those twelve dead that I had in the chart-room there must have heard me, and the men on the whaler must have heard me, and smiled their smile.
For, as to that whaler, I should have known better at once, if I had not been crazy, since she *looked* like a ship of death, her boom slamming to port and starboard on the gentle heave of the sea, and her foresail reefed that serene morning. Only when I was quite near her, and hurrying down to stop the engines, did the real truth, with perfect suddenness, drench my heated brain; and I almost ran into her, I was so stunned.
However, I stopped the *Boreal* in time, and later on lowered the kayak, and boarded the other.
This ship had evidently been stricken silent in the midst of a perfect drama of activity, for I saw not one of her crew of sixty-two who was not busy, except one boy. I found her a good-sized thing of 500 odd tons, ship-rigged, with auxiliary engine of seventy horsepower, and pretty heavily armour-plated round the bows. There was no part of her which I did not overhaul, and I could see that they had had a great time with whales, for a mighty carcass, attached to the outside of the ship by the powerful cant-purchase tackle, had been in process of flensing and cutting-in, and on the deck two great blankets of blubber, looking each a ton-weight, surrounded by twenty-seven men in many attitudes, some terrifying to see, some disgusting, several grotesque, all so unhuman, the whale dead, and the men dead, too, and death was there, and the rank-flourishing germs of Inanity, and a mesmerism, and a silence, whose dominion was established, and its reign was growing old.

===Chinese===

我冲到桅索边，很快就认出：一条捕鲸船。
同样的喘息激动、要到她身边去的疯狂，又一次攝住了我；我飞奔到指示器前，把杆推到全速，再奔回来打一把舵，随即攀上主桅桅梯，挥着一根随手抄起的瓦德麦粗呢长裹脚，等驶到离她五百码之内时，我已把自己煽到了那样一个高度，又在喊那徒劳的疯话了：「哈罗！喂！好啊！我『到过极点』了！」
海图室里我那十二个死人，必是听见我了；捕鲸船上的人必是听见我了，还各自微微一笑。
因为说到那条捕鲸船，若不是我疯了，本该一眼就明白的：她看去就像一条死船，帆杠随海面轻涌左一下右一下地拍打，那个晴朗无风的清晨她前帆却收着。直到我离她很近，赶着下去停机，那真相才以十足的突然，浇透我滚热的脑子；我险些撞上她，我懵到了那个地步。
不过我及时把「北极号」停住了，随后放下皮筏，登上了那条船。
这条船分明是在一场正演到热闹处的活剧当中，被击成寂静的：因为六十二名船员，我未见一人不在忙碌，只有一个孩子例外。她是一条五百多吨的好船，横帆船装，附一台七十马力的辅机，船头四周包着相当厚的装甲板。她的每个角落我都查遍了，看得出他们捕鲸正捕得红火：一副庞大的鲸尸，用强劲的起重复滑车吊在船侧外，正剥脂割肉到一半；甲板上两大张油脂毯，每张看去都有一吨重，围着二十七个人，姿态各异，有的望之恐怖，有的令人作呕，几个滑稽古怪，全都那样不像人；鲸死了，人也死了；死亡在那里，还有「虚无」那蓬勃旺盛的病菌，一种催眠术，一种寂静——它的统治既已确立，而且统治得日渐老练了。

===Original===

Four of them, who had been removing the gums from a mass of stratified whalebone at the mizzenmast foot, were quite imbedded in whale-flesh; also, in a barrel lashed to the top of the main topgallant masthead was visible the head of a man with a long pointed beard, looking steadily out over the sea to the S. W., which made me notice that five only of the probable eight or nine boats were on board; and after visiting the ’tween-decks, where I saw considerable quantities of stowed whalebone plates, and about fifty or sixty iron oil-tanks, and cut-up blubber; and after visiting cabin, engine-room, fo’cas’le, where I saw a lonely boy of fourteen with his hand grasping a bottle of rum under all the turned-up clothes in a chest, he, at the moment of death, being evidently intent upon hiding it; and after two hours’ search of the ship, I got back to my own, and half an hour later came upon all the three missing whaleboats about a mile apart, and steered zigzag near to each. They contained five men each and a steerer, and one had the harpoon-gun fired, with the loose line coiled round and round the head and upper part of the stroke line-manager; and in the others hundreds of fathoms of coiled rope, with toggle-irons, whale-lances, hand-harpoons, and dropped heads, and grins, and lazy *abandon*, and eyes that stared, and eyes that dozed, and eyes that winked.
After this I began to sight ships not infrequently, and used regularly to have the three lights burning all night. On the 12th July I met one, on the 15th two, on the 16th one, on the 17th three, on the 18th two﻿—all Greenlanders, I think: but, of the nine, I boarded only three, the glass quite clearly showing me, when yet far off, that on the others was no life; and on the three which I boarded were dead men; so that that suspicion which I had, and that fear, grew very heavy upon me.
I went on southward, day after day southward, sentinel there at my wheel; clear sunshine by day, when the calm pale sea sometimes seemed mixed with regions of milk, and at night the immense desolation of a world lit by a sun that was long dead, and by a light that was gloom.

===Chinese===

有四个人正在后桅脚下从一堆层叠的鲸须上剔齿龈，整个人都嵌进了鲸肉里；还有，主上桅桅顶绑着的一只木桶里，露出一个人头，留着尖长胡子，正定定望着西南的海面——这使我注意到，船上原有的八九条小艇，只剩五条；看过中甲板（那里堆着相当多的鲸须板，五六十只铁油罐，还有切碎的脂块），又看过舱房、机舱、水手舱（那里一个十四岁的孤单孩子，手抓着一瓶朗姆酒，藏在翻乱的箱底衣裳下面——死的当口，他分明一心想把酒藏起来），又在船上搜了两个钟头之后，我回到自己船上；半小时后，撞见了失踪的三条捕鲸艇，彼此相距约一英里，我曲折驶近了每一条。每条艇上五个人加一个舵手；有一条的捕鲸炮打过，松着的索一圈圈缠在射手兼线吏的头上半身；另几条艇里是几百英寻盘起的绳，连同炮镖、鲸枪、手掷鱼叉，还有掉落的鲸头，和龈着的牙，和懒洋洋的放任，和瞪着的眼，和打瞌的眼，和眨着的眼。
自此以后，我望见船的次数渐渐不少，也照例让三盏灯通夜点着。七月十二日遇见一条，十五日两条，十六日一条，十七日三条，十八日两条——我想全是格陵兰船：但九条之中我只登了三条，望远镜在老远便向我明白地显示，其余船上没有生息；而我登上的三条，满船死人。于是我心里那点疑心、那点恐惧，沉重起来。
我一天天向南，一天天向南，守在我的舵旁；白天是晴明的阳光，平静苍白的海有时仿佛掺进了一片片乳白；夜里则是一个被死去已久的太阳、被一种阴郁的光照亮的世界的无边荒凉。

===Original===

It was like Night blanched in death then; and wan as the very kingdom of death and Hades I have seen it, most terrifying, that neuter state and limbo of nothingness, when unreal sea and spectral sky, all boundaries lost, mingled in a vast shadowy void of ghastly phantasmagoria, pale to utter huelessness, at whose centre I, as if annihilated, seemed to swoon in immensity of space. Into this disembodied world would come anon waftures of that peachy scent which I knew: and their frequency rapidly grew. But still the *Boreal* moved, traversing, as it were, bottomless Eternity: and I reached latitude 72°, not far now from Northern Europe.
And now, as to that blossomy peach-scent﻿—even while some floes were yet around me﻿—I was just like some fantastic mariner, who, having set out to search for Eden and the Blessed Islands, finds them, and balmy gales from their gardens come out, while he is yet afar, to meet him with their perfumes of almond and champac, cornel and jasmine and lotus. For I had now reached a zone where the peach-aroma was constant; all the world seemed embalmed in its spicy fragrance; and I could easily imagine myself voyaging beyond the world toward some clime of perpetual and enchanting Spring.

===Chinese===

那时它像被死亡漂白了的夜；我见过它苍白如死亡与冥府的国度本身，最可怖的，是那种中性的状态、那种虚无的边境：不真实的海与幽灵似的天空，界限尽失，混入一场灰白得近乎无色的、庞大阴森的幻影虚空，而我仿佛被消灭了似的，在它中央，昏厥于空间的浩渺里。每隔一阵，那股我认得的桃子香气便飘进这个无肉身的的世界，而且越来越频。但「北极号」仍在移动，仿佛横渡着无底的永恒：我到了北纬 72 度，离北欧不远了。
说到那花一般的桃香——甚至在周围还有些浮冰的时候——我就像某个奇幻的水手，出海去寻找伊甸园和极乐群岛，居然找到了，而他们园中的香风，趁他还远呢，便带着扁桃与占婆香、山茱萸与茉莉和莲的芬芳，出迎他来。因为我此时已进入一个桃香恒定的地带；全世界仿佛都用它那带辛香的芬芳薰过；我不难想象，自己正在驶出这个世界，驶向某个永远是迷人春季的乐土。

===Original===

Well, I saw at last what whalers used to call “the blink of the ice”; that is to say, its bright apparition or reflection in the sky when it is left behind, or not yet come-to. By this time I was in a region where a good many craft of various sorts were to be seen; I was continually meeting them; and not one did I omit to investigate, while many I boarded in the kayak or the larch-wood pram. Just below latitude 70° I came upon a good large fleet of what I supposed to be Lafoden cod and herring fishers, which must have drifted somewhat on a northward current. They had had a great season, for the boats were well laden with curing fish. I went from one to the other on a zigzag course, they being widely scattered, some mere dots to the glass on the horizon. The evening was still and clear with that astral Arctic clearness, the sun just beginning his low-couched nightly drowse. These sturdy-looking brown boats stood rocking gently there with slow-creaking noises, as of things whining in slumber, without the least damage, awaiting the appalling storms of the winter months on that tenebrous sea, when a dark doom, and a deep grave, would not fail them. The fishers were braw carles, wearing, many of them, fringes of beard well back from the chin-point, with hanging woollen caps. In every case I found below-decks a number of cruses of corn-brandy, marked “aquavit,” two of which I took into the pram. In one of the smacks an elderly fisher was kneeling in a forward sprawling pose, clasping the lug-mast with his arms, the two knees wide apart, head thrown back, and the yellow eyeballs with their islands of grey iris staring straight up the mast-pole. At another of them, instead of boarding in the pram, I shut off the *Boreal*’s liquid air at such a point that, by delicate steering, she slackened down to a stoppage just abeam of the smack, upon whose deck I was thus able to jump down. After looking around I descended the three steps aft into the dark and garrety below-decks, and with stooping back went calling in an awful whisper: “*Anyone? Anyone?*” Nothing answered me: and when I went up again, the *Boreal* had drifted three yards beyond my reach.

===Chinese===

嗯，我终于见到了捕鲸人旧日所称的『冰映光』；就是说，冰已被抛在身后、或尚未到达时，它映在天上的明亮幻影或反光。这时我已进入一片可以望见许多各式船只的海域；我不断地遇上它们；没有一艘我没去查探过，其中许多我还乘着皮划艇或落叶松木平底船登了上去。刚过北纬70度，我遇上一支相当大的船队，我猜是罗弗敦的鳕鱼鲱鱼渔民，想必是被一股北向的洋流漂过来了一些。他们赶上了一个大丰年，因为船里满载着腌晒的鱼。它们散布得很开，有些在望远镜里只是地平线上的小点，我便走之字形，从一条船挨到另一条。傍晚静谧而晴朗，带着那种星辉般的北极式的清澈，太阳刚刚开始它低卧的每夜小寐。这些结结实实的棕色小船静静地泊在那里，缓缓吱呀地轻摇，像睡梦中呓语的东西，毫无损伤，只等着那片幽暗之海冬季月份的骇人风暴——那时，黑暗的劫数和深海的坟墓，必定不会放过它们。那些渔民是些魁伟的好汉，许多人下巴尖往后留着圈胡须边，戴下垂的毛线帽。每条船的舱下我都发现几小罐谷物烧酒，标着『阿夸维特』，我拿了两罐放进平底船。其中一条渔船上，一个上了年纪的渔民前倾跪仆着，双臂搂住斜桁桅，两膝大张，头向后仰，黄眼球嵌着灰虹膜的小岛，直勾勾瞪着桅杆上方。到另一条船时，我没有乘平底船过去，而是掐断了*博雷尔*号的液体空气，凭着细巧的操舵，让它在渔船正横处缓缓停住，这样我便能跳上那船的甲板。四下看过之后，我从船尾的三级踏步下到黑洞洞、低矮如阁舱的下层舱，弓着背，用可怖的耳语一路喊着：『*有人吗？有人吗？*』没有任何东西回答我：等我再上到甲板，*博雷尔*号已漂出了我够得着的范围三码开外。

===Original===

There being a dead calm, I had to plunge into the water, and in that half-minute there a sudden cold throng of unaccountable terrors beset me, and I can feel again now that abysmal desolation of loneliness, and sense of a hostile and malign universe bent upon eating me up: for the ocean seemed to me nothing but a great ghost.
Two mornings later I came upon another school, rather larger boats these, which I found to be Brittany cod-fishers. Most of these, too, I boarded. In every below-decks was a wooden or earthenware image of the Virgin, painted in gaudy faded colours; and in one case I found a boy who had been kneeling before the statue, but was toppled sideways now, his knees still bent, and the cross of Christ in his hand. These stalwart blue woollen blouses and tarpaulin sou’-westers lay in every pose of death, every detail of feature and expression still perfectly preserved. The sloops were all the same, all, all: with singsong creaks they rocked a little, nonchalantly: each, as it were, with a certain subconsciousness of its own personality, and callous unconsciousness of all the others round it: yet each a copy of the others: the same hooks and lines, disembowelling-knives, barrels of salt and pickle, piles and casks of opened cod, kegs of biscuit, and low-creaking rockings, and a bilgy smell, and dead men. The next day, about eighty miles south of the latitude of Mount Hekla, I sighted a big ship, which turned out to be the French cruiser *Lazare Tréport*. I boarded and overhauled her during three hours, her upper, main, and armoured deck, deck by deck, to her lowest black depths, even childishly spying up the tubes of her two big, rusted turret-guns. Three men in the engine-room had been much mangled, after death, I presume, by a burst boiler; floating about 800 yards to the northeast lay a longboat of hers, low in the water, crammed with marines, one oar still there, jammed between the rowlock and the rower’s forced-back chin; on the ship’s starboard deck, in the long stretch of space between the two masts, the bluejackets had evidently been piped up, for they lay there in a sort of serried disorder, to the number of two hundred and seventy-five.

===Chinese===

海面死寂无风，我只好纵身跳进水里；就在那半分钟里，一阵莫名的寒冷恐怖骤然围住了我，此刻我仍能重新感到那种深渊般孤绝的荒凉，感到一个存心要吞吃我的、怀敌意而恶毒的宇宙：因为在我看来，那海洋不过是一个巨大的幽灵。
两个早晨之后，我又遇上一队船，这批船大些，我发现是布列塔尼的鳕鱼渔民。这批船我也大多登了上去。每个下层舱里都有一尊木雕或陶塑的圣母像，涂着艳丽而褪色的彩；其中一艘上，我发现一个男孩本跪在像前，此刻已侧栽倒，双膝仍弯曲着，手里还握着基督的十字架。这些穿蓝色毛线上衣、戴油布防水帽的健壮汉子，横陈在各种死亡的姿势里，面貌和表情的每一处细节仍完好如初。这些单桅小船全都一模一样，全都是，全都是：带着吟唱般的吱呀声，满不在乎地轻摇：每一艘仿佛都隐约意识到自己的个性，又麻木不仁地无视四周所有别的船：可每一艘又是其余各艘的翻版：同样的钓钩和钓线、剖鱼刀、一桶桶盐和卤水、一堆堆一桶桶剖开的鳕鱼、一箱箱饼干，同样低低的吱呀摇曳，同样一股舱底水的臭气，同样死人。第二天，在海克拉山纬度以南约八十英里处，我望见一艘大船，原来是法国巡洋舰『拉扎尔·特雷波』号（*Lazare Tréport*）。我登上去查了三个钟头，从上甲板、主甲板到装甲甲板，一层层查到她最底下的黑暗深处，甚至孩子气地朝两门生锈的大炮塔的炮管里张望。机舱里的三个人已被大肆毁坏，我猜是死后遭爆裂的锅炉所害；东北方约800码外漂着她的一条大艇，吃水很深，塞满了海军陆战队员，一支桨还在，卡在桨架和桨手被迫后仰的下巴之间；在船的右舷甲板、两桅之间那一长片空地上，水兵们显然是被哨音集合上来的，因为他们横陈在那里，密集而凌乱，总数有二百七十五人。

===Original===

Nothing could be of suggestion more tragic than the wasted and helpless power of this poor wandering vessel, around whose stolid mass myriads of wavelets, busy as aspen-leaves, bickered with a continual weltering splash that was quite loud to hear. I sat a good time that afternoon in one of her steely port main-deck casemates on a gun-carriage, my head sunken on my breast, furtively eyeing the bluish turned-up feet, all shrunk, exsanguined, of a sailor who lay on his back before me; his soles were all that I could see, the rest of him lying head-downwards beyond the steel doorsill.
Drenched in seas of lugubrious reverie I sat, till, with a shuddering start, I awoke, paddled back to the *Boreal*, and, till sleep conquered me, went on my way. At ten the next morning, coming on deck, I spied to the west a group of craft, and turned my course upon them.
