They turned out to be eight Shetland sixerns, which must have drifted northeastward hither. I examined them well, but they were as the long list of the others: for all the men, and all the boys, and all the dogs on them were dead.

===Chinese===

再没有什么比这艘可怜漂泊之船白白荒废、无依无靠的威力更富悲剧意味的了：无数细浪围着它沉滞的船身，像白杨叶般忙碌，哗哗翻滚，水声聒耳。那天下午，我在她左舷主甲板一处钢炮廓里的炮架上坐了许久，头垂在胸前，偷偷打量着面前一个仰卧水兵那双朝天的青白脚掌，萎缩了，血已排空；我只能看见他的脚底，其余的身体头朝下躺在钢门槛外。
我坐在那里，浸在一片悲郁遐想的汪洋里，直到一个寒战惊醒了我，才划回*博雷尔*号，继续赶路，直到睡意征服了我。第二天上午十点，我上了甲板，望见西边有一群船，便转舵朝它们驶去。原来那是八条设得兰六人艇，想必是向东北漂到这里来的。我把它们细细查看了一遍，可它们同此前长长一串别的船一样：因为船上所有的男人、所有的男孩、所有的狗，全都死了。

===Original===

I could have come to land a long time before I did: but I would not: I was so afraid. For I was used to the silence of the ice: and I was used to the silence of the sea: but, God knows it, I was afraid of the silence of the land.

===Chinese===

我本可以早得多的时候就登岸的：可我不肯：我怕。因为我习惯了冰的寂静：也习惯了海的寂静：可是，天知道，我怕的是陆地的寂静。

===Original===

Once, on the 15th July, I had seen a whale, or thought I did, spouting very remotely afar on the S. E. horizon; and on the 19th I distinctly saw a shoal of porpoises vaulting the sea-surface, in their swift-successive manner, northward: and seeing them, I had said pitifully to myself: “Well, I am not quite alone in the world, then, my good God﻿—not quite alone.”
Moreover, some days later, the *Boreal* had found herself in a bank of cod making away northward, millions of fish, for I saw them, and one afternoon caught three, hand-running, with the hook.
So the sea, at least, had its tribes to be my mates.
But if I should find the land as still as the sea, without even the spouting whale, or school of tumbling sea-hogs﻿—if Paris were dumber than the eternal ice﻿—what then, I asked myself, should I do?

===Chinese===

有一回，7月15日，我看见过一头鲸鱼——或者以为看见了——在东南方遥远的天边喷水；19日，我又分明看见一群鼠海豚，以它们那疾速相接的姿态，接连跃出海面，向北而去；看见它们时，我曾凄然自语：『嗯，这么说，这世上还不只剩我一个，我的上帝——还不只剩我一个。』
再者，几天之后，*博雷尔*号驶入了一片北上的鳕鱼群，数以百万计，因为我是亲眼看见的，有一天下午还用钓钩接连钓起了三条。
所以说，至少这海里还有它的族群给我作伴。
可倘若我发现陆地同海一样死寂，连喷水的鲸、翻滚的海豚群都没有——倘若巴黎比永恒的冰原更喑哑——那可怎么办，我问自己，那时我该怎么办？

===Original===

I could have made short work, and landed at Shetland, for I found myself as far westward as longitude 11° 23′ W.: but I would not: I was so afraid. The shrinking within me to face that vague suspicion which I had, turned me first to a foreign land.
I made for Norway, and on the first night of this definite intention, at about nine o’clock, the weather being gusty, the sky lowering, the air sombrous, and the sea hard-looking, dark, and ridged, I was steaming along at a good rate, holding the wheel, my poor port and starboard lights still burning there, when, without the least notice, I received the roughest physical shock of my life, being shot bodily right over the wheel, thence, as from a cannon, twenty feet to the cabin-door, through it head-foremost down the companionway, and still beyond some six yards along the passage. I had crashed into some dark and dead ship, probably of large size, though I never saw her, nor any sign of her; and all that night, and the next day till four in the afternoon, the *Boreal* went driving alone over the sea, whither she would: for I lay unconscious. When I woke, I found that I had received really very small injuries, considering: but I sat there on the floor a long time in a sulky, morose, disgusted, and bitter mood; and when I rose, pettishly stopped the ship’s engines, seeing my twelve dead all huddled and disfigured. Now I was afraid to steam by night, and even in the daytime I would not go on for three days: for I was childishly angry with I know not what, and inclined to quarrel with Those whom I could not see.
However, on the fourth day, a rough swell which knocked the ship about, and made me very uncomfortable, coaxed me into moving; and I did so with bows turned eastward and southward.
I sighted the Norway coast four days later, in latitude 63° 19′, at noon of the 11th August, and pricked off my course to follow it; but it was with a slow and dawdling reluctance that I went, at much less than half-speed. In some eight hours, as I knew from the chart, I ought to sight the lighthouse light on Smoelen Island; and when quiet night came, the black water being branded with trails of still moonlight, I passed quite close to it, between ten and twelve, almost under the shadow of the mighty hills: but, oh my God, no light was there.

===Chinese===

我本可以抄近路，在设得兰登岸的，因为我发现自己已西至西经 11 度 23 分：可我不肯：我怕。心里那点不敢面对的隐约的疑惧，使我先转向了一片外国的土地。
我驶向挪威。拿定这个主意的头一夜，约莫九点钟，天气阵阵起风，天色阴沉，空气晦暗，海面铁硬、乌黑、起脊，我正以不错的速度开着船，握着舵，我那可怜的左右舷灯还点在那里，忽然毫无预兆，我捱了此生最重的一记皮肉之震：整个人从舵轮上直抛过去，又像出膛的炮弹，二十英尺飞到舱门，头朝前破门摉下升降梯，还在过道里又滑出六码开外。我撞上了一条黑黑的死船，兴许还很大，虽然我从未看见她，也没看见她一点踪影；那一整夜，直到次日下午四点，「北极号」都独自在海上随意漂驶：因为我一直不省人事。醒来时，我发现自己伤得实在很轻，就势而论；可我在地板上坐了许久，一腔乖戾、阴郁、作呕、怨苦；起身时，我赌气停了船机，眼见我那十二个死人挤作一堆、七歪八扭。这下我夜里不敢开船了，连白天也三天不肯走：因为我孩子气地恼着一样我不知是什么的东西，一心想同那些我看不见的「谁」吵架。
不过第四天，一场把船颠得东倒西歪、叫我很不好受的大涌，哄得我动了身；我便掉头向东、向南驶去。
四天后，八月十一日正午，我在北纬 63 度 19 分望见了挪威海岸，标好航线沿岸而行；可我去得拖沓迟缓，老大不情愿，船速不到一半。照海图推算，八个钟头左右，我该望见斯莫伦岛上的灯塔；静夜来临时，黑色的海面烙着一道道凝然的月光，十点到十二点之间，我贴着它驶过，几乎在大山黑影的笼罩之下：可是，噢，我的上帝，那里没有灯。

===Original===

And all the way down I marked the rugged seaboard slumber darkling, afar or near, with never, alas! one friendly light.

===Chinese===

一路南下，我望着那崎岖的海岸在明暗之间沉睡，或远或近，唉！始终没有一盏友好的灯。

===Original===

Well, on the 15th August I had another of those maniac raptures, whose passing away would have left an elephant racked and prostrate. During four days I had seen not one sign of present life on the Norway coast, only hills, hills, dead and dark, and floating craft, all dead and dark; and my eyes now, I found, had acquired a crazy fixity of stare into the very bottom of the vacant abyss of nothingness, while I remained unconscious of being, save of one point, rainbow-blue, far down in the infinite, which passed slowly from left to right before my consciousness a little way, then vanished, came back, and passed slowly again, from left to right continually; till some prick, or voice, in my brain would startle me into the consciousness that I was staring, whispering the profound confidential warning: You must not stare so, or it is over with you! Well, lost in a blank trance of this sort, I was leaning over the wheel during the afternoon of the 15th, when it was as if some instinct or premonition in my soul leapt up, and said aloud: “If you look just yonder, *you will see*﻿ ﻿… !” I started, and in one instant had surged up from all that depth of reverie to reality: I glanced to the right: and there, at last, my God, I saw something human which moved, rapidly moved: at last!﻿—and it came to me.
That sense of recovery, of waking, of new solidity, of the comfortable usual, a million-fold too intense for words﻿—how sweetly consoling it was! Again now, as I write, I can fancy and feel it﻿—the rocky solidity, the adamant ordinary, on which to base the feet, and live. From the day when I stood at the Pole, and saw there the dizzy thing that made me swoon, there had come into my way not one sign or trace that other beings like myself were alive on the earth with me: till now, suddenly, I had the sweet indubitable proof: for on the southwestern sea, not four knots away, I saw a large, swift ship: and her bows, which were sharp as a hatchet, were steadily chipping through the smooth sea at a pretty high pace, throwing out profuse ribbony foams that went wide-vawering, with outward undulations, far behind her length, as she ran the sea in haste, straight northward.
At the moment, I was steering about S. E. by S.

===Chinese===

嗯，8月15日，我又经历了一回那种疯癫的狂喜，它一旦退去，怕是连大象也要被折磨得瘫倒。整整四天，我在挪威海岸没有见到一丝现世生命的迹象，只有山、山，死寂而黝黑，还有漂浮的船只，同样死寂而黝黑；这时我发现，我的眼睛已经养成了一种疯狂的凝滞，直勾勾盯进那虚空的深渊之底；我对自身的存在浑然不觉，只意识到一个点，虹彩般的蓝色，在无限深处远远的，在我意识前面缓缓从左向右移过一小段，随即隐没，又回来，又缓缓移过，不住地从左到右；直到我脑子里的某一下刺痛，或某个声音，惊醒我、让我意识到自己正在凝视，一边低声说出那深沉的、知己般的警告：切不可这样盯视，不然你就完了！嗯，正是沉在这样一种茫然的出神里，15日下午，我伏在舵轮上，忽然仿佛我灵魂里某种本能或预感跳了起来，大声说：『往那边看一眼，*你会看见*……！』我一个激灵，一瞬间便从冥想的深处涌回了现实：我朝右一瞥：在那里，终于，我的上帝，我看见了一个人形的东西在动，飞快地动：终于！——它朝我来了。
那种复原之感、苏醒之感、重新结实之感、寻常安稳之感，强烈得千言万语也道不出百万分之一——它是何等甜蜜的慰藉！此刻写作，我又能想象它、感到它了——那岩石般的坚实，那坚不可摧的寻常，人可以在上面立足、活下去。自从我立在北极、看见那令我晕厥的眩晕之物的那一天起，一路上再没有一丝迹象、一点痕迹，表明还有别的像我一样的生灵活在世上：直到此刻，忽然间，我得到了甜蜜而确凿的证明：因为在西南方的海上，不出四海里，我看见一艘巨大的疾驶的船：她那斧刃般锋利的船首，正以相当高的速度不断削开平滑的海面，抛出大量缎带似的泡沫，一圈圈向外荡漾，在她船尾后远远扩展开去，她正急急地赶海，笔直向北。
那时我正朝东南偏南行驶

===Original===

, fifteen miles out from a shadowy-blue series of Norway mountains; and just giving the wheel one frantic spin to starboard to bring me down upon her, I flew to the bridge, leant my back on the mainmast, which passed through it, put a foot on the white iron rail before me, and there at once felt all the mocking devils of distracted revelry possess me, as I caught the cap from my long hairs, and commenced to wave and wave and wave, red-faced maniac that I was: for at the second nearer glance, I saw that she was flying an ensign at the main, and a long pennant at the main-top, and I did not know what she was flying those flags there for: and I was embittered and driven mad.
With distinct minuteness did she print herself upon my consciousness in that five minutes’ interval: she was painted a dull and cholera yellow, like many Russian ships, and there was a faded pink space at her bows under the line where the yellow ceased: the ensign at her main I made out to be the blue-and-white saltire, and she was clearly a Russian passenger-liner, two-masted, two-funnelled, though from her funnels came no trace of smoke, and the position of her steam-cones was anywhere. All about her course the sea was spotted with wobbling splendours of the low sun, large coarse blots of glory near the eye, but lessening to a smaller pattern in the distance, and at the horizon refined to a homogeneous band of livid silver.
The double speed of the *Boreal* and the other, hastening opposite ways, must have been thirty-eight or forty knots, and the meeting was accomplished in certainly less than five minutes: yet into that time I crowded years of life. I was shouting passionately at her, my eyes starting from my head, my face all inflamed with rage the most prone, loud and urgent. For she did not stop, nor signal, nor make sign of seeing me, but came furrowing down upon me like Juggernaut, with steadfast run. I lost reason, thought, memory, purpose, sense of relation, in that access of delirium which transported me, and can only remember now that in the midst of my shouting, a word, uttered by the fiends who used my throat to express their frenzy, set me laughing high and madly: for I was crying: “Hi! Bravo! Why don’t you stop? *Madmen! I have been to the Pole!

===Chinese===

，离一列影影绰绰的蓝色挪威群山十五英里；我把舵轮拚命往右一转，好朝她压下去，随即飞奔上舰桥，背靠着穿过舰桥的主桅，一脚蹬在面前的白铁栏杆上，顿时觉得所有狂欢乱舞的嘲弄魔鬼附上了我身：我从长发里抓下便帽，开始挥啊，挥啊，挥啊，我这个满脸涨红的疯子：因为再靠近一眼看去，我看见她主桅上飘着一面军旗，主桅顶上飘着长长的三角旗，我不明白她挂那些旗做什么：我又怨又狂。
就在那五分钟里，她以分明入微的清晰印进了我的意识：她漆着一色晦暗的霍乱黄，同许多俄国船一样，船首黄漆尽头以下有一块褪色的粉红：她主桅上的军旗我认出是蓝白斜十字旗，她显然是一艘俄国客轮，双桅双烟囱，虽然烟囱里不见一缕烟，她的蒸汽锥也不知去向。她的航路四周，海面布满低垂太阳摇曳晃动的光斑，近处是一团团粗大的辉煌，远处渐次缩成细小的纹样，到了天边，则凝成一色的青银光带。
*博雷尔*号与对方相向疾驶，合起来的速度总有三十八到四十节，相遇不出五分钟便完成了：可我把多年的生命挤进了那几分钟。我朝她狂热地呼喊，眼睛从头颅里迸出来，满脸烧着最暴烈、最喧腾、最急迫的狂怒。因为她不停船，不打信号，也不做出看见我的样子，只像一辆贾格纳特战车，岿然疾驰，破浪向我压来。在那阵攫住我的谵狂里，我失去了理智、思想、记忆、目的、关系之感，如今只记得：在我呼喊的正中间，一个字——那借我的喉咙宣泄狂热的魔鬼们喊出的——让我高高疯狂地大笑起来：因为我正叫着：『喂！好啊！你们怎么不停船？*疯子们！我到过北极！

===Original===

*”
That instant an odour arose, and came, and struck upon my brain, most detestable, most execrable; and while one might count ten, I was aware of her near-sounding engines, and that cursed charnel went tearing past me on her maenad way, not fifteen yards from my eyes and nostrils. She was a thing, my God, from which the vulture and the jackal, prowling for offal, would fly with shrieks of loathing. I had a glimpse of decks piled thick with her festered dead.
In big black letters on the round retreating yellow stern my eye-corner caught the word *Yaroslav*, as I bent over the rail to retch and cough and vomit at her. She was a horrid thing.
This ship had certainly been pretty far south in tropical or subtropical latitudes with her great crowd of dead: for all the bodies which I had seen till then, so far from smelling ill, seemed to give out a certain perfume of the peach. She was evidently one of those many ships of late years which have substituted liquid air for steam, yet retained their old steam-funnels, etc., in case of emergency: for air, I believe, was still looked at askance by several builders, on account of the terrible accidents which it sometimes caused. The *Boreal* herself is a similar instance of both motors. This vessel, the *Yaroslav*, must have been left with working engines when her crew were overtaken by death, and, her air-tanks being still unexhausted, must have been ranging the ocean with impunity ever since, during I knew not how many months, or, it might be, years.
Well, I coasted Norway for nearly a hundred and sixty miles without once going nearer land than two or three miles: for something held me back. But passing the fjord-mouth where I knew that Aadheim was, I suddenly turned the helm to port, almost before I knew that I was doing it, and made for land.
In half an hour I was moving up an opening in the land with mountains on either hand, streaky crags at their summit, umbrageous boscage below; and the whole softened, as it were, by veils woven of the rainbow.
This arm of water lies curved about like a thread which one drops, only the curves are much more pointed, so that every few minutes the scene was changed, though the vessel just crawled her way up, and I could see behind me nothing of what was passed, or only a landlocked gleam like a lake.

===Chinese===

*』就在那一瞬，一股气味升起、飘来、击上我的脑门，最可憎，最可咒；不过数十拍的工夫，我听见了她近在耳边的机器声，那艘该咒的藏骸堂便顺着她酒神祭司般的疯路，从我眼前鼻端不到十五码的地方呼啸而过。她是一件东西，我的上帝，连巡食腐肉的秃鹫和豺狼，见了也要厌恶地尖叫着飞逃。我瞥见她的甲板上层层堆满溃烂的死者。
我伏在栏杆上朝她干呕、咳嗽、呕吐时，眼角从她那圆圆退去的黄色船尾上，扫到几个黑色大字：*雅罗斯拉夫*（*Yaroslav*）。她是一件可怖的东西。
这艘船载着那一大群死者，肯定到过南方很远的热带或亚热带纬度：因为在那之前我见过的一切尸体，非但没有腐味，似乎还散着一股桃子的香气。她显然是近年来那许多以液体空气代替蒸汽、却保留旧蒸汽烟囱等等以备急用的船之一：因为据我所知，由于液体空气有时造成可怕的事故，几家造船厂对它仍心存疑忌。*博雷尔*号本身就是双动力并存的一个例子。这艘船，『雅罗斯拉夫』号，必定是船员们被死亡追上时机器还在运转；她的空气罐尚未耗尽，此后想必一直无人拘管地巡游大洋，不知已有多少个月，或者，多少年。
嗯，我沿着挪威海岸走了将近一百六十英里，一次也没有靠近陆地两三英里以内：因为有什么东西拽着我。可经过我知道有阿德海姆的那处峡湾口时，我几乎还没意识到自己在做什么，就突然把舵转向左舷，朝陆地驶去。
半小时后，我正驶入陆地上的一道豁口，两侧皆是高山，山顶是条纹斑驳的峭岩，山下是浓荫的树丛；整个景色仿佛被彩虹织成的面纱柔化了。
这条水臂弯弯曲曲，像一根随手落下的丝线，只是弯折得更尖些，所以每隔几分钟景色便换了一副，虽然船只是缓缓向上爬，我身后已驶过的一切再看不见，或只余一汪困在陆间、湖泊似的微光。

===Original===

I never saw water so polished and glassy, like clarid polished marble, reflecting everything quite clean-cut in its lucid abysm, over which hardly the faintest zephyr breathed that still sundown; it wimpled about the bluff *Boreal*, which seemed to move as if careful not to bruise it, in rich wrinkles and creases, like glycerine, or dewy-trickling lotus-oil; yet it was only the sea: and the spectacle yonder was only crags, and autumn-foliage and mountain-slope: yet all seemed caught-up and chaste, rapt in a trance of rose and purple, and made of the stuff of dreams and bubbles, of pollen-of-flowers, and rinds of the peach.
I saw it not only with delight, but with complete astonishment: having forgotten, as was too natural in all that long barrenness of ice and sea, that anything could be so ethereally fair: yet homely, too, human, familiar, and consoling. The air here was richly spiced with that peachy scent, and there was a Sabbath and a nepenthe and a charm in that place at that hour, as it were of those gardens of Hesperus, and fields of asphodel, reserved for the spirits of the just.
Alas! but I had the glass at my side, and for me nepenthe was mixed with a despair immense as the vault of heaven, my good God: for anon I would take it up to spy some perched hut of the peasant, or burg of the “bonder,” on the peaks: and I saw no one there; and to the left, at the third marked bend of the fjord, where there is one of those watchtowers that these people used for watching incoming fish, I spied, lying on a craggy slope just before the tower, a body which looked as if it must surely tumble headlong, but did not. And when I saw that, I felt definitely, for the first time, that shoreless despair which I alone of men have felt, high beyond the stars, and deep as hell; and I fell to staring again that blank stare of Nirvana and the lunacy of Nothingness, wherein Time merges in Eternity, and all being, like one drop of water, flies scattered to fill the bottomless void of space, and is lost.
The *Boreal*’s bow walking over a little empty fishing-boat roused me, and a minute later, just before I came to a new promontory and bend, I saw two people.

===Chinese===

我从没见过这样光洁如镜的水，像打磨莹澈的大理石，把万物清清楚楚映在它澄明的深底；那个静穆的日暮，几乎最轻的微风也不曾在水面上呼吸；它在敦实的*博雷尔*号四周泛起褶皱，浓密的皱纹曲曲折折，像甘油，像露珠滚淌的荷油，而船行其间，仿佛小心翼翼，唯恐碰伤了它：可这不过是海：那边的美景也不过是峭岩、秋叶和山坡：然而一切都仿佛被摄去了魂，圣洁无瑕，痴痴沉浸在玫瑰与紫色的恍惚里，由梦与泡沫、花之粉、桃之皮做成。
我见了它，不但欢喜，而且全然惊愕：在那漫长的冰与海的荒芜里，我太自然地忘了世上竟有如此缥缈的美景：而这美又是家常的，人性的，亲切的，抚慰的。这里的空气馥郁地调着那股桃子香，那个时辰的那个地方，有一种安息、一种忘忧、一种魅力，仿佛赫斯珀洛斯的花园、留给义人灵魂的水仙原野。
唉！可我手边带着望远镜，对我的忘忧药里，掺着同穹苍一样浩大的绝望，我的上帝：因为不一会儿我就会举起它，去窥探峰顶上某座悬踞的农舍，或哪位『邦德』的自耕农庄：我在那里看不到一个人；左边，在峡湾第三个显眼的弯道处，立着一座这些人用来瞭望鱼群来临的瞭望塔，我瞥见塔前的一片乱石坡上躺着一个身体，看样子必定一头要栽下去，却始终没有栽。一见那情形，我头一回确确切切感到了那种无岸的绝望——普天之下唯我一人尝过的绝望，高过星辰，深如地狱；我又堕入了那种涅槃般的茫然凝视、那虚无的癫狂：在那里，时间没入永恒，一切存在像一滴水，飞散开来去填满空间无底的虚空，就此消亡。
*博雷尔*号的船首跨过一条空空的小渔船，这才惊醒了我；一分钟后，就在我驶到一处新的海岬和弯道之前，我看见了两个人。

===Original===

The shore there is some three feet above the water, and edged with boulders of rock, about which grows a fringe of shrubs and small trees: behind this fringe is a path, curving upward through a sombre wooded little gorge; and on the path, near the water, I saw a driver of one of those Norwegian sulkies that were called karjolers: he, on the high front seat, was dead, lying sideways and backwards, with low head resting on the wheel; and on a trunk strapped to a frame on the axle behind was a boy, his head, too, resting sideways on the wheel, near the other’s; and the little pony was dead, pitched forward on its head and fore-knees, tilting the shafts downward; and some distance from them on the water floated an empty skiff.

===Chinese===

那里的岸高出水面约三英尺，镶着一圈巨砾，砾石四周长着一簇灌木和小树：树簇后面有一条小径，蜿蜒向上穿过一道幽暗多树的小峡谷；就在近水的径上，我看见一个车夫，赶的是挪威人称作『卡里奥尔』的那种单座轻马车：他坐在高高的前座上，死了，侧身向后仰着，低垂的头搁在车轮上；车轴后头捆在货架上的箱子上坐着一个男孩，他的头也侧枕在车轮上，挨着那车夫的头；那匹小马也死了，头和前膝栽在地上，把车辕压得斜翘；离他们一段距离的水面上，漂着一条空空的小船。

===Original===

When I turned the next foreland, I all at once began to see a number of craft, which increased as I advanced, most of them small boats, with some schooners, sloops, and larger craft, the majority aground: and suddenly now I was conscious that, mingling with that delicious odour of spring-blossoms﻿—profoundly modifying, yet not destroying it﻿—was another odour, wafted to me on the wings of the very faint land-breeze: and “Man,” I said, “is decomposing”: for I knew it well: it was the odour of human corruption.

===Chinese===

转过下一处海岬，我霎时开始望见许多船只，越往前越多，大都是小艇，其间夹着一些纵帆船、单桅船和更大的船，多数搁浅着：而此刻我忽然意识到，与那春花般的美妙气味相混——深深地改变了它，却尚未毁掉它——还有另一股气味，乘着极微弱的陆风飘到我这里：「人，」我说，「正在腐烂」：因为我太熟悉它了：那是人类腐坏的气味。

===Original===

The fjord opened finally in a somewhat wider basin, shut-in by quite steep, high-towering mountains, which reflected themselves in the water to their last cloudy crag: and, at the end of this I saw ships, a quay, and a modest, homely old town.
Not a sound, not one: only the languidly-working engines of the *Boreal*. Here, it was clear, the Angel of Silence had passed, and his scythe mown.
I ran and stopped the engines, and, without anchoring, got down into an empty boat that lay at the ship’s side when she stopped; and I paddled twenty yards toward the little quay. There was a brigantine with all her courses set, three jibs, staysails, square-sails, main and foresails, and gaff-top-sail, looking hanging and listless in that calm place, and wedded to a still copy of herself, mast-downward, in the water; there were three lumber-schooners, a forty-ton steamboat, a tiny barque, five Norway herring-fishers, and ten or twelve shallops: and the sailing-craft had all fore-and-aft sails set, and about each, as I passed among them, brooded an odour that was both sweet and abhorrent, an odour more suggestive of the very genius of mortality﻿—the inner mind and meaning of Azrael﻿—than aught that I could have conceived: for all, as I soon saw, were crowded with dead.
Well, I went up the old mossed steps, in that strange dazed state in which one notices frivolous things: I remember, for instance, feeling the lightness of my new clothes: for the weather was quite mild, and the day before I had changed to Summer things, having on now only a common undyed woollen shirt, the sleeves rolled up, and cord trousers, with a belt, and a cloth cap over my long hair, and an old pair of yellow shoes, without laces, and without socks. And I stood on the unhewn stones of the edge of the quay, and looked abroad over a largish piece of unpaved ground, which lay between the first house-row and the quay.
What I saw was not only most woeful, but wildly startling: woeful, because a great crowd of people had assembled, and lay dead, there; and wildly startling, because something in their tout ensemble told me in one minute why they were there in such number.
They were there in the hope, and with the thought, to fly westward by boat.

===Chinese===

峡湾最后敞开成一个稍宽的盆湾，四面围着陡峭高耸的群山，山影一直倒映到水中最后一线云雾的岩尖：在盆湾尽头，我望见了船只、一座码头，和一座朴素家常的旧城。
一点声音也没有，一点也没有：只有「北极号」的机器在懒懒地运转。这里，显然，寂静的天使已走过，他的镰刀已收割过了。
我跑去停了机，也不抛锚，顺着船停时搭在船侧的一只空船下去，朝小码头划了二十码。那里有一条双桅帆船，满帆都张着：三面三角帆、支索帆、横帆、主帆前帆俱全，还有斜桁顶帆，在那无风的地方懒散地垂着，与水中一个倒栽的、一动不动的自身结成了亲；还有三条运木的纵帆船，一条四十吨的汽船，一条小巴克船，五条挪威鲱鱼船，和十来条浅底船：帆船全都是纵帆张着的，我从它们中间穿过时，每条船四周都笼罩着一股又甜又可憎的气味，一股比我能设想的任何东西都更暗示着死亡之天才——亚斯拉天使的内心与真意——的气味：因为所有的船，我很快看到，都挤满了死人。
唉，我登上生了苔的旧石阶，人在那种恍恍惚惚的状态里，偏会注意些轻浮小事：我记得，比方说，我还留意到新换的衣服多么轻：因为天气已相当暖和，前一天我换上了夏装，此刻身上只是一件本色的普通毛料衬衫，袖子卷着，一条灯芯绒裤，系一条皮带，长头发上扣一顶布帽，脚上一双旧黄鞋，没有鞋带，也没有袜子。我站在码头边上那些未经凿琢的石头上，放眼打量第一排房屋与码头之间一块相当大的没有铺砌的空地。
我看见的景象，不但至为凄惨，而且惊人地骇异：凄惨，是因为曾有一大群人在这里聚集，死在了这里；骇异，是因为他们那整体的某种气象，一瞬间便告诉了我：他们为何如此成群地在此。
他们在此，是怀着乘船西逃的希望和念头。

===Original===

And the something which told me this was a certain *foreign* air about that field of the dead as the eye rested on it, something un-northern, southern, and Oriental.
Two yards from my feet, as I stepped to the top, lay a group of three: one a Norway peasant-girl in skirt of olive-green, scarlet stomacher, embroidered bodice, Scotch bonnet trimmed with silver lace, and big silver shoe-buckles; the second was an old Norway man in knee-breeches, and eighteenth-century small-clothes, and red worsted cap; and the third was, I decided, an old Jew of the Polish Pale, in gaberdine and skullcap, with ear-locks.
I went nearer to where they lay thick as reaped stubble between the quay and a little stone fountain in the middle of the space, and I saw among those northern dead two dark-skinned women in costly dress, either Spanish or Italian, and the yellower mortality of a Mongolian, probably a Magyar, and a big negro in zouave dress, and some twenty-five obvious French, and two Morocco fezes, and the green turban of a shereef, and the white of an Ulema.
And I asked myself this question: “How came these foreign stragglers here in this obscure northern town?”
And my wild heart answered: “There has been an impassioned stampede, northward and westward, of all the tribes of Man. And this that I, Adam Jeffson, here see is but the far-tossed spray of that monstrous, infuriate flood.”

===Chinese===

把这告诉我的，是那片死亡之场上眼睛一落便觉出的一种「外邦」气象，某种非北国的、南方的、东方的气息。
我踏上顶端的石阶，离脚两码的地方，躺着一组三个人：一个是挪威农家姑娘，橄榄绿的裙子，猩红的胸衣，绣花的紧身褂，镶银花边的苏格兰帽，鞋上大大的银扣；第二个是个挪威老人，穿及膝马裤和十八世纪的内衣裤，戴红色绒线帽；第三个，我断定是波兰栅栏区的一个老犹太人，穿长罩袍，戴小帽，鬓边垂着发卷。
我再走近些，到他们密集如收割后的麦茬、躺满码头与场地中央一座小石泉之间的地方，看见那些北国的死人中间，有两个衣着华贵的深肤色女子，不是西班牙人便是意大利人；有一个黄肤色的死者，多半是马扎尔人；有一个穿祖阿夫兵装的大个黑人；有二十五六个一望而知的法国人；有两顶摩洛哥毡帽；有一顶谢里夫的绿头巾；还有一顶乌理玛的白头巾。
于是我自问：「这些外邦的零落人等，怎么会到了这座偏僻的北方小城？」
我那狂乱的心答道：「人间的一切部族，曾有过一场向北、向西的狂热的奔逃。而我亚当·杰夫森在此所见，不过是那场狂怒洪峰远远抛溅起来的一星飞沫。」

===Original===

Well, I passed up a street before me, careful, careful where I trod. It was not utterly silent, nor was the quay-square, but haunted by a pretty dense cloud of mosquitoes, and dreamy twinges of music, like the drawing of the violin-bow in elf-land. The street was narrow, pavered, steep, and dark; and the sensations with which I, poor bent man, passed through that dead town, only Atlas, fabled to bear the burden of this Earth, could divine.

===Chinese===

唉，我从面前的一条街往上走，步步小心，步步留神脚下。这里并非全然无声，码头广场也不是，只是萦绕着相当稠密的一团团蚊子，和一阵阵梦似的、微微作痛的音乐，像精灵之国里拉动的小提琴弓。街道狭窄，石板铺就，陡而昏暗；而我这个可怜的伛偻之人，穿行在那座死城里时的种种滋味，恐怕只有传说中背负大地重担的阿特拉斯，才能揣想得出。

===Original===

I thought to myself: If now a wave from the Deep has washed over this planetary ship of earth, and I, who alone happened to be in the extreme bows, am the sole survivor of that crew?﻿ ﻿… What then, my God, shall I do?

===Chinese===

我心里想：倘若此刻，深渊里涌来的一股巨浪漫过了地球这艘行星之船，而我，只因碰巧独自待在最船头，成了那船员中唯一的幸存者？……那么，我的上帝，我该怎么办？

===Original===

I felt, I felt, that in this townlet, save the water-gnats of Norway, was no living thing; that the hum and the savour of Eternity filled, and wrapped, and embalmed it.
The houses are mostly of wood, some of them fairly large, with a porte-cochère leading into a semicircular yard, around which the building stands, very steep-roofed, and shingled, in view of the heavy snow-masses of winter. Glancing into one open casement near the ground, I saw an aged woman, stout and capped, lie on her face before a very large porcelain stove; but I paced on without stoppage, traversed several streets, and came out, as it became dark, upon a piece of grassland leading downward to a mountain-gorge. It was some distance along this gorge that I found myself sitting the next morning: and how, and in what trance, I passed that whole blank night is obliterated from my consciousness. When I looked about with the return of light I saw majestic fir-grown mountains on either hand, almost meeting overhead at some points, deeply shading the mossy gorge. I rose, and careless of direction, went still onward, and walked and walked for hours, unconscious of hunger; there was a profusion of wild mountain-strawberries, very tiny, which must grow almost into winter, a few of which I ate; there were blue gentianellas, and lilies-of-the-valley, and luxuriance of verdure, and a noise of waters. Occasionally, I saw little cataracts on high, fluttering like white wild rags, for they broke in the mid-fall, and were caught away, and scattered; patches also of reaped hay and barley, hung up, in a singular way, on stakes six feet high, I suppose to dry; there were perched huts, and a seemingly inaccessible small castle or burg, but none of these did I enter: and five bodies only I saw in the gorge, a woman with a babe, and a man with two small oxen.
About three in the afternoon I was startled to find myself there, and turned back. It was dark when I again passed through those gloomy streets of Aadheim, making for the quay, and now I felt both my hunger and a dropping weariness. I had no thought of entering any house, but as I passed by one open porte-cochère, something, I know not what, made me turn sharply in, for my mind had become as fluff on the winds, not working of its own action, but the sport of impulses that seemed external.

===Chinese===

我感觉到了，我感觉到了：这座小城里，除了挪威的水蚋，再无活物；永恒之国的嗡响与气息，灌满了它，裹住了它，把它香殓了。
房屋大半是木头的，有些相当大，带一座车马门，通进一个半圆形的院子，房子绕院而立，屋顶极陡，盖着木瓦——那是防着冬天的大雪。我朝近地一间敞开的窗里瞥了一眼，看见一个上了年纪的妇人，胖胖的，戴着睡帽，伏脸躺在一座很大的瓷炉前；可我脚步不停，一路走过，穿过几条街，天黑下来时，走到一片通往山峡的草地上。第二天早晨我发觉自己坐着的，便是离山峡口有些距离的地方；那一整夜空白的夜，我是怎样、在何种恍惚里熬过的，意识里已全然抹去了。天光回转，我四下一望，只见两边都是长满冷杉的巍峨群山，有几处几乎在头顶相接，把生苔的峡谷罩得浓荫匝地。我起身，不辨方向，依旧前行，走了又走，几个钟头不知饥饿；沿途野山莓极多，极小，想必一直长到将近冬天，我吃了几颗；还有蓝色龙胆、铃兰，一片繁绿，水声不断。偶尔，我望见高处的小瀑布，像白色的破布条般飘动，因为它们在半空便跌碎了，被风掠去、吹散；还有一堆堆收割的干草和大麦，以一种奇特的方式，挂在六英尺高的木架上，我猜是为了晾干；有高踞的小屋，有一座似乎无路可攀的小小城堡（或者说堡寨），这些我都没有进：整个峡谷里，我只见到五具尸体：一个抱着婴儿的妇人，和一个牵两头小牛的汉子。
下午三点左右，我猛然惊觉自己竟在那里，便转身折回。再次穿过亚德海姆那些阴森的街道走向码头时，天已黑了，这时我才觉出饿来，还有一阵阵下坠般的疲惫。我本无意进任何人家，可路过一扇敞开的车马门时，不知什么东西使我猛地一转身走了进去——我的心智已成了风中的绒絮，不能自主地运转，只任一些仿佛外来的冲动摆布。

===Original===

I went across the yard, and ascended a wooden spiral stair by a twilight which just enabled me to pick my way among five or six vague forms fallen there. In that confined place fantastic qualms beset me; I mounted to the first landing, and tried the door, but it was locked; I mounted to the second: the door was open, and with a chill reluctance I took a step inward where all was pitch darkness, the window-stores being drawn. I hesitated: it was very dark. I tried to utter that word of mine, but it came in a whisper inaudible to my ears: I tried again, and this time heard myself say: “*anyone?*” At the same time I had made another step forward, and trodden upon a soft abdomen; and at that contact terrors the most cold and ghastly thrilled me through and through, for it was as though I saw in that darkness the sudden eyeballs of Hell and frenzy glare upon me, and with a low gurgle of affright I was gone, helter-skelter down the stairs, treading upon flesh, across the yard, and down the street, with pelting feet, and open arms, and sobbing bosom, for I thought that all Aadheim was after me; nor was my horrid haste appeased till I was on board the *Boreal*, and moving down the fjord.
Out to sea, then, I went again; and within the next few days I visited Bergen, and put in at Stavanger. And I saw that Bergen and Stavanger were dead.
It was then, on the 19th August, that I turned my bow toward my native land.

===Chinese===

我穿过院子，就着一片刚好容我择路的暮色，登上一道木旋梯，梯上倒着五六个模糊的人形。在那逼仄的地方，种种离奇的惶惑攝住了我；我上到第一处平台，推了推门，门锁着；我上到第二处：门开着，我带着一阵寒噤般的却步，向里跨了一步——里面一片漆黑，窗板全拉上了。我踌躇了：真黑啊。我想喊出我的那个词，可出口的只是一声自己耳朵也听不见的气音；我再试，这回听见自己说：「有人吗？」与此同时，我又向前迈了一步，一脚踩在一个柔软的肚腹上；那一触，最冰冷、最阴森的恐怖顿时把我彻头彻尾穿透，因为仿佛我在那黑暗里，看见地狱与癫狂的眼球骤然瞪定了我；我低低嗬了一声惊惶，人已飞奔下楼，一路踩着皮肉，穿过院子，奔下街道，双脚乱撞，双臂大张，胸口啜泣——我以为整个亚德海姆都在我身后追来；直到登上「北极号」，驶下峡湾，那可怕的狂奔才算罢休。
于是我又出海了；随后几天里，我到访了卑尔根，又进了斯塔万格。我看见卑尔根和斯塔万格都死了。
就在那时，八月十九日，我把船头转向了我的祖国。

===Original===

From Stavanger I steered a straight course for the Humber.
I had no sooner left behind me the Norway coast than I began to meet the ships, the ships﻿—ship after ship; and by the time I entered the zone of the ordinary alternation of sunny day and sunless night, I was moving through the midst of an incredible number of craft, a mighty and widespread fleet.
Over all that great expanse of the North Sea, where, in its most populous days of trade, the sailor might perhaps sight a sail or two, I had now at every moment at least ten or twelve within scope of the glass, oftentimes as many as forty, forty-five.
And very still they lay on a still sea, itself a dead thing, livid as the lips of death; and there was an intensity in the calm that was appalling: for the ocean seemed weighted, and the air drugged.
Extremely slow was my advance, for at first I would not leave any ship, however remotely small, without approaching sufficiently to investigate her, at least with the spyglass: and a strange multitudinous mixture of species they were, trawlers in hosts, warships of every nation, used, it seemed, as passenger-boats, smacks, feluccas, liners, steam-barges, great four-masters with sails, Channel boats, luggers, a Venetian *burchiello*, colliers, yachts, *remorqueurs*, training ships, dredgers, two *dahabeeahs* with curving gaffs, Marseilles fishers, a Maltese *speronare*, American offshore sail, Mississippi steamboats, Sorrento lug-schooners, Rhine punts, yawls, old frigates and three-deckers, called to novel use, Stromboli caiques, Yarmouth tubs, xebecs, Rotterdam flat-bottoms, floats, mere gunwaled rafts﻿—anything from anywhere that could bear a human freight on water had come, and was here: and all, I knew, had been making westward, or northward, or both; and all, I knew, were crowded; and all were tombs, listlessly wandering, my God, on the wandering sea with their dead.
And so fair was the world about them, too: the brightest suavest autumn weather; all the still air aromatic with that vernal perfume of peach: yet not so utterly still, but if I passed close to the lee of any floating thing, the spicy stirrings of morning or evening wafted me faint puffs of the odour of mortality overripe for the grave.

===Chinese===

从斯塔万格出发，我取直航向亨伯河口。
挪威海岸刚一抛在身后，我就开始遇见船，到处是船——一艘接着一艘；等我驶入白昼与黑夜正常交替的地带时，我正穿行在多得难以置信的船只中间，一支浩大而铺展的船队。
在那片辽阔的北海上——在它航运最盛的年代，水手或许也只能望见一两片帆——此刻我每一时刻，望远镜里至少有十艘十二艘，常有四十艘、四十五艘之多。
它们静静地躺在静止的海上，那海本身就是一件死物，青灰得如同死者的嘴唇；那平静里有种骇人的沉重：海洋仿佛坠了铅，空气仿佛下了药。
我的行进极其缓慢，因为起初任何一艘船，哪怕再远再小，我都要靠近到足以查探的程度，至少用望远镜看上一看：而它们是一支何等驳杂的船种大杂烩：成群的拖网渔船，各国的军舰——看样子被当客船用了，斯麦克渔船，费卢卡帆船，班轮，蒸汽驳船，张着帆的巨型四桅船，海峡渡轮，卢格帆船，一艘威尼斯*布尔基耶洛*游船，运煤船，游艇，法国拖轮，练习舰，挖泥船，两艘弯斜桁的达哈比耶船，马赛渔船，一艘马耳他*斯佩罗纳雷*，美国近海帆船，密西西比明轮船，索伦托斜桁纵帆船，莱茵河平底船，小帆艇，被改作新用的老式快速帆舰和三层甲板舰，斯特龙博利的卡伊克小帆船，雅茅斯老木桶船，谢贝克船，鹿特丹平底船，浮具，乃至仅仅装了舷缘的木筏——凡是世上任何地方能在水上载人的东西，全都来了，全都在这里：而我知道，它们全都在向西、向北、或既向西又向北赶；我知道，全都挤满了人；而全都是坟墓，载着它们的死者，在漂泊的海上恹恹漂泊，我的上帝。
它们周围的世界又是那样美好：最明媚和煦的秋日天气；静止的空气里全是那春日般的桃花香：可也不是全然不动：每当我从任何漂浮物的下风处擦过，清晨或傍晚带香料的微风，便给我送来一缕缕淡淡的、熟烂待葬的死亡气味。

===Original===

So abominable and accursed did this become to me, such a plague and a hissing, vague as was the offence, that I began to shun rather than seek the ships, and also I now dropped my twelve, whom I had kept to be my companions all the way from the Far North, one by one, into the sea: for now I had definitely passed into a zone of settled warmth.
I was convinced, however, that the poison, whatever it might be, had some embalming, or antiseptic, effect upon the bodies: at Aadheim, Bergen and Stavanger, for instance, where the temperature permitted me to go without a jacket, only the merest hints and whiffs of the processes of dissolution had troubled me.

===Chinese===

这事变得如此可憎可咒，如此瘟疫、如此笑柄——尽管那罪愆何在还说不清——以致我开始躲避船只，而不是寻访它们了；而且这时，我也把那十二个一路从极北带在身边作伴的，一个一个投入了海里：因为此刻我确凿已驶入暖意稳定的区域了。
不过我确信，那毒——管它是什么——对尸体有某种防腐、杀菌之效：比如在阿德海姆、卑尔根和斯塔万格，气温容许我单衣出行，腐解过程只以最轻微的暗示和气息打扰过我。

===Original===

Very benign, I say, and pleasant to see, was sky and sea during all that voyage: but it was at sunset that my sense of the wondrously beautiful was roused and excited, in spite of that great burden which I carried. Certainly, I never saw sunsets resembling those, nor could have conceived of aught so flamboyant, extravagant, and bewitched: for the whole heaven seemed turned into an arena for warring Hierarchies, warring for the universe, or it was like the wild countenance of God defeated, and flying marred and bloody from His enemies. But many evenings I watched with unintelligent awe, believing it but a portent of the unsheathed sword of the Almighty; till, one morning, a thought pricked me like a sword, for I suddenly remembered the great sunsets of the later nineteenth century, witnessed in Europe, America, and, I believe, over the world, after the eruption of the volcano of Krakatoa.
And whereas I had before said to myself: “If now a wave from the Deep has washed over this planetary ship of earth﻿ ﻿… ,” I said now: “A wave﻿—but not from the Deep: a wave rather which she had reserved, and has spouted, from her own un-motherly entrails﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

我说，整个那段航程里，天和海都是极仁慈、极悦目的：可正是在日落时分，我那对奇美的感受才被唤起、被激动，尽管我驮着那副重担。的确，我从没见过那样的日落，也想象不出任何这样火焰般、这样恣肆、这样中魔的东西：因为整个天穹仿佛变成了一座角斗场，供交战的天军厮杀，为宇宙而战；又像上帝那狂野的面容战败了，带着残缺与血污，从祂的仇敌面前奔逃。可许多个傍晚，我只是怀着不明所以的畏怖仰望，以为那不过是全能者出鞘之剑的一个预兆；直到一天早晨，一个念头像剑一样刺了我一下——因为我猛然想起十九世纪末那些伟大的日落：喀拉喀托火山喷发之后，欧洲、美洲，我相信还有全世界，都曾见过。
而先前我对自己说：「倘若此刻，深渊里涌来的一股巨浪漫过了地球这艘行星之船……」如今我则说：「是巨浪——但不是从深渊里来：倒不如说，是她自己蓄下的、从她那全无慈母心肠的脏腑里喷出来的巨浪……」

===Original===

I had some knowledge of Morse telegraphy, and of the manipulation of tape-machines, telegraphic typing-machines, and the ordinary wireless transmitter and coherer, as of most little things of that sort which came within the outskirts of the interest of a man of science; I had collaborated with Professor Stanistreet in the production of a textbook called “Applications of Science to the Arts,” which had brought us some notoriety; and, on the whole, the minutiae of modern things were still pretty fresh in my memory. I could therefore have wired from Bergen or Stavanger, supposing the batteries not run down, to somewhere: but I would not: I was so afraid; afraid lest forever from nowhere should come one answering click, or flash, or stirring.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我懂些莫尔斯电报术，也会摆弄纸带机、电传打字机和普通的无线电发报机、金属屑检波器，大凡这类落在科学人兴趣边缘的小玩意，我多半都会；我曾与斯塔尼斯特里特教授合作写过一本教科书，《科学在艺术中的应用》，它给我们带来过不小的名声；总的说来，现代事物的种种细节在我记忆里还很新鲜。因此，倘若电池没有耗尽，我本可以从卑尔根或斯塔万格向某处发电报的：可我不肯：我太怕了；怕的是从任何地方都永远不会有一下应答的咔嗒，或一星闪光，或一丝动静。……

===Original===

I could have made short work, and landed at Hull: but I would not: I was so afraid. For I was used to the silence of the ice: and I was used to the silence of the sea: but I was afraid of the silence of England.

===Chinese===

我本可以抄近路，在赫尔登岸的：可我不肯：我怕。因为我习惯了冰的寂静：也习惯了海的寂静：可我怕的是英格兰的寂静。

===Original===

I came in sight of the coast on the morning of the 26th August, somewhere about Hornsea, but did not see any town, for I put the helm to port, and went on further south, no longer bothering with the instruments, but coasting at haphazard, now in sight of land, and now in the centre of a circle of sea; not admitting to myself the motive of this loitering slowness, nor thinking at all, but ignoring the deep-buried fear of the tomorrow which I shirked, and instinctively hiding myself in today. I passed the Wash, I passed Yarmouth, Felixstowe. By now the things that floated motionless on the sea were beyond numbering, for I could hardly lower my eyes ten minutes and lift them, without seeing yet another there: so that soon after dusk I, too, had to lie still among them all, till morning: for they lay dark, and to move at any pace would have been to drown the already dead.
Well, I came to the Thames-mouth, and lay pretty well in among the Flats and Pan Sands towards eight one evening, not seven miles from Sheppey and the North Kent coast: and I did not see any Nore Light, nor Girdler Light: and all along the coast I had seen no light: but as to that I said not one word to myself, not admitting it, nor letting my heart know what my brain thought, nor my brain know what my heart surmised; but with a daft and mock-mistrustful under-look I would regard the darkling land, holding it a sentient thing that would be playing a prank upon a poor man like me.
And the next morning, when I moved again, my furtive eye-corners were very well aware of the Prince’s Channel lightship, and also the Tongue ship, for there they were: but I would not look at them at all, nor go near them: for I did not wish to have anything to do with whatever might have happened beyond my own ken, and it was better to look straight before, seeing nothing, and concerning one’s-self with one’s-self.
The next evening, after having gone out to sea again, I was in a little to the E. by S. of the North Foreland: and I saw no light there, nor any Sandhead light; but over the sea vast signs of wreckage, and the coasts were strewn with old wrecked fleets. I turned about S. E.

===Chinese===

八月二十六日早晨，我在霍恩西一带望见了海岸，却没有看见什么城镇，因为我把舵转向左舷，继续向南，不再劳神摆弄仪器，只是信马由缰地沿岸而行，时而望得见陆地，时而被一圈海环在中央；不肯向自己承认这拖延迟缓的动机，也根本不去想，只把对明天那深埋的恐惧搁在一边，本能地把自己藏进今天里。我过了沃什湾，过了雅茅斯、费利克斯托。这时，一动不动漂在海上的东西已多得数不清，因为我几乎每低眼十分钟再抬起来，便会看见又一件：于是黄昏后不久，我也不得不躺在它们中间，一动不动，直到天明：因为它们黑压压躺着，一动就会撞上，任何速度的移动都等于把已死的再淹死一遍。
唉，我到了泰晤士河口，一天傍晚八点前后，正停在弗拉茨与潘桑兹中间，离谢佩岛和肯特北岸不到七英里：我没有望见诺尔灯船，也没有格德勒灯船：而且沿岸一路我没见过一盏灯：可这事我一个字也不对自己说，不承认，也不让我的心知道我的脑子在想什么，不让我的脑子知道我的心在猜什么；我只带着一种痴狂的、假意怀疑的眼波，去打量那昏暗的陆地，把它当作一个有知觉的东西，正在对一个像我这样的可怜人耍恶作剧。
第二天早晨我再动身时，我鬼祟的眼角早已清清楚楚望见了王子水道的灯船，还有塔恩灯船，因为它们就在那里：可我根本不看它们，也不靠近：因为凡是我自己见识之外可能发生的事，我一概不想沾惹；不如直望前方，什么也看不见，只管自己顾自己。
再下一天傍晚，重新出海之后，我到了北福兰角偏东偏南一点的地方：那里我没有望见灯，桑德海德也没有灯：只见海上大片大片失事的残迹，海岸上狼藉着陈年的失事船队。我转向东南。

===Original===

, very slowly moving﻿—for anywhere hereabouts hundreds upon hundreds of craft lay dead within a ten-mile circle of sea﻿—and by two in the fore-day had wandered up well in sight of the French cliffs: for I had said: “I will go and see the light-beam of the great revolving-drum on Calais pier that nightly beams halfway oversea to England.” And the moon shone clear in the southern heaven that morning, like a great old dying queen whose Court swarms distantly from around her, diffident, pale, and tremulous, the paler the nearer; and I could see the mountain-shadows on her spotty full-face, and her misty aureole, and her lights on the sea, as it were kisses stolen in the kingdom of sleep; and all among the quiet ships mysterious white trails and powderings of light, like palace-corridors in some fairyland forlorn, full of breathless wan whispers, scandals, and runnings-to-and-fro, with leers, and agitated last embraces, and flight of the princess, and deathbed of the king; and on the N. E. horizon a bank of brown cloud that seemed to have no relation with the world; and yonder, not far, the white coast-cliffs, not so low as at Calais near, but arranged in masses separated by vales of sward, each with its wreck: but no light of any revolving-drum I saw.

===Chinese===

，极慢地移动着——因为这带海上，方圆十英里之内，横七竖八躺着成百上千条死船——到天亮前两点，已游荡到望得见法国悬崖的地方：因为我曾对自己说：「我要去看看加来码头上那座大转鼓的灯，它夜夜把光柱投过半个海峡，照向英格兰。」那天早晨，月亮在南天清清地照着，像一位伟大而垂死的老王后，她的宫廷从她四周围远远散开，怯生生的、苍白而颤抖，越近的越苍白；我看得见她那带斑的圆脸上的山影，她雾蒙蒙的光晕，她落在海上的光，仿佛睡眠王国里偷来的吻；寂静的船群之间，到处是神秘的白色光痕和光屑，像某座荒凉仙乡里的宫中长廊，满是屏息的苍白低语、流言、奔走，斜眼瞟视，激动的最后拥抱，公主的出逃，国王的临终；东北天际上是一垛褐色的云堤，仿佛与世界全不相干；那边不远，是白色的海岸悬崖，不似近加来处那样低，而是成大片大片地排开，间隔着草皮的谷地，每一片下都有它的失事船：可我没有望见任何转鼓的灯。

===Original===

I could not sleep that night: for all the operations of my mind and body seemed in abeyance. Mechanically I turned the ship westward again; and when the sun came up, there, hardly two miles from me, were the cliffs of Dover; and on the crenulated summit of the Castle I spied the Union Jack hang motionless.
I heard eight, nine o’clock strike in the cabin, and I was still at sea. But some mad, audacious whisper was at my brain: and at 10:30, the 2nd September, immediately opposite the Cross Wall Custom House, the *Boreal*’s anchor-chain, after a voyage of three years, two months, and fourteen days, ran thundering, thundering, through the starboard hawsehole.
Ah heaven! but I must have been stark mad to let the anchor go! for the effect upon me of that shocking obstreperous hubbub, breaking in upon all that cemetery repose that blessed morning, and lasting it seemed a year, was most appalling; and at the sudden racket I stood excruciated, with shivering knees and flinching heart, God knows: for not less terrifically uproarious than the clatter of the last Trump it raged and raged, and I thought that all the billion dead could not fail to start, and rise, at alarm so excessive, and question me with their eyes.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

那一夜我睡不着：因为我的心智和身体的一切运转仿佛都停摆了。我机械地把船又转向西行；太阳升起时，离我不到两英里，就是多佛的白垩峭壁；城堡堞垛的顶上，我望见联合杰克旗纹丝不动地垂着。
我听见舱里的钟敲了八点、九点，我还在海上。可有个疯狂大胆的低语在我脑边牵缠：于是9月2日上午10点半，正对着十字墙海关，*博雷尔*号的锚链，历经三年两个月零十四天的航程，轰隆隆、轰隆隆地泻过了右舷锚链孔。
天啊！我放锚的那一刻准是彻底疯了！因为那震耳欲聋的喧闹，撞进那个蒙福的清晨墓园般的寂静里，一响仿佛响了一年，给我的震撼实在骇人；那突如其来的喧嚣里，我站在那里如受酷刑，膝盖发抖，心口畏缩，天知道：因为它翻滚又翻滚，其鼎沸不亚于最后号角的喧腾；我想，十亿死者听到这般惊天动地的警报，必定会一齐惊起、起身，用他们的眼睛质问我。……

===Original===

On the top of the Cross Wall near I saw a grey crab fearlessly crawl; at the end where the street begins, I saw a single gaslight palely burn that broad day, and at its foot a black man lay on his face, clad only in a shirt and one boot; the harbour was almost packed with every sort of craft, and on a Calais-Dover boat, eight yards from my stern, which must have left Calais crowded to suffocation, I saw the rotted dead lie heaped, she being unmoored, and continually grinding against an anchored green brig.
And when I saw that, I dropped down upon my knees at the capstan, and my poor heart sobbed out the frail cry: “Well, Lord God, Thou hast destroyed the work of Thy hand﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

近处十字墙的顶上，我看见一只灰蟹肆无忌惮地爬行；街道起始的那一头，大白天里还亮着一盏煤气灯，惨淡地燃着，灯下一个黑人俯面躺着，身上只有一件衬衫和一只靴子；港里几乎塞满了各式船只，离我船尾八码远的一条加来—多佛渡船上——它离开加来时必定挤得透不过气——我看见腐坏的死者堆叠横陈，船已脱了缆，不住地磨蹭着一条下锚的绿色双桅横帆船。
一见这情形，我扑倒在绞盘旁跪下，我那可怜的心哽咽出一句脆弱的哭喊：『罢了，主上帝，你毁了你手所造的工……』

===Original===

After a time I got up, went below in a state of somnambulism, took a packet of pemmican cakes, leapt to land, and went following the railway that runs from the Admiralty Pier. In an enclosed passage ten yards long, with railway masonry on one side, I saw five dead lie, and could not believe that I was in England, for all were dark-skinned people, three gaudily dressed, and two in flowing white robes. It was the same when I turned into a long street, leading northward, for here were a hundred, or more, and never saw I, except in Constantinople, where I once lived eighteen months, so variegated a mixture of races, black, brunette, brown, yellow, white, in all the shades, some emaciated like people dead from hunger, and, overlooking them all, one English boy with a clean Eton collar sitting on a bicycle, supported by a lamppost which his arms clasped, he proving clearly the extraordinary suddenness of the death which had overtaken them all.
I did not know whither, nor why, I went, nor had I the least idea whether all this was visually seen by me in the world which I had known, or in some other, or was all fantasy of my disembodied spirit﻿—for I had the thought that I, too, might be dead since old ages, and my spirit wandering now through the universe of space, in which there is neither north nor south, nor up nor down, nor measure nor relation, nor aught whatever, save an uneasy consciousness of a dream about bottomlessness. Of grief or pain, I think, I felt nothing; though I have a sort of memory now that some sound, resembling a sob or groan, though it was neither, came at regular clockwork intervals from my bosom during three or four days. Meantime, my brain registered like a tape-machine details the most frivolous, the most ludicrous﻿—the name of a street, Strond Street, Snargate Street; the round fur cap﻿—black fur for the side, white ermine for the top﻿—of a portly Karaite priest on his back, whose robes had been blown to his spread knees, as if lifted and neatly folded there; a violin-bow gripped between the thick, irregular teeth of a little Spaniard with brushed-back hair and mad-looking eyes; odd shoes on the foot of a French girl, one black, one brown.

===Chinese===

过了一阵我起身，梦游似的下到舱里，拿了一包干肉饼，跳上岸，便沿着那条从海军部码头出发的铁路走。在一条十码长的封闭过道里，一侧是铁路的砖石建筑，我看见躺着五个死人，却不能相信自己在英格兰，因为五个全是深肤色的人，三个衣着华丽，两个披着飘拂的白袍。拐进一条向北的长街时也是一样：这里有一百人，或者更多，我除了在君士坦丁堡——我曾在那里住过十八个月——从没见过如此驳杂的种族混合：黑的、深褐的、棕的、黄的、白的，深浅百般，有些人瘦削得像饿死的；而在这一切之上，一个英国男孩，戴着挺括的伊顿领，坐在一辆自行车上，靠着一根灯柱，双臂搂着柱子——他清清楚楚地证明：那场落到他们所有人头上的死亡，来得何等突然。
我不知道自己往哪里去，也不知道为什么去；这一切，究竟是我在我所识得的这个世界里亲眼看见的，还是在别的什么世界里，抑或全是我那脱壳的灵魂的幻境——我连这一点也毫无把握——因为我起过一个念头：说不定我自己早已死了千万年，我的灵魂此刻正在空间的宇宙里游荡，那里没有南，没有北，没有上，没有下，没有度量，没有关系，什么也没有，只有一场关于无底的梦的不安的意识。至于悲伤或痛苦，我想我是毫无所感的；虽然如今我模模糊糊记得，有那么一种声音，像呜咽，又像呻吟，却两样都不是，一连三四天，按着钟点似的间隔，从我胸中发出。与此同时，我的脑子却像一台纸带机，记录下种种最琐碎、最荒唐的细节——街名：斯特龙德街，斯纳盖特街；一个胖大的卡拉派祭司仰面躺着，他那顶圆毛帽——帽身黑毛，帽顶白貂——还有他那被风掀到张开的双膝上的袍子，仿佛被人提起、齐齐整整叠放在那里；一个小个子西班牙人，头发向后梳，眼睛发狂，不甚齐整的厚牙间咬着一支琴弓；一个法国姑娘脚上两只不成对的鞋，一只黑，一只棕。

===Original===

They lay in the street about as numerous as gunners who fall round their carriage, at intervals of five to ten feet, the majority﻿—as was the case also in Norway, and on the ships﻿—in poses of distraction, with spread arms, or wildly distorted limbs, like men who, the instant before death, called upon the rocks and hills to cover them.

===Chinese===

他们躺在街上，密集得如同阵亡在炮架四周的炮手，彼此相隔五到十英尺；大多数人——在挪威、在船上也是一样——摆着仓皇失措的姿势，双臂张开，四肢狂乱地扭曲，像是在断气前的一瞬，呼吁岩石和山冈来遮盖自己的人们。

===Original===

On the left I came to an opening in the land, called, I believe, “The Shaft,” and into this I turned, climbing a very great number of steps, almost covered at one point with dead: the steps I began to count, but left off, then the dead, and left off. Finally, at the top, which must be even higher than the Castle, I came to a great open space laid out with gravel-walks, and saw fortifications, barracks, a citadel. I did not know the town, except by passings-through, and was surprised at the breadth of view. Between me and the Castle to the east lay the district of crowding houses, brick and ragstone, mixed in the distance with vague azure haze; and to the right the harbour, the sea, with their ships; and visible around me on the heights seven or eight dead, biting the dust; the sun now high and warm, with hardly a cloud in the sky; and yonder a mist, which was the coast of France.
It seemed too big for one poor man.
My head nodded. I sat on a bench, black-painted and hard, the seat and back of horizontal boards, with intervals; and as I looked, I nodded, heavy-headed and weary: for it was too big for me. And as I nodded, with forehead propped on my left hand, and the packet of pemmican cakes in my right, there was in my head, somehow, an old street-song of my childhood: and I groaned it sleepily, like coronachs and drear funereal nenias, dirging; and the packet beat time in my right hand, falling and raising, falling heavily and rising, in time.

> I’ll buy the ring,

You’ll rear the kids:

Servants to wait on our ting, ting, ting.
> Ting, ting,

Won’t we be happy?

Ting, ting,

That shall be it:

I’ll buy the ring,

You’ll rear the kids:

Servants to wait on our ting, ting, ting.

So maundering, I fell forward upon my face, and for twenty-three hours, the living undistinguished from the dead, I slept there.

===Chinese===

左边我遇到一处豁口，名字大概是『竖井』，我便拐了进去，登上一大段台阶，有一段几乎被死人盖满：台阶我开始数，数着数着不数了；改数死人，数着数着也不数了。终于到了顶上——想必比城堡还高——我来到一片铺着砾石步道的开阔场地，看见了碉堡、兵营和一座要塞。这镇子我只是路过时经过，并不熟悉，那视野的开阔叫我吃了一惊。我与东面的城堡之间，是一片拥挤的房屋，砖房与糙砂岩房相间，远处融进一片迷离的青霭；右边是港口、海，和那些船；四周高地上，看得分明，横着七八个死人，嘴啃着尘土；太阳已高，暖融融的，天上几乎没有一片云；远处一带薄雾，那是法兰西的海岸。
这对一个可怜人来说，未免太大了。
我的头直打盹。我在一张长椅上坐下，椅子漆成黑色，硬梆梆的，椅面和椅背都是横板拼的，板与板之间留着缝；我望着望着，便垂头打起盹来，脑袋沉重，浑身困倦：因为这对我太大了。我打着盹，前额支在左手上，右手拿着一包干肉饼，脑子里不知怎么转起一支童年听来的老街歌；我瞌睡睡地哼着它，像送葬的挽歌、凄凉的哀曲，一声一声；那包干肉饼在我右手里打着拍子，落下，抬起，沉沉落下，又抬起，正合着节拍。

> 我来买戒指，

你来养娃娃：

仆人们伺候咱叮叮当。

> 叮叮，

咱们多快活？

叮叮，

就这么定了：

我来买戒指，

你来养娃娃：

仆人们伺候咱叮叮当。

就这样咿咿呀呀地哼着，我朝前扑倒在地，一睡睡了二十三个钟头，活人同死人已无分别。

===Original===

I was awakened by drizzle, leapt up, looked at a silver chronometer which, attached by a leather to my belt, I carried in my breeches-pocket, and saw that it was 10 a.m. The sky was dark, and a moaning wind﻿—almost a new thing now to me﻿—had arisen.
I ate some pemmican, for I had a reluctance﻿—needless as it turned out﻿—to touch any of the thousand luxuries here, sufficient no doubt, in a town like Dover alone, to last me five or six hundred years, if I could live so long; and, having eaten, I descended The Shaft, and spent the whole day, though it rained and blustered continually, in wandering about. Reasoning, in my numb way, from the number of ships on the sea, I expected to find the town overcrowded with dead: but this was not so; and I should say, at a venture, that not a thousand English, nor fifteen thousand foreigners, were in it: for that westward rage and stampede must have operated here also, leaving the town empty but for the ever new-coming hosts.
The first thing which I did was to go into an open grocer’s shop, which was also a post and telegraph office, with the notion, I suppose, to get a message through to London. In the shop a single gaslight was burning its last, and this, with that near the pier, were the only two that I saw: and ghastly enough they looked, transparently wannish, and as it were ashamed, like blinking night-things overtaken by the glare of day. I conjectured that they had so burned and watched during months, or years: for they were now blazing diminished, with streaks and rays in the flame, as if by effort, and if these were the only two, they must have needed time to all-but exhaust the works. Before the counter lay a fashionably-dressed negro with a number of tied parcels scattered about him, and on the counter an empty till, and behind it a tall thin woman with her face resting sideways in the till, fingers clutching the outer counter-rim, and such an expression of frantic terror as I never saw. I got over the counter to a table behind a wire-gauze, and, like a numb fool, went over the Morse alphabet in my mind before touching the transmitting key, though I knew no code-words, and there, big enough to be seen, was the A.B.C.

===Chinese===

一阵毛毛雨把我打醒，我一跃而起，看了看那只用皮带系在腰带上、揣在马裤袋里的银天文表：上午10点。天色阴沉，一股呜咽的风——如今于我几乎是件新鲜事——已经刮了起来。
我吃了些干肉饼，因为我不情愿——事后证明全无必要——去动这里成千上万的珍馐；单是多佛这样一个镇子里的存货，毫无疑问就够我吃五六百年，倘若我活得那样长的话；吃罢，我下了『竖井』，虽然雨下个不停、风呼啸不止，我还是在外面游荡了一整天。我以我麻木的方式，从海上船只的数目推想，满以为会看见镇上尸满为患：事实却不然；我斗胆估摸，镇里的英国人不足一千，外国人也不到一万五：因为那场向西的狂潮大奔逃想必在这里同样发作过，把镇子撇得空空荡荡，只剩下不断新涌来的大批人群。
我做的头一件事，是走进一家敞着门的杂货铺，它兼着邮局和电报局，我心里想的，大概是往伦敦发个电文。铺子里一盏煤气灯正燃着残焰，它加上码头附近那一盏，是我见过的仅有的两盏：它们看上去够凄惶的了，光色苍白透明，仿佛含羞带愧，像被白昼强光逮个正着的、眨着眼的夜行之物。我猜想它们就这样燃着、守着，已有好几个月，或好几年：因为它们此刻的火苗已燃得孱弱，焰里抽着一缕缕丝芒，仿佛在勉力支撑；倘若全城只有这两盏，那几乎耗尽气源，必定需要时日。柜台前躺着一个衣着时髦的黑人，身边散落着好几个捆好的包裹；柜台上是空的钱屉，抽屉后面是一个瘦高女人，脸侧伏在钱屉里，手指抠着柜台外沿，脸上那种狂乱的惊恐，我从未见过。我翻过柜台，走到铁丝网后面的一张桌前，像个麻木的傻子，碰发报键之前先在心里默过了一遍莫尔斯电码——虽然我什么密语也不懂——而那里，一眼就看得见的，是A.B.C.

===Original===

dial, and who was to answer my message I did not ask myself: for habit was still strong upon me, and my mind refused to reason from what I saw to what I did not see; but the moment I touched the key, and peered greedily at the galvanometer-needle at my right, I saw that it did not move, for no current was passing; and with a kind of fright, I was up, leapt, and got away from the place, though there was a great number of telegrams about the receiver which, if I had been in my senses, I would have stopped and read.
Turning the corner of the next street, I saw wide-open the door of a substantial large house, and went in. From bottom to top there was no one there, except one English girl, sitting back in an easy-chair in the drawing-room, which was richly furnished with Valenciennes curtains and azure-satin things. She was a girl of the lowest class, hardly clad in black rags, and there she lay with hanging jaw, in a very crooked and awkward pose, a jemmy at her feet, in her left hand a roll of banknotes, and in her lap three watches. In fact, the bodies which I saw here were, in general, either those of new-come foreigners, or else of the very poor, the very old, or the very young.
But what made me remember this house was that I found here on one of the sofas a newspaper: *The Kent Express*; and sitting unconscious of my dead neighbour, I pored a long while over what was written there.
It said in a passage which I tore out and kept:

===Chinese===

电码盘；至于谁来应答我的电文，我没有自问：因为习惯的惯性还牢牢抓着我，我的心智也拒绝从所见推想那不曾看见的；可我的手指一触到键，贪婪地盯住右边电流计的指针，便看见它纹丝不动——没有电流通过；我一阵惊惶，跳起身，夺路逃出了那地方，尽管收报机四周散落着大量电报纸条，若在神志清明时，我本会停下来读一读的。
拐过下一条街的街角，我看见一座殷实大宅的门大敞四开，便走了进去。从底层到顶楼，里面再无一人，只有一个英国姑娘，后仰坐在客厅的安乐椅里，那客厅陈设豪奢，挂着瓦朗谢讷花边的窗帘，摆着天青缎的物件。她是个最底层人家的姑娘，黑衣褴褛，几乎衣不蔽体，就那样下领垂坠地歪躺在那里，姿势别扭至极：脚边一根撬棍，左手一卷钞票，膝上三块怀表。事实上，我在这里见到的尸身，大体不是新来的外国人，便是最穷的人、最老的人、或最幼的人。
可叫我记住这座宅子的，是我在一张沙发上发现了一份报纸：《肯特快报》（*The Kent Express*）；我坐下来，浑然不觉身旁那死者，对着报上的文字出了好半天的神。
其中有一段，我撕了下来，留到今天：

===Original===

> “Telegraphic communication with Tilsit, Insterburg, Warsaw, Krakow, Przemysl, Gross Wardein, Karlsburg, and many smaller towns lying immediately eastward of the 21st parallel of longitude has ceased during the night. In some at least of them there must have been operators still at their duty, undrawn into the great westward-rushing torrent: but as all messages from Western Europe have been answered only by that dread mysterious silence which, just three months and two days since, astounded the world in the case of Eastern New Zealand, we can only assume that these towns, too, have been added to the long and mournful list; indeed, after last evening’s Paris telegrams we might have prophesied with some certainty, not merely their overthrow, but even the hour of it: for the rate-uniformity of the slow-riding vapour which is touring our globe is no longer doubtful, and has even been definitely fixed by Professor Craven at 100½ miles per day, or 4 miles 330 yards per hour. Its nature, its origin, remains, of course, nothing but matter of conjecture: for it leaves no living thing behind it: nor, God knows, is that of any moment now to us who remain. The rumour that it is associated with an odour of almonds is declared, on high authority, to be improbable; but the morose purple of its impending gloom has been attested by tardy fugitives from the face of its rolling and smoky march.
> “Is this the end? We do not, and cannot, believe it. Will the pure sky which we today see above us be invaded in nine days, or less, by this smoke of the Pit of Darkness? In spite of the assurances of the scientists, we still doubt. For, if so, to what purpose that long drama of History, in which we seem to see the Hand of the Dramaturgist? Surely, the end of a Fifth Act should be obvious, satisfying to one’s sense of the complete: but History, so far, long as it has been, resembles rather a Prologue than a Fifth Act. Can it be that the Manager, utterly dissatisfied, would sweep all off, and ‘hang up’ the piece forever? Certainly, the sins of mankind have been as scarlet: and if the fair earth which he has turned into Hell, send forth now upon him the smoke of Hell, little the wonder. But we cannot yet believe.

===Chinese===

> 『与蒂尔西特、因斯特堡、华沙、克拉科夫、普热梅希尔、大瓦尔德恩、卡尔斯堡，以及紧邻东经21度以东的许多较小城镇的电报联系，已于昨夜中断。其中至少有几处，想必还有报务员坚守岗位，未被卷入那股向西奔涌的大洪流：但发自西欧的一切电文，得到的回应唯有那可怕而神秘的沉默——正是这沉默，三个月零两天前在新西兰东部一案中震骇了世界——故而我们只能认定，这些城镇也已被添上那串漫长而哀伤的名单；的确，读过昨晚的巴黎电报之后，我们本可颇有把握地预言，不仅是它们的倾覆，连倾覆的时辰也未必算不出：因为那缕巡游全球、缓缓推进的毒雾，其速度之均一已不再存疑，克雷文教授甚至已明确测出：每日100.5英里，即每小时4英里330码。它的性质、它的来历，当然仍只是猜测之谈：因为它身后不留一个活物：而上帝知道，这对我等幸存者而言，也已无关紧要。有传言说它伴有一股杏仁气味，权威方面已声明此说不足凭信；但它那滚滚冒烟的行军袭来之前，天际便已郁结成阴沉的紫色，这一点，已由逃离较晚的难民所证实。
> 『这就是终结么？我们不这样信，也不能这样信。我们今日头顶这片纯净的天空，会不会在九天之内、或更短的时间里，被这黑暗深渊之坑的浓烟所侵占？尽管科学家们言之凿凿，我们仍然存疑。因为，倘若如此，那部漫长的历史正剧——我们在其中仿佛看见剧作家之手——又有何意义？诚然，第五幕的结局应当一目了然，方能餍足人心对完满的期许：可迄今为止的历史，尽管绵长，与其说像第五幕，不如说更像一出序幕。莫非是那位经理全然不满，要把一切扫荡干净，将这出戏永远『挂起』？诚然，人类的罪孽已红如朱颜；倘若被他化作地狱的这颗美丽尘寰，如今把地狱的烟喷还给他，那也无足为怪。可我们仍不能相信。

===Original===

There is a sparing strain in nature, and through the world, as a thread, is spun a silence which smiles, and on the end of events we find placarded large the words: ‘Why were ye afraid?’ A dignified Hope, therefore﻿—even now, when we cower beneath this worldwide shadow of the wings of the Condor of Death﻿—becomes us: and, indeed, we see such an attitude among some of the humblest of our people, from whose heart ascends the cry: ‘Though He slay me, yet will I trust in Him.’ Here, therefore, O Lord! O Lord, look down, and save!
> “But even as we thus write of hope, Reason, if we would hear her, whispers us ‘fool’: and inclement is the sky of earth. No more ships can New York Harbour contain, and whereas among us men die weekly of privations by the hundred thousand, yonder across the sea they perish by the million: for where the rich are pinched, how can the poor live? Already 700 out of the 1,000 millions of our race have perished, and the empires of civilisation have crumbled like sand-castles in a horror of anarchy. Thousands upon thousands of unburied dead, anticipating the more deliberate doom that comes and smokes, and rides and comes and comes, and does not fail, encumber the streets of London, Manchester, Liverpool. The guides of the nation have fled; the father stabs his child, and the wife her husband, for a morsel of food; the fields lie waste; wanton crowds carouse in our churches, universities, palaces, banks and hospitals; we understand that late last night three territorial regiments, the Munster Fusiliers, and the Lotian and East Lancashire Regiments, riotously disbanded themselves, shooting two officers; infectious diseases, as we all know, have spread beyond limit; in several towns the police seem to have disappeared, and, in nearly all, every vestige of decency; the results following upon the sudden release of the convicts appear to be monstrous in the respective districts; and within three short months Hell seems to have acquired this entire planet, sending forth Horror, like a rabid wolf, and Despair, like a disastrous sky, to devour and confound her. Hear, therefore, O Lord, and forgive our iniquities! O Lord, we beseech Thee! Look down, O Lord, and spare!”

===Chinese===

天性之中自有一种怜惜的血脉；贯穿世界，如一缕丝线，纺织着一种含笑的沉默；而在万事的末端，我们发现张贴着大字：『你们为何惧怕？』因此，一种庄重的希望——即便此刻，当我们蜷伏在死亡神鹫双翼的全球阴影之下——依然合乎我们的身份：的确，我们在最卑微的同胞中间看到了这样的气度，从他们心中升起呼喊：『即使他杀我，我仍要信靠他。』所以，主啊！主啊，垂顾下界，施予拯救！
> 『可正当我们这样书写希望之际，理性——倘若我们肯听她——却向我们耳语一声「痴人」：而大地的天空，严酷无情。纽约港已再容不下一艘船；我们这里每周有成千上万的人死于匮乏，海那边却整百万整百万地死亡：富人都已捉襟见肘，穷人又如何存活？我们十亿同胞，已经有七亿殒灭；文明各帝国已在无政府的恐怖中，像沙砌的城堡一样崩塌。成千上万未及掩埋的死者——他们不过是先走一步，等着那更从容的劫数：它来了，冒着烟，驰着，来了又来，从不爽约——壅塞着伦敦、曼彻斯特、利物浦的街头。国家的引导者们已经逃散；父亲为一口吃食捅死孩子，妻子捅死丈夫；田畴荒芜；放纵的人群在我们的教堂、大学、宫殿、银行和医院里纵酒狂欢；据悉昨夜深夜，三个本土团——芒斯特燧发枪团、洛锡安团与东兰开夏团——哗变解散，枪杀了两名军官；众所共知，疫病已蔓延得无边无际；好几座城镇里警察似已绝迹，而几乎所有城镇，体面的最后残余也已荡然；囚犯骤然开释的后果，在各自地区已是骇人听闻；短短三个月内，地狱仿佛已吞下了这整颗星球，放出恐怖，如一头疯狼，放出绝望，如一片凶岁的天空，来吞噬她、搅乱她。所以，主啊，垂听我们，赦免我们的罪孽！主啊，我们恳求你！主啊，垂顾下界，宽恕饶恕！』

===Original===

When I had read this, and the rest of the paper, which had one whole sheet-side blank, I sat a long hour there, eyeing a little patch of the purple ash on a waxed board near the corner where the girl sat with her timepieces, so useless in her Eternity; and there was not a feeling in me, except a pricking of curiosity, which afterwards became morbid and ravenous, to know something more of that cloud, or smoke, of which this man spoke, of its dates, its origin, its nature, its minute details. Afterwards, I went down, and entered several houses, searching for more papers, but did not find any; then I found a paper-shop which was open, with boards outside, but either it had been deserted, or printing must have stopped about the date of the paper which I had read, for the only three newspapers there were dated long prior, and I did not read them.
Now it was raining, and a blustering autumn day it was, distributing the odours of the world, and bringing me continual mixed whiffs of flowers and the hateful stench of decay. But I would not mind it much.
I wandered and wandered, till I was tired of spahi and bashi-bazouk, of Greek and Catalan, of Russian “pope” and Coptic abuna, of dragoman and Calmuck, of Egyptian maulawi and Afghan mullah, Neapolitan and sheik, and the nightmare of wild poses, colours, stuffs and garbs, the yellow-green keffiyeh of the Bedouin, shawl-turbans of Baghdad, the voluminous rose-silk tob of women, and face-veils, and stark distorted nakedness, and sashes of figured muslin, and the workman’s cords, and the red tarboosh. About four, for very weariness, I was sitting on a door-steep, bent beneath the rain; but soon was up again, fascinated no doubt by this changing bazaar of sameness, its chance combinations and permutations, and novelty in monotony. About five I was at a station, marked Harbour Station, in and about which lay a considerable crowd, but not one train. I sat again, and rested, rose and roamed again; soon after six I found myself at another station, called “Priory”; and here I saw two long trains, both crowded, one on a siding, and one at the up-platform.
I examined both engines, and found them of the old boiler steam-type with manholes, heaters, autoclaves, feed-pump, etc., now rare in western countries, except England.

===Chinese===

读完这篇，也读完了报纸余下的部分——有一整版是空白的——我在那里又坐了长久一个钟头，眼睛盯着角落近旁一块上过蜡的木板上一小片紫色的灰烬，那姑娘带着她的几件计时器就坐在那角落里，在她的永恒里，它们全无用处；我心里没有一丝感触，只有一种好奇的刺痒——后来这好奇变得病态而贪婪——想知道更多那云、或烟的事，想知道这人所说的它的日期、来历、性质和细枝末节。随后我下去，走进几户人家，想再找些报纸，却一张也没找到；后来找到一家开着门的报馆，门外还架着报板，可它要么早已人去楼空，要么印刷在我读到的那份报纸日期前后就停了，因为那里仅有的三份报纸日期都远在其前，我没有读。
这时下起雨来，是个呼啸的秋日，把世界种种的气味四处播撒，不断给我送来一阵阵混杂的气息：花的香，和腐朽那可憎的恶臭。可我不大理会。
我游荡啊游荡，直到看腻了斯帕希骑兵和巴什波祖克、希腊人和加泰罗尼亚人、俄国「神父」和科普特主教、译员和卡尔梅克人、埃及的毛拉维和阿富汗的毛拉、那不勒斯人和谢赫，看腻了那野姿势、颜色、料子和衣装的噩梦：贝都因人黄绿色的头巾，巴格达的披巾头巾，妇人繁复的玫瑰绸长袍，和面纱，和僵直扭曲的赤裸，和印花纱的腰带，和工人的灯芯绒，和红色塔布什帽。四点光景，实在乏了，我坐在一道门阶上，在雨下伛偻着；可不久又起身了，无疑是被这大同小异的变幻市场迷住了：它偶然的组合与排列，它单调里的新奇。五点前后，我到了一座车站，标着「港站」，站里站外躺着相当多的一群人，却没有一列火车。我又坐下歇了歇，起身再逛；六点过后不久，我发觉自己到了另一座车站，叫「普赖里」；在这里我望见两列长车，都挤满了人，一列停在岔线上，一列停在上行月台边。
我检查了两台机车，发现都是旧式锅炉蒸汽机，带人孔、加热器、高压釜、给水泵等等，如今在西方国家已属罕见，英格兰除外。

===Original===

In one there was no water, but in that at the platform, the float-lever, barely tilted toward the float, showed that there was some in the boiler. Of this one I overhauled all the machinery, and found it good, though rusted. There was plenty of fuel, and oil, which I supplemented from a near shop: and during ninety minutes my brain and hands worked with an intelligence as it were automatic, of their own motion. After three journeys across the station and street, I saw the fire blaze well, and the manometer move; when the lever of the safety-valve, whose load I lightened by half an atmosphere, lifted, I jumped down, and tried to disconnect the long string of carriages from the engine: but failed, the coupling being an automatic arrangement new to me; nor did I care. It was now very dark; but there was still oil for bull’s-eye and lantern, and I lit them. I forgot nothing. I rolled driver and stoker﻿—the guard was absent﻿—one to the platform, one upon the rails: and I took their place there. At about 8:30 I ran out from Dover, my throttle-valve pealing high a long falsetto through the bleak and desolate night.

===Chinese===

其中一台锅炉里没有水；但停在月台的那台，浮杆微微朝浮子倾着，表明锅炉里还有些水。我把这一台的机器整个检修了一遍，发现都好，只是锈了。燃料和油都不缺，我又从近旁一家铺子作了补充：九十分钟里，我的脑和手以一种仿佛自动的智能动着，全凭它们自己的主张。来回穿过了三趟车站和街道，我看见炉火烧旺了，压力表的针动了；安全阀的杆——我把它的配重减了半个大气压——抬起来时，我跳下去，想把那一长串车厢同机车摘开：却没成，那车钩是一种我不熟悉的自动装置；我也懒得管了。这时天已很黑；可牛眼灯和提灯还有油，我把它们点亮。我什么都没有忘。我把司机和司炉——车长不在——一个滚到月台上，一个滚到铁轨上：我顶了他们的班。约莫八点半，我驶出多佛，我的汽阀在凄凉荒寂的夜里，高高地拉出一声长长的假嗓。

===Original===

My aim was London. But even as I set out, my heart smote me: I knew nothing of the metals, their junctions, facing-points, sidings, shuntings, and complexities. Even as to whether I was going toward, or away from, London, I was not sure. But just in proportion as my first timorousness of the engine hardened into familiarity and self-sureness, I quickened speed, wilfully, with an obstinacy deaf and blind.
Finally, from a mere crawl at first, I was flying at a shocking velocity, while something, tongue in cheek, seemed to whisper me: “There must be other trains blocking the lines, at stations, in yards, and everywhere﻿—it is a maniac’s ride, a ride of death, and Flying Dutchman’s frenzy: remember your dark five-deep brigade of passengers, who rock and bump together, and will suffer in a collision.” But with mulish stubbornness I thought: “They wished to go to London”; and on I raged, not wildly exhilarated, so far as I can remember, nor lunatic, but feeling the dull glow of a wicked and morose Unreason urge in my bosom, while I stoked all blackened at the fire, or saw the vague mass of dead horse or cow, running trees and fields, and dark homestead and deep-slumbering farm, flit ghostly athwart the murky air, as the half-blind saw “men like trees walking.”
Long, however, it did not last: I could not have been twenty miles from Dover when, on a long reach of straight lines, I made out before me a tarpaulined mass opposite a signal-point: and at once callousness changed to terror within me. But even as I plied the brake, I felt that it was too late: I rushed to the gangway to make a wild leap down an embankment to the right, but was thrown backward by a quick series of rough bumps, caused by eight or ten cattle which lay there across the lines: and when I picked myself up, and leapt, some seconds before the impact, the speed must have considerably slackened, for I received no fracture, but lay in semi-coma in a patch of yellow-flowered whin on level ground, and was even conscious of a fire on the lines forty yards away, and, all the night, of vague thunder sounding from somewhere.

===Chinese===

我的目标是伦敦。可才出发，我的心便谴责起我来：我对铁轨、道岔、尖轨、侧线、调车及其种种纠葛一无所知。甚至连我是在朝伦敦去，还是在背它而行，我也没有把握。可恰恰随着我对机车最初的胆怯硬化成熟稔与自信，我便提速了，任性地提，以一种义聋义瞎的固执。
终于，从起初的蜗行，我飞驰到了骇人的速度，而有什么东西，语带讥诮，仿佛向我耳语：「线上必有别的火车堵着——在站上，在场里，到处都是——这是疯人的驰骋，是死亡之旅，是飞翔的荷兰人式的狂乱：想想你那五节车厢、黑压压的乘客，他们一起颠簸摇晃，一旦相撞，可是要遭殃的。」可我带着骡子般的顽固想：「他们本来就是要去伦敦的」；于是我一路狂奔，就我所记，倒不是狂喜，也没有发疯，只觉得胸中一股阴郁的火，一股邪恶而阴都的非理性在驱迫；我一身乌黑地在炉前添煤，望见死马死牛的模糊巨影、飞掠的树与田、黑暗的庄宅和沉睡的农舍，鬼影似的掠过昏沉的空气，正如半瞎的人看见「人好像树木，并且行走」。
然而，这没能持续多久：离多佛想来不到二十英里，在一段长直的线路上，我望见前方一处信号点旁卧着一大堆盖着油布的东西：霎时麻木变成了恐怖。可我刚一把住制动，便知道来不及了：我冲向过道，想从右面的路堤上狂跳下去，却被一阵急促的粗粝颠簸抛了回来——是八头还是十头牛，横卧在线路上；等我爬起来、跳出去的那几秒——在撞击之前——速度必定已大为减慢，因为我没有骨折，只躺在平地上一丛开黄花的荆豆里，处于半昏迷中，甚至还有知觉知道四十码外的线路上起了火，还有，整夜里，知道某处响着隐隐的雷声。

===Original===

About five, or half-past, in the morning I was sitting up, rubbing my eyes, in a dim light mixed with drizzle. I could see that the train of my last night’s debauch was a huddled-up chaos of fallen carriages and disfigured bodies. A five-barred gate on my left opened into a hedge, and swung with creaks: two yards from my feet lay a little shaggy pony with swollen wan abdomen, the very picture of death, and also about me a number of dead wet birds.
I picked myself up, passed through the gate, and walked up a row of trees to a house at their end. I found it to be a little country-tavern with a barn, forming one house, the barn part much larger than the tavern part. I went into the tavern by a small side-door﻿—behind the bar﻿—into a parlour﻿—up a little stair﻿—into two rooms: but no one was there. I then went round into the barn, which was paved with cobblestones, and there lay a dead mare and foal, some fowls, with two cows. A ladder-stair led to a closed trapdoor in the floor above. I went up, and in the middle of a wilderness of hay saw nine people﻿—labourers, no doubt﻿—five men and four women, huddled together, and with them a tin-pail containing the last of some spirit; so that these had died merry.
I slept three hours among them, and afterwards went back to the tavern, and had some biscuits of which I opened a new tin, with some ham, jam and apples, of which I made a good meal, for my pemmican was gone.
Afterwards I went following the rail-track on foot, for the engines of both the collided trains were smashed. I knew northward from southward by the position of the sun: and after a good many stoppages at houses, and by railway-banks, I came, at about eleven in the night, to a great and populous town.
By the Dane John and the Cathedral, I immediately recognised it as Canterbury, which I knew quite well. And I walked up Castle Street to the High Street, conscious for the first time of that regularly-repeated sound, like a sob or groan, which was proceeding from my throat. As there was no visible moon, and these old streets very dim, I had to pick my way, lest I should desecrate the dead with my foot, and they all should rise with hue and cry to hunt me.

===Chinese===

早晨五点，五点半吧，我坐起身，揉着眼睛，天光昏暗，混着细雨。我看清了昨夜那场狂奔的列车：倾颓的车厢和走了形的尸体，挤作一团混沌。左手一道五横木的门开进树篱，吱呀摇晃；离我脚两码，躺着一匹蓬毛的小马，肚腹苍白地胀着，活脱脱一幅死的图景；我四周还有些湿透的死鸟。
我爬起来，穿过那道门，沿一排树走到树尽头的一座房子前。原来是家小小的乡村酒馆，连着一座谷仓合成一幢，谷仓那半比酒馆那半大得多。我从一扇小小的边门进了酒馆——绕到柜台后——进了客厅——上了一道小楼梯——进了两个房间：可一个人也没有。我随后绕进谷仓，谷仓铺着鹅卵石，里面躺着一匹死母马和马驹，几只鸡，还有两头母牛。一道梯级通向头顶楼面上关着的活板门。我上去，在一片干草的荒野中央看见九个人——无疑是庄稼人——五男四女，挤作一堆，身边一只白铁桶，盛着某种酒的残滓：可见这几个人死得快活。
我在他们中间睡了三个钟头，随后回到酒馆，吃了几块饼干——开了听新的——外加火腿、果酱和苹果，美美吃了一顿，因为我的干肉饼已经完了。
然后我徒步沿着铁轨走，因为相撞的两台机车都粉碎了。我凭太阳的位置辨南北：一路在人家和路基旁停了许多回，夜里十一点左右，到了一座人烟稠密的大城。
凭着眼前的土冈「丹麦约翰」和大教堂，我立刻认出这是坎特伯雷，这城我很熟。我沿城堡街走到大街上，头一回清楚地意识到那个有规律地反复的声音，像呜咽，又像呻吟，正从我喉咙里发出。天上没有月亮，这些老街又极昏暗，我只得步步择路，生怕自己的脚亵渎了死者，生怕他们全体起身，喊杀连天地来追捕我。

===Original===

However, the bodies here were not numerous, most, as before, being foreigners: and these, scattered about this strict old English burg that mourning dark night, presented such a scene of the baneful wrath of God, and all abomination of desolation, as broke me quite down at one place, where I stood in travail with jeremiads and sore sobbings and lamentations, crying out upon it all, God knows.
Only when I stood at the west entrance of the Cathedral I could discern, spreading up the dark nave, to the lantern, to the choir, a phantasmagorical mass of forms: I went a little inward, and striking three matches, peered nearer: the two transepts, too, seemed crowded﻿—the cloister-doorway was blocked﻿—the southwest porch thronged, so that a great congregation must have flocked hither shortly before their fate overtook them.
Here it was that I became definitely certain that the after-odour of the poison was not simply lingering in the air, but was being more or less given off by the bodies: for the blossomy odour of this church actually overcame that other odour, the whole rather giving the scent of old mouldy linens long embalmed in cedars.
Well, away with stealthy trot I ran from the abysmal silence of that place, and in Palace Street near made one of those sudden immoderate rackets that seemed to outrage the universe, and left me so woefully faint, decrepit, and gasping for life (the noise of the train was different, for there I was flying, but here a captive, and which way I ran was capture). Passing in Palace Street, I saw a little lampshop, and wanting a lantern, tried to get in, but the door was locked; so, after going a few steps, and kicking against a policeman’s truncheon, I returned to break the window-glass. I knew that it would make a fearful noise, and for some fifteen or twenty minutes stood hesitating: but never could I have dreamed, my good God, of *such* a noise, so passionate, so dominant, so divulgent, and, O Heaven, so long-lasting: for I seemed to have struck upon the weak spot of some planet, which came suddenly tumbling, with protracted bellowing and debacle, about my ears. It was a good hour before I would climb in; but then quickly found what I wanted, and some big oilcans; and till one or two in the morning, the innovating flicker of my lantern went peering at random into the gloomy nooks of the town.

===Chinese===

不过这里的尸体不算多，而且同先前一样，大半是外国人：这些人，散布在这座恪守古风的英格兰老城里，在那个服丧的黑夜里，呈现出这样一幅上帝毒怒、荒凉可憎的景象，竟在一处把我彻底压垮了：我站在那里，如临产的阵痛，哀哀地哭号、抽噎、悲叹，对这一切呼号控诉，天知道。
直到站到大教堂西门，我才辨出，顺着黑暗的中殿、直到塔楼交叉处、直到唱诗席，铺展着一大团幻影般的人形：我略往里走了走，划了三根火柴，凑近细看：两翼的十字耳堂似乎也挤满了——回廊的门口堵死了——西南门廊里人潮涌涌——想是有一大群会众，在劫数追上他们之前不久，刚刚蜂拥到这里。
正是在这里，我确切地明白了：毒气的余味并非单纯地残留在大气里，而是多少正从尸体上发散出来：因为这座教堂里花一般的气味，竟压过了另一种气味，整体闻去，倒像长久封存在雪松木箱里的陈年发霉的亚麻布。
唉，我偷偷地小跑着，逃出那地方深渊般的寂静，到了近旁的宫殿街，又弄出了一场突兀的、不知轻重的喧哗，那喧哗仿佛冒犯了整个宇宙，也把我自己震得可怜地发晕、衰颓、喘息求生（火车的声响另当别论，因为在那儿我是在飞驰，而在这里我是囚徒，朝哪边跑都是被擒）。在宫殿街上经过时，我看见一家小灯铺，想要一盏提灯，便想进去，门却锁着；走了几步，脚踢到一根警棍，我又折回去砸窗玻璃。我知道那会发出可怕的响动，站着踌躇了一刻钟、二十分钟：可我做梦也想不到，我的上帝，会是『那样』一种声响，那样激越，那样霸道，那样无所顾忌，而且，噢，老天，那样经久不息：因为我仿佛敲中了一颗行星的死穴，整颗行星轰然崩塌，带着拖长的吼啸与溃散，在我耳边倒塌下来。足足过了一个钟头我才敢爬进去；随后很快找到了要的东西，还有几只大油壶；直到凌晨一两点，我这盏新点的提灯的火苗，一直闪着，在城里阴惨的角落里信游窥探。

===Original===

Under a deep old Gothic arch that spanned a pavered alley, I saw the little window of a little house of rubble, and between the two diamond-paned sashes rags tightly beaten in, the idea evidently being to make the place airtight against the poison. When I went in I found the door of that room open, though it, too, apparently, had been stuffed at the edges; and on the threshold an old man and woman lay low. I conjectured that, thus protected, they had remained shut in, till either hunger, or the lack of oxygen in the used-up air, drove them forth, whereupon the poison, still active, must have instantly ended them. I found afterwards that this expedient of making airtight had been widely resorted to; and it might well have proved successful, if both the supply of enclosed air, and of food, had been anywhere commensurate with the durability of the poisonous state.
Weary, weary as I grew, some morbid persistence sustained me, and I would not rest. About four in the morning I was at a station again, industriously bending, poor wretch, at the sooty task of getting another engine ready for travel. This time, when steam was up, I succeeded in uncoupling the carriages from the engine, and by the time morning broke, I was lightly gliding away over the country, whither I did not know, but making for London.

===Chinese===

在一道横跨石板小巷的古老的哥特式深拱下，我看见一座碎石小屋的小窗，两扇菱形格窗扇之间塞满了敲紧的破布——用意显然是把屋子封得不透气，以防毒气。我进去时发现那房间的门开着，虽然门缝边上显然也堵过；门槛上并排躺着一老翁一老妪。我推测，他们这样防护着，一直闭门不出，直到饥饿，或是耗尽的空气中氧气的匮乏，驱他们出来，于是那毒性犹存的毒气，必定顷刻结果了他们。后来我发现，这种封屋不透气的办法曾被人广泛采用；倘若密闭的空气与食物的存量，随处都能与毒效的持久相称，这法子本是很可成功的。
我越来越疲惫，疲惫啊，可一种病态的执拗支撑着我，我不肯歇。凌晨四点光景，我又到了一座车站，可怜的人，勤谨地弯着腰，干着烟熏火燎的活计，给另一台机车做出发的准备。这一回，汽一上来，我成功地把车厢从机车上摘了下来；待到天明，我已轻轻滑过田野乡间，驶向何方我并不知道，但目标是伦敦。

===Original===

Now I went with more intelligence and caution, and got on very well, travelling seven days, never at night, except it was very clear, never at more than twenty or twenty-five miles, and crawling through tunnels. I do not know the maze into which the train took me, for very soon after leaving Canterbury it must have gone down some branch-line, and though the names were marked at stations, that hardly helped me, for of their situation relatively to London I was seldom sure. Moreover, again and again was my progress impeded by trains on the metals, when I would have to run back to a shunting-point or a siding, and, in two instances, these being far behind, changed from my own to the impeding engine. On the first day I travelled unhindered till noon, when I stopped in open country that seemed uninhabited for ages, only that half a mile to the left, on a shaded sward, was a large stone house of artistic design, coated with tinted harling, the roof of red Ruabon tiles, and timbered gables. I walked to it after another row with putting out the fire and arranging for a new one, the day being bright and mild, with great masses of white cloud in the sky. The house had an outer and an inner hall, three reception rooms, fine oil-paintings, a kind of museum, and a large kitchen. In a bedroom above-stairs I found three women with servants’ caps, and a footman, arranged in a strange symmetrical way, head to head, like rays of a star. As I stood looking at them, I could have sworn, my good God, that I heard someone coming up the stairs. But it was some slight creaking of the breeze in the house, augmented a hundredfold to my inflamed and fevered hearing: for, used for years now to this silence of Eternity, it is as though I hear all sounds through an ear-trumpet. I went down, and after eating, and drinking some clary-water, made of brandy, sugar, cinnamon, and rose water, which I found in plenty, I lay down on a sofa in the inner hall, and slept a quiet sleep until near midnight.
I went out then, still possessed with the foolish greed to reach London, and after getting the engine to rights, went off under a clear black sky thronged with worlds and far-sown spawn, some of them, I thought, perhaps like this of mine, whelmed and drowned in oceans of silence, with one only inhabitant to see it, and hear its silence.

===Chinese===

这一回我走得更有心计、也更谨慎，一路顺利：走了七天，从不在夜间赶路，除非天色极清；从不超过二十或二十五英里；穿过隧道时则缓缓爬行。我不知道火车把我带进了怎样一座迷阵，因为离开坎特伯雷不久，它想必就拐上了某条支线；站名虽都标着，于我却无甚帮助，因为它们相对伦敦的位置，我十有八九拿不准。何况，一次次有别的火车横在铁轨上挡路，我只得把车退回到某处道岔或侧线；有两回，挡路的车远在身后，我便干脆丢下自己的车头，换上了拦路的那台。第一天我畅通无阻地走到正午，才停在一带旷野里，那地方仿佛久已无人居住，只是左面半英里外，一片树荫草坪上，立着一座艺术风格的大石宅，外墙抹着彩色的拉毛灰泥，屋顶铺鲁阿本红瓦，山墙带木构架。同熄火和重新生火那套工序又缠斗了一场之后，我向它走去；那天天气晴朗和煦，天上堆着大团大团的白云。宅子有外厅内厅，三间客厅，精美的油画，一间博物馆似的陈列室，还有一间大厨房。楼上卧室里我发现三个戴女佣帽的女人和一个男仆，摆成一种奇怪的对称姿势，头抵着头，像星芒的射线。我站在那里望着他们时，我的老天，我几乎可以发誓，听见有人上楼来了。其实只是微风在宅子里弄出的些微吱呀，落进我发炎发热的听觉里，被放大了百倍：因为多年来习惯了这永恒的寂静，我如今听一切声响，都像隔着一支助听筒。我下了楼，吃了东西，又喝了些克莱雷水——用白兰地、糖、肉桂和玫瑰水调的，存货多得很——便在内厅的沙发上躺下，安安静静一觉睡到近半夜。
我于是又出门，心里仍怀着抵达伦敦那痴傻的贪念；把机车弄妥之后，便顶着一片清朗的黑夜出发了，天上拥挤着一个个世界和远远播撒的星子，我想，它们当中或许也有同我这颗一样的，沉没淹溺在寂静的大洋里，只有一个居民去看它，听它的寂静。

===Original===

And all the long night I travelled, stopping twice only, once to get the coal from an engine which had impeded me, and once to drink some water, which I took care, as always, should be running water. When I felt my head nod, and my eyes close about 5 a.m., I threw myself, just outside the arch of a tunnel upon a grassy bank, pretty thick with stalks and flowers, the workings of early dawn being then in the east: and there, till near eleven, slept.
On waking, I noticed that the country now seemed more like Surrey than Kent: there was that regular swell and sinking of the land; but, in fact, though it must have been either, it looked like neither, for already all had an aspect of return to a state of wild nature, and I could see that for a year at the least no hand had tended the soil. Near before me was a stretch of lucerne of such extraordinary growth, that I was led during that day and the succeeding one to examine the condition of vegetation with some minuteness, and nearly everywhere I detected a certain hypertrophie tendency in stamens, calycles, pericarps, and pistils, in every sort of bulbiferous growth that I looked at, in the rushes, above all, the fronds, mosses, lichens, and all cryptogamia, and in the trefoils, clover especially, and some creepers.
