Here was a very considerable number of punt-shaped putts on carriages, and also wagons, such as took the new-mined coal from putt to pit-mouth; and raying out from this open standing, several avenues, some ascending as guggs, some descending as dipples, and the dead here all arranged in groups, the heads of this group pointing up this gugg, of that group toward that twin-way, of that other down that dipple, and the central space, where weighing was done, almost empty: and the darksome silence of this deep place, with all these multitudes, I found extremely gravitating and hypnotic, drawing me, too, into their great Passion of Silence in which they lay, all, all, so fixed and veteran; and at one time I fell a-staring, nearer perhaps to death and the empty Gulf than I knew; but I said I would be strong, and not sink into their habit of stillness, but let them keep to their own way, and follow their own fashion, and I would keep to my own way, and follow my own fashion, nor yield to them, though I was but one against many; and I roused myself with a shudder; and setting to work, caught hold of the drum-chain of a long gugg, and planting my feet in the chogg-holes in which rested the wheels of the putt-carriages that used to come roaring down the gugg, I got up, stooping under a roof only three feet high, till I came, near the end of the ascent, upon the scene of another battle: for in this gugg about fifteen of the mine-hands had clubbed to wall themselves in, and had done it, and I saw them lie there all by themselves through the broken cement, with their bare feet, trousers, naked bodies all black, visage all fierce and wild, the grime still streaked with sweat-furrows, the candle in their rimless hats, and, outside, their own “getting” mattocks and boring-irons to besiege them. From the bottom of this gugg I went along a very undulating twin-way, into which, every thirty yards or so, opened one of those steep putt-ways which they called topples, the twin-ways having plates of about 2½ ft.

===Chinese===

缓斜巷把我引到一处车场——带转车盘的一块场子——规模异乎寻常，我把它当作探察的大本营。这里有相当多平底船似的煤斗，架在车架上，还有斗车，都是从掌子把新采的煤运到矿口的那种；从这敞场放射出几条巷道，有的向上叫「上斜」，有的向下叫「下斜」；死人这里全是一组一组安放着的：这一组的头朝着这条上斜，那一组朝着那条双轨巷，另一组朝着那条下斜；当中称重的那块空场，几乎是空的：这深处的幽暗的寂静，连同这芸芸众生，我觉得极有一种引力与催眠之力，也把我拖进他们所躺的那场伟大的受难——寂静——之中，他们全体，全体，那样定住，那样老成；有一阵我竟自呆望起来，离死亡、离那空洞的深渊，怕是比我知道的更近；可我说我要坚强，不沉进他们静止的习惯里，让他们守他们的道、随他们的样，我守我的道、随我的样，绝不向他们屈服，虽然我以一敌众；我战栗着振作起来；随即动手，抓住一条长上斜的卷筒链，把脚踏进一个个闸洞——从前煤斗车轰隆隆冲下这条上斜时，轮子就卡在那些洞里——我弓着背，在只有三英尺高的顶下往上爬，快到头时，撞见了另一场战斗的现场：因为在这条上斜里，约莫十五个矿工凑起来把自己封在了里面，而且封成了；我隔着碎裂的水泥看见他们独自躺在里面，赤脚，裤子上身皆黑，面目狰狞狂乱，煤垢上还留着汗水的沟痕，无檐帽上还插着蜡烛；外面，是他们自己的采煤镐和钎子，用来围攻他们的。从这条上斜的底，我沿一条起伏很大的双轨巷走，每隔三十码左右，便有一条陡的斗道口开进来，他们管那叫「陡放」；双轨巷铺着约两英尺六寸的铁板。

===Original===

gauge for the putts from the headings, or workings, above to come down upon, full of coal and shale: and all about here, in twin-way and topples, were ends and corners, and not one had been left without its walling-in, and only one was then intact, some, I fancied, having been broken open by their own builders at the spur of suffocation, or hunger; and the one intact I broke into with a mattock﻿—it was only a thin cake of plaster, but airtight﻿—and in a space not seven feet long behind it I found the very ill-smelling corpse of a carting-boy, with guss and tugger at his feet, and the pad which protected his head in pushing the putts, and a great heap of loaves, sardines, and bottled beer against the walls, and five or six mice that suddenly pitched screaming through the opening which I made, greatly startling me, there being of dead mice an extraordinary number in all this mine-region.

===Chinese===

供上头掌子里的满载煤和页岩的斗车放下来：这一带，双轨巷和陡放里，到处是尽头的角落，没有一个不曾被人封砌过，而完好的只剩一处；有几处，我猜，是被封的人自己憋闷、饥饿难当，又砸开的；那处完好的，我用镐砸开了——只是一层薄薄的灰泥饼，可不透气——它后面不到七英尺长的空间里，躺着一个气味极坏的推车少年，脚边是垫肩和拉带，还有他顶罐护头用的垫子，墙根堆着一大堆面包、沙丁鱼和瓶装啤酒；五六只耗子突然尖叫着从我开的口子里窜出来，把我吓了一大跳——整个这片矿区，死耗子多得出奇。

===Original===

I went back to the standing, and at one point in the ground, where there was a windlass and chain, lowered myself down a “cut”﻿—a small pit sunk perpendicularly to a lower coal-stratum, and here, almost thinking I could hear the perpetual rat-tat of notice once exchanged between the putt-boys below and the windlass-boys above, I proceeded down a dipple to another place like a standing, for in this mine there were six, or perhaps seven, veins: and there immediately I came upon the acme of the horrible drama of this Tartarus, for all here was not merely crowded, but, at some points, a packed congestion of flesh, giving out a strong smell of the peach, curiously mixed with the stale coal-odour of the pit, for here ventilation must have been very limited; and a large number of these masses had been shot down by only three hands, as I found: for through three hermetical holes in a plaster-wall, built across a large gugg, projected a little the muzzles of three rifles, which must have glutted themselves with slaughter; and when, after a horror of disgust, having swum as it were through a dead sea, I got to the wall, I peeped from a small clear space before it through a hole, and made out a man, two youths in their teens, two women, three girls, and piles of cartridges and provisions; the hole had no doubt been broken from within at the spur of suffocation, when the poison must have entered; and I conjectured that here must be the mine-owner, director, manager, or something of that sort, with his family. In another dipple-region, when I had re-ascended to a higher level, I nearly fainted before I could retire from the commencement of a region of afterdamp, where there had been an explosion, the bodies lying all hairless, devastated, and grotesque. But I did not desist from searching every other quarter, no momentary work, for not till near six did I go up by the pumping-shaft rope-ladder.

===Chinese===

我回到车场，在一处装有绞盘和链子的地方，顺着一口「暗井」下去——垂直打到更低一层煤的小井；在这里，几乎觉得自己听得见那种永远的、笃笃的信号声——从前井下的推车童和井上的绞盘童就是这样互通音讯的——我又顺着一条下斜走到另一处像车场的所在，因为这矿里有六层，也许七层煤层：而在那里，我立刻撞上了这座塔耳塔洛斯可怖戏剧的极致：因为这里不但是拥挤，有几处简直是血肉填塞的壅塞，发出一股浓烈的桃子味，同矿井陈年的煤味古怪地相混——这里通风必是极有限；而这些尸堆，我发现是被仅仅三只手射倒的：因为横跨一条大上斜的一道灰泥墙上，有三个封得严严的洞，微微探出三支步枪的枪口，它们必定屠杀了个饱；待我怀着毛骨悚然的厌恶，仿佛泳过一片死海，捱到墙边，从墙前一小块空净处往洞里一窥，认出一个男人、两个十几岁的少年、两个妇人、三个姑娘，还有一堆堆子弹和口粮；那洞无疑是在憋闷难当时从里面砸开的，毒气必是那时进去的；我猜这里必是矿主、董事、经理之类的角色，带着他的家眷。在另一片下斜区——等我重新升到高一层之后——我险些晕倒在一处「爆后气」地带的入口，那里曾发生过爆炸，尸体个个无毛、焦烂而古怪。但其余每个角落我都没有放弃搜寻，那不是一时半刻的事：直到将近六点，我才顺着抽水井的绳梯上来。

===Original===

One day, standing in that wild region of bare rock and sea, called Cornwall Point, whence one can see the crags and postillion wild rocks where Land’s End dashes out into the sea, and all the wild blue sea between, and not a house in sight, save the chimney of some little mill-like place peeping between the rocks inland﻿—on that day I finished what I may call my official search.
In going away from that place, walking northward, I came upon a lonely house by the sea, a very beautiful house, made, it was clear, by an artist, of the bungalow type, with an exquisitely seaside expression. I went to it, and found its special feature a spacious loggia or verandah, sheltered by the overhanging upper story. Up to the first floor, the exterior is of stone in rough-hewn blocks with a distinct batter, while extra protection from weather is afforded by green slating above. The roofs, of low pitch, are also covered with green slates, and a feeling of strength and repose is heightened by the very long horizontal lines. At one end of the loggia is a hexagonal turret, opening upon the loggia, containing a study or nook. In front, the garden slopes down to the sea, surrounded by an architectural seawall; and in this place I lived three weeks. It was the house of the poet Machen, whose name, when I saw it, I remembered very well, and he had married a very beautiful young girl of eighteen, obviously Spanish, who lay on the bed in the large bright bedroom to the right of the loggia, on her left exposed breast being a baby with an india-rubber comforter in its mouth, both mother and child wonderfully preserved, she still quite lovely, white brow under low curves of black hair. The poet, strange to say, had not died with them, but sat in the sitting-room behind the bedroom in a long loose silky-grey jacket, at his desk﻿—actually writing a poem!

===Chinese===

有一天，我站在那片荒岩与大海之间、唤作康沃尔角的地方——从那里可以望见兰兹角扑进大海处的峭壁和群峰乱立的怪岩，以及其间全部蔚蓝的狂野之海，视野里没有一座房子，只有内陆岩缝间探出的一线小磨坊似的烟囱——就在那一天，我完成了可称之为官方搜查的任务。
离开那里往北走，我遇见海边一座孤零零的房子，一座极美的房子，一看便知出自一位艺术家之手，平房式的格局，带着精雅的海滨风致。我走进去，发现它最特别处是一条宽敞的敞廊，上有挑出的楼层遮蔽。底层外墙用粗斫的毛石砌成，带着明显的收分，上面覆着绿石板瓦更增防护。屋顶坡度平缓，也铺着绿石板，极长的水平线条更添了一种坚实与安宁之感。敞廊一端是一座六角小塔，开向廊内，里面是一间书房式的幽龛。房前，花园缓缓坡向大海，围着一道建筑式的防波墙；我在这地方住了三个星期。这是诗人梅琴的宅子，见到这名字时我记得清清楚楚；他娶了一位非常美丽的年轻姑娘，年方十八，一看便是西班牙人，她躺在大敞廊右侧那间明亮大卧室的床上，裸露的左乳上伏着一个婴儿，嘴里还叼着橡皮安抚奶嘴：母子俩都保存得出奇地完好，她依然那样可爱，黑发低低弯覆着雪白的额头。而诗人，说来奇怪，没有同她们死在一处，他坐在卧室后面的起居室里，穿一件宽松的长丝灰便服，伏在书桌前——竟然正在写诗！

===Original===

writing, I could see, furiously fast, the place all littered with the written leaves﻿—at three o’clock in the morning, when, as I knew, the cloud overtook this end of Cornwall, and stopped him, and put his head to rest on the desk; and the poor little wife must have got sleepy, waiting for it to come, perhaps sleepless for many long nights before, and gone to bed, he perhaps promising to follow in a minute to die with her, but bent upon finishing that poem, and writing feverishly on, running a race with the cloud, thinking, no doubt, “just two couplets more,” till the thing came, and put his head to rest on the desk, poor carle: and I do not know that I ever encountered aught so complimentary to my race as this dead poet Machen, and his race with the cloud: for it is clear now that the better kind of those poet men did not write to please the vague inferior tribes who might read them, but to deliver themselves of the divine warmth that thronged in their bosom; and if all the readers were dead, still they would have written; and for God to read they wrote. At any rate, I was so pleased with these poor people, that I stayed with them three weeks, sleeping under blankets on a couch in the drawing-room, a place full of lovely pictures and faded flowers, like all the house: for I would not touch the young mother to remove her. And finding on Machen’s desk a big notebook with soft covers, dappled red and yellow, not yet written in, I took it, and a pencil, and in the little turret-nook wrote day after day for hours this account of what has happened, nearly as far as it has now gone. And I think that I may continue to write it, for I find in it a strange consolation, and companionship.

===Chinese===

我看得分明，他写得飞快，激愤如焚，满屋狼藉着写满字的一页页——那是凌晨三点，据我所知，云雾正是在那时追上了康沃尔的这一端，拦住了他，让他的头伏在书桌上安息了；那可怜的年轻妻子想必等着等着困了——等它来，也许此前已有许多长夜不曾合眼——便先上了床，他或许答应一分钟就去，同她一起死，却一心要写完那首诗，发着烧似的往下写，同云雾赛跑，心里无疑想着『再有两联就好』，直到那东西来了，让他的头伏在书桌上安息，可怜的人：而我不记得可曾遇见过什么，比这位死去的诗人梅琴和他同云雾的赛跑，更给我的人类增光的了：因为现在很清楚，那些诗人中的优秀者，写作并不是为了取悦可能读他们的芸芸众生，而是要卸掉拥塞在他们胸中的神圣暖意；即便所有的读者都死绝了，他们照样会写；他们写给上帝读。无论如何，我是这样喜爱这两个可怜人，在他们中间住了三个星期，睡在客厅一张卧榻上，盖着毯子——那屋子同整座宅子一样，满挂着可爱的画、摆着凋残的花：因为我不忍碰动那年轻的母亲，把她挪开。我在梅琴书桌上发现一本软面大笔记本，红黄斑驳，还未曾写过一字，我便取了它，还有一支铅笔，在那座小塔的幽龛里，一天又一天，一写好几个钟头，写下了这篇所历之事的记述，几乎写到了如今这个地步。我想我还会继续写下去，因为我在其中找到了一种奇特的慰藉，和陪伴。

===Original===

In the Severn Valley, somewhere in the plain between Gloucester and Cheltenham, in a rather lonely spot, I at that time travelling on a tricycle-motor, I spied a curious erection, and went to it. I found it of considerable size, perhaps fifty feet square, and thirty high, made of pressed bricks, the perfectly flat roof, too, of brick, and not one window, and only one door: this door, which I found open, was rimmed all round its slanting rims with india-rubber, and when closed must have been perfectly airtight. Just inside I came upon fifteen English people of the dressed class, except two, who were evidently bricklayers: six ladies, and nine men: and at the further end, two more, men, who had their throats cut; along one wall, from end to end were provisions; and I saw a chest full of mixed potassic chlorate and black oxide of manganese, with an apparatus for heating it, and producing oxygen﻿—a foolish thing, for additional oxygen could not alter the quantity of breathed carbonic anhydride, which is a direct narcotic poison. Whether the two with cut throats had sacrificed themselves for the others when breathing difficulties commenced, or been killed by the others, was not clear. When they could bear it no longer, they must have finally opened the door, hoping that by then, after the passage of many days perhaps, the outer air would be harmless, and so met their death. I believe that this erection must have been run up by their own hands under the direction of the two bricklayers, for they could not, I suppose, have got workmen, except on the condition of the workmen’s admission: on which condition they would naturally employ as few as possible.
In general, I remarked that the rich must have been more urgent and earnest in seeking escape than the others: for the poor realised only the near and visible, lived in today, and cherished the always-false notion that tomorrow would be just like today.

===Chinese===

在塞文河谷，格洛斯特与切尔滕纳姆之间的平原上，一处相当偏僻的地方——当时我正骑一辆机动三轮车赶路——我望见一座古怪的构筑物，便向它驶去。我发现它相当大，约莫五十英尺见方、三十英尺高，用压制砖砌成，严整的平顶也是砖的，没有一扇窗，只有一扇门：这扇门我发现敞开着，倾斜的门框四周镶着橡胶，关起来想必密不透风。进门不远处，我撞见十五个英国人，皆衣饰体面，只有两个显见是砌砖匠：六位女眷，九个男人；而在最里头，还有两个男人，喉咙被割开了；沿一面墙从这头到那头堆满给养；我又看见一箱混合的氯酸钾和黑色氧化锰，配着加热产氧的装置——一件蠢事，因为补充氧气并不能改变呼出的碳酸气的量，而那是一种直接的麻醉性毒物。那两个割喉的，是呼吸困难开始时为其他人献祭了自己，还是被其他人所杀，不得而知。等到再也忍受不住，他们必定终于打开了门，指望那时——也许许多天已经过去——外面的空气已无害，于是迎头撞上了死亡。我相信这座构筑物是他们亲手在那两个砌砖匠指导下仓促砌起的，因为我想他们雇不到工人，除非以让工人也进来避难为条件：有这个条件，他们自然要用人越少越好。
大体上，我注意到富人求生的急切和认真必定远过他人：因为穷人只认得眼前和看得见的东西，活在今天，抱着那永远虚假的念头，以为明天会和今天一模一样。

===Original===

In an outpatients’ waiting-room, for instance, in the Gloucester infirmary, I chanced to see an astonishing thing: five bodies of poor old women in shawls, come to have their ailments seen-to on the day of doom; and these, I concluded, had been unable to realise that anything would really happen to the daily old earth which they knew, and had walked with assurance on: for if everybody was to die, they must have thought, who would preach in the Cathedral on Sunday evenings?﻿—so they could not have believed. In an adjoining room sat an old doctor at a table, the stethoscope-tips still clinging in his ears: a woman with bared chest before him; and I thought to myself: “Well, this old man, too, died doing his work.﻿ ﻿…”
In this same infirmary there was one surgical ward﻿—for in a listless mood I went over it﻿—where the patients had died, not of the poison, nor of suffocation, but of hunger: for the doctors, or someone, had made the long room airtight, double-boarding the windows, felting the doors, and then locking them outside; they themselves may have perished before their precautions for the imprisoned patients were complete: for I found a heap of maimed shapes, mere skeletons, crowded round the door within. I knew very well that they had not died of the cloud-poison, for the pestilence of the ward was unmixed with that odour of peach which did not fail to have more or less embalming effects upon the bodies which it saturated. I rushed stifling from that place; and thinking it a pity, and a danger, that such a horror should be, I at once set to work to gather combustibles to burn the building to the ground.
It was while I sat in an armchair in the street the next afternoon, smoking, and watching the flames of this structure, that something was suddenly born in me, something from the lowest Hell: and I smiled a smile that never yet man smiled. And I said: “I will burn, I will burn: I will return to London.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

比如，在格洛斯特贫民疗养院的一间门诊候诊室里，我偶然见到一桩骇人的事：五具披披肩的穷老妇的尸身，末日那天还来瞧她们的老毛病；我断定，她们至死没能明白，她们所熟识、每日安然行走的这颗老地球，当真会出什么事：因为，她们一定这样想，倘若人人都要死，星期天晚上还有谁在大教堂讲道呢？——所以她们不可能相信。隔壁房间里，一个老医生坐在桌旁，听诊器的听头还塞在耳中；他面前是一个袒开胸膛的女人；我心里想：「嗯，这老头，也是死在自己的岗位上。……」
还是在这所疗养院——因为我无精打采地把它巡查了一遍——有一间外科病房，病人们不是死于毒气，也不是死于窒息，而是死于饥饿：因为医生们，或者什么人，把那间长屋封得密不透风，窗子钉了双层板，门缝毡封，然后在外面落了锁；他们自己也许还没来得及完成对被困病人的这些防护，就先遭了难：因为我发现一堆残缺的形体，纯粹的骨架，拥挤在门内。我很清楚他们不是死于云雾之毒，因为病房里的疫气里丝毫不掺那种桃子香气——那香气浸透的尸体，多少总有防腐之效。我窒息般地夺路冲出那地方；一想这等恐怖存在既是可悯、又是祸患，我便立刻动手搜集引火之物，要把那座建筑烧成白地。
就在第二天下午，我坐在街上一张扶手椅里，抽着烟，望着那座建筑的大火时，有个东西在我体内骤然降生了，一个来自最底层地狱的东西：我露出了一个人类从未露出过的微笑。我说：「我要烧，我要烧：我要回伦敦去。……」

===Original===

While I was on this Eastward journey, stopping for the night at the town of Swindon, I had a dream: for I dreamed that a little brown bald old man, with a bent back, whose beard ran in one thin streamlet of silver from his chin to trail along the ground, said to me: “You think that you are alone on the earth, its sole Despot: well, have your fling: but as sure as God lives, as God lives, as God lives”﻿—he repeated it six times﻿—“sooner or later, later or sooner, you will meet another.﻿ ﻿…”
And I started from that frightful sleep with the brow of a corpse, wet with sweat.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

就在这次东行的路上，我在斯温登镇过夜，做了一个梦：梦里一个小个子、棕色皮肤、秃头的老人，背佝偻着，下巴上一缕稀薄的银须像一道细流，一直拖到地上，对我说：「你以为世上只剩你一人，是它唯一的专制君主：好，尽情作乐去吧：可是上帝活着，上帝活着，上帝活着，千真万确」——他把这话重复了六遍——「或迟或早，或早或迟，你会遇见另一个人。……」
我从那可怕的睡眠里惊起，额头像死人的额头，冷汗湿透。……

===Original===

I returned to London on the 29th of March, arriving within a hundred yards of the Northern Station one windy dark evening about eight, where I alighted, and walked to Euston Road, then eastward along it, till I came to a shop which I knew to be a jeweller’s, though it was too dark to see any painted words. The door, to my annoyance, was locked, like nearly all the shop-doors in London: I therefore went looking near the ground, and into a cart, for something heavy, very soon saw a labourer’s ponderous boots, cut one from the shrivelled foot, and set to beat at the glass till it came raining; then knocked away the bottom splinters, and entered.
No horrors now at that clatter of broken glass; no sick qualms; my pulse steady; my head high; my step royal; my eye cold and calm.

===Chinese===

3月29日我回到了伦敦，一个刮风的黑夜里八点光景，到北车站百码之内下了车，步行到尤斯顿路，再沿路向东，来到一家我知道是珠宝店的铺子前——虽然天太黑，看不清招牌上的字。门锁着，叫我着恼，伦敦的店门几乎全锁着：我便低头往地上、往一辆货车里找样重东西，很快看见一个劳工的笨重靴子，从干瘪的脚上剁下一只，动手砸那玻璃，直到它哗啦啦落雨般碎落；再把下圈的碎片敲掉，钻了进去。
如今这碎玻璃的哗啦声再引不起恐怖；不再恶心反胃；我的脉搏平稳；我的头颅高昂；我的步伐堂堂；我的眼睛冷静安然。

===Original===

Eight months previously, I had left London a poor burdened, cowering wight. I could scream with laughter now at that folly! But it did not last long. I returned to it﻿—the Sultan.

===Chinese===

八个月前，我离开伦敦时，还是个可怜的、负重累累、缩头缩脑的凡人。如今想起那副蠢相，我能尖声大笑！可那笑声没有持续多久。我又回到了那副心态——苏丹的心态。

===Original===

No private palace being near, I was going to that great hotel in Bloomsbury: but though I knew that numbers of candlesticks would be there, I was not sure that I should find sufficient: for I had acquired the habit within the past few months of sleeping with at least sixty lighted about me, and their form, pattern, style, age, and material was of no small importance I selected ten from the broken shop, eight gold and silver, and two of old ecclesiastical brass, and having made a bundle, went out, found a bicycle at the Metropolitan Station, pumped it, tied my bundle to the handlebar, and set off riding. But since I was too lazy to walk, I should certainly have procured some other means of travelling, for I had not gone ten jolted and creaking yards, when something went snap﻿—it was a front fork﻿—and I found myself half on the ground, and half across the bare knees of a Highland soldier. I flew with a shower of kicks upon the foolish thing: but that booted nothing; and this was my last attempt in that way in London, the streets being in an unsuitable condition.
All that dismal night it blew great guns: and during nearly three weeks, till London was no more, there was a storm, with hardly a lull, that seemed to behowl her destruction.

===Chinese===

附近没有私人的宫殿，我本打算去布卢姆斯伯里那家大饭店：虽然我知道那里会有许多烛台，却不敢保证够数：因为过去几个月里，我已养成习惯，睡觉时身边至少要点六十支蜡烛，而烛台的形制、纹样、风格、年代和材质，都非小事。我从那家被砸开的铺子里挑了十件，八件金银的，两件教会的老黄铜的，打成一个包，走了出去，在大都会车站找到一辆自行车，给它打足气，把包裹绑上龙头，骑车出发。可我既然懒到不愿走路，本来实在该另找一种代步的法子才对：因为车身颠着、响着，还没走出十码，咔嚓一声——是前叉断了——我发觉自己半个身子已坐在地上，半个身子正跨在一个苏格兰高地士兵光溜溜的膝盖上。我对那蠢东西飞起一顿乱踹：可那毫无用处；这也是我在伦敦最后一次作这等尝试，街道的状况实在不合适。
那凄惨的一夜，风刮得像开炮；此后近三个星期，直到伦敦不复存在，风暴几乎没有间歇，仿佛在为它的毁灭嚎丧。

===Original===

I slept in a room on the second-floor of a Bloomsbury hotel that night; and waking the next day at ten, ate with accursed shiverings in the cold banqueting-room; went out then, and under drear low skies walked a long way to the West district, accompanied all the time by a sound of flapping flags﻿—fluttering robes and rags﻿—and grotesquely grim glimpses of decay. It was pretty cold, and though I was warmly clad, the base bizarrerie of the European clothes which I wore had become a perpetual offence and mockery in my eyes: at the first moment, therefore, I set out whither I knew that I should find such clothes as a man might wear: to the Turkish Embassy in Bryanston Square.
I found it open, and all the house, like most other houses, almost carpeted with dead forms. I had been acquainted with Redouza Pasha, and cast an eye about for him amid that invasion of veiled *hanums*, fierce-looking Caucasians in skins of beasts, a Sheik-ul-Islam in green cloak, a khalifa, three emirs in cashmere turbans, two tziganes, their gaudy brown mortality more glaringly abominable than even the Western’s. I could recognise no Redouza here: but the stair was fairly clear, and I soon came to one of those boudoirs which sweetly recall the deep-buried inner seclusion and dim sanctity of the Eastern home: a door encrusted with mother-of-pearl, sculptured ceiling, candles clustered in tulips and roses of opal, a brazen brasero, and, all in disarray, the silken chemise, the long winter-cafetan doubled with furs, costly cabinets, sachets of aromas, babooshes, stuffs of silk. When, after two hours, I went from the house, I was bathed, anointed, combed, scented, and robed.

===Chinese===

那夜我睡在布卢姆斯伯里一家旅馆的二楼；次日十点醒来，在那冷冰冰的宴会厅里，伴着可恶的阵阵寒颤吃了饭；随后出门，在阴沉低压的天幕下，走了长长的路到西区去，一路陪伴着我的，是旗帜的拍打声——飘拂的袍子和破布——还有腐朽的一幅幅古怪而阴森的瞥见。天气颇冷，虽然我穿得暖，可我身上这套欧式衣裳的卑俗的怪相，在我眼里已成了一种不断的冒犯和讥嘲：于是头一件事，我便动身到一个我知道能找到男人穿的衣服的地方去：布赖恩斯顿广场的土耳其使馆。
使馆门开着，整座宅子同大多数宅子一样，几乎用死人的身形铺了地毯。我原与雷杜扎帕夏相识，便在那些拥进来的人里四处找他：戴面纱的「哈娜姆」们，穿兽皮、面目狰狞的高加索人，一位披绿斗篷的伊斯兰长老，一位哈里发，三位缠克什米尔头巾的埃米尔，两个茨冈人——他们花哨的棕色尸身，比西方人的更加刺眼可憎。这里我认不出什么雷杜扎：楼梯倒还算干净，我很快走到一间闺阁里——那甜蜜地叫人想起东方人家深藏的内闱与幽幽的圣洁：一扇嵌满珠母的门，雕花的顶，蜡烛簇插在蛋白石的郁金香与玫瑰里，一只黄铜火盆，还有狼藉一片的：丝质长衫、夹皮的冬日长袍、昂贵的衣柜、一袋袋香料、拖鞋、绸缎。两个钟头后我从这宅子里出来时，我已沐浴过、膏过油、梳过头、熏过香、换好了袍子。

===Original===

I have said to myself: “I will ravage and riot in my Kingdoms. I will rage like the Caesars, and be a withering blight where I pass like Sennacherib, and wallow in soft delights like Sardanapalus. I will build me a palace, vast as a city, in which to strut and parade my Monarchy before the Heavens, with stones of pure molten gold, and rough frontispiece of diamond, and cupola of amethyst, and pillars of pearl. For there were many men to the eye: but there was One only, really: and I was he. And always I knew it:﻿—some faintest secret whisper which whispered me: ‘*You* are the Arch-one, the motif of the world, Adam, and the rest of men not much.’ And they are gone﻿—all! all!﻿—as no doubt they deserved: and I, as was meet, remain. And there are wines, and opiums, and haschish; and there are oils, and spices, fruits and bivalves, and soft-breathing Cyclades, and scarlet luxurious Orients. I will be restless and turbulent in my territories: and again, I will be languishing and fond. I will say to my soul: ‘Be Full.’ ”

===Chinese===

我曾对自己说：「我要在我的王国里劫掠与纵乐。我要像凯撒们那样发狂，像西拿基立那样所过之处尽成焦土，像萨丹纳帕路斯那样在软玉温香里打滚。我要给自己造一座宫殿，大如一座城，好在诸天面前大摇大摆地炫耀我的君权：用纯熔的黄金砌石，用钻石镶粗犷的门面，用紫晶做穹顶，用珍珠做殿柱。因为世人看去虽有许多人：其实真正的只有一位：那就是我。这一点我一直都知道：——总有极细极密的低语向我低语：『*你*就是那元一，世界的主题，亚当，其余的人算不了什么。』如今他们去了——全去了！全去了！——想必是罪有应得：而我，理所当然，留了下来。这里有美酒，有鸦片，有大麻；这里有香膏，香料，鲜果与贝壳之属，有柔声呼吸的基克拉泽斯群岛，有绯红奢靡的东方诸邦。我要在我的疆土上躁动不宁、掀起风浪：我也要慵懒缠绵、温存旖旎。我要对我的灵魂说：『满足吧。』」

===Original===

I watch my mind, as in the old days I would watch a new precipitate in a test-tube, to see into what sediment it would settle.
I am very averse to trouble of any sort, so that the necessity for the simplest manual operations will rouse me to indignation: but if a thing will contribute largely to my ever-growing voluptuousness, I will undergo a considerable amount of labour to accomplish it, though without steady effort, being liable to side-winds and whims, and purposeless relaxations.
In the country I became very irritable at the need which confronted me of occasionally cooking some green vegetable﻿—the only item of food which it was necessary to take some trouble over: for all meats, and many fish, some quite delicious, I find already prepared in forms which will remain good probably a century after my death, should I ever die. In Gloucester, however, I found peas, asparagus, olives, and other greens, already prepared to be eaten without base cares: and these, I now see, exist everywhere in stores so vast comparatively to the needs of a single man, that they may be called infinite. Everything, in fact, is infinite compared with my needs. I take my meals, therefore, without more trouble than a man who had to carve his joint, or chicken: though even that little I sometimes find most irksome. There remains the detestable degradation of lighting fires for warmth, which I have occasionally to do: for the fire at the hotel invariably goes out while I sleep. But that is an inconvenience of this vile northern island only, to which I shall soon bid eternal glad farewells.
During the afternoon of my second day in London, I sought out a strong petrol motor in Holborn, overhauled and oiled it a little, and set off over Blackfriars Bridge, making for Woolwich through that other more putrid London on the south riverside. One after the other, I connected, as I came upon them, two drays, a cab, and a private carriage, to my motor in line behind, having cut away the withered horses, and using the reins, chain-harness, etc., as impromptu couplings. And with this novel train, I rumbled eastward.
Halfway I happened to look at my old silver chronometer of *Boreal*-days, which I have kept carefully wound﻿—and how I can be still thrown into these sudden frantic agitations by a nothing, a nothing, my good God! I do not know.

===Chinese===

我观察我的心智，就像旧日观察试管里新析出的沉淀，看它究竟会沉成什么样的渣。
我极其厌恶任何形式的麻烦，以至最简单的手上活计的必要也会激起我的愤懑：可一件事若能大大助长我日益增长的爱欲与逸乐，我便肯下相当的力气去办成它，虽然绝无恒心，动辄受旁风与任性的摆布，动辄无端地懈怠。
在乡间，面对偶尔得煮一样青菜的必要——这是唯一需要费点事的吃食——我变得十分暴躁：因为一切肉类和许多鱼——有的相当美味——我发现都已备好，做成估计在我死后一百年也不会坏的形态，如果我终有一死的话。不过，在格洛斯特，我找到了豌豆、芦笋、橄榄和其他青菜，也已做成开罐即食、无须卑贱操心的形态；这些，我如今明白，各处存货之多，同一个男人的需要相比，简直可称为无限。事实上，样样东西同我的需要相比都是无限的。于是我用膳所费的麻烦，不过同一个要切一盘烤肉或一只鸡的人相仿：虽然连这点小事，有时我也觉得最难以忍受。剩下的还有生火取暖这可憎的屈辱，这是我偶尔不得不做的：因为旅馆的炉火总在我睡着时熄掉。但这只是这座可恶的北方小岛独有的不便，我不久就要向它道永别，欢天喜地的永别。
回伦敦的第二天下午，我在霍尔本寻出一辆结实的汽油摩托，略加检修上油，便开过黑衣修士桥，穿过河南岸那另一个更加腐烂的伦敦，直奔伍利奇去。一路撞上什么，我就把什么挂到我摩托后面拖成一列：两辆运货马车、一辆出租马车、一辆私人马车——砍掉干瘪的马，用缰绳、链套等等权充连结。带着这支新颖的车队，我隆隆地向东开去。
半路上，我偶然看了看我那块「北极号」时代的旧银质天文表——我一直仔细地给它上着弦——而我竟还能被一个「无」，一个「无」，猝然掷进这样的狂乱里，我的好上帝！我自己也不明白。

===Original===

This time it was only the simple fact that the hands chanced to point to 3:10 p.m., the precise moment at which all the clocks of London had stopped﻿—for each town has its thousand weird forefingers, pointing, pointing still, to the moment of doom. In London it was 3:10 on a Sunday afternoon. I first noticed it going up the river on the face of the Big Ben of the Parliament-house, and I now find that they all, all, have this 3:10 mania, timekeepers still, but keepers of the end of Time, fixedly noting forever and ever that one moment. The cloud-mass of fine penetrating scoriae must have instantly stopped their works, and they had fallen silent with man. But in their insistence upon this particular minute I had found something so hideously solemn, yet mock-solemn, personal, and as it were addressed to *me*, that when my own watch dared to point to the same moment, I was thrown into one of those sudden, paroxysmal, panting turmoils of mind, half rage, half horror, which have hardly once visited me since I left the *Boreal*. On the morrow, alas, another awaited me; and again on the second morrow after.

===Chinese===

这回，不过是这样一个简单的事实：表针碰巧指向下午三点十分，正是伦敦所有的钟停摆的那一时刻——因为每座城都有它成千上万根怪异的指针，仍旧指指点点，指着那个末日的时刻。在伦敦，那是某个礼拜天下午的三点十分。我最初是在溯河而上时，从议会大厦的大本钟的钟面上注意到的；如今我发现它们全都、全都害着这「三点十分」的执念，仍是计时者，却只是「时间之终结」的守卫者，永远永远定定地记着那一刻。那细而钻透一切的熔渣云团，必是霎时就停了它们的机括，它们便同人类一齐哑了。可它们对这个特殊时刻的执拗，在我眼中显得那样骇人地肃穆，又是嘲弄地肃穆，那样地针对我个人，仿佛特意呈给我「看」：以致当我自己的表竟敢指向同一时刻，我顿时被掷进那种猝发的、阵挛的、喘息的心神狂乱里，一半狂怒，一半恐怖——自从离开「北极号」，这样的发作几乎不曾来过。谁想第二天，唉，又来了一场；第三天，又是一场。

===Original===

My train was execrably slow, and not until after five did I arrive at the entrance-gates of the Woolwich Royal Arsenal; and seeing that it was too late to work, I uncoupled the motor, and leaving the others there, turned back; but overtaken by lassitude, I procured candles, stopped at the Greenwich Observatory, and in that old dark pile, remained for the night, listening to a furious storm. But, astir by eight the next morning, I got back by ten to the Arsenal, and proceeded to analyse that vast and multiple entity. Many parts of it seemed to have been abandoned in undisciplined haste, and in the Cap Factory, which I first entered, I found tools by which to effect entry into any desired part. My first search was for time-fuses of good type, of which I needed two or three thousand, and after a wearily long time found a great number symmetrically arranged in rows in a range of buildings called the Ordnance Store Department. I then descended, walked back to the wharf, brought up my train, and began to lower the fuses in bag-fulls by ropes through a shoot, letting go each rope as the fuses reached the cart. However, on winding one fuse, I found that the mechanism would not go, choked with scoriae; and I had to resign myself to the task of opening and dusting every one: a wretched labour in which I spent that day, like a workman. But about four I threw them to the devil, having done two hundred odd, and then hummed back in the motor to London.

===Chinese===

我的列车慢得可恶，直到五点过后我才到伍利奇皇家兵工厂的大门；眼看天色已晚干不成活，我便摘下机车，把其余车厢撂在那里往回走；可被一阵困倦追上，我便弄了些蜡烛，在格林尼治天文台歇脚，在那座幽暗的老楼群里过了夜，听了一夜狂怒的风暴。但次日八点我便起身，十点赶回兵工厂，着手剖析那庞大而繁复的庞然大物。它有许多部分看得出是被仓皇失措地弃置的；我先进入的引信工厂里，就找到些工具，可以撬开任何想进的部门。我头一样找的是性能良好的定时引信，需要两三千枚，找了疲惫不堪的许久，才在一片唤作军械储运处的建筑群里，发现大量引信成排成行、整整齐齐码着。我随即下去，走回码头，把我的列车开上来，开始把引信整袋整袋用绳索溜槽吊下，袋子落到货车上便松绳。可是试上一枚，我发现机件不走，被炉渣塞死了；我只好认命，把每一枚都拆开掉尘：这天便像个工人似的耗在这件苦役里。到四点光景，弄完两百来枚，我把其余的全扔给魔鬼，嗡嗡地开着机车回了伦敦。

===Original===

That same evening at six I paid, for the first time, a visit to my old self in Harley Street. It was getting dark, and a bleak storm that hooted like whooping-cough swept the world. At once I saw that even *I* had been invaded: for my door swung open, banging, a lowered catch preventing it from slamming; in the passage the car-lamp showed me a young man who seemed a Jew, sitting as if in sleep with dropped head, a back-tilted silk-hat pressed down upon his head to the ears; and lying on face, or back, or side, six more, one a girl with Arlesienne headdress, one a negress, one a Deal lifeboat’s-man, and three of uncertain race; the first room﻿—the waiting-room﻿—is much more numerously occupied, though there still, on the table, lies the volume of *Punch*, the *Gentlewoman*, and the book of London views in heliograph. Behind this, descending two steps, is the study and consulting-room, and there, as ever, the revolving-cover oak writing-desk: but on my little shabby-red sofa, a large lady much too big for it, in shimmering brown silk, round her left wrist a trousseau of massive gold trinkets, her head dropped right back, almost severed by an infernal gash from the throat. Here were two old silver candlesticks, which I lit, and went upstairs: in the drawing-room sat my old housekeeper, placidly dead in a rocking-chair, her left hand pressing down a batch of the open piano-keys, among many strangers. But she was very good: she had locked my bedroom against intrusion; and as the door stands across a corner behind a green-baize curtain, it had not been seen, or, at least, not forced. I did not know where the key might be, but a few thumps with my back drove it open: and there lay my bed intact, and everything tidy. This was a strange coming-back to it, Adam.
But what intensely interested me in that room was a big thing standing at the maroon-and-gold wall between wardrobe and dressing-table﻿—that gilt frame﻿—and that man painted within it there. It was myself in oils, done by﻿—I forget his name now: a towering celebrity he was, and rather a close friend of mine at one time. In a studio in St. John’s Wood, I remember, he did it; and many people said that it was quite a great work of art. I suppose I was standing before it quite thirty minutes that night, holding up the bits of candle, lost in wonder, in amused contempt at that thing there.

===Chinese===

当天傍晚六点，我头一次回哈利街拜访旧日的我。天渐黑，一场像百日咳那样呜呜啸叫的阴冷风暴横扫世界。我一眼便看出，连「我」这里也曾被侵扰过：我家大门开着，哐当作响，落下的门闩使它关不上；门厅里，车灯照见一个像是犹太人的青年，垂头坐着仿佛睡着，一顶后仰的丝帽直压到耳朵；另躺着六个，或俯、或仰、或侧：一个戴阿尔勒少女式头巾的姑娘，一个黑人女子，一个迪尔救生艇的水手，还有三个种族难辨；第一间——候诊室——占得远为拥挤，可桌上还躺着那册《笨拙》、那册《淑女》，和那本日光胶版画法的伦敦风景。它后面下两级台阶，是书房兼诊室，那儿照旧立着带翻盖的橡木写字台：可在我那张寒酸的红色小沙发上，躺着一位大得实在放不下的太太，一身闪光的棕色绸衣，左腕上一套沉甸甸的金嫁奁首饰，头向后仰到极处，喉咙上一道地狱似的豁口，几乎把头割断。这里有两只旧银烛台，我点了，上楼去：客厅里坐着我那位老管家，在一把摇椅里安然死去，左手还按着钢琴一排掀开的琴键，四周尽是外乡人。可她真是忠心：她把我的卧室锁了，不许人闯入；由于那门立在绿呢帘幕后面的一处角落里，没人看见它，至少没撞开它。钥匙在哪里我不知道，可我用背撞了几下，把它撞开了：我的床原封未动，样样整齐。这样回到它这里，真是奇哉怪哉，亚当啊。
但那间房里叫我大感兴味的，是立在栗色和金色墙面、衣柜与梳妆台之间的一件大东西——那镀金的画框——和框里画着的那个人。那是油画的我，出自——如今我忘了他的名字：当年是个鼎鼎大名的人物，一度还同我颇为交好。我记得是在圣约翰伍德的一间画室里画的；许多人说它算得一件了不起的艺术品。那晚我在它面前，想必足足站了三十分钟，举着几截蜡烛，满心惊奇，又对框里那东西报以好笑的轻蔑。

===Original===

It is I, certainly: that I must admit. There is the high-curving brow﻿—really a King’s brow, after all, it strikes me now﻿—and that vacillating look about the eyes and mouth which used to make my sister Ada say: “Adam is weak and luxurious.” Yes, that is wonderfully done, the eyes, that dear, vacillating look of mine; for although it is rather a staring look, yet one can almost see the dark pupils stir from side to side: very well done. And there is the longish face; and the rather thin, stuck-out moustache, showing both lips which pout a bit; and there is the nearly black hair; and there is the rather visible paunch; and there is, oh good Heaven, the neat pink cravat﻿—ah, it must have been *that﻿—the cravat*﻿—that made me burst out into laughter so loud, mocking, and uncontrollable the moment my eye rested there! “Adam Jeffson,” I muttered reproachfully when it was over, “could that poor thing in the frame have been you?”
I cannot quite state why the tendency toward Orientalism﻿—Oriental dress﻿—all the manner of an Oriental monarch﻿—has taken full possession of me: but so it is: for surely I am hardly any longer a Western, “modern” mind, but a primitive and Eastern one. Certainly, that cravat in the frame has receded a million, million leagues, ten thousand forgotten aeons, from me! Whether this is a result due to my own personality, of old acquainted with Eastern notions, or whether, perhaps, it is the natural accident to any mind wholly freed from trammels, I do not know. But I seem to have gone right back to the very beginnings, and resemblance with man in his first, simple, gaudy conditions. My hair, as I sit here writing, already hangs a black, oiled string down my back; my scented beard sweeps in two opening whisks to my ribs; I have on the *izar*, a pair of drawers of yemeni cloth like cotton, but with yellow stripes; over this a soft shirt, or quamis, of white silk, reaching to my calves; over this a short vest of gold-embroidered crimson, the *sudeyree*; over this a kaftan of green-striped silk, reaching to the ankles, with wide, long sleeves divided at the wrist, and bound at the waist with a voluminous gaudy shawl of Cashmere for girdle; over this a warm wide-flowing torrent of white drapery, lined with ermine.

===Chinese===

是我，没错了：这我必须承认。那高高弯起的眉骨——如今想来，毕竟真是一副王者之眉——还有眼与唇边那游移不定的神气，从前总叫我姐姐艾达说：「亚当又软弱又贪图安逸。」是的，画得妙极了，那双眼睛，我那亲爱的、游移不定的眼神：那神气虽然相当呆瞪，可你几乎看得出黑色的瞳仁在左右轻转：画得好。还有那稍长的脸；那两撇翘起的稀疏髭须，露出的、微微攒起的双唇；那近黑的头发；那相当可观的肚子；还有，噢老天，那条雅致的粉色领结——啊，必是它——那条领结——叫我一眼落上去，便爆出一阵那样响亮、嘲弄、无法自持的大笑！笑完了，我责备似的喃喃：「亚当·杰夫森，框里那可怜东西，当年就是你么？」
我说不清这股东方主义的倾向——东方的衣装——一整套东方君主的做派——何以整个攝住了我：可事实就是如此：因为我确乎几乎不再是一个西方的、「现代」的心智，而是一个原始的、东方的心智了。当然，框里那条领结，此刻离我已退开了一百万、一百万里格，退开了被遗忘的一万个纪元！这是不是缘于我自己的性情——它旧日便熟稳东方的观念——抑或，是不是任何彻底挣脱了羁绊的心智自然而然要遭的变故，我不知道。可我仿佛径直退回了太初，同人在他最初、最简单、最花哨的境况里的模样重又相似。我此刻坐在这里写，头发已是一条油黑的发辫垂在背后；我熏香的胡须两边分张，两掠直扫到肋际；我下身穿着「伊扎尔」，一条也门布的衬裤，像棉布，却带黄条纹；外面一件软衫，即「卡米斯」，白绸的，长及腿肚；外面一件绣金的绯红短褂，「苏代里」；外面一件绿条纹绸的卡夫坦长袍，长及脚踝，宽而长的袖子在腕处开衩，腰间用一条宽大花哨的克什米尔披巾束作腰带；再外面，一袭暖热的、白浪般宽洒的斗篷，衬着貂皮。

===Original===

On my head is the skullcap, covered by a high crimson cap with deep-blue tassel; and on my feet is a pair of thin yellow-morocco shoes, covered over with thick red-morocco babooshes. My ankles﻿—my ten fingers﻿—my wrists﻿—are heavy with gold and silver ornaments; and in my ears, which, with considerable pain, I bored three days since, are two needle-splinters, to prepare the holes for rings.

===Chinese===

头上是小帽，罩一顶高高的绯红圆帽，垂着深蓝流苏；脚上一双薄薄的山羊皮黄鞋，外面套着厚山羊皮的红拖鞋。我的脚踝——我的十指——我的双腕——都沉沉地坠着金银的饰物；耳朵里——三天前我忍着相当的痛扎穿了它们——插着两根针状的小刺，好给将来的耳环开孔。

===Original===

O Liberty! I am free.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

哦，自由！我自由了。……

===Original===

While I was going to visit my old home in Harley Street that night, at the very moment when I turned from Oxford Street into Cavendish Square, this thought, fiercely hissed into my ears, was all of a sudden seething in me: “If now I should lift my eyes, and see a man walking yonder﻿—just yonder﻿—*at the corner there*﻿—turning from Harewood Place into Oxford Street﻿—what, my good God, should I do?﻿—I without even a knife to run and plunge into his heart?”
And I turned my eyes﻿—ogling, suspicious eyes of furtive horror﻿—reluctantly, lingeringly turned﻿—and I peered deeply with lowered brows across the murky winds at that same spot: but no man was there.
Hideously frequent is this nonsense now become with me﻿—in streets of towns﻿—in deep nooks of the country: the invincible assurance that, if I but turn the head, and glance *there*﻿—at a certain fixed spot﻿—I shall surely see﻿—I *must* see﻿—a man. And glance I must, glance I must, though I perish: and when I glance, though my hairs creep and stiffen like stirring amobse, yet in my eyes, I know, is monarch indignation against the intruder, and my neck stands stiff as sovereignty itself, and on my brow sits more than all the lordship of Persepolis and Iraz.
To what point of wantonness this arrogance of royalty may lead me, I do not know: I will watch, and see. It is written: “It is not good for man to be alone!” But good or no, the arrangement of One planet, One inhabitant, already seems to me, not merely a natural and proper, but the *only* natural and proper, condition; so much so, that any other arrangement has now, to my mind, a certain improbable, wild, and far-fetched unreality, like the Utopian schemes of dreamers and faddists. That the whole world should have been made for *me* alone﻿—that London should have been built only in order that *I* might enjoy the vast heroic spectacle of its burning﻿—that all history, and all civilisation should have existed only in order to accumulate for *my* pleasures its inventions and facilities, its stores of purple and wine, of spices and gold﻿—no more extraordinary does it all seem to me than to some little unreflecting Duke of my former days seemed the possessing of lands which his remote forefathers seized, and slew the occupiers: nor, in reality, is it even so extraordinary, I being alone.

===Chinese===

那天夜里我正要去看哈利街的老宅，就在从牛津街拐进卡文迪什广场的那一瞬，一个念头凶狠地嘶进我耳朵，霎时在我体内沸腾：「倘若此刻我抬起眼，看见一个人在那边走——就在那边——*就在那个拐角*——正从哈利伍德场所拐上牛津街——那可怎么办，我的老天？——我可是连一把捅进他心口的刀子都没有带？」
于是我转过眼去——那双偷偷觑探、满怀疑忌、暗怀恐怖的眼——不情愿地、迟迟疑疑地转过去——蹙着眉，透过阴惨的风，朝那个地点深深望去：但那里没有人。
如今这胡思乱想在我身上已频繁得骇人——在城镇的街上——在乡野的深处：总有一种不可战胜的确信，觉得我只要一回头，朝*那里*——朝某个固定的地点——一瞥，就必会看见——我*必定*看见——一个人。而我非瞥不可，非瞥不可，哪怕就此死去：而当我瞥去时，虽然我的毛发像蠕动的虫豸般乍起、僵直，可我知道，我眼里含着的是君主对入侵者的震怒，我的脖颈僵直得像主权本身，我的额上坐着比波斯波利斯和伊拉兹的全部领主治权更大的东西。
这王者的骄横会将我引到怎样放肆的地步，我不知道：我会看着，且看下文。经上写着：「人独居不好！」可是好不好且不论，一颗行星、一个居民这套安排，如今在我看来，已不光是自然而正当的，而且是*唯一*自然而正当的状态；以至于任何别的安排，在我心里都带上了一种荒诞、离奇、凿空之谈的不真实，像空想家和怪癖者们的乌托邦蓝图。整个世界专为*我*一人而造——伦敦之建起，只是为了*我*能享用它焚毁时那壮阔的英雄奇观——全部历史、全部文明之存在，只是为了给*我的*欢愉积攒它的种种发明与便利，它的紫红与美酒、香料与黄金的窖藏——这一切在我看来，并不比在我从前的日子里，某个不动脑子的小公爵之享有祖上夺得、杀尽原主而传下的田产更离奇：而且说实在的，既然只我一人，这压根算不得离奇。

===Original===

But what sometimes strikes me with some surprise is, not that the present condition of the world, with one sole master, should seem the commonplace and natural condition, but that it should have come to seem *so* commonplace and natural﻿—in nine months. The mind of Adam Jeffson is adaptable.

===Chinese===

可有时令我略感惊奇的，倒不是这独一主宰的世界现状竟显得寻常而自然，而是它竟然显得*这般*寻常自然——前后不过九个月。亚当·杰夫森这颗头脑，适应性真强。

===Original===

I sat a long time thinking such things by my bed that night, till finally I was disposed to sleep there. But I had no considerable number of candlesticks, nor was even sure of candles. I remembered, however, that Peter Peters, three doors away on the other side of the street, had had four handsome silver candelabra in his drawing-room, each containing six stems; and I said to myself: “I will search for candles in the kitchen, and if I find any, I will go and get Peter Peters’ candelabra, and sleep here.”
I took then the two lights which I had, my good God; went down to the passage; then down to the basement; and there had no difficulty in finding three packets of large candles, the fact being, I suppose, that the cessation of gaslighting had compelled everyone to provide themselves in this way, for there were a great many wherever I looked. With these I re-ascended, went into a little alcove on the second-floor where I had kept some drugs, got a bottle of carbolic oil, and for ten minutes went dashing all the corpses in the house. I then left the two lighted bits of candle on the waiting-room table, and, with the car-lamp, passed along the passage to the front-door, which was very violently banging. I stepped out to find that the storm had increased to a mighty turbulence (though it was dry), which at once caught my clothes, and whirled them into a flapping cloud about and above me; also, I had not crossed the street when my lamp was out. I persisted, however, half blinded, to Peters’ door. It was locked: but immediately near the pavement was a window, the lower sash up, into which, with little trouble, I lifted myself and passed. My foot, as I lowered it, stood on a body: and this made me angry and restless. I hissed a curse, and passed on, scraping the carpet with my soles, that I might hurt no one: for I did not wish to hurt anyone. Even in the almost darkness of the room I recognised Peters’ furniture, as I expected: for the house was his on a long lease, and I knew that his mother had had the intention to occupy it after his death. But as I passed into the passage, all was mere blank darkness, and I, depending upon the lamp, had left the matches in the other house.

===Chinese===

那夜，我就这样在床边坐了许久，直坐到最后竟打算就在这里睡下。可我手头没有多少烛台，连蜡烛也没有把握。不过我想起街对面隔三个门的彼得·彼得斯，他家客厅里有四座漂亮的银烛台，每座插六支烛；我便对自己说：「我去厨房找蜡烛，若找得到，就去把彼得·彼得斯的烛台取来，就在这儿睡。」
于是我拿起仅有的两点光，我的老天；下到过道；再下到地下室；毫不费事就找到三大包粗蜡烛——我想是因为煤气灯一断，家家都不得不这样备货，我到哪儿都见着一大堆。我拿着它们重新上楼，进了二楼一间我一直存着药品的小壁龛间，取了一瓶石炭酸油，用十分钟把全屋的尸首都浇了一遍。然后我把两截点着的蜡烛留在候诊室的桌上，提着车灯，沿着过道走到前门——门正被风撞得砰砰乱响。我跨出门去，发现风暴已壮大成一场狂暴的骚动（虽然没下雨），它一把扯住我的衣裳，卷成一片扑打翻飞的云裹在我周身上方；还有，我还没过街，灯就灭了。可我执拗地、半盲地摸到彼得斯家门口。门锁着：但紧挨人行道有一扇窗，下扇提着，我没费什么事就攀了进去。脚往下一落，正踩在一具尸身上：这叫我火起，烦躁。我嘶声咒了一句，继续往里走，鞋底擦着地毯蹭，生怕碰伤了谁：我并不想碰伤任何人。哪怕屋里几乎全黑，我也认出了彼得斯的家具，同我料想的一样：因为这宅子是他长租的，而且我知道他母亲曾打算在他死后住进来。可我一拐进过道，四下便只是一片纯粹的黑暗，而我仗着有灯，火柴竟留在了对面那座宅子里。

===Original===

I groped my way to the stairs, and had my foot on the first step, when I was stopped by a vicious shaking of the front-door, which someone seemed to be at with hustlings and the most urgent poundings: I stood with peering stern brows two or three minutes, for I knew that if I once yielded to the flinching at my heart, no mercy would be shown me in this house of tragedy, and thrilling shrieks would of themselves arise and ring through its haunted chambers. The rattling continued an inordinate time, and so instant and imperative, that it seemed as if it could not fail to force the door. But, though horrified, I whispered to my heart that it could only be the storm which was struggling at it like the grasp of a man, and after a time went on, feeling my way by the broad rail, in my brain somehow the thought of a dream which I had had in the *Boreal* of the woman Clodagh, how she let drop a fluid like pomegranate-seeds into water, and tendered it to Peter Peters: and it was a mortal purging draught; but I would not stop, but step by step went up, though I suffered very much, my brows peering at the utter darkness, and my heart shocked at its own rashness. I got to the first landing, and as I turned to ascend the second part of the stair, my left hand touched something icily cold: I made some quick instinctive movement of terror, and, doing so, my foot struck against something, and I stumbled, half falling over what seemed a small table there. Immediately a horrible row followed, for something fell to the ground: and at that instant, ah, I heard something﻿—a voice﻿—a human voice, which uttered words close to my ear﻿—the voice of Clodagh, for I knew it: yet not the voice of Clodagh in the flesh, but her voice clogged with clay and worms, and full of effort, and thick-tongued: and in that ghastly speech of the grave I distinctly heard the words:
Things being as they are in the matter of the death of Peter﻿ ﻿…
And there it stopped dead, leaving me so sick, my God, so sick, that I could hardly snatch my robes about me to fly, fly, fly, soft-footed, murmuring in pain, down the steps, down like a sneaking thief, but quick, snatching myself away, then wrestling with the cruel catch of the door which she would not let me open, feeling her all the time behind me, watching me.

===Chinese===

我摸到楼梯口，一只脚才踏上第一级，便被前门一阵凶暴的摇晃止住了：似乎有什么人正在门外挤撞，擂得万分急迫。我蹙紧双眉站着，站了两三分钟：因为我知道，我一旦向心头的畏缩屈服，这座悲剧之宅是决计不会饶过我的，尖利的嘶叫会自己升起来，响彻它那些闹鬼的房间。那撼门声持续了长得离谱的时间，那样急切、那样专横，仿佛不可能不把门撞开。可我虽然毛骨悚然，仍对自己的心低语说，那只能是风暴，是它像一只人手在那门上挣扎；过了一阵，我继续往上走，扶着宽扶手探路，脑子里不知怎么浮起在*博雷尔*号上做过的那个梦：梦见那女人克洛达，她把一种石榴籽色的液体滴进水里，递给彼得·彼得斯：那是一剂致命的泻药；可我不肯停下，一级一级往上走，虽然苦不堪言，眉头紧蹙望着无边的黑暗，心惊于自己的莽撞。我到了第一段楼梯的平台，转身要上第二段时，左手触到一样冰凉刺骨的东西：我惊恐地一缩，缩时脚又绊在什么上，踉跄着半跌在一张像是小桌子的东西上。紧接着便响起一片可怕的喧闹，有件东西摔到了地上：而就在那一瞬，啊，我听见了什么——一个声音——一个人的声音，就贴着我耳边吐出话来——是克洛达的声音，我听得出：却又不是血肉的克洛达的声音，而是她那塞满黏土和蛆虫的声音，满含吃力，舌头发滞：在那坟墓的阴森言语里，我分明听见这句：
事已至此，关于彼得的死……
它就此戛然停住，留给我的是那样一阵恶心，我的上帝，那样恶心，我几乎来不及拢起袍子就飞逃，飞逃，飞逃，蹑着脚，一边呻吟着痛，奔下楼梯，像偷儿似的溜下去，却快得很，恨不能把自己抽走，再同那扇她不肯让我打开的门的狠毒门闩搏斗，只觉得她始终在我背后，盯着我。

===Original===

And when I did get out, I was away up the length of the street, trailing my long *jubbah*, glancing backward, panting, for I thought that she might dare to follow, with her daring evil will. And all that night I lay on a common bench in the wind-tossed and dismal Park.

===Chinese===

等我终于脱身出来，我已逃到这条街远远的那头，曳着我的长*朱巴袍*，一边回头张望，一边喘息，因为我想，凭她那股邪恶胆大的意志，她兴许敢追出来。那一夜，我就躺在风撼木摇的凄惨公园里一张普通长椅上。

===Original===

The first thing which I did when the sun was up was to return to that place: and I returned with hard and masterful brow.
Approaching Peters’ house I saw now, what the darkness had hidden from me, that on his balcony was someone﻿—quite alone there. The balcony is a slight openwork wrought-iron structure, connected to a small roof by three slender voluted pillars, two at the ends, one in the middle: and at the middle one I saw someone, a woman﻿—kneeling﻿—her arms clasped tight about the pillar, and her face rather upward-looking. Never did I see aught more horrid: there were the gracious curves of the woman’s bust and hips still well preserved in a clinging dress of red cloth, very faded now; and her reddish hair floated loose in a large flimsy cloud about her; but her face, in that exposed position, had been quite eaten away by the winds to a noseless skeleton, which grinned from ear to ear, with slightly-dropped under-jaw﻿—most horrid in contrast with the body, and frame of hair. I meditated upon her a long time that morning from the opposite pavement. An oval locket at her throat contained, I knew, my likeness: for eight years previously I had given it her. It was Clodagh, the poisoner.
I thought that I would go into that house, and walk through it from top to bottom, and sit in it, and spit in it, and stamp in it, in spite of anyone: for the sun was now high. I accordingly went in again, and up the stairs to the spot where I had been frightened, and had heard the words. And here a great rage took me, for I at once saw that I had been made the dupe of the malign wills that beset me, and the laughingstock of Those for whom I care not a fig. From a little mahogany table there I had knocked sideways to the ground, in my stumble, a small phonograph with a great 25-inch japanned-tin horn, which, the moment that I now noticed it, I took and flung with a great racket down the stairs: for that this it was which had addressed me I did not doubt; it being indeed evident that its clockwork mechanism had been stopped by the volcanic scoriae in the midst of the delivery of a record, but had been started into a few fresh oscillations by the shock of the fall, making it utter those thirteen words, and stop.

===Chinese===

太阳升起后我做的头一件事，便是回到那地方：这一回，我眉头硬朗而专横。
走近彼得斯宅子时，我如今看清了黑夜睛过我的东西：他家阳台上有人——独自一个。那阳台是一座轻巧的镂花熟铁建筑，靠三根细而带涡卷的柱子连着一方小屋顶，两根在两头，一根居中：而就在中间那根柱子上，我看见一个人，一个女人——跪着——双臂紧紧搂住柱子，脸微微仰着。我从不曾见过比这更骇人的东西：她胸与臀的柔美曲线，在那条紧身的红布裙里还保存得很好，只是如今褪尽了色；她带红的头发松松地飘成一大片薄云，笼在她四周；可她那张脸，在那样暴露的位置上，已被风吃得只剩一具无鼻的骷髅，咧着嘴，从这只耳咧到那只耳，下颌微微垂着——同那身子、那头发的框子两相对照，可怖至极。那天早晨，我在对面的人行道上，对着她沉思了许久。她喉间那枚椭圆的金匣里装着，我知道，我的小像：因为八年前是我送给她的。那是克洛达赫，那个下毒的女人。
我想我要进那宅子去，从顶到底走一遍，坐在里面，往里面啐唾沫，在里面跺脚，管他什么人不人：因为太阳已经高了。于是我又进去了，上楼梯，走到我受惊、听见那句话的地方。而这一下，一股大怒把我攝住，因为我立刻看出：我一直被那班围困我的恶意意志玩弄于股掌，被那些我毫不在乎的「谁」当了笑柄。原来我绊倒时，侧着碰翻在地的，是那张红木小桌上的一台小留声机，带一只二十五英寸的大黑漆马口铁喇叭；此刻我一看见它，便抓起来，哐啷一声摔下了楼梯：因为向我说话的正是它，这我毫不怀疑；事情显然是：它的发条机括被火山熔渣卡住，停在一支唱片的半中央，却被我摔倒时的震动激起了几下新的摆荡，叫它吐出那十三个字，随即停住。

===Original===

I was sufficiently indignant at the moment, but have since been glad, for I was thereby put upon the notion of collecting a number of cylinders with records, and have been touched with indescribable sensations, sometimes thrilled, at hearing the silence of this Eternity broken by those singing and speaking voices, so lifelike, yet most ghostly, of the old dead.

===Chinese===

当时我怒得够呛，可后来倒高兴了：因为由此我起了收集一批唱片蜡筒的念头；听到这永恒的寂静被那些歌着、说着的嗓音打破——旧日死者的嗓音，那样栩栩如生，却又最是幽魂——我不时被一种无可名状的感觉所触动，有时竟至战栗。

===Original===

Well, the most of that same day I spent in a high chamber at Woolwich, dusting out, and sometimes oiling, time-fuses: a work in which I acquired such facility in some hours, that each finally occupied me no more than ninety to a hundred seconds, so that by evening I had, with the previous day’s work, close on 600. The construction of these little things is very simple, and, I believe, effective, so that I should have no difficulty in making them myself in large numbers, if it were necessary. Most contain a tiny dry battery, which sends a current along a bell or copper wire at the running-down moment, the clocks being contrived to be set for so many days, hours, and minutes, while others ignite by striking. I arranged in rows in the covered van those which I had prepared, and passed the night in an inn near the Barracks. I had brought candlesticks from London in the morning, and arranged the furniture﻿—a settee, chest-of-drawers, basin-stand, table, and a number of chairs﻿—in three-quarter-circle round the bed, so getting a triple-row altar of lights, mixed with vases of the house containing small palms and evergreens; with this I mingled a smell of ambergris from the scattered contents of some Turkish sachets which I had; in the bed a bottle of sweet Chypre-wine, with bonbons, nuts, and Havannas. As I lay me down, I could not but reflect, with a smile which I knew to be evil, upon that steady, strong, smouldering lust within me which was urging me through all those pains at the Arsenal, I who shirked every labour as unkingly. So, however, it was: and the next morning I was at it again after an early breakfast, my fingers at first quite stiff with cold, for it blew a keen and January gale. By nine I had 820 fuses; and judging those sufficient to commence with, got into the motor, and took it round to a place called the East Laboratory, a series of detached buildings, where I knew that I should find whatever I wanted: and I prepared my mind for a day’s labour.

===Chinese===

唉，那一天的大半，我都消磨在伍利奇一间高高的屋子里，给定时引信掸尘，有时上点油：这活计我几个钟头便熟练到这般地步，末了每只只消九十到一百秒，于是到傍晚，连同前一日的活，我已有将近六百只。这些小东西构造很简单，而且我相信很有效，若有必要，我自己动手大批制造也不难。它们大半内装一节小小的干电池，走完时辰的一刻便沿一根电铃线或铜线送出电流，钟机可以拨定几天、几点、几分；另一些则一击即燃。我把备好的引信一排排安在那辆带篷货车里，在兵营附近一家客栈过的夜。早上我从伦敦带来了烛台，把家具——一张软榻、一口五斗橱、脸盆架、一张桌子、若干椅子——绕床摆成四分之三的圆圈，围出一座三层的灯烛之坛，间杂着屋里花瓶中的小小棕榈与常青；我又洒了几只土耳其香囊里的碎屑，掺进一股龙涎香；床上放着一瓶塞浦路斯甜酒，配着糖果、果仁和哈瓦那雪茄。躺下时，我不禁带着一抹自知邪恶的笑，想起我胸中那股稳定、强大、闷燃的欲火——它驱使我吃了兵工厂里那些苦头的，正是我这个把一切劳作都当有失王者身份而推三阻四的人。然而事情就是这样：第二天清晨，我早早吃过饭又干起来，手指起初冻得发僵，因为刮着一月里那种磆骨的大风。到九点，我已有八百二十只引信；估摸着够开局之用，我便上了摩托，开到一处叫「东实验室」的地方——一连串彼此独立的建筑——我知道在那里想要什么有什么：我也定好了心思，来干上一整天。

===Original===

In this place I found incredible stores: mountains of percussion-caps, more chambers of fuses, small-arm cartridges, shells, and all those murderous explosive mixtures, a-making and made, with which modern savagery occupied its leisure in exterminating itself: or, at least, savagery civilised in its top-story only: for civilisation was apparently from the head downwards, and never once grew below the neck in all those centuries, those people being certainly much more mental than cordial, though I doubt if they were genuinely mental either﻿—reminding one rather of that composite image of Nebuchadnezzar, head of gold, breast brazen, feet of clay﻿—head manlike, heart cannibal, feet bestial﻿—like aegipeds, and mermaids, and puzzling undeveloped births. However, it is of no importance: and perhaps I am not much better than the rest, for I, too, after all, am of them. At any rate, their lyddites, melanites, cordites, dynamites, powders, jellies, oils, marls, and civilised barbarisms and obeahs, came in very well for their own destruction: for by two o’clock I had so worked, that I had on the first cart the phalanx of fuses; on the second a goodly number of kegs, cartridge-cases and cartridge-boxes, full of powder, explosive cottons and gelatines, and liquid nitroglycerine, and earthy dynamite, with some bombs, two reels of cordite, two pieces of tarred cloth, a small iron ladle, a shovel, and a crowbar; the cab came next, containing a considerable quantity of loose coal; and lastly, in the private carriage lay four big cans of common oil. And first, in the Laboratory, I connected a fuse-conductor with a huge tun of blasting-gelatine, and I set the fuse on the ground, timed for the midnight of the twelfth day thence; and after that I visited the Main Factory, the Carriage Department, the Ordnance Store Department, the Royal Artillery Barracks, and the Powder Magazines in the Marshes, traversing, as it seemed to me, miles of building; and in some I laid heaps of oil-saturated coal with an explosive in suitable spots on the ground-floor near woodwork, and in some an explosive alone: and all I timed for ignition at midnight of the twelfth day. Hot now, and black as ink, I proceeded through the town, stopping with perfect system at every hundredth door: and I laid the faggots of a great burning: and timed them all for ignition at midnight of the twelfth day.

===Chinese===

在这地方我发现了难以置信的库存：雷管堆成了山，还有一间间屋子的引信、枪弹、炮弹，以及现代野蛮人用来消磨闲暇、自相灭绝的一切杀人的爆炸混合物——或者说，至少是只在头脑那一层文明化了的野蛮人：因为文明显然是自上而下、从头部下延的，那许多世纪里从未长到脖颈以下；那些人确实心智远多于心血，虽然我怀疑他们连心智也未必真有——倒叫人想起尼布甲尼撒那尊复合的像：金头、铜胸、泥足——头似人，心似食人族，足似畜生——像羊足怪、像人鱼、像种种令人费解的未发育全的怪胎。不过这无关紧要：说不定我也比其余的强不到哪儿去，因为我毕竟也是他们中的一个。无论如何，他们的利达炸药、梅拉尼特炸药、柯达火药、达纳炸药、火药、胶质炸药、油类、泥灰，以及种种文明的野蛮和巫蛊，正好拿来毁了他们自己：因为到两点钟，我已干到这个地步：第一辆车上是引信的方阵；第二辆上是相当数量的火药桶、弹壳和弹药箱，满装着火药、火棉和胶质炸药，液态硝化甘油和土状达纳炸药，外加几颗炸弹、两卷柯达火药、两块油毡布、一只小铁勺、一把铲子、一根撬棍；出租马车随后，装着相当多的散煤；最后，私人马车里躺着四大罐寻常的油。先是在实验室里，我把一根引信导线连上一大桶爆破胶质炸药，把引信搁在地上，拨定十二天后那一天的午夜；随后我走遍了总厂、车械所、军械库、皇家炮兵营房和沼地里的火药库，走过的屋宇，仿佛有好几英里；有几处我在底层的木作近旁，堆上浸透了油的煤，配上炸药，择地放好；有几处只放炸药：全部拨定十二天后那一天的午夜引爆。此刻我浑身滚烫，黑得像墨，穿城而过，每第一百户门口便极有章法地停一停：我布下了一场大火的柴堆：全部拨定十二天后那一天的午夜点燃。

===Original===

Whatever door I found closed against me I drove at it with a maniac malice.

===Chinese===

凡是有门闭着不纳我，我便怀着疯癫的恶意向它猛撞。

===Original===

Shall I commit the whole dark fact to paper?﻿—that deep, deep secret of the human organism?
As I wrought, I waxed wicked as a demon! And with lowered neck, and forward curve of the lower spine, and the blasphemous strut of tragic playactors, I went. For here was no harmless burning which I did﻿—but the crime of arson; and a most fiendish, though vague, malevolence, and the rage to burn and raven and riot, was upon me like a dog-madness, and all the mood of Nero, and Nebuchadnezzar: and from my mouth proceeded all the obscenities of the slum and of the gutter, and I sent up such hisses and giggles of challenge to Heaven that day as never yet has man let out. But this way lies a spinning frenzy.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我该把这桩黑暗的事实全写上纸吗？——人体那最深、最深的秘密？
我干着干着，就恶得像恶魔一样！我压低脖颈，腰椎前弓，迈着悲剧戏子渎神的台步，一路走去。因为我干的已不是无害的焚烬——而是纵火的罪；一种最凶邪、虽然模糊的恶毒，一种焚烧、掠夺、暴乱的狂怒，像疯犬病一样扑在我身上，兼有尼禄的全部心绪、尼布甲尼撒的全部心绪：从我嘴里吐出的，尽是贫民窟和阴沟里的污言秽语；那一天，我向老天发出的那种挑衅的嘶声和窃笑，是人类从未发出过的。可沿着这条路走下去，就是天旋地转的癫狂。……

===Original===

I have taken a dead girl with wild huggings to my bosom; and I have touched the corrupted lip, and spat upon her face, and tossed her down, and crushed her teeth with my heel, and jumped and jumped upon her breast, like the snake-stamping zebra, mad, mad﻿ ﻿… !

===Chinese===

我曾疯狂地拥抱过一个死去的姑娘，把她搂在怀里；我也曾碰过那腐烂的唇，朝她的脸啐唾沫，把她掼在地上，用鞋跟碾碎她的牙，在她胸口一跳又一跳，像那踩蛇的斑马，疯了，疯了……！

===Original===

I was desolated, however, that first day of the faggot-laying, even in the midst of my sense of omnipotence, by one thing, which made me give some kicks to the motor: for it was only crawling, so that a good part of the way I was stalking by its side; and when I came to that hill near the Old Dover Road, the whole thing stopped, and refused to move, the weight of the train being too great for my horsepower traction. I did not know what to do, and stood there in angry impotence a full half-hour, for the notion of setting up an electric station, with or without automatic stoking-gear, presented so hideous a picture of labour to me, that I would not entertain it. After a time, however, I thought that I remembered that there was a comparatively new power station in St. Paneras driven by turbines: and at once, I uncoupled the motor, covered the drays with the tarpaulins, and went driving at singing speed, choosing the emptier by-streets, and not caring whom I crushed. After some trouble I found, in fact, the station in an obscure by-street made of two long walls, and went in by a window, a rage upon me to have my will quickly accomplished. I ran up some stairs, across two rooms, into a gallery containing a switchboard, and in the room below saw the works, all very neat-looking, but, as I soon found, very dusty. I went down, and fixed upon a generating set﻿—there were three﻿—that would give a decent load, and then saw that the switch-gear belonging to this particular generator was in order. I then got some cloths and thoroughly cleaned the dust off the commutators; ran next﻿—for I was in a strange fierce haste﻿—and turned the water into the turbines, and away went the engine; I hurried to set the lubricators running on the bearings, and in a couple of minutes had adjusted the speed, and the brushes of the generators, and switched the current on to the line. By this time, however, I saw that it was getting dark, and feared that little could be done that day; still, I hurried out, the station still running, got into the car, and was off to look for a good electric one, of which there are hosts in the streets, in order at least to clean up and adjust the motor that night. I drove down three by-streets, till I turned into Euston Road: but I had no sooner reached it than I pulled up﻿—with sudden jerk﻿—with a shout of astonishment.

===Chinese===

然而，堆柴的头一天，即便正踌躇满志，也有一件事叫我大为败兴，气得我冲着机车踢了几脚：因为它只能蠕动，大半路我只好在它旁边跨着大步走；等到了老多佛路附近那道山坡，整套车干脆停住，死活不动了——列车的重量对我的马力牵引来说实在太重。我不知道该怎么办，又怒又无可奈何地在那里站了整整半个钟头：因为一想到要建一座发电站，带不带自动加煤机都一样——那幅劳作图景在我眼里实在可怕，我不肯考虑。不过过了一阵，我想起自己似乎记得，圣潘克拉斯有一座相当新的涡轮发电站：我立刻摘开机车，给货车罩上油布，驱车以歌唱的速度奔去，专挑空一些的后街，也不管辗着谁。费了些周折，我果然在一条晦暗的后街里找到了那座电站——两道长墙围成——从一扇窗子钻了进去，满腔狂怒，只想把我的意志快快办成。我跑上楼梯，穿过两个房间，进了一条装有配电盘的廊道，在下面机房里看见了整套设备，样子都很整洁，但很快我发现，蒙满了灰。我下去，选定了一组发电机组——一共有三组——能给出像样的负载，又查看了属于这组发电机的开关设备是否完好。然后我找了些抹布，把整流子上的灰彻底擦净；接着跑——因为我正处在一种奇怪的狂暴的急切里——去放了水进涡轮，机器便转了起来；我赶紧给各轴承上好油杯，两分钟内调好了转速和发电机的电刷，把电流送上了线路。可这时，我眼看天色渐黑，恐怕当天干不成什么了；我仍然急忙出去，让电站继续运转着，上了车，去找一辆好电动车——街上多的是——好当晚至少把机车修整好。我驶过三条后街，拐上尤斯顿路：可我一到那儿，便猛地刹住——猛地一挫——惊呼出声。

===Original===

That cursed street was all lighted up and gay! and three shimmering electric globes, not far apart, illuminated every feature of a ghastly battlefield of dead.
And there was a thing there, the grinning impression of which I shall carry to my grave: a thing which spelled and spelled at me, and ceased, and began again, and ceased, and spelled at me. For, above a shop which faced me was a flag, a red flag with white letters, fluttering on the gale the words: “Metcalfe’s Stores”; and beneath the flag, stretched right across the house, was the thing which spelled, letter by letter, in letters of light: and it spelled two words, deliberately, coming to the end, and going back to recommence:

> Drink

Roboral.

And that was the last word of civilised Man to me, Adam Jeffson﻿—its final counsel﻿—its ultimate gospel and message﻿—to *me*, my good God! *Drink Roboral!*
I was put into such a passion of rage by this blatant ribaldry, which affected me like the laughter of a skeleton, that I rushed from the car, with the intention, I believe, of seeking stones to stone it: but no stones were there: and I had to stand impotently enduring that rape of my eyes, its victoriously-dogged iteration, its taunting leer, its Drink Roboral﻿—*D*, *R*, *I*, *N*, *K* *R*, *O*, *B*, *O*, *R*, *A*, *L*.
It was one of those electrical spelling-advertisements, worked by a small motor commutator driven by a works-motor, and I had now set it going: for on some night before that Sabbath of doom the chemist must have set it to work, but finding the works abandoned, had not troubled to shut it down again. At any rate, this thing stopped my work for that day, for when I went to shut down the works it was night; and I drove to the place which I had made my home in sullen and weary mood: for I knew that Roboral would not cure the least of all my sores.

===Chinese===

那条该死的街道竟灯火通明、一派欢腾！三只晶莹的电球，相隔不远，把一片死人组成的凄惨战场照得纤毫毕现。
而那里有一样东西，它那咧嘴的讪笑，我要带进坟墓：一样东西朝我一个字母一个字母地拼着，停了，又从头拼起，停了，又朝我拼。因为，正对我的一家铺子上方挂着一面旗，一面白字红旗，在狂风里抖出这几个词：「梅特卡夫商店」；旗下，横贯整座房子的，就是那拼字的东西，用光的字母，一个字母一个字母地拼；它拼两个词，从容不迫，拼到头，又回去重新开始：

> 请饮

罗博拉尔（Roboral）。

这就是文明人对亚当·杰夫森说的最后一句话——它最后的忠告——它终极的福音与讯息——说给*我*，我的老天！*请饮罗博拉尔！*
这赤裸裸的嘲弄气得我暴跳如雷——它刺激我，就像一副骷髅的笑声——我从车里冲出去，我相信，是想找石头砸它：可那里没有石头：我只得干站着，忍着它对我双眼的强暴，忍着它得胜而不依不饶的重复，它挑衅的睨视，它的请饮罗博拉尔——*D*、*R*、*I*、*N*、*K*，*R*、*O*、*B*、*O*、*R*、*A*、*L*。
那是一种电动拼字广告，靠一台小整流马达带动，由厂里的电机驱动，如今是我把它弄转了起来：想必是在末日那个安息日之前的某个夜晚，那位药剂师让它开了工，后来见厂里人去楼空，也就懒得再关。无论如何，这东西断送了我那天的活计：因为等我去把电站关掉，天已经黑透；我驱车回到我安家的地方，满腔阴郁与疲惫：因为我知道，罗博拉尔治不好我哪怕最小的一处创痛。

===Original===

The next morning I awoke in quite another frame of mind, disposed to idle, and let things go. After rising, dressing, washing in cold diluted rose-water, and descending to the salle-à-manger, where I had laid my morning-meal the previous evening, I promenaded an hour the only one of these long sombrous tufted corridors in which there were not more than two dead, though behind the doors on either hand, all of which I had locked, I knew that they lay in plenty. When I was warmed, I again went down, looked into my motor, got three cylinders from one of a number of motors standing near, lit up, and drove away﻿—to Woolwich, as I thought at first: but instead of crossing the river by Blackfriars, I went more eastward; and having passed from Holborn into Cheapside, which was impassable, unless I crawled, was about to turn, when I noticed a phonograph-shop: into this I got by a side-door, suddenly seized by quite a curiosity to hear what I might hear. I took a good one with microphone diaphragm, and a number of record-cylinders in a brass-handled box, and I put them into the car, for there was still a very strong peach-odour in this closed shop, which displeased me. I then proceeded southward and westward through by-streets, seeking some probable house into which to go from the rough cold winds, when I saw the Parliament-house, and thither, turning river-ward by Westminster Hall to Palace Yard, I went, and with my two parcels, one weighting each arm, walked into this old place along a line of purple-dusted busts; I deposited my boxes on a table beside a massive brass thing lying there, which, I suppose, must be what they called the Mace; and I sat to hear.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨我醒来，心境全然换了一副，只想偷闲，任事情自去。起身、穿衣、用稀释的冷玫瑰水洗了脸，下到饭厅——前一晚我把早饭摆在了那里——之后，我在这些幽暗的长地毯走廊里的一条上踱了一个钟头：这一条里死人不超过两个，虽然两侧门后——门全叫我锁了——我知道躺着的不计其数。身上暖过来后，我又下去，看了看摩托，从旁边停着的一排摩托里的一辆上取了三只蜡筒，点了灯，开车走了——起初想的是去伍利奇：可我没有从黑衣修士桥过河，而是更往东去；从霍尔本进了奇普赛德街，那里除非爬着走否则过不去，正要折回，忽然看见一家留声机铺：我便从一道边门进去，突然生出一股极强的好奇，想听听我能听见什么。我挑了一台带麦克风膜片的好机器，又拿了一盒黄铜提手的蜡筒，把它们放进车里，因为这间闭着的铺子里桃子的气味还很浓，叫我着恼。随后我穿过小街向南、向西，寻一处像样的房子躲一躲凛冽的寒风，这时我望见了议会大厦；便从威斯敏斯特厅折向河边，进了宫殿院，带着我两件包裹，一边一压，顺着一行蒙着紫灰的半身像，走进这古老的地方；我把盒子搁在桌上一件躺着的厚重铜器旁——那东西，我猜，必是他们叫作「权杖」的——便坐下来听。

===Original===

Unfortunately, the phonograph was a clockwork one, and when I wound it, it would not go: so that I got very angry at my absurdity in not bringing an electric mechanism, as I could with much less trouble have put in a chemical than cleaned the clockwork; and this thing put me into such a rage, that I nearly tore it to pieces, and was half for kicking it: but there was a man sitting in an old straight-backed chair quite near me, which they called the Speaker’s Chair, who was in such a pose, that he had, every time I glanced suddenly at him, precisely the air of bending forward with interest to watch what I was doing, a Mohrgrabim kind of man, almost black, with Jewish nose, crinkled hair, keffiyeh, and flowing robe, probably, I should say, an Abyssinian Galla; with him were only five or six people about the benches, mostly leaning forward with rested head, so that this place had quite a void sequestered mood. At all events, this Galla, or Bedouin, with his grotesque interest in my doings, restrained my hands: and, finally, by dint of peering, poking, dusting, and adjusting, in an hour’s time I got the phonograph to go very well.
And all that morning, and far into late afternoon, forgetful of food, and of the cold which gradually possessed me, I sat there listening, musing﻿—cylinder after cylinder: frivolous songs, orchestras, voices of famous men whom I had spoken with, and shaken their solid hands, speaking again to me, but thick-tongued, with hoarse effort and gurgles, from out the vague void beyond the grave: most strange, most strange. And the third cylinder that I put on, ah, I knew, with a fearful start, that voice of thunder, I knew it well: it was the preacher, Mackay’s; and many, many times over I heard those words of his that day, originally spoken, it seems, when the cloud had just passed the longitude of Vienna; and in all that torrent of speech not one single word of “I told you so”: but he cries:
“… praise Him, O Earth, for He is He: and if He slay me, I will laugh raillery at His Sword, and banter Him to His face: for His Sword is sharp Mercy, and His poisons kill my death. Fear not, therefore, little flock of Man!

===Chinese===

不巧，那留声机是发条式的，我上了弦，它却不动：我登时气坏了，恨自己荒唐，没带电式的来——装一套化学电源本远比擦洗发条省事；这东西气得我几乎要把它撕碎，一半想踹它一脚：可偏偏有一人坐在离我相当近的一把旧直背椅里——他们管那叫「议长席」——他那副姿势，害得我每次突然瞟他一眼，都觉得他恰像饶有兴味地倾身向前，看我做些什么：一个摩尔-阿拉伯人一类的人，几乎全黑，犹太式的鼻子，鬀发，戴头巾，披长袍，我该说，多半是个阿比西尼亚的盖拉人；长凳四周只陪着他五六个人，大都倾着头伏着，所以这地方颇有一种空旷幽僻的意味。无论如何，正是这个盖拉人，或贝都因人，用他对我所作所为的那份古怪的兴趣，镇住了我的手：末了，靠着一番眯眼察看、拨弄、掸灰、调校，一个钟头后，我让留声机转得非常之好了。
整个那个上午，直到下午很晚，我忘了吃，忘了渐渐侵上身的寒冷，坐在那里听，坐在那里想——一筒又一筒：轻佻的歌，管弦乐，我同他们说过话、握过他们结实的手的鼎鼎大名的人物们，又向我说话了，却口舌黏滞，嗓音嘶哑而咕噜作响，来自坟墓那边一片渺茫的虚空：最奇怪，最奇怪了。而我放上的第三只蜡筒，啊，我心怀恐惧地一惊，认出了那雷霆之声，我熟得很：那是麦凯，那布道人的；那一天我把他的话听了千遍万遍，当初说这话，似乎是在那云刚过维也纳的经度线时；而在那滔滔的言语里，没有一个字说「我早告诉过你们」：他喊的是：
「……赞美祂吧，大地啊，因为祂就是祂：祂即便杀我，我也要笑祂的剑，当面同祂打趣：因为祂的剑是锋利的慈悲，祂的毒药杀死的是我的死亡。所以不要怕，小小的人群哪！

===Original===

but take my comfort to your heart tonight, and my sweets to your tongue: for though ye have sinned, and hardened yourselves as brass, and gone far, far astray in these latter wildernesses, yet He is infinitely greater than your sin, and will lead you back. Break not, break not, poor broken heart of Earth: for from Him I run herald to thee this night with the sweet and secret message, that of old He chose thee, and once mixed conjugally with thee in an ancient sleep, O Afflicted: and He is thou, and thou art He, flesh of His flesh, and bone of His bone; and if thou perish utterly, it is that He has perished utterly, too: for thou art He. Hope, therefore, most, and cheeriest smile, at the very apsis and black nadir of Despair: for He is nimble as a weasel, and He twists like Proteus, and His solstices and equinoxes, His tropics and turning-points and recurrences are innate in Being, and when He falls He falls like harlequin and shuttlecocks, shivering plumb to His feet, and each third day, lo, He is risen again, and His defeats are but the stepping-stones and rough scaffolding from which He builds His Parthenons, and from the densest basalt gush His rills, and the last end of this Earth shall be no poison-cloud, I say to you, but Carnival and Harvest-home﻿ ﻿… though ye have sinned, poor hearts﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

只要今夜把我的安慰收进你们心里，把我的甘甜放上你们的舌尖：因为你们虽然犯了罪，硬得像黄铜，在这些末日的荒野里走迷了很远、很远，祂却无限地大过你们的罪，必领你们回来。碎裂吧，不要碎，地球啊，你这可怜的碎心：因为今夜我为祂向你跑腿传信，带着那甘甜而秘密的消息：祂在太古便拣选了你，曾在一场古老的沉睡中与你婚配相合，受苦者啊：祂就是你，你就是祂，祂肉中的肉，祂骨中的骨；你若彻底灭亡，那便是祂也彻底灭亡了：因为你就是祂。所以要存最大的指望，露出最欢畅的笑，就在绝望的天底和至黑的最低点：因为祂伶俐如鼬，祂扭动如普罗透斯，祂的至点和分点、祂的回归线和转折点与循环，都是与生俱来的；祂跌倒时，跌得像丑角和羽毛球，笔直落回自己脚上；每逢第三日，看哪，祂又起来了，祂的诸败不过是垫脚石和粗陋的脚手架，祂就在上面起造祂的帕特农；最致密的玄武岩里也涌出祂的细流；这大地的最后结局，我告诉你们，必不是什么毒云，而是狂欢节，是丰收祭……虽然你们犯了罪，可怜的心哪……」

===Original===

So Mackay, with thick-tongued metallic effort. I found this brown room of the Commons-house, with its green benches, and grilled galleries, so agreeable to my mood, that I went again the next morning, and listened to more records, till they tired me: for what I had was a prurient itch to hear secret scandals, and revelations of the festering heart, but these cylinders, gathered from a shop, divulged nothing. I then went out to make for Woolwich, but in the car saw the poet’s notebook in which I had written: and I took it, went back, and was writing an hour, till I was tired of that, too; and judging it too late for Woolwich that day, wandered about the dusty committee-rooms and recesses of this considerable place. In one room another foolishness suddenly seized upon me, showing how my slightest whim has become more imperious within me than all the Jaws of the Medes and Persians: for in that room, Committee Room No. 15, I found an apparently young policeman lying flat on his back, who pleased me: his helmet tilted under his head, and near one white-gloved hand a blue official envelope; the air of that stagnant quiet room was still perceptibly peach-scented, and he gave not the slightest odour that I could detect, though he had been corporal and stalwart, his face now the colour of dark ashes, in each hollow cheek a ragged hole about the size of a sixpence, the flimsy vaulted eyelids well embedded in their caverns, from under whose fringe of eyelash seemed whispered the word: *Eternity*. His hair seemed very long for a policeman, or perhaps it had grown since death; but what interested me about him, was the envelope at his hand: for “what,” I asked myself, “was this fellow doing here with an envelope at three o’clock on a Sunday afternoon?” This made me look closer, and then I saw by a mark at the left temple that he had been shot, or felled; whereupon I was thrown into quite a great rage, for I thought that this poor man was killed in the execution of his duty, when many of his kind perhaps, and many higher than he, had fled their post to pray or riot. So, after looking at him a long time, I said to him: “Well, D. 47, you sleep very well: and you did well, dying so: I am pleased with you, and to mark my favour, I decree that you shall neither rot in the common air, nor burn in the common flames: for by my own hand shall you be distinguished with burial.”

===Chinese===

麦凯的声音就是这样，舌滞音金，勉力而出。我发觉下院这间棕色大厅——绿长椅，铁栅旁听廊——这样合我的心绪，以致次日早晨又去了，听了更多唱片，直听到腻：因为我怀的是一种痒痒的邪念，想听秘辛丑闻、溃烂之心的自供，可这些从铺子里取来的蜡筒，什么也不吐露。随后我出门要去伍利奇，却在车里看见了那本我写着一半的诗人笔记本：我便取了它回去，写了一个钟头，直到也写腻了；估摸那天再去伍利奇已太晚，便在这座巍峨之所积灰的委员会室和角落里闲逛。在一间屋里，一阵荒唐劲又突然攫住了我，可见我最轻微的一时兴起，在我体内已比米底和波斯的全部法条还要不可违逆：因为在那间第十五号委员会室里，我看见一个仰卧的、看样子很年轻的警察，他让我欢喜：头盔垫在头下，一只戴白手套的手旁放着一只蓝色公函信封；那间滞闷安静的屋子里的空气，仍隐约可闻桃子香，而他身上，我竟嗅不出一点气味，虽然他生前想必结实魁伟，如今他的脸已成深灰烬色，两边凹陷的面颊上各有一个六便士大小的破洞，薄薄的穹顶似的眼睑深深陷在眼窝里，睫毛的流苏下面，仿佛呢喃着一个词：*永恒*。他的头发对一个警察来说似乎太长了些，或许是死后又长了的；可我对他感兴趣的，是他手边那只信封：因为「这个家伙，」我自问，「星期天下午三点钟，揣着一封公函，在这里做什么？」这使我凑近细看，才看见他左太阳穴上有一处痕，他先吃了枪子，或是挨了重击；这下我可动了真怒，因为我想，这可怜人是殉职而死，而他那一行的许多人，许多比他位分高的人，想必早已弃职逃走，去祷告或去作乱了。于是我久久地看着他，然后对他说：「好吧，D47，你睡得很好：你死得也光彩：我对你满意，为彰我的恩典，我特许你既不在庸众的空气里腐烂，也不进庸众的火焰里焚化：因为我要亲自动手，以葬礼彰显你。」

===Original===

And this wind so possessed me, that I at once went out: with the crowbar from the car I broke the window of a near ironmonger’s in Parliament Street, got a spade, and went into Westminster Abbey. I soon prised up a grave-slab of some famous man in the north transept, and commenced to shovel: but, I do not know how, by the time I had digged a foot the whole impulse passed from me: I left off the work, promising to resume it: but nothing was ever done, for the next day I was at Woolwich, and busy enough about other matters.

===Chinese===

这股劲头这样地攫住了我，我立刻出了门：用车里的撬棍撬开议会街一家铁器行的橱窗，取了一把铁锹，进了威斯敏斯特教堂。我很快起出北耳堂某位名人的一方墓石，动手铲土：可不知怎么，挖到一英尺深，那股冲动便整个从我身上退去了：我撂下活计，许诺日后再续：可终究什么也没做成，因为第二天我已到了伍利奇，忙别的去了。

===Original===

During the next nine days I worked with a fever on me, and a map of London before me.
There were places in that city!﻿—secrets, vastnesses, horrors! In the wine-vaults at London Docks was a vat which must certainly have contained between twenty and thirty thousand gallons: and with dancing heart I laid a train there; the tobacco-warehouse must have covered eighty acres: and there I laid a fuse. In a house near Regent’s Park, standing in a garden, and shut from the street by a high wall, I saw a thing﻿ ﻿… ! and what shapes a great city hid I now first know.

===Chinese===

接下来的九天里，我浑身发着热地干，面前摊着一张伦敦地图。
那座城里有的是地方！——多少隐秘、多少浩大、多少恐怖！伦敦船坞的酒窖里有一只大桶，装的必有两三万加仑：我心跳着舞在那里布了火线；烟草仓库怕是有八十英亩：我在那里安了引信。在摄政公园附近一座宅子里——独门独院，一道高墙隔开街道——我看见了一样东西……！一座大城藏着些什么样的形骸，我这才知道。

===Original===

I left no quarter unremembered, taking a train, no longer of four, but of eight, vehicles, drawn by an electric motor which I recharged every morning, mostly from the turbine station in St. Pancras, once from a steam-station with very small engine and dynamo, found in the Palace Theatre, which gave little trouble, and once from a similar little station in a Strand hotel. With these I visited West Ham and Kew, Finchley and Clapham, Dalston and Marylebone; I exhausted London; I deposited piles in the Guildhall, in Holloway Gaol, in the new pillared Justice-hall of Newgate, in the Tower, in the Parliament-house, in St. Giles’ Workhouse, in the Crypt and under the organ of St. Paul’s, in the South Kensington Museum, in the Royal Agricultural Society, in Whiteley’s place, in the Trinity House, in Liverpool Street, in the Office of Works, in the secret recesses of the British Museum; in a hundred inflammable warehouses, in five hundred shops, in a thousand private dwellings. And I timed them all for ignition at midnight of the 23rd April.
By five in the afternoon of the 22nd, when I left my train in Maida Vale, and drove alone to the solitary house on high ground near Hampstead Heath which I had chosen, the work was well finished.

===Chinese===

我没有漏掉任何一个角落；我拉的车不再是一列四节，而是一列八节，由电动机车牵引，我每天早晨给它充电，多半在圣潘克拉斯的涡轮电站，有一回在宫殿剧院找到的一座小引擎小电机的蒸汽电站——没添什么麻烦——还有一回在斯特兰德街一家旅馆的小电站。就这样，我跑遍了西哈姆与邱园，芬奇利与克拉彭，达尔斯顿与马里勒本；我把伦敦榨了个遍；我把柴堆进了市政厅、霍洛韦监狱、新门监狱新的列柱法庭、伦敦塔、议会大厦、圣贾尔斯贫民习艺所、圣保罗教堂的地下圣室和管风琴底下、南肯辛顿博物馆、皇家农业协会、怀特利商厦、三一议院、利物浦街、工程局、大英博物馆的幽密库藏；堆进了一百座易燃的货栈、五百家铺子、一千所私宅。我把它们全部定在4月23日午夜一齐点火。
到22日下午五点，我把列车撂在梅达韦尔，独自驱车去汉普斯特德荒原附近高地上我选定的那座孤宅，那时，工程已干得妥妥帖帖。

===Original===

The great morning dawned, and I was early astir: for I had much to do that day.
I intended to make for the seashore the next morning, and had therefore to choose a good petrol motor, store it, and have it in a place of safety; I had also to drag another vehicle after me, stored with trunks of time-fuses, books, clothes, and other little things.
My first journey was to Woolwich, whence I took all that I might ever require in the way of mechanism; thence to the National Gallery, where I cut from their frames the “Vision of St. Helena,” Murillo’s “Boy Drinking,” and “Christ at the Column”; and thence to the Embassy to bathe, anoint myself, and dress.
As I had anticipated, and hoped, a blustering spring gale was blowing from the north.
Even as I set out from Hampstead, about 9 a.m., I had been able to guess that some of my fuses had somehow anticipated the appointed hour: for I saw three red hazes at various points in the air, and heard the far vague booming of an occasional explosion; and by 11 a.m. I felt sure that a large region of northeastern London must be in flames. With the solemn feelings of bridegrooms and marriage-mornings﻿—with a flinching, a flinching heart, God knows, yet a heart up-buoyed on thrilling joys﻿—I went about making preparations for the Gargantuan orgy of the night.

===Chinese===

那个盛大的早晨来了，我一早便起身：因为那天我有许多事要做。
我打算次日早晨去海边，因此得挑一辆好汽油摩托，装好货，安置在安全的地方；还得在我后面拖上另一辆车，装满一箱箱定时引信、书、衣裳和别的小东西。
我的头一趟是去伍利奇，从那里取了一切机械方面我也许用得着的东西；接着去国家美术馆，从画框上割下《圣海伦娜的异象》、牟利罗的《饮水少年》和《柱边的基督》；再去使馆沐浴、膏油、更衣。
正如我预期的、也盼望的，一场怒号的春风正从北面刮来。
早晨九点光景从汉普斯特德出发时，我便已猜到，我的一些引信不知怎么提前于约定的时辰炸了：因为我望见空气里三个不同的地点浮着淡淡的红霾，还听见偶尔一声爆炸传来的遥远的闷响；到十一点，我已确信，伦敦东北的一大片必已陷在火海里了。怀着新郎与婚日的庄严心情——怀着畏缩、一颗畏缩的心，天知道，却又是一颗被战栗的欢喜托起的心——我四处奔走，为今夜那场巨人般的狂欢做着准备。

===Original===

The house at Hampstead, which no doubt still stands, is of rather pleasing design in quite a stone and rural style, with good breadths of wall-surface, two plain coped gables, mullioned windows, and oversailing slate verge roofs, but, rather spoiling it, a high square three-storied tower at the southeast angle, on the topmost floor of which I had slept the previous night. There I had provided myself with a jar of pale tobacco mixed with rose-leaves and opium, found in a foreign house in Seymour Street, also a genuine Saloniki hookah, together with the best wines, nuts, and so on, and a gold harp of the musician Krasinski, stamped with his name, taken from his house in Portland Street.
But so much did I find to do that day, and so many odd things turned up which I thought that I would take with me, that it was not till near six that I drove finally northward through Camden Town. And now an ineffable awe possessed my soul at the solemn noise which everywhere encompassed me, an ineffable awe, a blissful terror. Never, never could I have dreamed of aught so great and potent. All above my head there rushed southward with widespread wing of haste a sparkling smoke; and mixed with the immense roaring I heard mysterious hubbubs of tumblings and rumblings, which I could not at all comprehend, like the moving-about of furniture in the houses of Titans; while pervading all the air was a most weird and tearful sound, as it were threnody, and a wild wail of pain, and dying swan-songs, and all lamentations and tribulations of the world. Yet I was aware that, at an hour so early, the flames must be far from general; in fact, they had not well commenced.

===Chinese===

汉普斯特德那座宅子——想必至今还立着——样式相当可喜，一派乡野石造风格，墙面大片大片留白，两座朴素的带盖顶山墙，直棂窗，石板瓦的檐口出挑得很远；只是东南角一座三层方塔稍嫌败坏景致，前一夜我就睡在它的顶层。我在那里给自己备下了一罐掺玫瑰花瓣和鸦片的淡色烟草——西摩街一户外国人家找到的——还有一只货真价实的萨洛尼卡水烟壶，外加最好的美酒、干果等等，以及乐师克拉辛斯基的一架金竖琴，琴上打着他的名字，是从他波特兰街的宅子里取来的。
可那天我发现要办的事竟那样多，冒出来要随身带走的零碎又那样不少，直到近六点，我才最终驱车向北，穿过卡姆登镇。此刻，一种不可言说的敬畏攫住了我的灵魂——四面八方围拢着庄严的巨响，一种不可言说的敬畏，一种极乐的恐怖。我做梦也梦不到如此伟大、如此有力的事物。我头顶上方，一团闪烁的烟张着仓皇的巨翼向南奔涌；而在那浩大的轰鸣里，还混着我全然无法索解的神秘骚响，訇然翻滚、隆隆不绝，像提坦巨人们家里挪动家具；弥漫整个空气的，还有一种至为怪异、如泣如诉的声音，仿佛挽歌，仿佛痛楚的野嚎，仿佛垂死的天鹅之歌，仿佛世上一切的哀哭与苦难。然而我心里明白，时辰这样早，火势必定远未燎原；事实上，它还没怎么烧起来呢。

===Original===
