As I had left a good semicircular region of houses, with a radius of four hundred yards, without combustibles to the south of the isolated house which I was to occupy, and as the wind was so strongly from the north, I simply left my two vehicles at the door of the house, without fear of any injury: nor did any occur. I then went up to the top of the tower, lit the candles, and ate voraciously of the dinner which I had left ready, for since the morning I had taken nothing; and then, with hands and heart that quivered, I arranged the clothes of the low spring-bed upon which to throw my frame in the morning hours. Opposite the wall, where lay the bed, was a Gothic window, pretty large, with low sill, hung with poppy-figured muslin, and looking directly south, so that I could recline at ease in the red-velvet easy-chair, and see. It had evidently been a young lady’s room: for on the toilette were cut-glass bottles, a plait of brown hair, powders, *rouge-aux-lèvres*, one little bronze slipper, and knickknacks, and I loved her and hated her, though I did not see her anywhere. About half-past eight I sat at the window to watch, all being arranged and ready at my right hand, the candles extinguished in the red room: for the theatre was opened, was opened: and the atmosphere of this earth seemed turned into Hell, and Hell was in my soul.

===Chinese===

由于我在将要住的那座孤屋以南，留出了半径四百码的一片良好的半圆地带，房里没放任何易燃之物，风又这样猛地从北面刮，我便径直把两辆车停在门口，不怕任何损伤：也确实没有出事。随后我登上塔顶，点亮蜡烛，狼吞虎咽地吃起我预先备好的那顿正餐，因为从早晨起我还滴水未进；然后，我用一双颤抖的手、一颗颤抖的心，把那张矮矮的弹簧床的铺陈整理好，预备凌晨时分把自己那副身架扔上去。床的对面墙上，是一扇哥特式的窗，相当大，窗台很低，挂着罂粟花图案的纱帘，正朝南，这样我尽可安躺在红丝绒的安乐椅里，眺望一切。这里显见得原是一位小姐的闺房：妆台上摆着雕花的玻璃瓶、一绺棕发、香粉、唇脂、一只小小的青铜拖鞋，还有种种小玩意儿；我爱她，又恨她，虽然我到处也没见着她。八点半，我在窗前坐下观望，一切都布置停当，就在我右手边，红房间里烛已吹熄：因为戏园开锣了，开锣了：这大地的大气仿佛翻成了地狱，而地狱就在我灵魂里。

===Original===

Soon after midnight there was a sudden and very visible increase in the conflagration. On all hands I began to see blazing structures soar, with grand hurrahs, on high. In fives and tens, in twenties and thirties, all between me and the remote limit of my vision, they leapt, they lingered long, they fell. My spirit more and more felt, and danced﻿—deeper mysteries of sensation, sweeter thrills. I sipped exquisitely, I drew out enjoyment leisurely. Anon, when some more expansive angel of flame would arise from the Pit with steady aspiration, and linger with outspread arms, and burst, I would lift a little from the chair, leaning forward to clap, as at some famous acting; or I would call to them in shouts of cheer, giving them the names of Woman. For now I seemed to see nothing but some bellowing pandemonic universe through crimson glasses, and the air was wildly hot, and my eyeballs like theirs that walk staring in the inner midst of burning fiery furnaces, and my skin itched with a fierce and prickly itch. Anon I touched the chords of the harp to the air of Wagner’s *Walküren-ritt*.
Near three in the morning, I reached the climax of my guilty sweets. My drunken eyelids closed in a luxury of pleasure, and my lips lay stretched in a smile that dribbled; a sensation of dear peace, of almighty power, consoled me: for now the whole area which through streaming tears I surveyed, mustering its ten thousand thunders, and brawling beyond the stars the voice of its southward-rushing torment, billowed to the horizon one grand Atlantic of smokeless and flushing flame; and in it sported and washed themselves all the fiends of Hell, with laughter, shouts, wild flights, and holiday; and I﻿—first of my race﻿—had flashed a signal to the nearer planets.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

午夜刚过，大火骤然又猛又显地旺了起来。四面八方，我看见燃烧的建筑呼啸着、轰然欢呼着腾空而起。三座五座，十座廿座，三十座五十座，在我与视野尽头之间，它们跃起，久久悬停，又颓然倒下。我的灵魂越感越深，翩翩起舞——感觉的秘境更深，快意的震颤更甜。我细细地啜饮，从容地抻长了享受。不一会儿，当某位更恢弘的火焰天使从深渊里挟着沉稳的志向奋然升起，张着双臂悬停，訇然炸开，我便会从椅子里欠身前倾，鼓起掌来，像在看名角的演出；或者朝他们高声喝彩，把女人的名字一个个赐给他们。因为此刻，我隔着绛红的镜片看出去，仿佛只看见一个怒吼的全魔的宇宙；空气灼热得发狂，我的眼球像那些瞪眼行走在燃烧炉膛最深处的人，我的皮肤上起着一阵凶猛刺痛的痒。我不时拨响竖琴的弦，和着瓦格纳《女武神的骑行》的调子。
凌晨近三点，我的罪中之甜达到了顶点。我醉了的双眼在极乐的餍足里阖上，嘴唇咧开一个淌着口水的微笑；一种亲爱安宁之感、一种全能大力之感抚慰着我：因为此刻，我透过簌簌的泪所俯瞰的这整片天地，正集结着它的万钧雷霆，以其南奔的苦痛在星辰之外咆哮，把一片无烟而红透的火焰大西洋翻涌到天边；地狱的群魔全在里面嬉戏、洗浴，大笑，欢呼，狂飞，过节；而我——我种族中的第一人——已向较近的行星们闪出了一道信号。……

===Original===

Those words: “signal to the nearer planets” I wrote nearly fourteen months ago, some days after the destruction of London, I being then on board the old *Boreal*, making for the coast of France: for the night was dark, though calm, and I was afraid of running into some ship, yet not sleepy, so I wrote to occupy my fingers, the ship lying still. The book in which I wrote has been near me: but no impulse to write anything has visited me, till now I continue; not, however, that I have very much to put down.
I had no intention of wearing out my life in lighting fires every morning to warm myself in the inhospitable island of Britain, and set out to France with the view of seeking some palace in the Riviera, Spain, or perhaps Algiers, there, for the present at least, to make my home.
I started from Calais toward the end of April, taking my things along, the first two days by train, and then determining that I was in no hurry, and a petrol motor easier, took one, and maintained a generally southern and somewhat eastern direction, ever-anew astonished at the wildness of the forest vegetation which, within so short a space since the disappearance of man, chokes this pleasant land, even before the definite advent of summer.
After three weeks of very slow travelling﻿—for though I know several countries very well, France with her pavered villages, hilly character, vines, forests, and primeval country-manner, is always new and charming to me﻿—after three weeks I came unexpectedly to a valley which had never entered my head; and the moment that I saw it, I said: “Here I will live,” though I had no idea what it was, for the monastery which I saw did not look at all like a monastery, according to my ideas: but when I searched the map, I discovered that it must be La Chartreuse de Vauclaire in Périgord.
It is my belief that this word “Vauclaire” is nothing else than a corruption of the Latin *Vallis Clara*, or Bright Valley, for *l*’s and *u*’s did interchange about in this way, I remember: *cheval* becoming *chevau(x)* in the plural, like “fool” and “fou,” and the rest: which proves the dear laziness of French people, for the *l* was too much trouble for them to sing, and when they came to *two* *l*’s they quite succumbed, shying that vault, or vo*u*te, and calling it some *y*.

===Chinese===

「向较近的诸行星发信号」这几个字，是我将近十四个月前写的，伦敦毁掉之后没几天；那时我在老「北极号」上，正开往法国海岸：因为夜里虽然无风，天却黑，我怕撞上什么船，又没有睡意，便写点什么占住手指，船停着不动。写这些的那本簿子一直在手边：可再没有动笔的冲动来过我这里，直到现在才续下去；不过，我也并没有多少要记下的。
我没打算在这不好客的不列颠岛上，把命耗在每天早晨生火取暖上，便出发去法国，打算在里维耶拉、西班牙、也许阿尔及尔寻一座宫殿，至少目前在那里安家。
四月下旬我从加莱出发，带着我的东西，头两天坐火车，后来认定我并不赶时间，汽油摩托更便当，便取了一辆，取大体偏南稍偏东的方向行进，一路不断惊异于那野性的草木——人消失还不过这样短的时日，它们便在这片宜人的土地上横生蔓延，甚至不等夏天正式到来。
三个星期的慢行之后——因为虽然我好几个国家都很熟，法国，连同她那石板铺的村子、丘陵的性子、葡萄藤、森林和太古的乡风，于我永远是新鲜迷人的——三个星期后，我意外来到一条从不曾进过我脑子的山谷；一见它我就说：「我就住在这里，」虽然我全然不知它是什么地方，因为我望见的那座修道院，照我的观念一点也不像修道院：可一查地图，我发现它必是佩里戈尔的沃克莱尔加尔都西会修道院。
我相信「Vauclaire」这个词，无非是拉丁文 Vallis Clara（明谷）的讹变，因为 l 和 u 就是这样换来换去的，我记得：cheval 复数成了 chevaux，就像 fool 和 fou，等等：这证明了法国人那可爱的懒惰，因为 l 唱起来太费事，通到一个 l 他们就发懒，遇到两个 l 便干脆投降，把那 voûte（拱顶）的 u 也吞掉，发成别的什么音。

===Original===

But at any rate, this Vauclaire, or Valclear, was well named: for here, if anywhere, is Paradise, and if anyone knew how and where to build and brew liqueurs, it was those good old monks, who followed their Master with entrain in that Cana miracle, and in many other things, I fancy, but aesthetically shirked to say to any mountain: “Be thou removed.”

===Chinese===

但无论如何，这沃克莱尔，或「明谷」，名起得恰当：因为这里，若真有天堂在，便是在这里；而若说有谁懂得怎样选址、怎样酿甜酒，那便是那些善良的老修士：他们追随他们的主，兴致勃勃地行了迦拿的奇迹，我想还在许多别的事上如此，只是从美学上，他们避不肯对任何一座山说：「你挪开吧。」

===Original===

The general hue of the vale is a deep cerulean, resembling that blue of the robes of Albertinelli’s Madonnas; so, at least, it strikes the eye on a clear forenoon of spring or summer. The monastery consists of an oblong space, or garth, around three sides of which stand sixteen small houses, with regular intervals between, all identical, the cells of the fathers; between the oblong space and the cells come the cloisters, with only one opening to the exterior; in the western part of the oblong is a little square of earth under a large cypress-shade, within which, as in a home of peace, it sleeps: and there, straight and slanting, stand little plain black crosses over graves.﻿ ﻿…
To the west of the quadrangle is the church, with the hostelry, and an asphalted court with some trees and a fountain; and beyond, the entrance-gate.
All this stands on a hill of gentle slope, green as grass; and it is backed close against a steep mountainside, of which the tree-trunks are conjectural, for I never saw any, the trees resembling rather one continuous leafy treetop, run out high and far over the extent of the mountain.

===Chinese===

山谷的总体色调是一种深蔚蓝，近似阿尔贝蒂内利笔下圣母衣袍的那种蓝——至少，在春夏晴朗的上午，落在眼里是这样。修道院由一方长方形场地，即庭院，构成，十六座小屋沿其三面立着，间距规整，形制全同，是神父们的静室；长方场地与静室之间是回廊，只开一个口通向外面；长方场地西头，有一小方泥土，掩在大柏树荫下，那东西就安睡在里面，如在安宁的家园：那里，或正或斜，立着一座座朴素的小黑十字架，覆着坟茔。……
回场以西是教堂，连同客栈，一方铺着沥青、种着几棵树、有一眼泉的小院；再往外，便是山门。
这一切立在一座缓坡的小山上，绿如芳草；背后紧贴着一面陡峭的山坡——坡上的树干纯属臆测，因为我从未见过一根：那些树更像一整片绵延不断的树冠，在山坡上远远地、高高地铺展开去。

===Original===

I was there four months, till something drove me away. I do not know what had become of the fathers and brothers, for I only found five, four of whom I took in two journeys in the motor beyond the church of Saint Martial d’Artenset, and left them there; and the fifth remained three weeks with me, for I would not disturb him in his prayer. He was a bearded brother of forty years or thereabouts, who knelt in his cell robed and hooded in all his phantom white: for in no way different from whatever is most phantom, visionary and eerie must a procession of these people have seemed by gloaming, or dark night. This particular brother knelt, I say, in his small chaste room, glaring upward at his Christ, who hung long-armed in a little recess between the side of three narrow bookshelves and a projection of the wall; and under the Christ a gilt and blue Madonna; the books on the three shelves few, leaning different ways. His right elbow rested on a square plain table, at which was a wooden chair; behind him, in a corner, the bed: a bed all enclosed in dark boards, a broad perpendicular board along the foot, reaching the ceiling, a horizontal board at the side over which he got into bed, another narrower one like it at the ceiling for fringe and curtain, and another perpendicular one hiding the pillow, making the clean bed within a very shady and cosy little den, on the wall of this den being another smaller Christ and a little picture. On the perpendicular board at the foot hung two white garments, and over a second chair at the bedside another: all very neat and holy. He was a large stern man, blond as corn, but with some red, too, in his hairy beard; and appalling was the significance of those eyes that prayed, and the long-drawn cavity of those saffron cheeks. I cannot explain to myself my deep reverence for this man; but I had it, certainly. Many of the others, it is clear, had fled: but not he: and to the near-marching cloud he opposed the Cross, holding one real as the other﻿—he alone among many. For Christianity was an élite religion, in which all were called, but few chosen, differing from Mohammedanism and Buddhism, which grasped and conquered all within their reach: the effect of Christ rather resembling Plato’s and Dante’s, it would seem: but Muhammad’s more like Homer’s and Shakespeare’s.

===Chinese===

我在那里待了四个月，直到什么东西把我赶走。神父们和修士们遭了什么，我不知道，因为我只找到五个，其中四个我分两趟用摩托运过圣马夏尔·达尔泰内教堂，丢在了那里；第五个同我一起待了三个星期，因为我不肯打扰他的祈祷。他是个蓄须的修士，四十岁上下，跪在他的小间里，一袭幻影似的白袍白兜帽，穿戴齐全：因为黄昏或黑夜里，这一班人排成行列走过，那模样同任何最幻影、最灵异、最森然的东西都没有两样。我说，这位修士跪在他那间素净的小屋里，瞪眼仰望着他的基督——那像悬在三条窄书架侧面与一处墙凸之间的小龛里，双臂很长；基督像下是一尊描金着蓝的圣母；三格架上的书寥寥，东倒西歪。他的右肘支在一张方方的素面桌上，桌边一把木椅；他身后角落里，是床：一张四围全用深色木板封起的床，脚头一块宽板直抵天花板，侧面一块横板，他从那上面上床，头顶又一块稍窄的同类横板权作缘饰和帐幔，另一块立板遮住枕头，把里面干净的床弄成一个十分荫凉惬意的小窝，窝的墙上又有一尊小些的基督和一幅小画。脚头那块立板上挂着两件白色衣袍，床头第二把椅背上又搭着一件：一切都整洁而圣洁。他是个高大严厉的人，头发金黄如麦，毛茸茸的胡须里却也捻着些红；那双祈祷的眼睛的含义，那两颊腊黄色拉长的凹陷，骇人。我说不清自己对这人深深的敬意从何而来；可它确确实实在。其余的人，显然大半逃了：可他不：对那逼近的云，他以十字架相对，认定这一个同那一个一样真实——众人之中，唯他一人。因为基督教原是一种精英的宗教，人人蒙召，蒙选者寡，同伊斯兰教和佛教不同，那两者是把够得着的一切都抓取、都征服：基督的效应，看来更像柏拉图和但丁的：而穆罕默德的，更像荷马和莎士比亚的。

===Original===

It was my way to plant at the portal the big, carved chair from the chancel on the hot days, and rest my soul, refusing to think of anything, drowsing and smoking for hours. All down there in the plain waved gardens of delicious fruit about the prolonged silver thread of the river Isle, whose course winds loitering quite near the foot of the monastery-slope. This slope dominates a tract of distance that is not only vast, but looks immense, although the horizon is bounded by a semicircle of low hills, rather too stiff and uniform for perfect beauty; the interval of plain being occupied by yellow ploughed lands which were never sown, weedy now, and crossed and recrossed by vividly-green ribbons of vine, with stretches of pale-green lucerne, orchards, and the white village of Monpont near the railway, all embowered, the Isle drawing its mercurial streams through the village-meadow, which is dark with shades of oaks: and to have played there a boy, and used it familiarly from birth as one’s own hand or foot, must have been very sweet and homely; after this, the river divides, and takes the shape of a heart; and very far away are visible the grey banks of the Gironde. On the semicircle of hills, when there was little distance-mist, I saw the ruins of some seigneurial château, for the seigneurs, too, knew where to build; and to my left, between a clump of oaks and an avenue of poplars, the bell-tower of the village﻿—church of Saint Martial d’Artenset﻿—a very ancient type of tower, I believe, and common in France, rather ponderous, consisting of a square mass with a smaller square mass stuck on, the latter having large Gothic windows; and behind me the west face of the monastery-church, over the door being the statue of Saint Bruno.

===Chinese===

大热天里，我的习惯是把圣坛上那把雕花大椅搬到大门洞下，让灵魂歇着，什么也不肯想，打盹、抽烟，一几个钟头。下面平原上，可口的果树之园层层围着伊勒河那条延绵的银线，河道蜿蜒逶迤，紧挨着修道院坡的坡脚。这道坡俯瞰的那片远景，不但辽阔，而且看去浩瀚，虽然天际只围着一道半圆的低冈，冈身稍嫌僵硬划一，算不得十全的美；中间的平原上铺着黄的犁地——从没播过种，如今长满草——一道道鲜绿的葡萄带纵横其间，夹着成片的淡绿苜蓿、果园，和铁路边那座白村子蒙蓬，全都掩在树荫里；伊勒河把它水银似的几股水流穿过村边的草场，草场因橡树的浓荫而幽暗：在那儿做过孩子，从生下来就把它当自己的手脚一样使唤惯了，想必又甜美又家常；再往前，河分了岔，摆出一个心形；极远处望得见吉伦特灰色的两岸。那道半圆的冈上，天没有多少远景薄雾时，我望得见某座领主城堡的废墟，因为领主们也懂得在哪里盖房子；在我左边，一丛橡树与一条白杨林荫道之间，是村子——圣马夏尔·达尔泰内——教堂的钟楼：一种极古的塔型，我想，在法国很常见，相当笨重，一座方墩上再摞一座小些的方墩，小墩开着大扇哥特窗；而我背后，是修道院教堂的西立面，门上方立着圣布鲁诺的像。

===Original===

Well, one morning after four months, I opened my eyes in my cell to the piercing consciousness that I had burned Monpont overnight: and so overcome was I with regret for this poor inoffensive little place, that for two days, hardly eating, I paced between the oak and walnut pews of the nave, massive stalls they are, separated by grooved Corinthian pilasters, wondering what was to become of me, and if I was not already mad; and there are some little angels with extraordinarily human Greuze-like faces, supporting the nerves of the apse, which, after a time, every time I passed them, seemed conscious of me and my existence there; and the woodwork which ornaments the length of the nave, and of the choir also, elaborate with carved marguerites and roses, here and there took in my eyes significant forms from certain points of view; and there is a partition﻿—for the nave is divided into two chapels, one for the brothers and one for the fathers, I conclude﻿—and in this partition a massive door, which yet looks quite light and graceful, carved with oak and acanthus leaves, and every time I passed through I had the impression that the door was a sentient thing, subconscious of me; and the delicate Italian-Renaissance brick vault which springs from the vast nave seemed to look upon me with a gloomy knowledge of me, and of the heart within me; and at about four in the afternoon of the second day, after pacing the church for hours, I fell down at one of the two altars near that carved door of the screen, praying God to have mercy upon my soul; and in the very midst of my praying, I was up and away, the devil in me, and I got into the motor, and did not come back to Vauclaire for another month, and came leaving great tracts of burned desolation behind me, towns and forests, Bordeaux burned, Lebourne burned, Bergerac burned.

===Chinese===

唉，四个月后的一个早晨，我在小间里睁开眼，一阵锥心的知觉：我夜里把蒙蓬烧了。我为这个可怜无辜的小地方悔恨得那样厉害，以致两天里几乎不吃东西，在中殿那些橡木和胡桃木的长凳间来回踱步——都是厚重的席位，隔着带凹槽的科林斯壁柱分开——心里嘀咕我究竟会变成什么，我是不是已经疯了；后殿拱肋上有几个小天使，面孔出奇地有人味，像格勒兹画的；过了一阵，每次我从旁走过，他们仿佛都意识到了我，意识到了我在此的存在；那装点中殿全长、连唱诗班席也一样、雕满雏菊和玫瑰的木作，从某些角度看去，也不时在我眼里现出有含义的形状；还有一道隔屏——因为中殿分作两间小堂，我断定一间属修士、一间属神父——隔屏上一扇厚重的门，看去却又轻盈优雅，雕着橡叶和莨苕叶，每次我穿门而过，总觉得那门是一个有知觉的东西，暗暗晓得我；从中殿拔起的精巧的意大利文艺复兴式砖拱顶，仿佛带着一种阴郁的知悉，知我、知我心里的东西，俯视着我；到第二天下午四点光景，在教堂里踱了几个钟头之后，我扑倒在隔屏那雕花门旁两座祭坛中的一座前，求上帝怜悯我的灵魂；可就在祈祷到一半，我一跃而起，跑掉了，心里那个魔鬼，我上了摩托，一个月没有再回沃克莱尔；回来时，身后丢下大片大片烧焦的荒凉，城镇和森林，波尔多烧了，勒布尔讷烧了，贝尔热拉克烧了。

===Original===

I returned to Vauclaire, for it seemed now my home; and there I experienced a true, a deep repentance; and I humbled myself before my Maker. And while in this state, sitting one bright day in front of the monastery-gate, something said to me: “You will never be a good man, nor permanently escape Hell and Frenzy, unless you have an aim in life, devoting yourself heart and soul to some great work, which will exact all your science, your thought, your ingenuity, your knowledge of modern things, your strength of body and will, your skill of head and hand: otherwise you are bound to succumb. Do this, therefore, beginning, not tomorrow nor this afternoon, but now: for though no man will see your work, there is still the Almighty God, who is also something, in His way: and He will see how you strive, and try, and groan: and perhaps, seeing, He may have mercy upon you.”

===Chinese===

我回到了沃克莱尔，因为如今它似乎已是我的家；在那里，我经历了一场真切而深切的忏悔；我在造物主面前卑抑了自己。正当这心境，一个晴朗的日子，我坐在修道院山门前，有东西对我说：「你永远成不了一个好人，也永远逃不出地狱与癫狂，除非你有一个人生的目标，把全副身心献给一项伟大的事业，它将征用你全部的科学、你的思想、你的巧思、你对现代事物的知识、你的体力与意志、你手脑的技能：否则你必沉沦。所以，这就去做，不是明天，不是下午，而是现在：因为虽然无人将看见你的事业，还有全能的上帝在，按祂的方式，祂也算得一位：祂会看见你怎样奋斗、尝试、呻吟：看见了，或许祂会怜悯你。」

===Original===

In this way arose the idea of the Palace﻿—an idea, indeed, which had entered my brain before, but merely as a bombastic and visionary outcome of my raving moods: now, however, in a very different way, soberly, and soon concerning itself with details, difficulties, means, limitations, and every kind of practical matter-of-fact; and every obstruction which, one by one, I foresaw was, one by one, as the days passed, overborne by the vigour with which that thought, rapidly becoming a mania, possessed me. After a week of incessant meditation, I decided Yes: and I said: I will build a palace, which shall be both a palace and a temple: the first human temple worthy the King of Heaven, and the only human palace worthy the King of Earth.

===Chinese===

宫殿的构想便这样萌生了——这念头其实从前也曾入过我的脑，却只是我狂乱心绪里一种浮夸的空想：如今却以截然不同的方式，冷静地，并且很快便操持起细节、困难、办法、限度，和一切切实的实务；我预见的每一重障碍，随着时日流转，都一重一重地，被那念头攫住我时日益增长的狂热劲头碾了过去。经过一个星期不间断的冥思，我决定了：干。我说：我要造一座宫殿，它既是宫殿，也是神庙：第一座配得上天国之王的人间神庙，唯一一座配得起地上之王的世间宫殿。

===Original===

After this decision I remained at Vauclaire another week, a very different man to the lounger it had seen, strenuous, converted, humble, making plans of this and of that, of the detail, and of the whole, drawing, multiplying, dividing, adding, conic sections and the rule-of-three, totting up the period of building, which came out at a little over twelve years, estimating the quantities of material, weight and bulk, my nights full of nightmare as to the *sort*, deciding as to the size and structure of the crane, forge, and workshop, and the necessarily-limited weights of their component parts, making a list of over 2,400 objects, and finally, up to the third week after my departure from Vauclaire, skimming through the topography of nearly the whole earth, before fixing upon the island of Imbros for my site.

===Chinese===

决定之后，我在沃克莱尔又待了一个星期，同先前那个闲荡汉判若两人：发奋，皈依，谦卑，为这个为那个制定方案，为细节，为整体，画图，乘除加减，圆锥曲线和三率法，把工期加出来——算得十二年多一点——估算材料的数量、重量与体积，夜里尽做关于材料*品类*的噩梦，定下起重机、锻炉和工场的大小与结构，以及其部件必然受限的重量，开列出一张两千四百多件物品的清单；最后，直到离开沃克莱尔后的第三个星期，我几乎把全世界的地形都翻检了一遍，才选定印布罗斯岛做我的基址。

===Original===

I returned to England, and, once more, to the hollow windows and strewn streets of black, burned-out and desolate London: for its bank-vaults, etc., contained the necessary complement of the gold brought from Paris, and then lying in the *Speranza* at Dover; nor had I sufficient familiarity with French industries and methods to find, even with the aid of *Bottins*, one half of the 4,000 odd objects which I had now catalogued. My ship was the *Speranza*, which brought me from Havre, for at Calais, to which I first went, I could find nothing suitable for all purposes, the *Speranza* being an American yacht, very palatially fitted, three-masted, air-driven, with a carrying capacity of 2,000 tons, Tobin-bronzed, in good condition, containing sixteen interacting tanks, with a five-block pulley-arrangement amidships that enables me to lift very considerable weights without the aid of the hoisting air-engine, high in the water, sharp, handsome, containing a few tons only of sand-ballast, and needing when I found her only three days’ work at the waterline and engines to make her decent and fit. I threw out her dead, backed her from the Outer to the Inner Basin to my train on the quai, took in the twenty-three hundredweight bags of gold, and the half-ton of amber, and with this alone went to Dover, thence to Canterbury by motor, and thence in a long train, with a store of dynamite from the Castle for blasting possible obstructions, to London: meaning to make Dover my depot, and the London rails my thoroughfare from all parts of the country.
Instead of three months, as I had calculated, it took me nine: a harrowing slavery. I had to blast no less than forty-three trains from the path of my loaded wagons, several times blasting away the metals as well, and then having to travel hundreds of yards without metals: for the labour of kindling the obstructing engines, to shunt them down sidings perhaps distant, was a thing which I would not undertake. However, all’s well that ends well, though if I had it to go through again, certainly I should not.

===Chinese===

我回了英格兰，又一次回到那黑洞洞的窗和遍地狼藉的、烧焦荒凉的伦敦：因为它的银行金库等等，藏着从巴黎运来的那批黄金所需的补充，此刻黄金正躺在多佛的「斯佩兰扎号」上；何况我对法国的产业和方法不够熟，即便有《博坦名录》帮忙，如今已编入清单的四千多件物件，一半也未必找得齐。我的船是「斯佩兰扎号」，她从勒阿弗尔载我来：因为在我先到的加莱，我找不出一条样样合用的船；「斯佩兰扎号」是一艘美国游艇，装修得极华丽，三桅，空气动力，载重两千吨，托宾青铜的船壳，状况良好，有十六只联动空气罐，船腹中有一套五轮的滑轮装置，不用起吊气压机我也能吊起相当重的物件；她吃水浅，船形尖削漂亮，只装着几吨沙压舱；我找到她时，只需在水线和机器上干三天活，便能使她像样、合用。我把她的死人扔出去，把她从外池倒进内池，靠到码头边我的列车旁，装上两千三百袋（每袋一英担）黄金和半吨琥珀，单带着这些去了多佛，再从那里乘摩托到坎特伯雷，再挂上一列长长的火车，带着从城堡取来的一批达纳炸药，预备炸开可能的障碍，进了伦敦：打算把多佛作我的总库，把伦敦的铁路作我从全国各地来往的大道。
原算三个月的活，花了我九个月：一场剐心的苦役。我不得不从满载货车的路上炸掉不下四十三列火车，有几次连铁轨一并炸飞，只好几百码没有铁轨也硬开过去：因为把挡路的机车生了火、调进也许很远的侧线这种活，我是不肯干的。不过，结局好便是好，虽然这事若要我再从头来一遍，我断断不肯。

===Original===

The *Speranza* is now lying seven miles off Cape Roca, a heavy mist on the still water, this being the 19th of June at 10 in the night: no wind, no moon: cabin full of mist: and I pretty listless and disappointed, wondering in my heart why I was such a fool as to take all that trouble, nine long servile months, my good God, and now seriously thinking of throwing the whole vile thing to the devil; she pretty deep in the water, pregnant with the palace. When the thirty-three﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

「斯佩兰扎号」此刻正停在离罗卡角七英里的海上，静水上罩着一层浓雾，今天是六月十九日，夜里十点：无风，无月：舱里满是雾：我颇觉无精打采，满怀失望，心里嘀咕自己怎么蠢到这个地步，吃了那许多苦，九个月漫长的奴工，我的好上帝，此刻竟认真想把这一整包破烂丢给魔鬼了；她吃水颇深，怀着一座宫殿。等到那三十三……

===Original===

Those words: “when the thirty-three” were written by me over seventeen years since﻿—long years﻿—seventeen in number, nor have I now any idea to what they refer. The book in which I wrote I had lost in the cabin of the *Speranza*, and yesterday, returning to Imbros from an hour’s aimless cruise, discovered it there behind a chest.
I find now considerable difficulty in guiding the pencil, and these few lines now written have quite an odd look, like the handwriting of a man not very proficient in the art: it is seventeen years, seventeen, seventeen﻿ ﻿… ah! And the expression of my ideas is not fluent either: I have to think for the word a minute, and I should not be surprised if the spelling of some of them is queer. My brain has been thinking inarticulately perhaps, all these years: and the English words and letters, as they now stand written, have rather an improbable and foreign air to me, as a Greek or Russian book might look to a man who has not so long been learning those languages as to forget the impossibly foreign impression received from them on the first day of tackling them. Or perhaps it is only my fancy: for that I have fancies I know.
But what to write? The history of those seventeen years could not be put down, my good God: at least, it would take me seventeen more to do it.

===Chinese===

「待那三十三」这几句字，是我十七年多前写下的——漫长的岁月——整整十七年，如今我全然想不起它们指的是什么了。写这些字的那本簿子，我丢失在「斯佩兰扎」号的船舱里，昨天，我出海漫无目的地荡了一个钟头回印布罗斯，却在舱里一只箱子后面发现了它。
我现在握笔已相当吃力，方才写下的这几行，模样颇为古怪，像是一个不谙此道的人的笔迹：十七年了，十七年，十七年……啊！我的思想表达也不流利了：一个词要想上一分钟，其中有些拼写得古怪，我也不会奇怪。我的脑子这些年来思考得含混而不成言语：此刻写下的这些英文字母，在我看来竟带着一种未必可能的异邦之气，就像一本希腊文或俄文书，在一个学这两种语言的时候还不够久、还没忘掉初学第一天那种不可思议的陌生印象的人眼里那样。又或者，这只是我的幻觉：因为我知道，我素来多幻。
可写什么呢？那十七年的历史是记不下来的，我的老天：至少，再给我十七年也未必写得完。

===Original===

If I were to detail the building of the palace alone, and how it killed me nearly, and how I twice fled from it, and had to return, and became its bounden slave, and dreamed of it, and grovelled before it, and prayed, and raved, and rolled; and how I forgot to make provision on the west side for the contraction and expansion of the gold in the colder weather and the heats of summer, and had to break down nine months’ work, and how I cursed Thee, how I cursed Thee; and how the lake of wine evaporated faster than the conduits replenished it, and the three journeys which I had to take to Constantinople for shiploads of wine, and my frothing despairs, till I had the thought of placing the reservoir in the platform; and how I had then to break down the south side of the platform to the very bottom, and of the monthlong nightmare of terror that I had lest the south side of the palace would undergo subsidence; and how the petrol failed, and of the three-weeks’ search for petrol along the coast; and how, after list-rubbing all the jet, I found that I had forgotten the necessary rouge for polishing; and how, in the third year, I found the fluate, which I had for waterproofing the pores of the platform-stone, nearly all leaked away in the *Speranza*’s hold, and I had to get silicate of soda at Gallipoli; and how, after two years’ observation, I had to come to the conclusion that the lake was leaking, and discovered that this Imbros sand was not suitable for mixing with the skin of Portland cement which covered the cement concrete, and had to substitute sheet-bitumen in three places; and how I did all, all for the sake of God, thinking: “I will work, and be a good man, and cast Hell from me: and when I see it stand finished, it will be an Altar and a Testimony to me, and I shall find peace, and be well”: and how I have been cheated﻿—seventeen years, long years of my life﻿—for there is no God; and how my plasterers’-hair failed me, and I had to use flock, hessian, scrym, wadding, wood-street paving-blocks, and whatever I could find, for filling the interspaces between the platform cross-walls; and of the espagnolette bolts, how a number of them mysteriously disappeared, as if snatched to Hell by harpies, and I had to make them; and how the crane-chain would not reach two of the silver-panel castings when they were finished, and they were too heavy for me to lift, and t

===Chinese===

倘若单把建宫殿的事细说一遍——它怎样险些要了我的命，我怎样两次从它跟前逃走，又不得不回去，成了它俯首帖耳的奴隶，梦里也梦它，在它面前匍匐，祷告，狂言，打滚；我怎样忘了给西面的黄金留出寒天收缩、暑天膨胀的余地，只好把九个月的活计推倒重来，我怎样诅咒你，怎样诅咒你；酒湖蒸发得比导管补给还快，我怎样三次远赴君士坦丁堡运整船的酒，怎样口吐白沫地绝望，直到想起把储池放进台基里；随后我又不得不把台基南面拆到最底层，担惊受怕整整一个月，夜夜都是噩梦，生怕宫殿的南面会沉降；汽油怎样耗尽，我怎样沿海岸找了三个星期的汽油；我怎样把煤精全都用清单擦了一遍，才发现忘了备抛光必需的红粉；第三年，我怎样发现用来给台基石材毛孔防水的氟酸盐在「斯佩兰扎」号舱里漏掉了几乎全部，只好去加利波利弄硅酸钠；经过两年的观察，我怎样不得不认定酒湖在渗漏，查明这种印布罗斯的砂子不宜拌进包着水泥混凝土的那层波特兰水泥皮，只好在三处改铺沥青卷材；我怎样做这一切、一切，都为了上帝的缘故，心想：「我要劳作，要做好人，把地狱从我身上撵出去：等我看见它落成，它便是我的祭坛，我的见证，我便得安宁，便得痊愈」：而我怎样被骗了——十七年，我生命里漫长的岁月——因为根本没有上帝；抹灰的麻刀怎样用尽，我只好拿短毛屑、粗麻布、稀纱布、软填料、木铺路砖，凡找得着的，去填台基横墙之间的空隙；还有那些长插销螺栓，一批怎样无端失踪，仿佛被鸟妖劫进了地狱，我只好自己打造；起重链怎样够不着两块完工的银面板，它们又重得我抬不动，还有

===Original===

he wringing of the hands of my despair, and my biting of the earth, and the transport of my fury; and how, for a whole wild week, I searched in vain for the textbook which describes the ambering process; and how, when all was nearly over, in the blasting away of the forge and crane with dynamite, a long crack appeared down the gold of the east platform-steps, and how I would not be consoled, but mourned and mourned; and how, in spite of all my tribulations, it was sweetly interesting to watch my power slowly grow from the first feeble beginnings of the landing of materials and unloading them from the motor, a hundredweight at a time, till I could swing four tons﻿—see the solid metals flow﻿—enjoy the gliding sounds of the handle, crankshaft, and system of levers, forcing inwards the mould-end, and the upper and lower plungers, for pressing the material﻿—build at ease in a travelling-cage﻿—and watch from my hut-door through sleepless hours, under the electric moonlight of this land, the three piles of gold stones, the silver panels, the two-foot squares of jet, and be comforted; and how the putty-wash﻿—but it is past, it is past: and not to live over again that vulgar nightmare of means and ends have I taken to this writing again﻿—but to put down something else, if I dare.

===Chinese===

我绝望里绞扭的双手，我啃啮泥土，我狂怒的迷乱；整整疯狂的一个星期，我怎样遍寻那本讲琥珀化工艺的教科书而不得；万事将成的时候，用炸药炸掉锻炉和起重机，东面台基台阶的黄金上又怎样裂下一条长缝，我怎样不肯受安慰，哀恸了又哀恸；而尽管有这千般苦难，看我自己的力量从最初卸运材料、从机车上一次一英担地搬下来的微末起步，慢慢长成，直到我能甩动四吨——看坚硬的金属流动——听手柄、曲轴和杠杆系统迫近模端、迫上下冲头压料时那滑润的声音——在移动吊笼里从容建造——在我茅屋门口，在这片土地的电月光下，彻夜无眠地望着那三堆金石、银面板、两英尺见方的煤精块，得着安慰，又是怎样一桩甜趣盎然的事；还有那油灰浆——可都过去了，都过去了：我重新提笔，并非要再把那手段与目的的庸俗噩梦重活一遍——而是要记下别的什么，如果我敢。

===Original===

Seventeen years, my good God, of that delusion! I could write down no sort of explanation for all those groans and griefs, at which a reasoning being would not shriek with laughter. I should have lived at ease in some palace of the Middle-Orient, and burned my cities: but no, I must be “a good man”﻿—vain thought. The words of a wild madman, that preaching man in England who prophesied what happened, were with me, where he says: “the defeat of Man is *His* defeat”; and I said to myself: “Well, the last man shall not be quite a fiend, just to spite That Other.” And I worked and groaned, saying: “I will be a good man, and burn nothing, nor utter aught unseemly, nor debauch myself, but choke back the blasphemies that Those Others shriek through my throat, and build and build, with moils and groans.” And it was Vanity: though I do love the house, too, I love it well, for it is my home on the waste earth.
I had calculated to finish it in twelve years, and I should undoubtedly have finished it in fourteen, instead of in sixteen and seven months, but one day, when the south, north, and east platform-steps were already finished﻿—it was in the July of the third year, and near sunset﻿—as I left off work, instead of going to the tent where my dinner lay ready, I walked down to the ship﻿—most strangely﻿—in a daft, mechanical sort of way, without saying a word to myself, an evil-meaning smile of malice on my lips; and at midnight I was lying off Mitylene, thirty miles to the south, having bid, as I thought, a last farewell to all those toils. I was going to burn Athens.
I did not, however: but kept on my way westward round Cape Matapan, intending to destroy the forests and towns of Sicily, if I found there a suitable motor for travelling, for I had not been at the pains to take the motor on board at Imbros; otherwise I would ravage parts of southern Italy. But when I came thereabouts, I was confronted with an awful horror: for no southern Italy was there, and no Sicily was there, unless a small new island, probably not five miles long, was Sicily; and nothing else I saw, save the still-smoking crater of Stromboli. I cruised northward, searching for land, and for a long time would not believe the evidence of the instruments, thinking that they wilfully misled me, or I stark mad.

===Chinese===

十七年，我的老天，守着那场幻妄！对那一切的呻吟与悲苦，我写不出任何像样的解释，换了明白人，只会尖声大笑。我本可以在中东哪座宫殿里安享清福，烧我的城市，可不，我偏要做「好人」——虚妄之念。英格兰那个预言了这一切的布道疯子，他的话一直跟着我，他说：「人的失败，便是*祂*的失败」；我便对自己说：「好吧，最后一个人也不该全然是个恶魔，偏要气一气那一位。」于是我劳作、呻吟，说：「我要做好人，什么都不烧，不出恶言，不自我糟蹋，咽下那些借我的喉咙尖叫的『他们』的渎神之言，只是建造、建造，含辛茹苦，一路呻吟。」而这是虚妄：虽然我也爱这座殿，爱得深，因为它是我在这片荒凉大地上的家。
我原算定十二年完工，无疑本可在十四年内完成，而不是十六年零七个月，可有一天，南、北、东三面台基台阶都已落成——那是第三年的七月，将近日落——我收了工，却没有走向备好晚饭的帐篷，反而往船边走去——极其蹊跷——带着一种痴痴的、机械的样子，心里一句话也没有，唇边挂着一丝不怀好意的狞笑；半夜时分，我已泊在以南三十英里的米蒂利尼外海，自以为向那一切劳苦永远道了别。我要去烧雅典。
然而我没有烧：而是继续西行，绕过马塔潘角，打算毁掉西西里的森林和城镇——倘若我在那里找得到一台合适的行路机车，因为我在印布罗斯没费那个事把机车装船；不然就蹂躏南意大利的部分地方。可等我到了那一带，迎头撞上一场骇人的恐怖：那里根本没有南意大利，也没有西西里——除非一座长约不足五英里的新小岛算是西西里；此外我所见的，只有斯特龙博利还在冒烟的火山口。我向北巡航，搜寻陆地，好久不肯相信仪表的证据，只当它们成心骗我，再不然就是我彻底疯了。

===Original===

But no: no Italy was there, till I came to the latitude of Naples, it, too, having disappeared, engulfed, engulfed, all that stretch. From this monstrous thing I received so solemn a shock and mood of awe, that the evil mind in me was quite chilled and quelled: for it was, and is, my belief that a widespread rearrangement of the earth’s surface is being purposed, and in all that drama, O my God, how shall *I* be found?
However, I went on my way, but more leisurely, not daring for a long time to do anything, lest I might offend anyone; and, in this foolish cowering mind, coasted all the western coast of Spain and France during five weeks, in that prolonged intensity of calm weather which now alternates with storms that transcend all thought, till I came again to Calais: and there, for the first time, landed.
Here I would no longer contain myself, but burned; and that magnificent stretch of forest that lay between Agincourt and Abbéville, covering five square miles, I burned; and Abbéville I burned; and Amiens I burned; and three forests between Amiens and Paris I burned; and Paris I burned; burning and burning during four months, leaving behind me smoking districts, a long tract of ravage, like some being of the Pit that blights where pass his flaming wings.

===Chinese===

可不是：再没有意大利，直到我到了那不勒斯的纬度——连它也无影无踪了，沉没了，沉没了，整片整片地沉没了。这件骇人听闻的事给了我那样庄严的一震、那样一腔敬畏，我体内的邪心竟被镇住、被压服了：因为我一向相信、如今也相信，大地表面的一场广泛重排正在进行，而在那整出大戏里，哦我的上帝，*我*将被怎样处置？
然而我继续赶路，只是更从容了，好长时间不敢有所举动，唯恐冒犯了哪一位；就怀着这愚蠢畏缩的心思，我用五个星期巡完了西班牙和法兰西的全部西海岸——正值如今同超乎想象的狂风暴雨交替出现的漫长的酷烈平静——直到重新来到加来：并在那里头一回上了岸。
到了这里我再也按捺不住，放火烧了：阿金库尔与阿布维尔之间那绵延五平方英里的壮丽森林，我烧了；阿布维尔，我烧了；亚眠，我烧了；亚眠与巴黎之间的三片森林，我烧了；巴黎，我烧了；烧啊烧，一连烧了四个月，身后留下条条冒烟的焦土，一道长长的浩劫之痕，像深渊里来的某个精怪，烈焰之翼掠过哪里，哪里便焦枯。

===Original===

This of city-burning has now become a habit with me more enchaining﻿—and infinitely more debased﻿—than ever was opium to the smoker, or alcohol to the drunkard. I count it among the prime necessaries of my life: it is my brandy, my bacchanal, my secret sin. I have burned Calcutta, Peking, and San Francisco. In spite of the restraining influence of this palace, I have burned and burned. I have burned two hundred cities and countrysides. Like Leviathan disporting himself in the sea, so I have rioted in this earth.

===Chinese===

这烧城的勾当，如今成了我一种戒不掉的习惯——比鸦片之于烟客、酒精之于酒徒更束人，也更无限地更下贱。我把它算作我生命的头等必需：它是我的白兰地，我的酒神祭，我的隐秘之罪。我烧了加尔各答，烧了北京，烧了旧金山。尽管有这座宫殿约東着，我仍旧烧了又烧。我烧了两百座城池和乡野。像利维坦在海里翻腾戏耍，我便这样在大地上纵欲横行。

===Original===

After an absence of six months, I returned to Imbros: for I was for looking again upon the work which I had done, that I might mock myself for all that unkingly grovelling: and when I saw it, standing there as I had left it, frustrate and forlorn, and waiting its maker’s hand, some pity and instinct to build took me﻿—for something of God was in Man﻿—and I fell upon my knees, and spread my arms to God, and was converted, promising to finish the palace, with prayers that as I built so He would build my soul, and save the last man from the enemy. And I set to work that day to list-rub the last few dalles of the jet.

===Chinese===

离别六个月之后，我回了伊姆罗兹：因为我想再看一看自己干下的那件活计，好就那一身有失王者身份的匍匐爬行自嘲一番；可我一见它——还照我离开时那样立在那里，挫败而孤零，等着它的造主之手——一阵怜惜和一种建造的本能便攝住了我——因为人身上原有几分神性——我便双膝跪倒，向上帝张开双臂，皈依了，许愿要把宫殿造完，祈求祂怎样叫我营造，也怎样营造我的灵魂，救这最后的人脱离仇敌。当天我便动手，把突堤上最后几块石板磨平。

===Original===

I did not leave Imbros after that during four years, except for occasional brief trips to the coast﻿—to Kilid-Bahr, Gallipoli, Lapsaki, Gamos, Rodosto, Erdek, Erekli, or even once to Constantinople and Scutari﻿—if I happened to want anything, or if I was tired of work: but without once doing the least harm to anything, but containing my humours, and fearing my Maker. And full of peaceful charm were those little cruises through this Levantic world, which, truly, is rather like a light sketch in watercolours done by an angel than like the dun real earth; and full of self-satisfaction and pious contentment would I return to Imbros, approved of my conscience, for that I had surmounted temptation, and lived tame and stainless.
I had set up the southern of the two closed-lotus pillars, and the platform-top was already looking as lovely as heaven, with its alternate two-foot squares of pellucid gold and pellucid jet, when I noticed one morning that the *Speranza*’s bottom was really now too foul, and the whim took me then and there to leave all, and clean her as far as I could. I at once went on board, descended to the hold, took off my *sudeyree*, and began to shift the ballast over to starboard, so as to tilt up her port bottom to the scraper. This was wearying labour, and about noon I was sitting on a bag, resting in the almost darkness, when something seemed to whisper to me these words: You dreamed last night that there is an old Chinaman alive in Peking. Horridly I started: I *had* dreamed something of the sort, but, from the moment of waking, till then, had forgotten it: and I leapt livid to my feet.
I cleaned no *Speranza* that day, nor for four days did I anything, but sat on the cabin-house and mused, my supporting palm among the hairy draperies of my chin: for the thought of such a thing, if it could by any possibility be true, was detestable as death to me, changing the colour of the sun, and the whole aspect of the world: and anon, at the outrage of that thing, my brow would flush with wrath, and my eyes blaze: till, on the fourth afternoon, I said to myself: “That old Chinaman in Peking is likely to get burned to death, I think, or blown to the clouds!”
So, a second time, on the 4th March, the poor palace was left to build itself.

===Chinese===

那以后整整四年，我没有离开伊姆罗兹，只偶尔往海岸作短短的出行——去基利德巴赫尔、加利波利、拉普萨基、加莫斯、罗多斯托、埃尔德克、埃雷克利，甚至有一次去了君士坦丁堡和斯库塔里——若是碰巧要用什么，或是做烦了工：可我不曾再损坏一草一木，只是括着自己的性子，敬畏着我的造主。穿行这黎凡特世界的小小巡游，满是安宁的魅力：这地方，实在说，与其像土褐色的真实大地，不如像天使画的一幅轻盈的水彩速写；而我回到伊姆罗兹时，总是满怀自得与度诚的满足，良心认可我，因为我胜过了试探，活得驯顺而无垢。
我已把两根闭莲柱中南边的那根立了起来，平台顶已开始美如天庭：透明的金与透明的黑玉两英尺见方，彼此相间——就在这时，一天早晨，我发现「斯佩兰扎号」的船底实在积垢太厚，一时兴起，便当场撂下一切，去把她尽可能清理一番。我立刻上船，下到底舱，脱掉「苏代里」，开始把压舱物移向右舷，好把她左舷的船底翘起刮蹭。这是累人的活，约莫中午，我坐在一只袋子上，在近乎全黑中歇息，忽然有什么仿佛向我耳语了这样一句：你昨夜梦见北京还有一个活着的中国老头。我骇然一惊：我确实梦见过类似的东西，可从醒来到方才，一直忘了：我面无人色地跳了起来。
那天我没有再清理「斯佩兰扎号」，一连四天我什么也没干，只是坐在舱棚上沉思，手掌托在我下巴那毛茸茸的须帘里：因为这样一件事，倘若有一丝可能是真的，于我便可憎如死，会改变太阳的颜色、整个世界的面目；而不时地，为着那东西的污辱，我的额头会烧起怒火，双眼冒火：直到第四天下午，我对自己说：「北京那中国老头，我看多半会被烧死，要不就被炸上天！」
于是，第二次，三月四日，那可怜的宫殿又被撇下，自己造自己去了。

===Original===

For, after a short trip to Gallipoli, where I got some young lime-twigs in boxes of earth, and some preserved limes and ginger, I set out for a long voyage to the East, passing through the Suez Canal, and visiting Bombay, where I was three weeks, and then destroyed it.

===Chinese===

因为，短访了一趟加利波利——在那里我弄到几株幼嫩的酸橙苗，栽在带土的木箱里，还有蜜饯酸橙和姜——我便动身作了一次远航：穿过苏伊士运河，游历孟买，在那里待了三个星期，然后把它毁了。

===Original===

I had the thought of going across Hindustan by engine, but did not like to leave my ship, to which I was very attached, not sure of finding anything so suitable and good at Calcutta; and, moreover, I was afraid to abandon my petrol motor, which I had taken on board with the air-windlass, since I was going to uncivilised land. I therefore coasted down western Hindustan.
All that northern shore of the Arabian Sea has at the present time an odour which it wafts far over the water, resembling odours of happy vague dream-lands, sweet to smell in the early mornings as if the earth were nothing but a perfume, and life an inhalation.
On that voyage, however, I had, from beginning to end, twenty-seven fearful storms, or, if I count that one near the Carolines, then twenty-eight. But I do not wish to write of these rages: they were too inhuman: and how I came alive through them against all my wildest hope, Someone, or Something, only knows.
I will write down here a thing: it is this, my God﻿—something which I have observed: a definite obstreperousness in the mood of the elements now, when once roused, which grows, which grows continually. Tempests have become very very far more wrathful, the sea more truculent and unbounded in its insolence; when it thunders, it thunders with a venom new to me, cracking as though it would split the firmament, and bawling through the heaven of heavens, as if roaring to devour all things; in Bombay once, and in China thrice, I was shaken by earthquakes, the second and third marked by a certain extravagance of agitation, that might turn a man grey. Why should this be, my God? I remember reading very long ago that on the American prairies, which from time immemorial had been swept by great storms, the storms gradually subsided when man went to reside permanently there. If this be true, it would seem that the mere presence of man had a certain subduing or mesmerising effect upon the native turbulence of Nature, and his absence now may have removed the curb. It is my belief that within fifty years from now the huge forces of the earth will be let fully loose to tumble as they will; and this planet will become one of the undisputed playgrounds of Hell, and the theatre of commotions stupendous as those witnessed on the face of Saturn.

===Chinese===

我起过念头，想乘机车横穿印度斯坦，却舍不得丢下我的船——我对它感情很深——又不敢担保在加尔各答还能找到这样合用的好船；何况我也不愿撇下我的汽油机车，它连同风动绞车一起被我装上了船，因为我要去的是蛮荒之地。于是我便沿着西印度斯坦的海岸南下。
阿拉伯海的整片北岸，如今有一种气味，远远地飘送过水面，像幸福而缥缈的梦土的气味，清晨嗅来那样甜，仿佛大地不过是一缕芬芳，生命不过是一次吸纳。
然而在那次航行里，我自始至终遇上了二十七场可怕的风暴，若把卡罗琳群岛旁那场也算上，则是二十八场。可我不愿写这些狂怒：它们太非人了：我怎样违背自己最大胆的希望活了下来，只有某一位，或某一物，知道。
我要在此记下一件事：就是这样一件事，我的上帝——一样我观察到的现象：如今的天时地利，一旦被惊动，便有一种明确的暴烈，它增长着，不住地增长着。风暴已变得远比从前狂怒得多，海也更凶横、更无忌惮；一打雷，便带着一种我不识的新毒，炸裂得仿佛要把穹苍劈开，吼彻九重天，仿佛要吞噬万物；在孟买我遇上过一次地震，在中国三次，第二、第三次震得那样剧烈，足以叫人一夜白头。为什么会这样，我的上帝？我记得很久以前读到过：美洲的大草原，自太古以来一直被大风暴横扫，可当人类前去定居之后，风暴便渐渐平息了。倘若此说不虚，那么单是人的存在，对天性的暴烈似乎便有一种震慑或催眠之效，如今人去位空，缰绳便卸了。我相信，自今而后不出五十年，大地的巨力将被全然放开，任其翻腾；这颗行星将成为地狱无可争议的游乐场之一，成为骚动的剧场，其剧烈将不亚于土星面上所见的那些。

===Original===

The Earth is all on my brain, on my brain, O dark-minded Mother, with thy passionate cravings after the Infinite, thy regrets, and mighty griefs, and comatose sleeps, and sinister coming doom, O Earth: and I, poor man, though a king, sole witness of thy bleak tremendous woes. Upon her I brood, and do not cease, but brood and brood﻿—the habit, if I remember right, first becoming fixed and fated during that long voyage eastward: for what is in store for her God only knows, and I have seen in my broodings long visions of her future, which, if a man should see with the eye of flesh, he would spread the arms, and wheel and wheel through the mazes of a hiccuping giggling frenzy, for the vision only is the very verge of madness. If I might cease but for one hour that perpetual brooding upon her! But I am her child, and my mind grows and grows to her like the offshoots of the banyan-tree, that take root downward, and she sucks and draws it, as she draws my feet by gravitation, and I cannot take wing from her: for she is greater than I, and there is no escaping her; and at the last, I know, my soul will dash itself to ruin, like erring sea-fowl upon pharos-lights, against her wild and mighty bosom. Often a whole night through I lie open-eyed in the dark, with bursting brain, thinking of that hollow Gulf of Mexico, how identical in shape and size with the protuberance of Africa just opposite, and how the protuberance of the Venezuelan and Brazilian coast fits in with the in-curve of Africa: so that it is obvious to me﻿—it is quite *obvious*﻿—that they once were one; and one night rushed so far apart; and the wild Atlantic knew that thing, and ran gladly, hasting in between: and how if eye of flesh had been there to see, and ear to hear that cruel thundering, my God, my God﻿—what horror! And if now they meet again, so long apart﻿ ﻿… but that way fury lies. Yet one cannot help but think: I lie awake and think, for she fills my soul, and absorbs it, with all her moods and ways. She has meanings, secrets, plans.

===Chinese===

地球整个压在我脑子里，压在我脑子里，哦心黑的母亲，你渴慕着无限，你有悔恨，有浩大的悲恸，有昏迷的沉睡，有阴森将临的劫数，哦大地：而我，可怜人，虽贵为君王，却独独自睹你凄凉而惊天动地的苦难。我伏在她身上冥想，不肯停歇，只是想啊，想啊——这习惯，如果我记得不错，是在那次漫长的东航中开始定下、成为宿命的：因为她面前储着什么，唯有上帝知道；而我在冥想中看见过她未来的长卷，倘若有人用肉眼看它，他会张开双臂，在一阵打嗝狂笑的癫乱里团团打转、打转，因为那异象本身便是疯狂的边缘。哪怕能有一小时停止这对她不停的冥想也好！可我是她的孩子，我的心神像榕树的气根一样朝她越长越长、向下扎根，她吮吸它、牵引它，如同以重力牵引我的双脚，我无法从她身边振翅飞离：因为她比我大，逃不出她的掌心；而且我知道，到最后，我的灵魂必会像迷途的海鸟撞上灯塔之火，一头撞碎在她狂野而壮伟的胸膛上。常常整夜整夜，我睁着眼躺在黑暗里，脑袋欲裂，想着那凹陷的墨西哥湾，它的形状大小，同正对面的非洲凸块何其一致，委内瑞拉和巴西海岸的凸弧，又怎样同非洲的内曲严丝合缝：于是这对我显而易见——*显而易见*——它们曾合为一体；而后一夜之间骤然迸离；狂野的大西洋知道那件事，欢然奔涌，急急地灌进中间：倘若当时有肉眼看它，有肉耳听那残酷的雷鸣，我的上帝，我的上帝——何等的恐怖！而倘若如今它们重逢，暌违如此之久……可那是通往狂怒的路。然而人不能不想：我醒着躺着想，因为她以她全部的心绪和行止，充满我的灵魂，将它吸尽。她有含义，有秘密，有图谋。

===Original===

Strange, strange, for instance, that similarity between the scheme of Europe and the scheme of Asia: each with three southern peninsulas pointing south: Spain corresponding with Arabia, Italy with India, the Morea and Greece, divided by the Gulf of Corinth, corresponding with the Malay Peninsula and Annam, divided by the Gulf of Siam; each with two northern peninsulas pointing south, Sweden and Norway, and Korea and Kamschatka; each with two great islands similarly placed, Britain and Ireland, and the Japanese Hondo and Yezo; the Old World and the New has each a peninsula pointing north﻿—Denmark and Yucatan: a forefinger with long nail﻿—and a thumb﻿—pointing to the Pole. What does she mean? What can she mean, O Ye that made her? Is she herself a living being, with a will and a fate, as sailors said that ships were living entities? And that thing that wheeled at the Pole, wheels it still yonder, yonder, in its dark ecstasy? Strange that volcanoes are all near the sea: I don’t know why; I don’t think that anyone ever knew. This fact, in connection with submarine explosions, used to be cited in support of the chemical theory of volcanoes, which supposed the infiltration of the sea into ravines containing the materials which form the fuel of eruptions: but God knows if that is true. The lofty ones are intermittent﻿—a century, two, ten, of silent waiting, and then their talk silenced forever some poor district; the low ones are constant in action. Who could know the dark way of the world? Sometimes they form a linear system, consisting of several vents which extend in one direction, near together, like chimneys of some long foundry beneath. In mountains, a series of serrated peaks denotes the presence of dolomites; rounded heads mean calcareous rocks; and needles, crystalline schists. The preponderance of land in the northern hemisphere denotes the greater intensity there of the causes of elevation at a remote geologic epoch: that is all that one can say about it: but whence that greater intensity? I have some knowledge of the earth for only ten miles down: but she has eight thousand miles: and whether through all that depth she is flame or fluid, hard or soft, I do not know, I do not know.

===Chinese===

蹊跷，蹊跷，比如欧洲的图式与亚洲的图式那般相似：各自伸着三支指向南方的半岛：西班牙对阿拉伯，意大利对印度，被科林斯湾分开的摩里亚和希腊，对被暹罗湾分开的马来半岛和安南；各有两支向南的北端半岛——瑞典和挪威，朝鲜和堪察加；各有两座位置相仿的大岛——不列颠与爱尔兰，日本的本州与虾夷；旧大陆与新大陆各有一支指向北方的半岛——日德兰和尤卡坦：一根蓄着长指甲的食指——和一根拇指——指着北极。她想说明什么？她能说明什么，哦造她的你们？她本身莫非是一个活物，有自己的意志和命数，像水手们从前说船是活物那样？而在北极转动的那个东西，如今可还在那边转动，在那边，在它黑暗的狂喜里？蹊跷的是火山全都在海附近：我不知道为什么；我想从没有人知道过。这一事实，连同海底的爆炸，从前常被引来支持火山的化学说，即海水渗入储着喷发燃料之材的裂谷：可天知道真假。高者间歇——百年、两百年、千年地默然守候，然后一开口，便叫哪块可怜的地域永远噤声；低者则无休无止。谁能知晓这世界的暗路？有时它们连成一线，由几个近旁同向排列的喷口组成，像底下某座长工场的烟囱。山间，一列锯齿状的峰群指明白云岩的存在；浑圆的山头意味着石灰岩；而针峰，则是结晶片岩。陆地在北半球占多，说明远古地质时代抬升之力在那里更烈：能说的只有这些：可那更烈的力又从何而来？我对大地的知识只有十英里深：可她有八千英里：而那整深处，她是火焰还是流体，是硬还是软，我不知道，我不知道。

===Original===

Her method of forming coal, geysers and hot sulphur-springs, and the jewels, and the atols and coral reefs; the metamorphic rocks of sedimentary origin, like gneiss, the plutonic and volcanic rocks, rocks of fusion, and the unstratified masses which constitute the basis of the crust; and harvests, the burning flame of flowers, and the passage from the vegetable to the animal: I do not know them, but they are of her, and they are like me, molten in the same furnace of her fiery heart. She is dark and moody, sudden and ill-fated, and rends her young like a cannibal lioness; and she is old and wise, and remembers Hur of the Chaldees which Uruk built, and that Temple of Bel which rose in seven pyramids to symbolise the planets, and Birs-i-Nimrud, and Haran, and she bears still, as a thing of yesterday, old Persepolis and the tomb of Cyrus, and those cloister-like vihara-temples of the ancient Buddhists, cut from the Himalayan rock; and returning from the Far East, I stopped at Ismailia, and so to Cairo, and saw where Memphis was, and stood one bright midnight before that great pyramid of Shafra, and that dumb Sphynx, and, seated at the well of one of the rock-tombs, looked till tears of pity streamed down my cheeks: for great is the earth, and her Ages, but man “passeth away.” These tombs have pillars extremely like the two palace-pillars, only that these are round, and mine are square: for I chose it so: but the same band near the top, then over this the closed lotus-flower, then the small square plinth, which separates them from the architrave, only mine have no architrave; the tombs consist of a little outer temple or court, then comes a well, and inside another chamber, where, I suppose, the dead were, a ribbon-like astragal surrounding the walls, which are crowned with boldly-projecting cornices, surmounted by an abacus. And here, till the pressing want of food drove me back, I remained: for more and more the earth overgrows me, wooes me, assimilates me; so that I ask myself this question: “Must I not, in time, cease to be a man, and become a small earth, precisely her copy, extravagantly weird and fierce, half-demoniac, half-ferine, wholly mystic﻿—morose and turbulent﻿—fitful, and deranged, and sad﻿—like her?”

===Chinese===

她造煤的法子，间歇泉和热硫磺泉，宝石，环礁和珊瑚礁；沉积来源的变质岩，如片麻岩，深成岩与火山岩，熔岩，和那些构成地壳基底的无层理岩体；还有收成，百花燃烧的火焰，和从植物到动物的过渡：我不懂它们，可它们属于她，它们像我，同熔在她火热心肠的同一座炉里。她阴郁而多变，暴烈而命蹇，像吃同类的母狮撕碎自己的幼崽；她又苍老而智慧，记得乌鲁克所建的迦勒底之胡尔，记得那座以七重金字塔象征七曜的贝勒神庙，记得比尔斯尼姆鲁德和哈兰；波斯波利斯故城和居鲁士的陵，在她身上还恍如昨日之事，还有古佛家从喜马拉雅岩壁里凿出的那些精舍般的毗诃罗石窟；从远东回航时，我在伊斯梅利亚停留，转赴开罗，看了孟菲斯的故址，在一个晴朗的午夜，立于谢夫拉那座大金字塔和那喑哑的斯芬克斯面前，又坐在一座岩墓的井口边，望着望着，怜悯的泪顺着双颊淌下来：因为大地伟大，她的世纪伟大，而人「如烟而逝」。这些墓的柱子，同我宫殿的两根殿柱极其相像，只是它们的圆，我的方：因为是我特意选的方形：可柱顶附近同样有一圈束带，带上覆着闭合的莲苞，再上是一方小柱础板，把柱身同楣梁隔开——只是我的柱没有楣梁；墓的格局是：一座小小的前殿或前庭，然后一口井，井内另有一堂，我想，死者便在那里；四壁绕着一圈绦带似的圆饰，壁顶挑出大胆的飞檐，檐上再压柱顶板。在这里，直到觅食的急迫把我驱回，我一直流连：因为大地越来越把我覆没、向我求爱、将我同化；以致我自问：「久而久之，我不是必定要不复为人，而变成一尊小小的地，恰恰是她的摹本，奇诡凶悍得不着边际，半魔半野，全然神秘——阴郁而狂乱——忽冷忽热，神志乖张，悲怆——正像她么？」

===Original===

A whole month of that voyage, from May the 15th to June the 13th, I wasted at the Andaman Islands near Malay: for that any old Chinaman could be alive in Peking began, after some time, to seem the most quixotic notion that ever entered a human brain; and these jungled islands, to which I came after a shocking vast orgy one night at Calcutta, when I fired not only the city but the river, pleased my fancy to such an extent, that at one time I intended to abide there. I was at the one called in the chart “Saddle Hill,” the smallest of them, I think: and seldom have I had such sensations of peace as I lay a whole burning day in a rising vale, deeply-shaded in palm and tropical ranknesses, watching thence the *Speranza* at anchor: for there was a little offing here at the shore whence the valley arose, and I could see one of its long peaks lined with coconut-trees, and all cloud burned out of the sky except the flimsiest lawn-figments, and the sea as absolutely calm as a lake roughened with breezes, yet making a considerable noise in its breaking on the shore, as I have noticed in these sorts of places: I do not know why. These poor Andaman people seem to have been quite savage, for I met a number of them in roaming the island, nearly skeletons, yet with limbs and vertebrae still, in general, cohering, and in some cases dry-skinned and mummified relics of flesh, and never anywhere a sign of clothes: a very singular thing, considering their nearness to high old civilisations all about them. They looked small and black, or almost; and I never found a man without finding on or near him a spear and other weapons: so that they were eager folk, and the wayward dark earth was in them, too, as she should be in her children. They had in many cases some reddish discoloration, which may have been the traces of betel-nut stains: for betel-nuts abound there. And I was so pleased with these people, that I took on board with the gig one of their little tree-canoes: which was my foolishness: for gig and canoe were only three nights later washed from the decks into the middle of the sea.

===Chinese===

那次航行，从五月十五日到六月十三日，我整整一个月都荒废在马来附近的安达曼群岛：因为「北京可能还有一个活着的中国老头」这念头，过了一些时日，渐渐显得是人脑里最异想天开的想头了；而在加尔各答一夜骇人的大狂欢之后——那晚我不但烧了城，连河也点了火——我来到这些丛林岛屿，它们那样中我的意，有一阵我竟打算住下来。我待的那座，图上叫「鞍山」，怕是其中最小的：我躺在一条渐升的谷地里，浓荫匝地，棕榈与热带的繁茂深深遮着我，从那里守望着抛锚的「斯佩兰扎号」，躺了整整一个灼热的天——这样安宁的感觉，我少有过：因为谷口海边有一小片锚泊的湾，我望得见它一条长长的峰脊排满椰子树，天上烧得一丝云也不剩，只余最轻薄的一点草地似的痕迹；海面平得绝对像一面被微风起的湖，却在扑岸时弄出相当的声响，这些地方我注意到都是如此：不知何故。这些可怜的安达曼人，看去相当野蛮，因为我漫游全岛时遇见他们许多个，几乎只剩骷髅，四肢和脊柱倒大抵还连着，有几具还皮干肉僵，成了木乃伊似的残躯，却到处寻不见一点衣着的痕迹：考虑到他们四面都挨着古老的高度文明，这是件非常奇异的事。他们看去矮小，黑，或近乎黑；我每见一个男子，必见他身上或身边有矛和其他武器：可见是些血性的人，任性的黑暗大地也在他们身体里，正如她该在她儿女们身体里那样。他们身上多有带红的斑渍，也许是嚼核槟的痕迹：因为那里槟榔极多。我太喜欢这些人了，竟用小艇把他们一只树皮小独木舟带了上船：这是我的愚蠢：因为只隔三夜，小艇和独木舟就一齐被冲下甲板，冲到了海中央。

===Original===

I passed down the Straits of Malacca, and in that short distance between the Andaman Islands, and the S. W. corner of Borneo I was thrice so mauled, that at times it seemed quite out of the question that anything built by man could escape such unfettered cataclysms, and I resigned myself, but with bitter reproaches, to perish darkly. The effect of the third upon me, when it was over, was the unloosening afresh of all my evil passion: for I said: “Since they mean to slay me, death shall find me rebellious”; and for weeks I could not sight some specially happy village, or umbrageous spread of woodland, that I did not stop the ship, and land the materials for their destruction; so that nearly all those spicy lands about the north of Australia will bear the traces of my hand for many a year: for more and more my voyage became dawdling and zigzaged, as the merest whim directed it, or the movement of the pointer on the chart; and I thought of eating the lotus of surcease and nepenthe in some enchanted nook of this bowering summer, where from my hut-door I could see through the pearl-hues of opium the sea-lagoon slaver lazily upon the old coral atol, and the coconut-tree would droop like slumber, and the breadfruit tree would moan in sweet and weary dream, and I should watch the *Speranza* lie anchored in the pale atol-lake, year after year, and wonder what she was, and whence, and why she dozed so deep forever, and after an age of melancholy peace and burdened bliss, I should note that sun and moon had ceased revolving, and hung inert, opening anon a heavy lid to doze and drowse again, and God would sigh “Enough,” and nod, and Being would swoon to sleep: for that any old Chinaman should be alive in Peking was a thing so fantastically maniac, as to draw from me at times sudden fits of wild red laughter that left me faint.
During a space of four months, from the 18th June to the 23rd October, I visited the Fijis, where I saw skulls still surrounded with remnants of extraordinary haloes of stiff hair, women clad in girdles made of thongs fixed in a belt, and, in Samoa near, bodies crowned with coronets of nautilus-shell, and traces of turmeric-paint and tattooing, and in one townlet a great assemblage of carcasses, suggesting by their look some festival, or dance: so that I believe that these people were overthrown without the least foreknowledge of anything.

===Chinese===

我驶下马六甲海峡；而就在从安达曼群岛到婆罗洲西南角的这一短短航程里，我接连三次被风暴摧折得那样厉害，以致有时看来，人造的任何东西要想逃过这样肆无忌惮的大灾，全然无望，我也就听天由命，只带着苦苦的怨无，准备黑暗里灭亡。第三次风暴过后，它在我身上的效应，是把我一切的恶欲重新松了绑：因为我说：「他们既然存心杀我，死亡就必看见我反叛」；一连几个星期，只要望见哪个格外安乐的村子，或哪片格外清荫的林地，我必停下船，运下毁它们的材料；以致澳大利亚以北那些香料之国的地面，许多年里都将带着我手的痕迹：因为我的航程越来越游荡，越来越曲折，全凭一时的兴致，或图上指针的一动；我想在这浓荫如盖的仲夏里，寻一处着魔的角落，食忘忧的陀罗与忘忧药：从我茅舍门口，透过鸦片的珠光宝色，望海湖懒懒地淌口水在古老的珊瑚环礁上，椰子树将垂得像睡眠，面包果树将在甜蜜而疲倦的梦里呻吟，我将守望「斯佩兰扎号」年复一年锚在苍白的环礁湖里，纳罕她是什么、从哪里来、为什么永远睡得这样沉，在一段忧郁的安宁与负重的至福之后，我将发现太阳和月亮都停了转，懸着不动，偶尔掀起沉重的眼皮打个盹又睡去，上帝将叹息一声「够了」，点一点头，万有将昏然睡去：因为「北京还有一个活着的中国老头」，这事实在痴狂得离谱，不时从我这里诱出一阵阵狂野的、血红的大笑，笑得我发晕。
从六月十八日到十月二十三日，四个月里，我到访了斐济，在那里看见髑髅四周还留着硬发编成的非凡光轮的残余，女人们系着皮带缀皮条的腰裳；在近旁的萨摩亚，看见尸体上戴着鹦鹉螺壳编的冠，还有姜黄彩绘和文身的痕迹；在一座小镇上看见一大片尸骸，那情状像是逢着什么节庆，或舞会：所以我相信这些人是在对一切毫无预知的情况下被撂倒的。

===Original===

The women of the Maoris wore an abundance of green-jade ornaments, and I found a peculiar kind of shell-trumpet, one of which I have now, also a tattooing chisel, and a nicely-carved wooden bowl. The people of New Caledonia, on the other hand, went, I should think, naked, confining their attention to the hair, and in this resembling the Fijians, for they seemed to wear an artificial hair made of the fur of some creature like a bat, and also they wore wooden masks, and great rings﻿—for the ear, no doubt﻿—which must have fallen to the shoulders: for the earth was in them all, and made them wild, perverse and various like herself. I went from one to the other without any system whatever, searching for the ideal resting-place, and often thinking that I had found it: but only wearying of it at the thought that there was a yet deeper and dreamier in the world. But in this search I received a check, my God, which chilled me to the marrow, and set me flying from these places.

===Chinese===

毛利人的女子戴着大量的绿玉饰物，我还寻见一种特别的螺号，其中一只现在就在我手上；还有一支文身的小凿，和一只雕工精美的木碗。新喀里多尼亚的人呢，我看是光着身子的，心思全用在头发上，这一点像斐济人：因为他们似乎戴一种人造的发，用什么蝙蝠之类的兽毛做成，还戴木制面具，还有大环——想必是耳环——一定垂到肩膀：因为大地就在他们全体里面，把他们造得同她自己一样野蛮、乖张而多态。我从一岛到另一岛，毫无章法，寻那理想的歇脚之地，屡屡以为寻到了：却只在想到世上必有更幽更深的一处时，又厌倦了。可就在这寻觅中，我捱了一记，我的上帝，那把我冷到骨髓里，叫我从这些地方仓皇飞逃。

===Original===

One evening, the 29th November, I dined rather late﻿—at eight﻿—sitting, as was my custom in calm weather, cross-legged on the cabin-rug at the port aft corner, a small semicircle of *Speranza* gold-plate before me, and near above me the red-shaded lamp with green conical reservoir, whose creakings never cease in the stillest mid-sea, and beyond the plates the array of preserved soups, meat-extracts, meats, fruit, sweets, wines, nuts, liqueurs, coffee on the silver spirit-tripod, glasses, cruet, and so on, which it was always my first care to select from the storeroom, open, and lay out once for all in the morning on rising. I was late, seven being my hour: for on that day I had been engaged in the occasionally necessary, but always deferred, task of overhauling the ship, brushing here a rope with tar, there a board with paint, there a crank with oil, rubbing a door-handle, a brass-fitting, filling the three cabin-lamps, dusting mirrors and furniture, dashing the great neat-joinered plains of deck with bucketfulls, or, high in air, chopping loose with its rigging the mizzen topmast, which since a month was sprained at the clamps, all this in cotton drawers under loose *quamis*, barefooted, my beard knotted up, the sun ablaze, the sea smooth and pale with the smooth pallor of strong currents, the ship still enough, no land in sight, yet great tracts of seaweed making eastward﻿—I working from 11 a.m. till near 7, when sudden darkness interrupted: for I wished to have it all over in one obnoxious day. I was therefore very tired when I went down, lit the central chain-lever lamp and my own two, washed and dressed in my bedroom, and sat to dinner in the dining-hall corner. I ate voraciously, with sweat, as usual, pouring down my eager brow, using knife or spoon in the right hand, but never the Western fork, licking the plates clean in the Mohammedan manner, and drinking pretty freely. Still I was tired, and went upon deck, where I had the threadbare blue-velvet easy-chair with the broken left arm before the wheel, and in it sat smoking cigar after cigar from the Indian D box, half-asleep, yet conscious.

===Chinese===

十一月二十九日那天傍晚，我吃得颇晚——八点才开饭——照无风天气的习惯，盘腿坐在舱室地毯的左舷后角，面前摆着小半圈「斯佩兰扎号」的金盘，头顶近处挂着红灯罩、绿锥形油罐的灯，那灯即使在大洋最静的中夜也吱呀不停；盘子那边列着罐装汤、肉精、肉、果品、甜食、酒、果仁、利口酒，银制酒精三脚架上的咖啡，杯子、调料架等等——这些素来是我清早起床后的头一件操心：从库房里挑出来、开好、一次摆停当。我吃得迟了，平时七点是我的饭点：因为那天我一直在干那偶尔必要、却总是拖延的活计：检修全船，这儿给一根绳刷沥青，那儿给一块板上漆，给一处曲柄上油，擦一擦门把、一件铜活，给三盏舱灯添油，掸镜子和家具的灰，一桶桶水泼甲板那大片刨得光洁的平面，或者高悬半空，连索具一齐砍掉那后桅上桅——它在夹箍处扭伤已有一个月了——这一切都穿着棉布衬裤、罩着宽松的「卡米斯」，赤着脚，胡须打起结，太阳灼灼，海面平滑而苍白，是强流特有的那种平滑的白，船静得很，望不见陆地，却有大片大片的海草向东去——我从上午十一点干到将近七点，突来的夜色才打断：因为我巴不得这讨厌的一天里一气干完。所以下舱时我很累了，点着中链杆的大灯和我自己的两盏，在卧室洗漱更衣，然后在饭厅一角落座用膳。我狼吞虎咽，照例汗如雨下，急切地淌过额头，右手用刀或勺，却从不用西式叉子，照穆罕默德的方式把盘子舔干净，酒也喝得相当放开。可我还是累，便上了甲板；舵轮前摆着我那把磨旧了的蓝丝绒安乐椅，左扶手已断，我坐在里面，一支接一支地抽着印度 D 字盒里的雪茄，半睡着，却还有知觉。

===Original===

The moon came up into a pretty cloudless sky, and she was bright, but not bright enough to outshine the enlightened flight of the ocean, which that night was one continuous swamp of Jack-o’-lantern phosphorescence, a wild but faint luminosity mingled with stars and flashes of brilliance, the whole trooping unanimously eastward, as if in haste with elfin momentous purpose, a boundless congregation, in the sweep of a strong oceanic current. I could hear it, in my slumbrous lassitude, struggling and gurgling at the tied rudder, and making wet sloppy noises under the sheer of the poop; and I was aware that the *Speranza* was gliding along pretty fast, drawn into that procession, probably at the rate of four to six knots: but I did not care, knowing very well that no land was within two hundred miles of my bows, for I was in longitude 173°, in the latitude of Fiji and the Society Islands, between those two: and after a time the cigar drooped and dropped from my mouth, and sleep overcame me, and I slept there, in the lap of the Infinite.

===Chinese===

月亮升上相当无云的夜空，很亮，却亮不过那晚大洋的流光：那一夜的海，是一整片鬼火般磷光的沼泽，一种狂野而幽微的亮，混着星点和骤闪的光华，全体步调一致地向东行军，仿佛怀着精灵的什么重大使命匆匆赶路，一支无边无际的队伍，被一股强劲的洋流裹卷着。在我昏昏欲睡的倦怠里，我听得见它绑死的舵边挣扎着、汩汩作响，在船尾楼虚弧下弄出湿漉漉的咂嘴声；我也知道「斯佩兰扎号」正滑行得相当快，被吸进了那支队伍，速度约莫四到六节：可我并不在意，深知我船头两百英里之内没有陆地，因为我正处西经 173 度，斐济与社会群岛之间的那个纬度上：过了一阵，雪茄从我嘴角垂下、掉落，睡意征服了我，我就睡在了那里，睡在无限的膝上。

===Original===

So that something preserves me, Something, Someone: *and for what?*﻿ ﻿… If I had slept in the cabin, I must most certainly have perished: for lying there on the poop, I dreamed a dream which once I had dreamed on the ice, far, far yonder in the forgotten hyperborean North: that I was in an Arabian paradise, a Garden of Peaches; and I had a very long vision of it, for I walked among the trees, and picked the fruit, and pressed the blossoms to my nostrils with breathless inhalations of love: till a horrible sickness woke me: and when I opened my eyes, the night was black, the moon gone down, everything wet with dew, the sky arrayed with most glorious stars like a thronged bazaar of tiaraed rajahs and begums with spangled trains, and all the air fragrant with that mortal scent; and high and wide uplifted before me﻿—stretching from the northern to the southern limit﻿—a row of eight or nine inflamed smokes, as from the chimneys of some Cyclopean foundry a-work all night, most solemn, most great and dreadful in the solemn night: eight or nine, I should say, or it might be seven, or it might be ten, for I did not count them; and from those craters puffed up gusts of encrimsoned material, here a gust and there a gust, with tinselled fumes that convolved upon themselves, and sparks and flashes, all veiled in a garish haze of light: for the foundry worked, though languidly; and upon a rocky land four miles ahead, which no chart had ever marked, the *Speranza* drove straight with the current of the phosphorus sea.
As I rose, I fell flat: and what I did thereafter I did in a state of existence whose acts, to the waking mind, appear unreal as dream. I must at once, I think, have been conscious that here was the cause of the destruction of mankind; that it still surrounded its own neighbourhood with poisonous fumes; and that I was approaching it. I must have somehow crawled, or dragged myself forward. There is an impression on my mind that it was a purple land of pure porphyry; there is some faint memory, or dream, of hearing a long-drawn booming of waves upon its crags: I do not know whence I have them. I think that I remember retching with desperate jerks of the travailing intestines; also that I was on my face as I moved the regulator in the engine-room: but any recollection of going down the stairs, or of coming up again, I have not.

===Chinese===

所以说，有什么在保全我，某物，某位：*又是为了什么呢？*……倘若我睡在舱里，必死无疑：因为我躺在船尾楼上，做了一个梦，一个从前在冰原上做过的梦——在那遥远的、被遗忘的极北之北：梦见自己置身一座阿拉伯的天堂，一座桃花园；那异象很长很长，因为我在树间行走，采摘果子，把花朵贴上鼻端，屏息深深嗅着爱意：直到一阵可怕的恶心把我弄醒：睁眼一看，夜是黑的，月亮已落，万物披着露水，天上缀满极壮丽的星，像一座拥挤的集市，尽是戴宝冠的土王和拖着闪亮裙裾的贵妇；而整个空气里，弥漫着那股致死的香气；在我面前，高高宽宽地拔起一排——从北端直到南端——八九道燃烧的烟柱，仿佛某座独眼巨人的工场通宵开工的烟囱，在庄严的夜里，至庄严、至伟大、至可怖：我说八九道，或者七道，或者十道，因为我没有数；那些火山口喷出一阵阵赤红的物，这里一阵，那里一阵，带着自我卷绕的金银丝般的烟，火星和闪光，全都罩在一层刺目的光霾里：因为那工场还在运作，虽然懒懒地；而在前方四英里一处任何海图都从未标过的多岩陆地上，「斯佩兰扎」号正随着磷光之海的洋流，直直驶去。
我起身，却扑面栽倒：此后我所做的一切，都是在一种对清醒心智而言恍如梦境的存在状态里做的。我想我当时必定立刻就明白了：这就是毁灭人类的祸因所在；它至今仍以毒雾环绕着自己的左近；而我正在驶近它。我必定是以某种方式爬着、拖着身子向前。我脑中有个印象，那是一片纯斑岩的紫色陆地；还有些模糊的记忆，或梦，记得听见海浪拍它峭岩的长长的轰鸣：我不知道这些印象从何而来。我想我记得自己一阵阵干呕，劳苦的肠子绝望地抽搐；还记得我挪动机舱里的调速器时，整个人是俯伏着的：可下楼梯、又上来的任何记忆，我一概没有。

===Original===

Happily, the wheel was tied, the rudder hard to port, and as the ship moved, she must, therefore, have turned; and I must have been back to untie the wheel in good time, for when my senses came, I was lying there, my head against the under gimbal, one foot on a spoke of the wheel, no land in sight, and morning breaking.
This made me so sick, that for either two or three days I lay without eating in the chair near the wheel, only rarely waking to sufficient sense to see to it that she was making westward from that place; and on the morning when I finally roused myself I did not know whether it was the second or the third morning: so that my calendar, so scrupulously kept, may be a day out, for to this day I have never been at the pains to ascertain whether I am here writing now on the 5th or the 6th of June.

===Chinese===

幸好舵轮是绑死的，舵硬压在左舷，船一动，便必然转了向；而我必定又及时回去解开了舵轮，因为待我神志回转，我正躺在那里，头抵着罗盘平衡环的底架，一只脚搭在舵轮的辐条上，看不见陆地，天正破晓。
这一场把我弄得那样虚弱，一连两三天，我不吃不喝躺在舵轮旁的椅子里，只是偶尔清醒到足以察看船是否在向西驶离那地方；等我终于挣扎起身，那是第几个早晨，我自己也不知道：所以我那本一丝不苟记着的历簿，可能差了一天，因为直到今天，我也从未费心查证过：此刻提笔，究竟是6月5日，还是6月6日。

===Original===

Well, on the fourth, or the fifth, evening after this, just as the sun was sinking beyond the rim of the sea, I happened to look where he hung motionless on the starboard bow: and there I saw a clean-cut black-green spot against his red﻿—a most unusual sight here and now﻿—a ship: a poor thing, as it turned out when I got near her, without any sign of mast, heavily waterlogged, some relics of old rigging hanging over, even her bowsprit apparently broken in the middle (though I could not see it), and she nothing more than a hirsute green mass of old weeds and sea-things from bowsprit-tip to poop, and from bulwarks to waterline, stout as a hedgehog, only awaiting there the next high sea to founder.
It being near my dinner-hour and night’s rest, I stopped the *Speranza* some fifteen yards from her, and commenced to pace my spacious poop, as usual, before eating; and as I paced, I would glance at her, wondering at her destiny, and who were the human men that had lived on her, their Christian names, and family names, their age, and thought, and way of life, and beards; till the desire arose within me to go to her, and see; and I threw off my outer garments, uncovered and unroped the cedar cutter﻿—the only boat, except the air-pinnace, left to me intact﻿—and got her down by the mizzen five-block pulley-system. But it was a ridiculous nonsense, for having paddled to her, I was thrown into paroxysms of rage by repeated failures to scale her bulwarks, low as they were; my hands, indeed, could reach, but I found no hold upon the slimy mass, and three rope-ends which I caught were also untenably slippery: so that I jerked always back into the boat, my clothes a mass of filth, and the only thought in my blazing brain a twenty-pound charge of guncotton, of which I had plenty, to blow her to uttermost Hell. I had to return to the *Speranza*, get a half-inch rope, then back to the other, for I would not be baulked in such a way, though now the dark was come, only slightly tempered by a half-moon, and I getting hungry, and from minute to minute more fiendishly ferocious. Finally, by dint of throwing, I got the rope-loop round a mast-stump, drew myself up, and made fast the boat, my left hand cut by some cursed shell: and all for what? the imperiousness of a whim.

===Chinese===

唉，这以后第四天还是第五天傍晚，太阳正沉下海缘，我碰巧朝右舷船头他悬着不动的地方一望：只见他那一团红上衬着一个边缘齐整的黑绿点子——此地此时一桩极不寻常的景象——一艘船：等驶近了才知道是条可怜的东西，桅杆一点不剩，严重进水，几缕旧索具垂挂下来，连她的斜桅看去也从中间断了（虽然我望不真切），从斜桅尖到船尾楼，从舷墙到水线，她整个不过是一大团毛茸茸的绿——旧海草和海中生物，刺猬似的坚密，只等下一场合浪来送她沉底。
这时将近我的晚饭钟点和安寝时分，我把「斯佩兰扎号」停在她十五码开外，照常在宽敞的尾楼上来回踱步，然后再吃；踱步时我不时瞟她一眼，纳罕她的命运，纳罕在她上面活过的都是些什么人，他们的教名、姓氏、年纪、心思、活法、胡须；直到一股欲望从我里面升起：过去，看看；我脱掉外袍，揭开雪松小艇的帆罩、解了索——除了那艘气压小艇，她是我仅剩完好的一条船——用后桅的五轮滑轮组把她放了下去。可这实在荒唐透顶：划到她跟前之后，我一次次翻她那道舷墙——尽管那样低——却一次次失败，气得我阵阵暴怒：我的手确实够得着，可那黏滑的一大团上找不到一个抓手，抓着的三条绳头也滑得握不住：以致我一次次跌回艇里，衣服糊成一团秽物，滚烫的脑子里只剩一个念头：二十磅的一包火棉——我有的是——把她炸进最深层地狱。我只好回「斯佩兰扎号」取了半英寸的绳，再回她那儿，因为我不肯这样败兴，虽然这时天已黑，半月只给了点微光，我也餓了，一分钟比一分钟更凶神恶煞。末了，靠抛掷，我把绳圈套上一截桅桩，把自己拽上去，系牢了艇，左手叫什么该死的贝壳割了一道：而这都是为了什么？一时兴起的专横。

===Original===

The faint moonlight showed an ample tract of deck, invisible in most parts under rolled beds of putrid seaweed, and no bodies, and nothing but a concave, large esplanade of seaweed. She was a ship of probably 1,500 tons, three-masted, and a sailer. I got aft (for I had on thick outer babooshes), and saw that only four of the companion-steps remained; by a small leap, however, I could descend into that desolation, where the stale sea-stench seemed concentrated into a very essence of rankness. Here I experienced a singular ghostly awe and timorousness, lest she should sink with me, or something: but striking matches, I saw an ordinary cabin, with some fungoids, skulls, bones and rags, but not one cohering skeleton. In the second starboard berth was a small table, and on the floor a thick round ink-pot, whose continual rolling on its side made me look down; and there I saw a flat square book with black covers, which curved half-open of itself, for it had been wet and stained. This I took, and went back to the *Speranza*: for that ship was nothing but an emptiness, and a stench of the crude elements of life, nearly assimilated now to the rank deep to which she was wedded, and soon to be absorbed into its nature and being, to become a sea in little, as I, in time, my God, shall be nothing but an earth in little.
During dinner, and after, I read the book, with some difficulty, for it was pen-written in French, and discoloured, and it turned out to be the journal of someone, a passenger and voyager, I imagine, who called himself Albert Tissu, and the ship the *Marie Meyer*. There was nothing remarkable in the narrative that I could see﻿—commonplace descriptions of South Sea scenes, records of weather, cargoes, and the like﻿—till I came to the last written page: and that was remarkable enough. It was dated the 13th of April﻿—strange thing, my good God, incredibly strange﻿—that same day, twenty long years ago, when I reached the Pole; and the writing on that page was quite different from the neat look of the rest, proving immoderate excitement, wildest haste; and he heads it “*Cinq Heures*,”﻿—I suppose in the evening, for he does not say: and he writes: “Monstrous event! phenomenon without likeness!

===Chinese===

淡淡的月光照出一片宽大的甲板，大半埋在成卷的腐海草下，看不见尸体，什么也没有，只有一片凹形的海草大广场。她约莫是一千五百吨的三桅帆船。我走到船尾（因为我脚上套着厚外拖鞋），看见升降口的踏级只剩四级；不过轻轻一跳，我便降进了那片荒凉，陈年的海腥仿佛浓缩成了一剂恶臭的精髓。在这里我尝到一种奇异的鬼气森森的敬畏与胆怯，生怕她带上我沉下去，或者出什么事：可划了几根火柴，我看见一间寻常的舱房，有些菌类、髑髅、骨头和破布，却没有一具完整的髑髅。右舷第二个铺位里有张小桌，地板上一只厚厚的圆墨水瓶，不住地侧着滚，引我低头；我便看见一本黑壳的方形簿子，自己半卷着，因为它浸过水，又浸坏了。我拿了它，回到「斯佩兰扎号」上：因为那船只是一个空壳，一阵生命原质的恶臭，如今几乎同她所嫁的这片恶臭的深海同化了，不久就要被吸进它的性体与存在，化作一小片海——正如我，到时候，我的上帝，也不过化作一小片地。
饭时和饭后，我读那簿子，读得颇费力，因为它是钢笔写的法文，又变了色；原来是谁的一本日记——我想是一位乘客、一位旅人——自称阿尔贝·蒂絮，船叫「玛丽·迈耶号」。叙述里我看不出什么出众之处——南太平洋风物的寻常描写，天气、货物之类的记录——直到我读到写下的最后一页：那可就够出众了。页首的日期是四月十三日——怪事，我的好上帝，怪得难以置信——正是二十年前我抵达极点的那一天；那一页的笔迹同其余各页的齐整全然不同，显出过度的亢奋、最疯狂的仓促；页首他写着「五时」——我猜是傍晚，因为他没有说；他写道：「骇人听闻的事件！无可比拟的奇观！

===Original===

the witnesses of which must forever live immortalised in the annals of the universe, an event which will make even Mama, Henri and Juliette admit that I was justified in undertaking this most eventful voyage. Talking with Captain Tombarel on the poop, when a sudden exclamation from him﻿—‘*Mon Dieu!*’ His visage whitens! I follow the direction of his gaze to eastward! I behold! eight kilomètres perhaps away﻿—, *ten monstrous waterspouts*, reaching up, up, high enough﻿—all apparently in one straight line, with intervals of nine hundred *mètres*, very regularly placed. They do not wander, dance, nor waver, as waterspouts do; nor are they at all lily-shaped, like waterspouts: but ten hewn pillars of water, with uniform diameter from top to bottom, only a little twisted here and there, and, as I divine, fifty mètres in girth. Five, ten, stupendous minutes we look, Captain Tombarel mechanically repeating and repeating under his breath ‘*Mon Dieu!*’ ‘*Mon Dieu!*’ the whole crew now on the poop, I agitated, but collected, watch in hand. And suddenly, all is blotted out: the pillars of water, doubtless still there, can no more be seen: for the ocean all about them is steaming, hissing higher than the pillars a dense white vapour, vast in extent, whose venomous sibilation we at this distance can quite distinctly hear. It is affrighting, it is intolerable! the eyes can hardly bear to watch, the ears to hear! it seems unholy travail, monstrous birth! But it lasts not long: all at once the *Marie Meyer* commences to pitch and roll violently, and the sea, a moment since calm, is now rough! and at the same time, through the white vapour, we see a dark shadow slowly rising﻿—the shadow of a mighty back, a newborn land, bearing upwards ten flames of fire, slowly, steadily, out of the sea, into the clouds. At the moment when that sublime emergence ceases, or seems to cease, the grand thought that smites me is this: ‘I, Albert Tissu, am immortalised: my name shall never perish from among men!’ I rush down, I write it. The latitude is 16° 21′ 13″ South; the longitude 176° 58′ 19″ West.2 There is a great deal of running about on the decks﻿—they are descending. There is surely a strange odour of almonds﻿—I only hope﻿—it is so dark, *mon D*﻿—”
So the Frenchman, Tissu.

===Chinese===

它的目击者必永远不朽，铭刻在宇宙的编年史里；这一件事，将使妈妈、亨利和朱丽叶也不得不承认，我作这次多事之航是有道理的。正与通巴雷尔船长在尾楼上谈话，他忽然一声惊呼——『Mon Dieu!』他的脸白了！我顺着他的目光望向东方！我看见了！约莫八公里外——十根骇人的水柱，向上、向上，够着天高——显然全在一条直线上，间隔九百米，排列得极规整。它们不游移，不舞蹈，不摇晃，像寻常水龙卷那样；也全不是水龙卷那样的百合形：而是十根凿出来的水柱，上下一般粗细，只在这里那里略略拧转，据我目测，周长有五十米。五分钟，十分钟，我们惊看着，通巴雷尔船长机械地一遍又一遍低念『Mon Dieu!』『Mon Dieu!』全体现都聚在尾楼上，我激动，却镇定，手里握着表。突然，一切都被抹去了：那些水柱想必还在，却再也看不见了：因为它们四周的海洋在沸腾、嘶嘶地喷出比水柱还高的一片白汽，广袤无际，那毒蛇般的嘶声，我们隔着这段距离也听得清清楚楚。骇人，骇人得受不了！眼睛几乎不忍看，耳朵不忍听！这像是不洁的阵痛、怪异的分娩！但它持续不久：忽然间，「玛丽·迈耶号」开始剧烈地颠簸摇晃，方才还平静的海，此刻翻了脸！与此同时，透过白汽，我们看见一道暗影缓缓升起——是一副巨背的影子，一片新生的大陆，驮着十道火焰，慢慢地、稳稳地，从海里，升进云中。在那崇高的升现停止、或仿佛停止的一刻，击中我的伟大念头是这一个：『我，阿尔贝·蒂絮，不朽了：我的名字在人间永不磨灭了！』我冲下去，写下这些。纬度南纬 16 度 21 分 13 秒；经度西经 176 度 58 分 19 秒。甲板上一片奔跑——他们在往下降。确乎有一股杏仁的怪味——我只盼——天这样黑，mon D——」
这个法国人，蒂絮，便到此为止。

===Original===

With all that region I would have no more to do: for all here, it used to be said, lies a great sunken continent; and I thought it would be rising and showing itself to my eyes, and driving me stark mad: for the earth is full of these contortions, sudden monstrous grimaces and apparitions, which are like the face of Medusa, affrighting a man into spinning stone; and nothing could be more appallingly insecure than living on a planet.
I did not stop till I had got so far northward as the Philippine Islands, where I was two weeks﻿—exuberant, odorous places, but so hilly and rude, that at one place I abandoned all attempt at travelling in the motor, and left it in a valley by a broad, shallow, noisy river, full of mossy stones: for I said: “Here I will live, and be at peace”; and then I had a fright, for during three days I could not rediscover the river and the motor, and I was in the greatest despair, thinking: “When shall I find my way out of these jungles and vastnesses?” For I was where no paths were, and had lost myself in deeps where the lure of the earth is too strong and rank for a single man, since in such places, I suppose, a man would rapidly be transformed into a tree, or a snake, or a tiger. At last, however, I found the place, to my great joy, but I would not show that I was glad, and to hide it, fell upon a front wheel of the car with some kicks. I could not make out who the people were that lived here: for the relics of some seemed quite black, like New Zealand races, and I could still detect the traces of tattooing, while others suggested Mongolian types, and some looked like pygmies, and some like whites. But I cannot detail the two-years’ incidents of that voyage: for it is past, and like a dream: and not to write of that﻿—of all that﻿—have I taken this pencil in hand after seventeen long, long years.

===Chinese===

那片海域我再不愿沾惹：因为据说这整片海底，沉着一块浩大的古陆；我想它说不定会升上来，摊在我眼前，把我生生逼疯：因为大地满是这等扭曲，骤然的狞怪与显形，一如美杜莎的脸，能把人吓成滴溜溜转的石头；而住在一颗行星上，再没有什么比这更骇人地没有安稳了。
我一路北去，直到菲律宾群岛才停，在那里盘桓了两星期——那些岛屿丰饶而芬芳，只是多山粗砺，有一处我索性放弃了驾机车赶路的打算，把它撇在一条宽阔、湍浅、喧闹、满布生苔碎石的河边的谷地里：因为我对自己说：「我就在这里住下，得个安宁」；随即我着了慌，因为一连三天我找不回那条河和那台机车，我陷入了最大的绝望，心想：「我几时才能寻路走出这些丛林和莽莽？」因为我所在的地方没有路径，我在深处迷了路，在那里，大地的魅惑对孤身一人来说太浓太烈，因为我想，在这种地方，人很快就会变成一棵树，或一条蛇，或一只虎。可最后我找到了那地方，大喜过望，却又不肯露出欢喜，为了掩饰，便对机车的前轮踢了几脚。我弄不清住在那里的都是些什么人：因为有些人的遗骸看去相当黑，像新西兰的种族，还辨得出文身的痕迹，另一些却像蒙古人种，有的像俾格米人，有的像白人。但那两年航程中的种种际遇，我无法细述：因为它已过去了，像一场梦：而不写那个——不写这一切——才是我在十七个漫长漫长的年头之后，重新拿起这支铅笔的缘故。

===Original===

Singular my reluctance to put it on paper.

===Chinese===

我竟是这般不愿落笔。

===Original===

I will write rather of the voyage to China, and how I landed the motor on the wharf at Tientsin, and went up the river through a maize and rice-land most charming in spite of intense cold, I thick with clothes as an Arctic traveller; and of the three dreadful earthquakes within two weeks; and how the only map which I had of the city gave no indication of the whereabouts of its military depositories, and I had to seek for them; and of the three days’ effort to enter them, for every gate was solid and closed; and how I burned it, but had to observe its flames, without deep pleasure, from beyond the walls to the south, the whole place being one cursed plain; yet how, at one moment, I cried aloud with wild banterings and glad laughters of Tophet to that old Chinaman still alive within it; and how I coasted, and saw the hairy Ainus, man and woman hairy alike; and how, lying one midnight awake in my cabin, the *Speranza* being in a still glassy water under a cliff overhung by drooping trees﻿—it was the harbour of Chemulpo﻿—to me lying awake came the thought: “Suppose now you should hear a step walking to and fro, leisurely, on the poop above you﻿—*just suppose*”; and the night of horrors which I had, for I could not help supposing, and at one time really thought that I heard it: and how the sweat rolled and poured from my brow; and how I went to Nagasaki, and burned it;
