I then walked on past the arch, on which a green oblong, once inscribed, as usual, with some text in gilt hieroglyphs, is still discernible; and, emerging, saw the great panorama of destruction, a few vast standing walls, with hollow Oriental windows framing deep sky beyond, and here and there a pillar, or half-minaret, and down within the walls of the old Seraglio still some leafless, branchless trunks, and in Eyoub and Phanar leafless forests, and on the northern horizon Pera with the steep upper-half of the Iani-Chircha street still there, and on the height the European houses, and all between blackness, stones, a rolling landscape of ravine, like the hilly pack-ice of the North if its snow were ink, and to the right Scutari, black, laid low, with its vast region of tombs, and rare stumps of its forests, and the blithe blue sea, with the widening semicircle of floating debris, looking like brown foul scum at some points, congested before the bridgeless Golden Horn: for I stood pretty high in the centre of Stamboul somewhere in the region of the Suleimanieh, or of Sultan-Selim, as I judged, with immense purviews into abstract distances and mirage.

===Chinese===

早晨八点来钟我起身，朝前院走去，打定主意那该是我在这片废墟的最后一夜：因为一整夜，睡着醒着，发生的那件事都塞满我的脑子，从一层难以置信陷进更深的一层，到最后我几乎确信，那只能是一场醉酒的梦：可待我重新睁开眼，那件不可能之事深切入骨的真实，便像一道闪电的剧痛劈过我的全身：于是我说：“我要再去远东，忘了它”：我走出院子，不知道她夜里怎么样了，直到进了外间，我惊得一个趔趄——她就躺在门口，躺在我昨晚摔下她的那个地方，侧身睡着，头枕着胳膊……我轻轻地，轻轻地，从她身上跨过去，出了门，迈开一种鬼祟的小跑，一路跑着。
早晨正盛大节日似的欢快，最清新，最纯净，呼吸一下就叫人年轻，眼看这样的阳光连如此浩大的废墟都照亮，不由人不欢畅。跑到两百码外一座残破的大市场门洞，我回头看了看有没有人跟来：那片空间空得荒凉。随后我走过那道拱门，门上一方绿色长匾还依稀可辨，像通常那样，原先题着金色花体字的经文；一走出来，便望见那幅毁灭的大全景：几面兀立的高墙，空空的东方式窗洞里嵌着深远的蓝天；这里一根石柱，那里半座宣礼塔；旧苏丹后宫（塞拉利奥）的宫墙内还立着些无叶无枝的树干，埃于普和芬纳尔是无叶的森林；北面地平线上是佩拉，亚尼-切尔沙街陡峭的上一半还立在那里，高岗上还有欧人的宅子；这中间的一切是黑，是石头，是一片起伏的沟壑之景，像北方的丘状浮冰群，只是它的雪都换成了墨；右边是斯库塔里，黑压压，塌平了，连同它那大片大片的墓园和森林剩下的稀疏残桩；还有欢快的蓝海，漂着半圆的浮碴，越摊越宽，有些地方像褐色的脏沫子，淤塞在没有桥的金角湾前：因为我站得相当高，正在斯坦布尔中心某处——我判断是在苏莱曼尼耶一带，或是苏丹塞利姆寺一带——一眼望进去，是无穷无尽的抽象远方和蜃景。

===Original===

And to me it seemed too vast, too lonesome, and after advancing a few hundred yards beyond the bazaar, I turned again.

===Chinese===

这一切在我看来太浩大，太荒凉了；走出市场几百码之后，我又折了回去。

===Original===

I found the girl still asleep at the house-door, and stirring her with my foot, woke her. She leapt up with a start of surprise, and a remarkable sinuous agility, and gazed an astounded moment at me, till, separating reality from dream and habit, she realised me: but immediately subsided to the floor again, being in evident pain. I pulled her up, and made her limp after me through several halls to the inner court, and the well, where I set her upon the weedy margin, took her foot in my lap, examined it, drew water, washed it, and bandaged it with a strip torn from my caftan-hem, now and again speaking gruffly to her, so that she might no more follow me.
After this, I had breakfast by the kiosk-steps, and when I was finished, put a mass of truffled foie gras on a plate, brushed through the thicket to the well, and gave it her. She took it, but looked foolish, not eating. I then, with my forefinger, put a little into her mouth, whereupon she set hungrily to eat it all. I also gave her some gingerbread, a handful of bonbons, some Krishnu wine, and some anisette.
I then started out afresh, gruffly bidding her stay there, and left her sitting on the well, her hair falling down the opening, she peering after me through the bushes. But I had not half reached the ogival bazaar-portal, when looking anxiously back, I saw that she was limping after me. So that this creature tracks me in the manner of a nutshell following about in the wake of a ship.
I turned back with her to the house, for it was necessary that I should plan some further method of eluding her. That was five days ago, and here I have stayed: for the house and court are sufficiently agreeable, and form a museum of real objets d’art. It is settled, however, that tomorrow I return to Imbros.

===Chinese===

我见那姑娘还睡在宅门口，用脚碰了碰她，把她碰醒。她惊得一跃而起，浑身扭摆，敏捷得惊人，怔怔望了我一刻，直到把现实从梦和习惯里剥出来，才认出我：可随即又瘫坐到地上，显然疼得厉害。
我把她拽起来，让她一瘸一拐地跟着我穿过几重厅堂，到内院井边，让她坐在长满荒草的井栏上，把她的脚搁在我膝上，验看，汲水，洗净，又从我长袍下摆撕下一条替她包扎，一面不时粗声粗气地叱她，好叫她从此别再跟着我。
这之后，我在凉亭台阶边用了早餐，吃完，盛了一盘子松露鹅肝，穿过树丛到井边给她。她接了，却傻乎乎望着，不吃。我便用食指抹了一点送进她嘴里，她这才饿狼似的扑上去吃了个精光。我又给了她些姜饼、一把糖果、一点克里希努酒，还有一点茴香酒。
随后我重新出发，粗声命令她待在那儿，撇下她坐在井栏上，头发垂进井口，她隔着树丛盯着我走。可我还没走到那尖拱市场门洞的一半，惊惶地回头一望，她已一瘸一拐地跟了上来。这生灵追踪我，活像一颗跟着大船打转的果壳。
我只好带她折回宅子，因为显然得另想一条甩脱她的法子。那是五天前，我至今还待在这儿：这宅子和院子实在宜人，简直就是一座真正的艺术品博物馆。不过已定了：明天我就回伊姆罗兹岛。

===Original===

It seems certain that she never wore, saw, nor knew of, clothes.
I have dressed her, first sousing her thoroughly with sponge and soap in lukewarm rose-water in the silver cistern of the harem-bath, which is a circular marbled apartment with a fountain and the complicated ceilings of these houses, and frescoes, and gilt texts of the Koran on the walls, and pale rose-silk hangings. On the divan I had heaped a number of selected garments, and having showed her how to towel herself, I made her step into a pair of the trousers called *shintiyan* made of yellow-striped white-silk; this, by a running string, I tied loosely round the upper part of her hips; then, drawing up the bottoms to her knees, tied them there, so that their voluminous baggy folds, overhanging still to the ankles, have rather the look of a skirt; over this I put upon her a blue-striped chiffon chemise, or qamis, reaching a little below the hips; I then put on a short jacket or vest of scarlet satin, thickly embroidered in gold and precious stones, reaching somewhat below the waist, and pretty tight-fitting; and, making her lie on the couch, I put upon her little feet little yellow baboosh-slippers, then anklets, on her fingers rings, round her neck a necklace of sequins, finally dyeing her nails, which I cut, with henna. There remained her head, but with this I would have nothing to do, only pointing to the tarboosh which I had brought, to a square kerchief, to some corals, and to the fresco of a woman on the wall, which, if she chose, she might copy. Lastly, I pierced her ears with the silver needles which they used here: and after two hours of it left her.
About an hour afterwards I saw her in the arcade round the court, and, to my great surprise, she had a perfect plait down her back, and over her head and brows a green-silk feredjeh, or hood, precisely as in the picture.

===Chinese===

看来可以断定，衣裳她从来没有穿过，没有见过，也从来没有听说过。
我给她穿戴起来：先在内院浴房的银槽里用海绵和肥皂，就着温热的玫瑰水，把她通身擦洗了一遍——那浴房是一间圆形的大理石屋子，有一眼泉，这些宅子特有的繁复的穹顶，壁画，墙上镀金的古兰经文，和浅玫瑰色的绸帷幔。长榻上我堆了一摞拣好的衣裳；教过她怎么用浴巾擦身之后，我让她迈进去一条叫欣提扬（shintiyan）的裤子，白地黄条纹的绸子做的；我用一根抽绳把它松松系在她胯骨上；再把裤脚挽到膝下扎住，于是那宽宽垮垮、仍旧垂到脚踝的裤褶，倒更像一条裙子；在这外面我给她套上一件蓝条纹的雪纺衬衣，也就是卡米斯（qamis），长到臀下一点；然后穿上一件大红缎的短上衣或曰坎肩，密密绣着金线和宝石，长到腰下一点，相当贴身；再让她在软榻上躺下，给她的小脚套上小小的黄色巴布什拖鞋，再戴上脚镯，手指上套戒指，脖子上挂一串亮片项链，最后把我替她剪好的指甲染上指甲花。剩下那颗头，我可不管了，只指了指我带来的塔布什帽、一方包头巾、几串珊瑚，还有墙上那幅女人的壁画：她要愿意，尽可以照着打扮。末了，我用此地惯用的银针，替她穿了耳洞：这样忙了两个钟头，我丢下她走了。
约莫一个钟头后，我在绕院的拱廊里看见她：叫我大吃一惊的是，她背上垂着一条完好的辫子，头上眉上罩着一方绿绸的费雷杰（feredjeh），也就是兜帽——同画上那个女人一模一样。

===Original===

Here is a question, the answer to which would be interesting to me: Whether or not for twenty years﻿—or say rather twenty centuries, twenty eternal aeons﻿—I have been stark mad, a raving maniac; and whether or not I am now suddenly sane, sitting here writing in my right mind, my whole mood and tone changed, or rapidly changing? And whether such change can be due to the presence of only one other being in the world with me?

===Chinese===

这里有一个问题，它的答案想必会叫我有趣：这二十年——不如说这二十个世纪、二十永恒的纪元——我到底是不是彻头彻尾地疯了，一个胡言乱语的疯子？而我现在是不是突然清醒了，坐在这儿神志健全地书写，我整个的情绪和腔调都变了，或者正在飞快地变？还有，这样的变化，能不能只是因为这世上除我之外，又多了一个生灵？

===Original===

This singular being! Where she has lived﻿—and how﻿—is a problem to which not the faintest solution is conceivable. She had, I say, never seen clothes: for when I began to dress her, her perplexity was unbounded; also, during her twenty years, she has never seen almonds, figs, nuts, liqueurs, chocolate, conserves, vegetables, sugar, oil, honey, sweetmeats, orange-sherbet, mastic, salt, raki, tobacco, and many such things: for she showed perplexity at all these, hesitation to eat them: but she has known and tasted *white wine*: I could see that. Here, then, is a mystery.

===Chinese===

这奇异的生灵！她住在哪里——又怎么活到今天——是个连最模糊的解也想不出来的谜。我说过，她从没见过衣裳：因为我开始给她穿衣时，她的惶惑简直无边；同样，在她这二十年里，她从没见过杏仁、无花果、坚果、各式甜酒、巧克力、蜜饯、蔬菜、糖、油、蜂蜜、糖果、橘子露、乳香脂、盐、拉基酒、烟草，以及诸如此类的许多东西：因为面对这一切她都露出惶惑，吃着也犹豫：可是她认得、也尝过*白葡萄酒*：这个我看得出来。那么，这里头就有一个谜。

===Original===

I have not gone to Imbros, but remained here some days longer observing her.
I have allowed her to sit in a corner at mealtime, not far from where I eat, and I have given her food.
She is wonderfully clever! I continually find that, after an incredibly short time, she has most completely adapted herself to this or that. Already she wears her outfit as coquettishly as though born to clothes. Without at all seeming observant﻿—for, on the contrary, she gives an impression of great flightiness﻿—she watches me, I am convinced, with pretty exact observation. She knows precisely when I am speaking roughly, bidding her go, bidding her come, tired of her, tolerant of her, scorning her, cursing her. If I wish her to the devil, she quickly divines it by my face, and will disappear. Yesterday I noticed something queer about her, and soon discovered that she had been staining her lids with black kohl, like the *hanums*, so that, having found a box, she must have guessed its use from the pictures. Wonderfully clever!﻿—imitative as a mirror. Two mornings ago I found an old mother-of-pearl kittur, and sitting under the arcade, touched the strings, playing a simple air; I could just see her behind one of the arch-pillars on the opposite side, and she was listening with apparent eagerness, and, I fancied, panting. Well, returning from a walk beyond the Phanar walls in the afternoon, I heard the same air coming out from the house, for she was repeating it pretty faultlessly by ear.
Also, during the forenoon of the previous day, I came upon her﻿—for footsteps make no sound in this house﻿—in the pacha’s visitors’-hall: and what was she doing?﻿—copying the poses of three dancing-girls frescoed there! So that she would seem to have a character as light as a butterfly’s, and is afraid of nothing.

===Chinese===

我没有去因姆罗兹，又在这儿多留了几天，观察她。
我容许她吃饭时坐在一个角落里，离我吃饭的地方不远，我给她吃的。
她聪明极了！我不断发现，只需短得叫人难以置信的工夫，她就把这样那样的事完全适应了。如今她穿戴那一身，妖娆得仿佛生来就穿衣裳的。她表面上毫不留心——恰恰相反，她给人一副极轻浮的印象——可我确信，她在用相当精确的观察打量我。我什么时候说话粗声粗气，什么时候打发她走、唤她来，什么时候厌了她、容着她、鄙她、咒她，她知道得清清楚楚。我要是心里咒她滚去见魔鬼，她立刻从我脸上猜出来，转眼就不见了。昨天我发觉她有点异样，很快就发现，她学哈纳姆（hanums）们的样子，用黑色的科尔眼墨染了眼睑——可见她找到了一个妆盒，还必定是从那些画上猜出了它的用处。聪明极了！——像一面镜子一样会模仿。两天前的早晨，我寻出一把珍珠母的旧基图尔琴，坐在拱廊下拨弄琴弦，弹了一支简单的曲调；我恰好望得见她坐在对面一根廊柱后头，她显然听得急切，而且，我觉得，气都喘了。好，当天下午我从法纳尔城墙外散步回来，听见同一支曲调从宅子里传出来：她凭耳朵，已经把它复现得几乎一点不错。
还有，前一天上午，我撞见她——在这宅子里脚步是没有声音的——在帕夏的会客厅里：她在干什么？——在模仿画在那里的三个舞女的姿势！这么说来，她的性子轻得像蝴蝶，又什么都不怕。

===Original===

Now I know.
I had observed that at the beginning of every meal she seemed to have something on her mind, going toward the door, hesitating as if to see whether I would follow, and then returning. At length yesterday, after sitting to eat, she jumped up, and to my infinite surprise, said her first word: said it with a most quaint, experimental effort of the tongue, as a fledgling trying the air: the word “*Come.*”
That morning, meeting her in the court, I had told her to repeat some words after me: but she had made no attempt, as if shy to break the long silence of her life; and now I felt some sort of foolish pleasure in hearing her utter that word, often no doubt heard from me: and after hurriedly eating, I went with her, saying to myself: “She must be about to show me the food to which she is accustomed: and perhaps that will solve her origin.”
And so it has proved. I have now discovered that to the moment when she saw me, she had tasted only her mother’s milk, dates, and that white wine of Ismidt which the Koran permits.
As it was getting dark, I lit and took with me the big red-silk lantern, and we set out, she leading, and walking confoundedly fast, slackening when I swore at her, and getting fast again: and she walks with a certain levity, flightiness, and liberated furore, very hard to describe, as though space were a luxury to be revelled in. By what instinctive cleverness, or native vigour of memory, she found her way I cannot tell, but she led me such a walk that night, miles, miles, till I became furious, darkness having soon fallen with only a faint moon obscured by cloud, and a drizzle which haunted the air, she without light climbing and picking her thinly-slippered steps over mounds of debris and loosely-strewn masonry with unfailing agility, I occasionally splashing a foot with horror into one of those little ponds which always marked the Stamboul streets. When I was nearer her, I would see her peer across and upward toward Pera, as if that were a remembered landmark, and would note the perpetual aspen oscillations of the long coral drops in her ears, and the nimble ply of her limbs, wondering with a groan if Pera was our goal.
Our goal was even beyond Pera.

===Chinese===

如今我知道了。
我早注意到，每回开饭，她总像有心事，朝门口走，迟疑着，像看我跟不跟去，然后又回来。昨天，她坐下才吃，忽地跳起来，说了她的第一句话，叫我惊愕不已：那话说得舌头又古怪又试探地费着劲，像雏鸟初次试风：那个词是“*来。*”
那天早上在院子里碰见她，我让跟我念几个词：她却试也不试，仿佛羞于打破她一生的长寂；此刻听见她说出这个词——无疑是从我这里听去的——我竟感到一种傻乎乎的高兴：匆匆吃完，我便随她去了，心里自语：“她准是要领我看她吃惯的食物：这也许能解开她的来历。”
果不其然。我如今查明，直到看见我那一刻，她只尝过三样东西：母亲的奶、椰枣，还有古兰经准许的那种伊斯米德白葡萄酒。
天将黑时，我点起那盏大红绸灯笼带在身边，我们出发了，她在前头引路，走得快得要命，我咒骂一声她便放慢些，随即又快起来：她走起路来带着一种轻飘、疯癫、解放了似的狂劲，很难形容，仿佛空间是一件供她纵情享用的奢侈品。她凭什么本能的机巧，或天生的记忆之力认得路，我说不上；可那晚她领我走的这一程，一英里又一英里，走得我火冒三丈——夜很快黑透，只有一线淡月被云遮着，空气里游着一层毛毛雨；她不用灯，踩着薄底便鞋，在瓦砾堆和散乱的砖石上攀上跨下，敏捷从不出错；我却时不时恐怖地把一只脚踩进斯坦布尔街头惯常有的那些小水洼里。挨近她时，我见她不时斜眼朝佩拉方向瞟，仿佛那是她记得的界标；也看见她耳上长长的珊瑚坠子像白杨叶子般不住摇晃，四肢轻捷地屈伸，我不禁呻吟着疑心：我们的目的地莫非就是佩拉。
我们的目的地竟还在佩拉之外。

===Original===

When we came to the Golden Horn, she pointed to my caique which lay at the Old Seraglio steps, and over the water we went, she lying quite at ease now, with her face at the level of the water in the centre of the crescent-shape, as familiarly as a *hanum* of old engaged in some escapade through the crowded Babel of Galata and that north side of the Horn.
Through Galata we passed, I already cursing the journey: and, following the line of the coast and the great steep thoroughfare of Pera, we came at last, almost in the country, to a great wall, and the entrance to an immense terraced garden, whose limits were invisible, many of the trees and avenues being still intact.
I knew it at once: I had lain a special fuse-train in the great palace at the top of the terraces: it was the royal palace, Yildiz.
Up and up we went through the grounds, a few unburned old bodies in rags of uniform still discernible here and there as the lantern swung past them, a musician in sky-blue, a fantassin and officer-of-the-guard in scarlet, forming a cross, with domestics of the palace in red-and-orange.
The palace itself was quite in ruins, together with all its surrounding barracks, mosque, and seraglio, and, as we reached the top of the grounds, presented a picture very like those which I have seen of the ruins of Persepolis, only that here the columns, both standing and fallen, were innumerable, and all more or less blackened; and through doorless doors we passed, down immensely-wide short flights of steps, and up them, and over strewed courtyards, by tottering fragments of arcades, all roofless, and tracts of charcoal between interrupted avenues of pillars, I following, expectant, and she very eager now. Finally, down a flight of twelve or fourteen rather steep and narrow steps, very dislocated, we went to a level which, I thought, must be the floor of the palace vaults: for at the bottom of the steps we stood on a large plain floor of plaster, which bore the marks of the flames; and over this the girl ran a few steps, pointed with excited recognition to a hole in it, ran further, and disappeared down the hole.

===Chinese===

到了金角湾，她一指我那条泊在旧苏丹后宫台阶边的狭长艇，我们便渡水过去；此刻她躺得安然自在，脸贴着新月形艇心齐平的水面，熟稔得像旧日一位*哈努姆*贵妇，正溜出去到加拉塔那片嘈杂的巴比伦和湾北岸寻欢作乐。
我们穿加拉塔而过，我已在咒骂这趟差事：沿着海岸线，顺着佩拉那条陡峭的大道，我们终于到了几乎算乡下的地方，一堵高墙前，是一座无边的台地园林的大门，望不到园子的尽头，许多树木和林荫道还完好无损。
我一眼就认出来了：台地顶上那座大宫里，我专门布过一条导火索：这是王家宫苑，伊尔迪兹。
我们在园中一路向上，又向上；灯笼摇过时，这里那里还看得见几具未烧的老尸首，穿着褴褛的号衣：一个穿天蓝的乐师，一个穿猩红的步兵和一个卫队军官，摆成十字，还有穿红橙号衣的宫中仆役。
宫殿本身已完全倾颓，连同四周的营房、清真寺和后宫；待我们上到园顶，眼前一幅景象，极像我见过的波斯波利斯废墟图，只是这里的石柱，立着的倒着的，数也数不清，而且都或多或少熏黑了；我们从没有门的门洞里穿过，下了极宽的短石阶，又上石阶，踏过狼藉的庭院，靠着摇摇欲坠的拱廊残段——全都没了顶——在时断时续的柱列之间是一片片炭迹；我怀着期待跟着，此刻她急切得很。最后，顺着一道十二三级又陡又窄、七歪八扭的石阶，我们下到一个层面，我想必是宫殿地窖的地板：因为阶底是一片宽大的素灰泥地，带着火燎的痕迹；姑娘在上面跑了几步，认出来了似的，兴奋地一指地上的一个窟窿，又跑几步，便从窟窿里消失下去。

===Original===

When I followed, and lowered the lantern a little, I saw that the drop down was about eight feet, made less than six feet by a heap of stone-rubbish below, the falling of which had caused the hole: and it was by standing on this rubbish-heap, I knew at once, that she must have been enabled to climb out into the world.
I dropped down, and found myself in a low flat-roofed cellar, with a floor of black earth, very fusty and damp, but so very vast in extent that even in the daytime, I suppose, I could not have discerned its boundaries; I fancy, indeed, that it extends beneath the whole palace and its environs﻿—an enormous stretch of space: with the lantern I could only see a very limited portion of its area. She still led me eagerly on, and I presently came upon a whole region of flat boxes, each about two feet square, and nine inches high, made of very thin laths, packed to the roof; and about a-hundred-and-fifty feet from these I saw, where she pointed, another region of bottles, fat-bellied bottles in chemises of wickerwork, stretching away into gloom and total darkness. The boxes, of which a great number lay broken open, as they can be by merely pulling with the fingers at a pliant crack, contain dates; and the bottles, of which many thousands lay empty, contain, I saw, old Ismidt wine. Some fifty or sixty casks, covered with mildew, some old pieces of furniture, and a great cube of rotting, curling parchments, showed that this cellar had been more or less loosely used for the occasional storage of superfluous stores and knickknacks.
It was also more or less loosely used as a domestic prison. For in the lane between the region of boxes and the region of bottles, near the former, there lay on the ground the skeleton of a woman, the details of whose costume were still appreciable, with thin brass gyves on her wrists: and when I had examined her well, I knew the whole history of the creature standing silent by my side.
She is the daughter of the Sultan, as I assumed when I had once determined that the skeleton is both the skeleton of her mother, and the skeleton of the Sultana.

===Chinese===

我跟着到洞口，把灯笼往下照了照：洞深约八英尺，底下积了一堆石渣，把落距缩到不足六英尺，窟窿正是这堆石渣塌落造成的：我立刻明白，她必是踩着这堆渣石，才得以爬出到人世来。
我跳下去，发觉自己置身一间低矮平顶的地窖，地是黑土，霉湿逼人，可它大得出奇，我想纵在白天也未必望得见边；我真疑心它整个卧在宫殿和宫苑地底下——一片浩浩荡荡的空间：灯笼照亮的，只是它极小的一角。她仍热切地在前面引路，不久我撞见整整一片扁匣子，每只约两英尺见方、九英寸高，薄板条钉成，一直码到窖顶；离这些约一百五十英尺，顺她指的方向，我又望见另一片酒瓶——裹着柳条衣的胖肚瓶，一直延伸进幽暗和全黑里。匣子里装的是椰枣，许多匣子敞着口，那只需用手指抠住裂缝一拉就能打开；瓶里装的，我看了看，是陈年伊斯米德酒，几千只空瓶横陈着。五六十只发霉的酒桶，几件旧家具，还有一大堆朽卷曲的羊皮纸，可见这地窖原本就随随便便当个仓库，堆些多余的储备和小零小碎。
它也随随便便当过家内的牢狱。因为在匣子区和瓶子区之间的过道里，靠匣子这头，地上躺着一具女人的骸骨，衣饰的细部还看得出来，腕上锁着细黄铜锶：我把她端详够了，便知道了默默立在我身边的这生灵的全部来历。
她是苏丹的女儿——当我断定那具骸骨既是她母亲的骸骨、又是苏丹皇后的骸骨时，我便认定了这一点。

===Original===

That the skeleton was her mother is clear: for the cloud occurred just twenty-one years since, and the dead woman was, of course, at that moment in the prison, which must have been airtight, and with her the girl: but since the girl is quite certainly not much more than twenty﻿—she looks younger﻿—she must at that time have been either unborn or a young babe: but a babe would hardly be imprisoned with another than its own mother. I am rather inclined to think that the girl was unborn at the moment of the cloud, and was born in the cellar.
That the mother was the Sultana is clear from her fragments of dress, and the symbolic character of her every ornament, crescent earrings, heron-feather, and the blue campaca enamelled in a bracelet. This poor woman, I have thought, may have been the victim of some unbounded fit of imperial passion, incurred by some domestic crime, real or imagined, which may have been pardoned in a day had not death overtaken her master and the world.
There are four steep stone steps at about the centre of the cellar, leading up to a locked iron trapdoor, apparently the only opening into this great hole: and this trapdoor must have been so nearly airtight as to bar the intrusion of the poison in anything like deadly quantity.
But how rare﻿—how strange﻿—the coincidence of chances here. For, if the trapdoor was absolutely airtight, I cannot think that the supply of oxygen in the cellar, large as it was, would have been sufficient to last the girl twenty years, to say nothing of what her mother used up before death: for I imagine that the woman must have continued to live some time in her dungeon, sufficiently long, at least, to teach her child to procure its food of dates and wine; so that the door must have been only just sufficiently hermetic to bar the poison, yet admit some oxygen; or else, the place may have been absolutely airtight at the time of the cloud, and some crack, which I have not seen, opened to admit oxygen after the poison was dispersed: in any case﻿—the all-but-infinite rarity of the chance!
Thinking these things I climbed out, and we walked to Pera, where I slept in a great white-stone house in five or six acres of garden overlooking the cemetery of Kassim, having pointed out to the girl another house in which to sleep.
This girl! what a history!

===Chinese===

骸骨是她母亲，这确凿无疑：紫云降临到如今恰好二十一年，死去的女人当时自然在这座牢里——牢必是密闭的——女孩同她在一起：而女孩的年纪肯定不过二十出头——她看着还更小——可见当时她要么尚未出生，要么还是襁褓婴孩：可婴孩不至于同别人、只会同亲娘一道被囚。我倒倾向认为，紫云降临那一刻她尚未出生，她就生在这地窖里。
母亲是苏丹皇后，从她衣衫的残片和每件首饰的象征意味看得明白：新月耳坠、鹭羽，还有手镯上珐琅的蓝色黄兰。我寻思，这可怜的女人，许是栽在帝王一场无边无际的盛怒里，起因是宫闱里一桩或真或幻的罪过；倘若死亡不曾吞没她的主子和整个世界，那罪过也许一天之间就得了赦免。
地窖中央有四级陡石阶，通向一扇上了锁的铁活板门，显见是通进这个大洞的唯一开口：这扇活板门想必密得几乎不透气，才挡住了毒云致命剂量地渗入。
可这里的机缘巧合，何等罕见——何等离奇！因为，倘若活板门绝对不透气，我想地窖里的氧气纵然不少，也决不够女孩用上二十年，更不说她母亲死前耗去的份：因为我想那女人在囚牢里必还活了一阵，至少长得足以教会孩子靠椰枣和酒取食；这么说，那扇门须是恰好密到挡住毒气、却又放进些许氧气；要不然，紫云降临时此地原是绝对密封的，此后某条我没发现的裂缝又裂开，放进了氧气：无论哪种情形——这机缘之巧，几乎无穷稀有！
想着这些，我爬出洞去，我们步行到佩拉；我指给姑娘另一所房子过夜，自己睡进一座白石大宅，宅子带五六英亩园子，俯瞰着卡西姆墓园。
这姑娘！何等的一部身世！

===Original===

After existing twenty years in a sunless world hardly three acres wide, she one day suddenly saw the only sky which she knew collapse at one point! a hole appeared into yet a world beyond! It was I who had come, and kindled Constantinople, and set her free.

===Chinese===

在一个不见天日、方圆不过三英亩的世界里活了二十年之后，有一天，她忽然看见她所知的唯一的天空在一处塌陷了！出现了一个洞，通向洞外又一个世界！是我来了，点燃了君士坦丁堡，放了她自由。

===Original===

Ah, I see something now! I see! it was for this that I was preserved: I to be a sort of newfangled Adam﻿—and this little creature to be my Eve! That is it! *The White* does not admit defeat: he would recommence the Race again! At the last, the eleventh hour﻿—in spite of all﻿—he would turn defeat into victory, and outwit that Other.
However, if this be so﻿—and I seem to see it quite clearly﻿—then in that White scheme is a singular flaw: at *one point*, it is obvious, that elaborate Forethought fails: for I have a free will﻿—and I refuse, I refuse.
Certainly, in this matter I am on the side of the Black: and since it depends absolutely upon me, this time Black wins.
No more men on the earth after me, ye Powers! To *you* the question may be nothing more than a gambling excitement as to the final outcome of your aerial squabble: but to the poor men who had to bear the wrongs, Inquisitions, rack-rents, Waterloos, unspeakable horrors, it was hard earnest, you know! Oh the wretchedness﻿—the deep, deep pain﻿—of that bungling anthill, happily wiped out, my God! My sweetheart Clodagh﻿ ﻿… she was not an ideal being! There was a man called Judas who betrayed the gentle Founder of the Christian Faith, and there was some Roman king named Galba, a horrid dog, and there was a French devil, Gilles de Raiz: and the rest were all much the same, much the same. Oh no, it was not a good race, that small infantry which called itself Man: and here, falling on my knees before God and Satan as I write, I swear, I swear: Never through me shall it spring and fester again.

===Chinese===

啊，我如今明白了！我明白了！保全我原来是为这个：让我做一个新式的亚当——这小造物做我的夏娃！就是这样！「白」不肯认输：他要重新开始这个种族！到末了，十一点钟的最后关头——不顾一切——他要把败局翻成胜局，赢过那另一位。
不过，倘若当真如此——而我仿佛看得清清楚楚——那么「白」的谋划里就有一处奇特的破绽：在这一点上，显而易见，那精密的天算失灵了：因为我有自由意志——而我拒绝，我拒绝。
在这件事上，我当然站在「黑」的一边：而如今事情全然系于我一身，这一回赢的是黑。
我之后，地上不许再有人，你们这些神力！对你们，这问题不过是空中争吵的赌局里一点输赢的刺激；可对那些不得不承受冤屈、宗教裁判、重租盘剥、滑铁卢和种种难言之恐怖的可怜人，那是实实在在的苦，你们可知道！哦那悲惨——那深深的、深深的痛——那座胡乱搭建的蚁丘，幸而已经抹去了，我的上帝！我的甜心克洛达……她也不是什么理想的人物！从前有个叫犹大的，出卖了基督教信仰那位温柔的创立者；还有个罗马王叫加尔巴，一条可憎的狗；还有个法国魔鬼，吉尔·德·雷；其余的也都大同小异，大同小异。哦不，那不是个好种族，那支自称「人」的小小步卒：此刻，我一边写，一边跪倒在上帝和撒旦面前起誓，我起誓：绝不经由我，让它再度滋生溃烂。

===Original===

I cannot realise her! Not at all, at all, at all! If she is out of my sight and hearing ten minutes, I fall to doubting her reality. If I lose her for half a day, all the old feelings, resembling certainties, come back, that I have only been dreaming﻿—that this appearance cannot be an actual objective fact of life, since the impossible is impossible.
Seventeen long years, seventeen long years, of madness.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我把握不了她！完全、完全、完全把握不了！她离开我的视听十分钟，我就开始怀疑她的真实。她离开我半天，那一切旧的感觉——近似确信的感觉——便都回来了：我不过在做梦——这景象不可能是生活里一件真实的客观事实，因为不可能的事终究不可能。
整整十七年，整整十七年的疯狂。……

===Original===

Tomorrow I start for Imbros: and whether this girl chooses to follow me, or whether she stays behind, I will see her from the moment I land no more.

===Chinese===

明天我动身去因姆罗兹：这姑娘愿意跟着也罢，留在后头也罢，从登岸那一刻起，我不再见她。

===Original===

She must rise very early. I who am now regularly on the palace-roof at dawn, sometimes from between the pavilion-curtains of the galleries, or from the steps of the telescope-kiosk, may spy her far down below, a dainty microscopic figure, generally running about the sward, or gazing up in wonder at the palace from the lake-edge.
It is now three months since she came with me to Imbros.
I left her the first night in that pale-yellow house with the two green jalousies facing the beach, where there was everything that she would need; but I knew that, like all the houses there now, it leaked profusely, and the next day I went down to the curving stair, cut through the rock at the back and south of the village, climbed, and half a mile beyond found that park and villa with gables, which I had noted from the sea. The villa is almost intact, very strongly built of purplish marble, though small, and very like a Western house, with shingles, and three gables, so that I think it must have been the yali of some Englishman, for it contains a number of English books, though the only body I saw there was what looked like an Aararat Kurd, with spiral string wound down his turban, yellow ankle-pantaloons, and flung red shoulder-cloak; and all in the heavily-wooded park, and all about the low rock-steps up the hill, profusions of mandragora; and from the rock-steps to the house a narrow long avenue of acacias, mossy underfoot, that mingle overhead, the house standing about four yards from the edge of the perpendicular sea-cliff, whence one can see the *Speranza*’s main topmast, and broken mizzenmast-head, in her quiet haven. After examining the place I went down again to the village, and her house: but she was not there: and two hours long I paced about among the weeds of these amateur little alleys and flat-roofed windowless houses (though some have terrace-roofs, and a rare aperture), whose once-raw yellows, greens, and blues look now like sunset tints when the last flush is gone, and they fade dun. When at last she came running with open mouth, I took her up the rock-steps, and into the house, and there she has lived, one of the gable-tips, I now find (that overlooking the sea), being just visible from the northeast corner of the palace-roof, two miles from it.
That night again, when I was leaving her, she made an attempt to follow me.

===Chinese===

她一定起得很早。我如今照例天一亮就上宫殿屋顶，有时从游廊的亭子帘幕间，有时从望远镜凉亭的台阶上，可以远远窥见底下的她，一个玲珑的、小小的人影，多半在草地上跑来跑去，要么站在湖边，仰着头惊叹地望这宫殿。
她跟我来因姆罗兹，如今已经三个月了。
头一夜我把她安置在那座淡黄色的宅子里——两扇绿百叶窗朝着海滩——屋里她需要的一应俱全；可我知道那屋子同这里如今所有的屋子一样，漏得厉害，第二天我便下到村子后南面凿穿山岩的那道弯梯，爬上去，走过半英里，寻见那座带山墙的园子和别墅，是我从海上就注意到的。别墅几乎完好，用带紫的大理石造得非常坚固，虽然不大，很有点像西方的宅子，木瓦顶，三座山墙，所以我想它多半是哪个英国人的雅宅，因为里面有好多英文书，虽然我在那儿见到的唯一一个人，看样子是个亚拉拉特库尔德人，头巾上螺旋地缠着绳，黄色的扎脚裤，红披肩甩在肩上；浓荫的园子里，上坡的低石阶四周，到处都是一片一片的曼德拉草；从石阶到宅子是一条又窄又长的金合欢林荫道，脚下生着青苔，树冠在头顶交织；宅子离直立的悬崖边约四码，从那儿望得见斯佩兰扎号静泊的港湾里它的中桅桅顶和断掉的后桅桅头。察看过地方，我又下到村里，到她那座宅子：可她不在：我在这些外行的小巷和没有窗的平顶屋之间的荒草里踱了两个钟头（有几座带露台顶，偶尔开个豁口），那些当初生生的黄、绿、蓝，如今像落日褪尽最后一抹红晕的颜色，暗淡成灰褐。末了她张着嘴跑回来，我带她上石阶，进了别墅，从那时起她就住在那儿；那个山墙尖（朝着海的那个），我如今发觉，从宫殿屋顶的东北角恰好看得见——离它两英里。
那天夜里，我离开她的时候，她又想跟我走。

===Original===

But I was resolved to end it, then: and cutting a sassafras-whip I cut her deep, three times, till she ran, crying.

===Chinese===

可我当时拿定主意了结这事：砍了一根黄樟枝条，狠狠抽了她三下，抽得见血，她哭着跑开了。

===Original===

So, then, what is my fate henceforth?﻿—to think always, from sun to moon, and from moon to sun, of one only thing﻿—and that thing an object for the microscope?﻿—to become a sneaking Paul Pry to spy upon the silly movements of one little sparrow, like some fatuous motiveless gossip of old, his occupation to peep, his one faculty to scent, his honey and his achievement to unearth the infinitely unimportant? I would kill her first!

===Chinese===

那么，我今后的命运是什么？——从日到月、从月到日，只想一件事——而那件事不过是显微镜底下的一个物件？——变成一个鬼鬼祟祟的包打听，窥探一只小麻雀的傻气举动，像旧日那种没头没脑的长舌汉，职业是偷看，唯一的能事是嗅探，他的蜜糖和成就是掘出那无穷无尽的无聊琐事？我宁愿先杀了她！

===Original===

I am convinced that she is no stay-at-home, but roams continually over the island: for thrice, wandering myself, I have come upon her.
The first time she was running with flushed face, intent upon striking down a butterfly with a twig held in the left hand (for both hands she uses with dexterity). It was at about nine in the morning, in her park, near the bottom where there are high grass-growths and ferny luxuriance between the close tree-trunks, and shadow, and the broken wall of an old funeral-kiosk sunk aslant under moss, creepers, and wild flowers, behind which I peeped hidden and wet with dew. She has had the assurance to modify the dress I put upon her, and was herself a butterfly, for instead of the *shintiyan*, she had on a zouave, hardly reaching to the waist, of saffron satin, no feredjeh, but a scarlet fez with violet tassel, and baggy pantaloons of azure silk; down her back the long auburn plait, quite neat, but all her front hair loose and wanton, the fez cocked backward, while I caught glimpses of her fugitive heels lifting out of the dropping slipper-sole. She is pretty clever, but not clever enough, for that butterfly escaped, and in one instant I saw her change into weary and sad, for on this earth is nothing more fickle than that Proteus face, which resembles a landscape swept with cloud-shadows on a bright day. Fast beat my heart that morning, owing to the consciousness that, while I saw, I was unseen, yet might be seen.
Another noontide, three weeks afterwards, I came upon her a good way up yonder to the west of the palace, sleeping on her arm in an alley between overgrown old trellises, where rioting wild vine buried her in gloom: but I had not been peeping through the bushes a minute, when she started up and looked wildly about, her quick consciousness, I imagine, detecting a presence: though I think that I managed to get away unseen. She keeps her face very dirty: all about her mouth was dry-stained with a polychrome of grape, *mûrs*, and other coloured juices, like slobbering gamins of old. I could also see that her nose and cheeks are now sprinkled with little freckles.
Four days since I saw her a third time, and then found that the primitive instinct to represent the world in pictures has been working in her: for she was drawing.

===Chinese===

我确信她不是个守在家里的人，而是整天在岛上乱逛：因为我自己闲逛时，就撞见过她三回。
第一回，她满脸通红地跑着，一心要用左手攒着的小树枝打下一只蝴蝶（她两只手都用得灵巧）。那是早晨九点来钟，在她的园子里，靠近坡底，密密的树干之间长着高草和繁藏，一片荫翳，一座旧殡礼凉亭的残墙斜陷在苔藓、藤蔓和野花底下；我就躲在墙后窥看，浑身让露水洇湿了。她竟敢擅自改了我给她穿的衣裳，把自己也变成了一只蝴蝶：*欣蒂扬*裤她没穿，只罩一件藏红花色缎子的祖阿夫短上衣，刚过腰际；不披费雷杰斗篷，却戴一顶紫缨的猩红菲斯帽，下面是碧蓝绸子的灯笼裤；背后那条长长的赤褐发辫编得整整齐齐，满头前发却披散放纵，菲斯帽歪向脑后；我不时瞰见她的脚跟从趿拉着的鞋底里一挑一挑地露出来。她相当机灵，可还不够机灵：那只蝴蝶逃掉了，一霎眼我便看见她变作了倦怠和哀伤——这世上再没有比那张普罗透斯脸更善变的了，它像晴日里被云影扫过的风景。那天早晨我的心跳得飞快，因为我意识到：我看见她，她却没看见我——可我仍可能被看见。
又过了三个星期，另一个晌午，我在宫殿西边高处的僻远所在撞见她：她枕着胳膊，睡在两条荒废老棚架之间的小径上，恣意的野藤把她埋在浓荫里：可我隔灌木窥了还不到一分钟，她就猛地坐起，惶惶四顾——我想是她那灵敏的知觉察觉了有人；不过我想我总算溜脱了，没被瞧见。她把脸弄得脏极了：嘴巴四周干结着葡萄、熟果子之类五颜六色的渍痕，像旧日流口水的野孩子。我还看出她的鼻子和两颊如今点点撒开了小雀斑。
四天前我第三回见到她，那时我发现，用图画描画世界的原始本能已在她说不上什么时候动起来了：因为她在画画。

===Original===

It was down in the middle one of the three east-and-west village streets, for thither I had strolled toward evening, and coming out upon the street from between an old wall and a house, saw her quite near. I pulled up short﻿—and peered. She was lying on her face all among grasses, a piece of yellow board before her, and in her fingers a chalk-splinter: and very intently she drew, her tongue-tip travelling along her short upper-lip from side to side, regularly as a pendulum, her fez tipped far back, and the left foot swinging upward from the knee. She had drawn her yali at the top, and now, as I could see by peering well forward, was drawing underneath the palace﻿—from memory, for where she lay it is all hidden: yet the palace it was, for there were the waving lines meant for the steps, the two slanting pillars, the slanting battlements of the outer court, and before the portal, with turban reaching above the roof, and my two whisks of beard sweeping below the knees﻿—myself.
Something spurred me, and I could not resist shouting a sudden “Hi!” whereupon she scrambled like a springbok to her feet, I pointing to the drawing, smiling.
This creature has a way of mincing her pressed lips, while she shakes the head, intensely cooing a fond laugh: and so she did then.
“You are a clever little wretch, you know,” said I, she cocking her eye, trying to divine my meaning with vague smile.
“Oh, yes, a clever little wretch,” I went on in a gruff voice, “clever as a serpent, no doubt: for in the first case it was the Black who used the serpent, but now it is the White. But it will not do, you know. Do you know what you are to me, you? You are my Eve!﻿—a little fool, a little piebald frog like you. But it will not do at all, at all! A nice race it would be with you for mother, and me for father, wouldn’t it?﻿—half-criminal like the father, half-idiot like the mother: just like the last, in short. They used to say, in fact, that the offspring of a brother and sister was always weak-headed: and from such a wedlock certainly came the human race, so no wonder it was what it was: and so it would have to be again now. Well no﻿—unless we have the children, and cut their throats at birth: and *you* would not like that at all, I know, and, on the whole, it would not work, for the White would be striking a poor man dead with His lightning, if I attempted that.

===Chinese===

那是在村里三条东西向街巷居中的一条上；傍晚时分我信步踱到那边，从一堵老墙和一座房子中间跨上街面，就看见她离得很近。我猛地收步——窥看。
她趴在草丛里，面前支着一块黄木板，指间捏着一小截粉笔：她画得极专注，舌尖在短短的上唇上来回逡巡，规律得像钟摆，菲斯帽远远翘向脑后，左脚从膝盖向上悠荡着。她已在板子顶上画好了她的*yali*水榭，此刻，我把脖子探得老远才看清——正在底下画宫殿：凭记忆画的，因为从她趴的地方全望不见宫殿；可那正是宫殿：那几道波浪线是台阶，两根斜柱，外院斜斜的雉堞；而大门前，一个缠头巾高过屋顶、两撇胡子直扫到膝下的人——是我自己。
什么东西攏摄了我，我忍不住突兀地喝了声：“嗨！”她便像跳羚似的蹿起来，我指着那画，微笑。
这生灵有个习惯：抿着嘴细细地笑出声，一边摇头；她当时正是这样。
“你是个机灵的小东西，知道吗。”我说。她斜吊着眼，带着茫然的笑，猜我的意思。
“哦，是啊，一个机灵的小东西，”我粗声粗气地接着说，“机灵得像条蛇，没说的：头一回用蛇的是‘黑’，如今用蛇的是‘白’。可是行不通的，你懂吗。你知道你于我是什么吗，你？你是我的夏娃！——像你这样的小傻瓜、小花斑蛙。可这万万行不通，行不通！你来当母亲、我来当父亲，生出那么一个种族来，岂不妙哉？——一半随父亲犯罪，一半随母亲犯傻：总之同上一茬一模一样。从前人们就说，亲兄妹生养的孩子必定愚弱：人类那一族，可不正出自这样一门亲事，怪不得是那副德行：如今也只好再来一遍。算了，不——除非生下孩子，落地就掐死：那种事*你*是断断不肯的，我知道，再说总归行不通，我若真干，‘白’的闪电立刻要把一个可怜人劈死。”

===Original===

No, then: the modern Adam is some eight to twenty thousand years wiser than the first﻿—you see? less instinctive, more rational. The first disobeyed by commission: I shall disobey by omission: only his disobedience was a sin, mine is a heroism. I have not been a particularly ideal sort of beast so far, you know: but in me, Adam Jeffson﻿—I swear it﻿—the human race shall at last attain a true nobility, the nobility of self-extinction. I shall turn out trumps: I shall prove myself stronger than Tendency, World-Genius, Providence, Currents of Fate, White Power, Black Power, or whatever is the name for it. No more Clodaghs, Lucrezia Borgias, Semiramises, Pompadours, Irish Landlords, Hundred-Years’ Wars﻿—you see?”
She kept her left eye obliquely cocked like a little fool, wondering, no doubt, what I was saying.
“And talking of Clodagh,” I went on, “I shall call you that henceforth, to keep me reminded. So that is your name﻿—not Eve﻿—but Clodagh, who was a Poisoner, you see? She poisoned a poor man who trusted her: and that is your name now﻿—not Eve, but Clodagh﻿—to remind me, you most dangerous little speckled viper! And in order that I may no more see your foolish little pretty face, I decree that, for the future, you wear a yashmak to cover up your lips, which, I can see, were meant to be seductive, though dirty; and you can leave the blue eyes, and the little white-skinned freckled nose uncovered, if you like, they being commonplace enough. Meantime, if you care to see how to draw a palace﻿—I will show you.”
Before I stretched my hand, she was presenting the board﻿—so that she had guessed something of my meaning! But some hard tone in my talk had wounded her, for she presented it looking very glum, her underlip pushing a little obliquely out, very pathetically, I must say, as always when she is just ready to cry.
In a few strokes I drew the palace, and herself standing at the portal between the pillars: and now great was her satisfaction, for she pointed to the sketched figure, and to herself, interrogatively: and when I nodded “yes,” she went cooing her fond murmurous laugh, with pressed and mincing lips: and it is clear that, in spite of my beatings, she is in no way afraid of me.
Before I could move away, I felt some raindrops, and down in some seconds rushed a shower.

===Chinese===

不，这样才对：现代的亚当比头一个亚当聪明了八千年到两万年——明白吗？本能少了，理性多了。头一个亚当因做了什么而违命：我要因不做而违命：只是他的违逆是罪，我的违逆是壮举。你要知道，到此为止我并不是一头多么理想的畜生：可是在我身上，在亚当·杰夫森身上——我起誓——人类终将达成一种真正的高贵，自我灭绝的高贵。我要打个翻身仗：我要证明我比趋势、世界天才、天意、命运的洪流、白之神力、黑之神力，比随便它叫什么的名字，都更强。从此再没有克洛达们、卢克雷齐娅·博尔吉亚们、萨米拉米斯们、蓬巴杜夫人们、爱尔兰地主们、百年战争们——明白吗？
她始终斜吊着左眼，像个小傻瓜，多半在纳闷我说的什么。
“说到克洛达，”我接着说，“往后我就这么叫你，好叫我时时记着。这就是你的名字——不叫夏娃——叫克洛达，她是个下毒的女人，明白吗？她毒死了一个信任她的可怜人：这就是你如今的名字——不是夏娃，是克洛达——好提醒我，你这最危险的小花斑蝰蛇！还有，为了让我不再看见你那张傻乎乎的小俏脸，我规定，今后你要戴上亚什马克面纱，把嘴盖上——我看得出，你那张嘴生来就是要勾人的，虽说脏；那双蓝眼睛，还有那白皮肤的小雀斑鼻子，倒可以露着，随你，反正平常得很。这会儿，你若想看看宫殿该怎么画——我做给你看。”
我的手还没伸出去，她已把木板递了上来——可见她猜出了我几分意思！可我话里某种硬邦邦的调子伤了她的心：她递板子时沉着脸，下唇微微斜斜地嘟起，那模样，我得说，怪可怜的——每回她要哭时都是这样。
几笔我便画好了宫殿，还画了她站在门前两柱之间：这下她大大地满足，指指画上的人，又指指自己，一副询问的样子；我点头说“是”，她便抿着嘴，咕咕地笑出那亲昵的呢喃；看来，尽管挨过我的打，她一点也不怕我。
我还没走开，就觉出几点雨，转眼一场骤雨泼了下来。

===Original===

I looked, saw that the sky was rapidly darkening, and ran into the nearest of the little cubical houses, leaving her glancing sideways upward, with the quaintest artlessness of interest in the downpour: for she is not yet quite familiarised with the operations of nature, and seems to regard them with a certain amiable inquisitive seriousness, as though they were living beings, comrades as good as herself. She presently joined me, but even then stretched her hand out to feel the drops.
Now there came a thunderclap, the wind was rising, and rain spattering about me: for the panes of these houses, made, I believe, of paper saturated in almond-oil, have long disappeared, and rains, penetrating by roof and rare window, splash the bones of men. I gathered up my skirts to run toward other shelter, but she was before me, saying in her strange experimental voice that word of hers: “*Come.*”
She ran in advance, and I, with the outer robe over my head, followed, urging flinching way against the whipped rain-wash. She took the way by the stone horsepond, through an alley to the left between two blind walls, then down a steep path through wood to the rock-steps, and up we ran, and along the hill, to her yali, which is a mile nearer the village than the palace, though by the time we pelted into its dry shelter we were wet to the skin.
Sudden darkness had come, but she quickly found some matches, lit one, looking at it with a certain meditative air, and applied it to a candle and to a bronze Western lamp on the table, which I had taught her to oil and light. Near a Western fireplace was a Turkish mangal, like one which she had seen me light to warm bath-waters in Constantinople, and when I pointed to it, she ran to the kitchen, returned with some chopped wood, and very cleverly lit it.

===Chinese===

我抬头一望，见天迅速黑下来，便跑进最近的一座四四方方的小屋，撇下她在那儿侧头仰望，对这场倾盆大雨怀着顶天真的好奇：她还没同自然的运作混熟，看它们总带着一种和气的、好究诘的正经，仿佛它们是活物，是同她自己一样好的伙伴。她随即也进来了，可即便那时还伸手出去接雨点。
这时炸了一声雷，风起来了，雨水溅到我身上：这些屋子的窗页，我想原是杏仁油浸的纸做的，早没了影，雨水从屋顶和稀罕的窗口灌进来，溅打在人的骨头上。我掠起袍角要往别的掩蔽处跑，她却抢在我前面，用她那古怪试探的嗓音说出了她的那个词：“*来。*”
她在前面跑，我把外袍顶在头上，顶着鞭子似的雨水，畏畏缩缩地跟着。她领的路经过石砌的马槽池，穿一条两边都是盲墙的左巷，再下一条穿林的陡径到石级，我们跑上去，沿山脊一路到她的*yali*水榭——那地方比宫殿离村子近一英里；待我们冲进它干燥的庇护里，两人都已浑身湿透。
天说黑就黑，可她很快摸出几根火柴，点着一根，带着一种沉思的神气看了看，便去点桌上那盏西方的青铜灯——上油和点灯都是我教的。西式壁炉旁有一只土耳其的曼加尔炭盆，同她在君士坦丁堡见我烧热洗澡水时点的那只相仿；我一指，她就跑进厨房，抱来些劈柴，麻利地点着了。

===Original===

And there for several hours I sat that night, reading (the first time for many years): it was a book by the poet Milton, found in a glazed bookcase on the other side of the fireplace: and most strange, most novel, I found those august words about warring angels that night, while the storm raved: for this man had evidently taken no end of pains with his book, and done it gallantly well, too, making the thing hum: and I could not conceive why he should have been at that trouble﻿—unless it were for the same reason that I built the palace, because some spark bites a man, and he would be like﻿—but that is all vanity, and delusion.
Well, there is a rage in the storms of late years which really transcends bounds; I do not remember if I have noted it in these sheets before: but I never could have conceived a turbulence so huge. Hour after hour I sat there that night, smoking a chibouque, reading, and listening to the batteries and lamentations of that haunted air, shrinking from it, fearing even for the *Speranza* by her quay in the sequestered harbour, and for the palace-pillars. But what astonished me was that girl: for, after sitting on the ottoman to my left some time, she fell sideways asleep, not the least fear about her, though I should have thought that nervousness at such a turmoil would be so natural to her: and whence she has this light confidence in the world into which she has so abruptly come I do not know, for it is as though someone inspired her with the mood of nonchalance, saying: “Be of good cheer, and care not a pin about anything: for God is God.”
I heard the ocean swing hoarse like heavy ordnance against the cliffs below, where they meet the outer surface of the southern of the two claws of land that form the harbour: and the thought came into my mind: “If now I taught her to speak, to read, I could sometimes make her read a book to me.”
The winds seemed wilfully struggling for the house to snatch and wing it away into the drear Eternities of the night: and I could not but heave the sigh: “Alas for us two poor waifs and castaways of our race, little bits of flotsam and seaweed-hair cast up here a moment, ah me, on this shore of the Ages, soon to be dragged back, O turgid Eternity, into thy abysmal gorge; and upon what strand﻿—who shall say?

===Chinese===

那晚我在那儿坐了好几个钟头，读书（多年来头一遭）：那是诗人弥尔顿的一部书，从壁炉另一侧带玻璃门的书柜里找到的：风暴狂吼着，我却在读那些讲争战天使的庄严字句，只觉得最陌生、最新奇：这个人显然为他的书费了无穷的心血，也写得漂亮之至，字字铿锵：我不明白他何苦下这份功夫——除非同我造宫殿是同一个缘故：某种火花咬了人一口，人就想学个像——可那全是虚空，是妄想。
嗯，近几年的风暴里有一种火气，实在没了边；我记不得先前在这册页里写过没有：可这样巨大的骚乱，我从来想象不出。那晚我一小时又一小时地坐在那里，抽着长烟斗，读书，听那鬼气森森的天空开炮和哀号，缩着身子，甚至为泊在幽僻港中码头边的*斯佩兰扎号*担心，为宫殿的柱子担心。可叫我惊奇的还是那姑娘：她在左边那张软榻上坐了一阵，便侧身睡着了，心里没有一丝惧意——虽说我想，这样天翻地覆的动静，她若是胆怯才合常理：她这股子对世界的轻松信心是打哪儿来的，她那么突兀闯进的世界——我不知道；倒像有谁给她灌进了一种满不在乎的心境，对她说：“放心高兴吧，万事不必挂心：因为上帝终归是上帝。”
我听见大海像重炮似的哑着嗓子，撞在下面崖壁上——那是形成港湾的两道陆爪中南边一道的外侧：心里忽然起了一个念头：“倘若我教会她说话，识字，我便可以偶尔让她给我念书了。”
风像是存心争夺这座房子，要把它抓起来，带着它飞进夜的凄凉的永恒里去：我不由叹出一口气：“哀哉，我们这两个种族的可怜漂流者、被弃儿，两小片浮货、两绺海草头发，被抛在这儿，唉，万古的岸上，片刻之后，哦，鼓胀的永恒啊，又要被拖回你那无底的喉咙里去；而下一个滩头——谁能说得准？

===Original===

﻿—shall she next be flung, and I, divided then perhaps by all the stretch of the trillion-distanced astral gulf?” And such a pity, and a wringing of the heart, seemed in things, that a tear fell from my eyes that ominous midnight.
She started up at a gust of more appalling volume, rubbing her eyes, with dishevelled hair (it must have been about midnight), listening a minute, with that demure, droll interest of hers, to the noise of the elements, and then smiled to me; rose then, left the room, and presently returned with a pomegranate and some almonds on a plate, also some delicious old sweet wine in a Samian cruche, and an old silver cup, gilt inside, standing in a zarf. These she placed on the table near me, I murmuring: “Hospitality.”
She looked at the book, which I read as I ate, with lowered left eyelid, seeking to guess its use, I suppose. Most things she understands at once, but this must have baffled her: for to see one looking fixedly at a thing, and not know what one is looking at it for, must be very disconcerting.
I held it up before her, saying:
“Shall I teach you to read it? If I did, how would you repay me, you Clodagh?”
She cocked her eyes, seeking to comprehend. God knows, at that moment I pitied the poor dumb waif, alone in all the whole round earth with me. The candle-flame, moved by the wind like a slow-painting brush, flickered upon her face, though every cranny was closed.
“Perhaps, then,” I said, “I will teach you. You are a pitiable little derelict of your race, you know: and two hours every day I will let you come to the palace, and I will teach you. But be sure, be careful. If there be danger, I will kill you: assuredly﻿—without fail. And let me begin with a lesson now: say after me: ‘White.’ ”
I took her hand, and got her to understand that I wanted her to repeat after me.
“White,” said I.
“Hwhite,” said she.
“Power,” said I.
“Pow‑wer,” said she.
“White Power,” said I.
“Hwhite Pow-wer,” said she.
“Shall not,” said I.
“Sall not,” said she.
“White Power shall not,” said I.
“Hwhite Pow-wer sall not,” said she.
“Prevail,” said I.
“Fffail,” said she, pronouncing the *v* with a long fluttering *f*-sound.
“Prevail,” said I.
“Pe‑vvvail,” said she.
“White Power shall not prevail,” said I.
“Hwhite Pow‑wer sall not‑fffail,” said she.

===Chinese===

——她下一次会被抛上哪个滩头？而我呢，那时或许隔着万亿里星辰深渊的整个跨度，同她天各一方？”万物之中仿佛真有一种悲惘，一种绞心的痛，以致那个不祥的午夜，一滴泪从我眼里落了下来。
一阵更骇人的狂风把她惊起，她揉着眼，头发散乱（想必已近午夜），端端正正带着她那滑稽的好奇，听了一阵风声雨势，然后朝我一笑；随即起身出去，很快端着一只盘子回来，盘里一只石榴、几枚杏仁，还有一壶萨摩斯老甜酒——那酒好极了——盛在萨摩斯陶壶里，外加一只内壁镀金的旧银杯，坐在查尔夫杯托里。她把这些摆到我近旁的桌上，我哺哺了一句：“宾至如归。”
她半阖着左眼，看我边吃边读的那本书，我想是在猜它的用途。多数东西她一看就懂，这一样却准把她难住了：眼见一个人死死盯着一物看，又不知道他看它做什么，必是叫人摸不着头脑的。
我把书举到她面前，说：“要不要我教你读它？我教了，你怎么报答我，呃，克洛达？”
她吊起眼角，努力想懂。上帝知道，那一刻我真是怜惜这可怜的哑巴弃儿——同我一起，孤零零在这圆圆的整个大地上。烛焰被风撼动，像一支慢慢描画的笔，在她脸上晃——虽然每道缝都已堵严。
“那么，也行，”我说，“我教你。你是你们种族一个可怜的小遭弃者，知道吗：每天两个钟头，准你到宫殿里来，我教你。可是记住，小心。若有危险，我就杀了你：一定——绝不失约。现在就上第一课：跟我念：‘白。’”
我握住她的手，让她明白我要她跟我念。
“白。”我说。
“白——白。”她说。
“神力。”我说。
“神——力。”她说。
“白之神力。”我说。
“白之神——力。”她说。
“必不。”我说。
“必、不。”她说。
“白之神力必不。”我说。
“白之神——力必、不。”她说。
“得胜。”我说。
“得、得胜。”她说，把那个*胜*音发成了一长串打颤的*斯*音。
“得胜。”我又说。
“得——胜。”她说。
“白之神力必不能得胜。”我说。
“白之神——力必、不能得——胜。”她说。

===Original===

A thunder which roared as she said it seemed to me to go laughing through the universe, and a minute I looked upon her face with positive shrinking fear; till, starting up, I thrust her with violence from my path, and dashed forth to re-seek the palace and my bed.
Such was the ingratitude and fatality which my first attempt, four nights since, to teach her met with. It remains to be seen whether my pity for her dumbness, or some servile tendency toward fellowship in myself, will result in any further lesson. Certainly, I think not: for though I have given my word, the most solemnly-pledged word may be broken.
Surely, surely, her presence in the world with me﻿—for I suppose it is that﻿—has wrought some profound changes in my mood: for gone now apparently are those turbulent hours when, stalking like a peacock, I flaunted my monarchy in the face of the Eternal Powers, with hissed blasphemies; or else dribbled, shaking my body in a lewd dance; or was off to fire some vast city and revel in redness and the chucklings of Hell; or rolled in the drunkenness of drugs. It was mere frenzy!﻿—I see it now﻿—it was “not good,” “not good.” And it rather looks as if it were past﻿—or almost. I have clipped my beard and hair, removed the earrings, and thought of modifying my attire. I will just watch to see whether she comes loitering down there about the gate of the lake.

===Chinese===

她念这句话时炸响的那声雷，在我听来像是一路笑着滚过了宇宙；有一分钟，我带着实实在在的畏缩恐惧望着她的脸；随后我一跃而起，粗暴地把她从路上推开，冲出去找宫殿和我的床。
四天前的夜里我头一遭教她，遭到的就是这样的忘恩负义和不祥之兆。我对她哑语的怜惘，或我自己身上某种奴性的、渴望做伴的倾向，会不会再招来第二课，走着瞧吧。我想肯定不会：话虽许下了，可许得再重的诺言，也可以撕毁。
真的，真的，她在这世上同我在一起——我想正是这个缘故——已在我心绪里造出了某种深刻的变化：那些狂乱的时刻显然已经过去了——那时我像孔雀开屏，向永恒诸神当面炫示我的君权，嘶声亵渎；或是浑身乱颤，跳猥亵的舞，淌着口水；或是跑出去烧一座大城，沉醉于一片红光和地狱的窃笑；或是滚在药物的醉里。那不过是疯狂！——如今我看清了——那“不好”，“不好”。而如今看来，它多半已成过去——或者快了。我剪了胡子和头发，摘下了耳环，还想着改一改装束。我且看着，她会不会又晃悠到下面湖门那一带去。

===Original===

Her progress is like.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

她的进展，就像。……

===Original===

It is nine months since I have written, on these sheets, those words, “Her progress is like.﻿ ﻿…” being the beginning of some narrative in which something interrupted me: and since then I have had no impulse to write.
But I was thinking just now of the curious tricks and uncertainties of my memory, and seeing the sheets, will record it here. I have lately been trying to recall the name of a sister of mine﻿—some perfectly simple name, I know﻿—and the name of my old home in England: and they have completely passed out of my cognizance, though she was my only sister, and we grew up closely together: some quite simple name, I forget it now. Yet I can’t say that my memory is bad: there are things﻿—quite unexpected, unimportant things﻿—which come up in my mind with considerable clearness. For instance, I remember to have met in Paris (I think), long before the poison-cloud, a little Brazilian boy of the colour of weak coffee-and-milk, of whom she now constantly reminds me. He wore his hair short like a convict’s, so that one could spy the fish-white flesh beneath, and delighted to play solitary about the stairs of the hotel, dressed up in the white balloon-dress of a Pierrot. I have the impression now that he must have had very large ears. Clever as a flea he was, knowing five or six languages, as it were by nature, without having any suspicion that that was at all extraordinary. She has that same light, unconscious, and nonchalant cleverness, and easy way of life. It is little more than a year since I began to teach her, and already she can speak English with a quite considerable vocabulary, and perfect correctness (except that she does not pronounce the letter *r*); she has also read, or rather devoured, a good many books; and can write, draw, and play the harp. And all she does without effort: rather with the flighty naturalness with which a bird takes to the wing.
What made me teach her to read was this: One afternoon, fourteen months or so ago, I from the roof-kiosk saw her down at the lake-rim, a book in hand; and as she had seen me looking steadily at books, so she was looking steadily at it, with pathetic sideward head: so that I burst into laughter, for I saw her clearly through the glass, and whether she is the simplest little fool, or the craftiest serpent that ever breathed, I am not yet sure.

===Chinese===

自打我在这些纸页上写下「她的进展，就像……」那几个字——那是某篇叙述的开头，什么事打断了它——已经过去九个月：此后我一直没有提笔的冲动。
可我方才正想到我记忆里那些古怪的花招和无常，看见这些纸页，便把它记在这儿吧。我近来一直在回想我一个妹妹的名字——我知道是个再简单不过的名字——还有我在英格兰老家的宅名：它们竟完全逸出了我的认知，虽然她是我唯一的妹妹，我们自小亲密地一起长大：一个挺简单的名字，我如今忘了。可也不能说我的记性坏：有些事——全然料想不到的、无关紧要的事——会相当清晰地浮上我心头。比如，我记得（我想是在巴黎）在毒云之前很久，遇见过一个巴西小男孩，皮肤是浅奶咖啡的颜色——她如今老叫我想起他。他头发剃得像苦役犯那么短，看得见底下鱼肉似的白头皮，爱独自一人在旅馆楼梯间玩，穿着皮埃罗的白气球衣。我如今还有个印象：他的耳朵一定很大。他聪明得像跳蚤，会五六种语言，仿佛天生的，丝毫不想到这有什么稀奇。她就有那种同样轻快、无心、散漫的聪明，和随遇而安的活法。我教她到现在不过一年多一点，她已经能说英语，词汇相当可观，语法全对（只是不发音字母 r）；她还读了——不如说吞了——好些书；会写字、画画、弹竖琴。而这一切她全不费劲：倒像鸟儿起飞时那种轻浮的天性。
叫我动念教她读书的是这么一回事：十四个来月前的一个下午，我在屋顶凉亭里望见她下到湖边，手里拿着一本书；她见我一直盯着书看，便也一直盯着那书看，脑袋可怜巴巴地歪着：我禁不住放声大笑，因为隔着望远镜我把她看得清清楚楚——她到底是个天下最简单的傻丫头，还是条最狡黠的蛇，我到现在也没有拿准。

===Original===

If I thought that she has the least design upon my honour, it would be ill for her.
I went to Gallipoli for two days in the month of May, and brought back a very pretty little caique, a perfect slender crescent of the colour of the moon, though I had two days’ labour in cutting through bush-thicket for the passage of the motor in bringing it up to the lake. It has pleased me to see her lie among the silk cushions of the middle, while I, paddling, taught her her first words and sentences between the hours of eight and ten in the evening, though later they became 10 a.m. to noon, when the reading began, we sitting on the palace-steps before the portal, her mouth invariably well covered with the yashmak, the lesson-book being a large-lettered old Bible found at her yali. *Why* she must needs wear the yashmak she has never once asked; and how much she divines, knows, or intends, I have no idea, continually questioning myself as to whether she is all simplicity, or all cunning.
That she is conscious of some profound difference in our organisation I cannot doubt: for that I have a long beard, and she none at all, is among the most patent of facts.

===Chinese===

我若疑心她对我的名节存有半点图谋，那对她就糟了。
五月里我去加利波利待了两天，带回一条非常漂亮的小卡伊克艇，一弯月牙，颜色也像月亮，虽然为了把摩托艇引进湖里，我劈了两天灌木丛，开出一条水道。看她卧在艇中间的丝缎靠垫之间，令我快意；我一边划桨，一边在晚上八点到十点之间教她最初的词语和句子——后来改到上午十点到正午，开始教阅读，我们坐在宫门前的宫殿台阶上，她的口鼻始终严严实实罩着亚什马克面纱，课本是在她那座雅宅里寻到的一本大字旧《圣经》。她为什么非戴那面纱不可，她一次也没有问过；而她揣度到多少、知道多少、存着什么心，我全无头绪，只不住地自问：她是纯然的天真，还是纯然的狡黠。
她意识到我们构造上某种深刻的差异，这我不怀疑：因为我长着一部大胡子，而她一根也没有，这是再明白不过的事实。

===Original===

I have thought that a certain *Western-ness*﻿—a growing modernity of tone﻿—may be the result, as far as I am concerned, of her presence with me? I do not know.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

我曾想，我身上这一股某样的「西方味」——腔调里日益增长的现代气——会不会就是她陪在我身边的结果？我不知道。……

===Original===

There is the gleam of a lake-end just visible in the north forest from the palace-top, and in it a good number of fish like carp, tench, roach, etc., so in May I searched for a tackle-shop in the Gallipoli Fatmeh-bazaar, and got four 12-foot rods, with reels, silk-line, quill-floats, a few yards of silkworm gut, with a packet of No. 7 and 8 hooks, and split-shot for sinkers; and since red-worms, maggots and gentles are common on the island, I felt sure of a great many more fish than the number I wanted, which was none at all. However, for the mere amusement, I fished several times, lying at my length in a patch of long-grass overwaved by an enormous cedar, where the bank is steep, and the water deep. And one mid-afternoon she was suddenly there with me, questioned me with her eyes, and when I consented, stayed: and presently I said I would teach her bottom-angling, and sent her flying up to the palace for another rod and tackle.
That day she did nothing, for after teaching her to thread the worm, and put the gentles on the smaller hooks, I sent her to hunt for worms to chop up for ground-baiting the pitch for the next afternoon; and when this was done it was dinnertime, and I sent her home, for by then I was giving the reading-lessons in the morning.
The next day I found her at the bank, taught her to take the sounding for adjusting the float, and she lay down not far from me, holding the rod. So I said to her:
“Well, this is better than living in a dark cellar twenty years, with nothing to do but walk up and down, sleep, and consume dates and Ismidt wine.”
“Yes!” says she.
“Twenty years!” said I: “How did you bear it?”
“I was not closs,” says she.
“Did you never suspect that there was a world outside that cellar?” said I.
“Never,” says she, “or lather, yes: but I did not suppose that it was *this* world, but another where he lived.”
“He who?”
“He who spoke with me.”
“Who was that?”
“Oh! a bite!” she screamed gladly.
I saw her float bob under, and started up, rushed to her, and taught her how to strike and play it, though it turned out when landed to be nothing but a tiny barbel: but she was in ecstasies, holding it on her palm, murmuring her fond coo.
She re-baited, and we lay again. I said:
“But what a life: no exit, no light, no prospect, no hope﻿—”
“Plenty of *hope*!” says she.
“Good Heavens! hope of what?”

===Chinese===

从宫殿顶上，恰好望得见北面林子里一线湖尾的微光，湖里鱼不少，像鲤鱼、丁鱥、拟鲤等等，于是五月里我去加利波利的法特梅巴扎寻钓具店，买了四根十二英尺的钓竿，带绕线轮、丝线、羽毛浮子、几码蚕肠线，一包七号和八号钩子，还有做坠子的劈铅；红蚯蚓、蛆和饵蛆岛上都多的是，我有把握钓到远多于我想要的鱼——我想要的数量是零。不过图个乐子，我还是钓过几回：整个人躺在一大片长草里，头顶一株大雪松缓缓摇曳，那里的岸陡，水也深。一天午后三点来钟，她忽然来了，用眼睛问我；我一点头，她便留下了：不久我说要教她底钓，打发她飞奔回宫里再取一根竿子和钓具。
那天她什么也没钓：因为教过她穿蚯蚓、往小钩上挂蛆之后，我派她去挖蚯蚓，剁碎了做第二天下午打窝的诱饵；这做完就到了开饭的时候，我让她回家去，因为那时读书课已经改在上午了。
第二天我到岸边时她已经在那儿了，我教她测水深调浮子，她便在离我不远处躺下，握着竿子。于是我对她说：
「怎么样，这可比在一间黑地窖里待二十年强——没事可做，只有走过来走过去，睡觉，吃椰枣，喝伊斯米德酒。」
「是！」她说。
「二十年哪！」我说，「你怎么受的？」
「我不恼。」她说。
「你就从来没疑心过，地窖外头还有一个世界？」
「从来没有，」她说，「——或者毋宁说，疑心过：可我没料到是这个世道，是他在里头住着的另一个世界。」
「他是谁？」
「同我说话的那位。」
「那是谁？」
「哦！咬钩了！」她欢天喜地地叫起来。
我看见她的浮子往下一沉，跳起来奔到她跟前，教她怎样扬竿、怎样遛鱼——虽然上了岸不过是条小小的鲃鱼：可她欢喜得入了迷，把它托在掌心里，咕咕地呢喃着她的疼爱。
她重新上了饵，我们又躺下。我说：
「可那是过的什么日子：没有出口，没有光，没有指望，没有希望——」
「有的是希望！」她说。
「天哪！希望什么？」

===Original===

“I knew vely well that something was lipening over the cellar, or under, or alound it, and would come to pass at a certain fixed hour, and that I should see it, and feel it, and it would be vely nice.”
“Ah, well, you had to wait for it, at any rate. Didn’t those twenty years seem *long*?”
“No﻿—at least sometimes﻿—not often. I was always so occupied.”
“Occupied in doing what?”
“In eating, or dlinking, or lunning, or talking.”
“Talking to your*self*?”
“Not myself.”
“To whom, then?”
“To the one who told me when I was hungly, and put the dates to satisfy my hunger.”
“I see. Don’t wriggle about in that way, or you will never catch any fish. The maxim of angling is: ‘Study to be quiet’﻿—”
“O! another bite!” she called, and this time, all alone, very agilely landed a good-sized bream.
“But do you mean that you were never sad?” said I when she was resettled.
“Sometimes I would sit and cly,” says she﻿—“I did not know why. But if that was ‘sadness,’ I was never miserlable, never, never. And if I clied, it did not last long, and I would soon fall to sleep, for he would lock me in his lap, and kiss me, and wipe all my tears away.”
“He who?”
“Why, what a question! he who told me when I was hungly, and of the thing that was lipening outside the cellar, which would be so nice.”
“I see, I see. But in all that dingy place, and thick gloom, were you never at all afraid?”
“Aflaid! *I!* of what?”
“Of the unknown.”
“I do not understand you. How could I be *aflaid*? The known was the very opposite of tellible: it was merely hunger and dates, thirst and wine, the desire to lun and space to lun in, the desire to sleep and sleep: there was nothing tellible in that: and the unknown was even less tellible than the known: for it was the nice thing that was lipening outside the cellar. I do not understand﻿—”
“Ah, yes,” said I, “you are a clever little being: but your continual fluttering about is fatal to all angling. Isn’t it in your nature to keep still a minute? And with regard now to your habits in the cellar﻿—?”
“*Another!*” she cried with happy laugh, and landed a young chub. And that afternoon she caught seven, and I none.

===Chinese===

「我早知道有一样东西正在窖上头，或者窖底下，或者四围，慢慢酿熟，到了某个定死的时辰就要成，我会亲眼见到它，亲手摸到它，它一定很好。」
「啊哈，反正你总得等它。那二十年不显得长吗？」
「不长——至多样儿——不常有。我总是有得忙。」
「忙什么？」
「忙着吃，忙着喝，忙着跑，忙着说话。」
「跟你自个儿说？」
「不跟自个儿。」
「那跟谁？」
「跟那一位——我饿了他会告诉我，拿椰枣来填我肚子的那一位。」
「明白了。别那么扭来扭去的，不然一条鱼也钓不着。钓鱼的格言是：『学着安静』——」
「哦！又咬了！」她喊，这一回全凭自己，麻利地钓上来一条不小的鳊鱼。
「可你是说你从来不难过？」等她重新安顿好，我说。
「有时我会坐着哭，」她说，「我不知道为什么。可要是那叫『难过』，我从来没有苦到哪儿去，从来没有，从来没有。我要是哭了，也哭不长，一会儿就睡着了，因为他会把我搂进怀里，亲我，把我的眼泪全擦掉。」
「他是谁？」
「咦，这叫什么问题！就是我饿了他告诉我的那一位，告诉我窖外头正酿着的那件好事的那一位，那件事会好得很。」
「明白，明白。可那样一个暗地方，那样一团黑，你就从来不害怕？」
「怕！我！怕什么？」
「怕那不知道的。」
「我不懂你的话。我怎么会怕？『知道的』一点也不吓人：不过是饿和椰枣，渴和酒，想跑和跑的地方，想睡和觉：这里头没有一样吓人的：而『不知道的』比知道的更不吓人：因为那正是窖外头正酿着的那件好事。我不懂——」
「啊，是，」我说，「你是个聪明的小东西：可你这么不停地扑腾，钓鱼全给你扑腾光了。你的天性里就没有安静一分钟这一条？再说回你在地窖里的那些习惯——？」
「又一条！」她笑着欢叫，钓上来一条小雅罗鱼。那天下午她钓了七条，我一条没有。

===Original===

Another day I took her from the pitch to one of the kitchens in the village with some of the fish, till then always thrown away, and taught her cooking: for the only cooking-implement in the palace is the silver alcohol-lamp for coffee and chocolate. We both scrubbed the utensils, and boil and fry I taught her, and the making of a sauce from vinegar, bottled olives, and the tinned American butter from the *Speranza*, and the boiling of rice mixed with flour for ground-baiting our pitch. And she, at first astonished, was soon all deft housewifeliness, breathless officiousness, and behind my back, of her own intuitiveness, grated some dry almonds found there, and with them sprinkled the fried tench. And we ate them, sitting on the floor together: the first new food, I suppose, tasted by me for twenty-one years: nor did I find it disagreeable.
The next day she came up to the palace reading a book, which turned out to be a cookery-book in English, found at her yali; and a week later, she appeared, out of hours, presenting me a yellow-earthenware dish containing a mess of gorgeous colours﻿—a boiled fish under red peppers, bits of saffron, a greenish sauce, and almonds: but I turned her away, and would have none of her, or her dish.

===Chinese===

又有一天，我带她离开钓台，拎着几条鱼——那以前一直被扔掉的——到村里一间厨房去，教她做菜：因为宫里唯一的炊具，就是煮咖啡和巧克力用的那盏银酒精灯。
我们俩把炊具都擦洗了一遍；我教她煮、教她煎，教她用醋、瓶装橄榄和*斯佩兰扎号*上拿来的美国罐头黄油调沙司，还教她把米和面粉煮在一起，做打窝子喂钓点的诱饵。她起初惊讶，很快就一身麻利的主妇做派，风风火火地抢着干；又趁我背过身，凭自己的直觉，把屋里找出的几枚干杏仁擦成屑，撒在了煎好的丁鱥鱼上。我们一起坐在地板上把它们吃了：我想，这是我二十一年来尝到的第一样新食物：我并不觉得它难吃。
第二天她上宫里来，手里捧着一本书在读——原来是本英文食谱，在她的水榭找到的；一星期后，她不合时辰地出现了，捧给我一只黄陶盘，盛着一大摊绚丽的颜色——红辣椒下卧着煮鱼，星星点点的藏红花，发绿的沙司，还有杏仁：可我把她打发走了，不要她的人，也不要她的菜。

===Original===

About a mile up to the west of the palace is a very old ruin in the deepest forest, I think of a mosque, though only three truncated internal pillars under ivy, and the weedy floor, with the courtyard and portal-steps remain, before it being a long avenue of cedars, gently descending from the steps, the path between the trees choked with long-grass and wild rye reaching to my middle. Here I saw one day a large disc of old brass, bossed in the middle, which may have been either a shield or part of an ancient cymbal, with concentric rings graven round it, from centre to circumference. The next day I brought some nails, a hammer, a saw, and a box of paints from the *Speranza*; and I painted the rings in different colours, cut down a slim lime-trunk, nailed the thin disc along its top, and planted it well, before the steps: for I said I would make a bull’s-eye, and do rifle and revolver practice before it, from the avenue. And this the next evening I was doing at four hundred feet, startling the island, it seemed, with that unusual noise, when up she came peering with enquiring face: at which I was very angry, because my arm, long unused, was firing wide: but I was too proud to say anything, and let her look, and soon she understood, laughing every time I made a considerable miss, till at last I turned upon her saying: “If you think it so easy, you may try.”
She had been wanting to try, for she came eagerly to the offer, and after I had opened and showed her the mechanism, the cartridges, and how to shoot, I put into her hands one of the *Speranza* Colt’s. She took her bottom-lip between her teeth, shut her left eye, vaulted out the revolver like an old shot to the level of her intense right eye, and sent a ball through the geometrical centre of the boss.
However, it was a fluke-shot, for I had the satisfaction of seeing her miss every one of the other five, except the last, which hit the black. That, however, was three weeks since, and now my hitting record is forty percent, and hers ninety-six﻿—most extraordinary: so that it is clear that this creature is the protégée of someone, and favouritism is in the world.

===Chinese===

往西离宫约一英里的山上，密林深处有一处很古老的废墟；我想是一座清真寺的遗迹，虽然只剩三根截断的内柱爬满带藤，一片荒草萋萋的地坪，还有院子和门前的台阶；废墟前是一条长长的雪松林荫道，从台阶缓缓向下，树间的路被长草和野黑麦堵得严严实实，齐到我腰。有一天我在这里看见一大面旧铜盘，中间鼓起一块，或许是一面盾，或许是古代铜钹的一部分，盘面上刻着一圈套一圈的同心环，从圆心直到圆边。
第二天我从*斯佩兰扎号*上取来钉子、锤子、锯子和一盒颜料；我把那些环涂成不同的颜色，砍倒一根细长的落树干，把薄铜盘钉在干顶，牢牢竖在台阶前：因为我说要做一个靶子，从林荫道这头，在它跟前练习步枪和左轮。于是第二天傍晚我就这么干了起来，在四百英尺的距离上，那不寻常的枪声似乎惊动了整座岛，正打着，她上来了，探着头，一脸探究：这叫我非常恼火，因为我这条久不射击的胳膊把子弹打得满靶乱飞：可我骄傲得不肯说什么，由她看，她很快就看懂了，每回我脱靶脱得离谱她就笑，直到我终于转身冲她说：“你若觉得这么容易，你来试。”
她早就想试了，一听这话便急切地迎上来；我打开枪给她看了机件、子弹和射击的方法，然后把*斯佩兰扎号*上的一支科尔特左轮放进她手里。她咬住下唇，闭起左眼，像个老枪手那样把左轮平稳地扬起，举到她那专注的右眼齐平处，一枪打穿了靶心圆包的几何中心。
不过那是碰巧，因为后五发她枪枪脱靶，只有最后一发正中黑心，这让我很出了口气。但那是三个星期前的事了；如今我的命中率是百分之四十，她的百分之九十六——真是异乎寻常：由此可见，这生灵分明是受着某一位的庇护，偏心眼儿在这世上确实是有的。

===Original===

Her book of books is the Old Testament. Sometimes, at noon or afternoon, I may look abroad from the roof or galleries, and see a remote figure sitting on the sward under the shade of plane or black cypress: and I always know that the book she cons there is the Bible﻿—like an old Rabbi. She has a passion for stories: and there finds a store.
Three nights since when it was pretty late, and the moon very splendid, I saw her passing homewards close to the lake, and shouted down to her, meaning to say “Good night”; but she thought that I had called her, and came: and sitting out on the top step we talked for hours, she without the yashmak.
We fell to talking about the Bible. And says she: “What did Cain to Abel?”
“He knocked him over,” I replied, liking sometimes to use such idioms, with the double object of teaching and perplexing her.
“Over what?” says she.
“Over his heels,” said I.
“I do not complehend!”
“He killed him, then.”
“That I know. But how did Abel feel when he was killed? What is it to be *killed*?”
“Well,” said I, “you have seen bones all around you, and the bones of your mother, and you can feel the bones in your fingers. Your fingers will become mere bone after you are dead, as die you must. Those bones which you see around you, are, of course, the bones of the men of whom we often speak: and the same thing happened to them which happens to a fish or a butterfly when you catch them, and they lie all still.”
“And the men and the butterfly feel the same after they are dead?”
“Precisely the same. They lie in a deep drowse, and dream a nonsense-dream.”
“That is not dleadful. I thought that it was much more dleadful. I should not mind dying.”
“Ah!﻿ ﻿… so much the better: for it is possible that you may have to die a great deal sooner than you think.”
“I should not mind. Why were men so vely aflaid to die?”
“Because they were all such shocking cowards.”
“Oh, not all! not all!”
(This girl, I know not with what motive, has now definitely set herself up against me as the defender of the dead race. With every chance she is at it.)
“Nearly all,” said I: “tell me one who was not afraid﻿—”
“There was Isaac,” says she: “when Ablaham laid him on the wood to kill him, he did not jump up and lun to hide.”

===Chinese===

她的书中之书是《旧约》。有时晌午或午后，我从屋顶或廊上眺望，会望见远远一个人影坐在草坪上，躲在悬铃木或黑柏树的荫下：我总是知道，她埋头苦读的那本，是《圣经》——活像位老拉比。她对故事有一股痴情：而那里面有的是存货。
三夜之前，夜已颇深，月色极美，我看见她沿着湖边走回家去，便朝下喊了一声，本想说“晚安”；她却以为我在叫她，就上来了：我们坐在最高一级台阶上谈了几个钟头，她没戴面纱。
我们谈起了《圣经》。她问：“该隐对亚伯做了什么？”
“他把他打翻在地。”我答道——我有时爱用这类俚语，一半为教她，一半为懵她。
“翻到什么上头？”她说。
“翻了个跟头。”我说。
“我听不懂滴！”
“那么说，他杀了他。”
“这个我知道。可亚伯被杀的时候是什么滋味？*被杀*是怎么回事？”
“呢，”我说，“你四下里见过那么多骨头，也见过你母亲的骨头，还摸得到自己手指里的骨头。你死后——你横竖是要死的——你的手指也会变成光秃秃的骨头。你周围那些骨头，自然就是我们常说起的那些人的骨头：他们的下场，同你捉住的鱼或蝴蝶一样，一动不动地躺着。”
“人和蝴蝶死了以后，感觉一样？”
“一模一样。他们躺在深深的昏睡里，做一个胡话梦。”
“那并不可怕滴。我原以为可怕得多呢。死就死吧，我不在乎。”
“啊！……那敢情好：因为你说不定得死得比你以为的早得多。”
“我不在乎。从前的人为什么那么狠怕死呢？”
“因为他们全都是些不折不扣的胆小鬼。”
“哦，不是全部！不是全部！”
（这姑娘，不知存的什么心，如今明摆着处处同我作对，当起死绝人种的辩护人了。一有机会她就要辩。）
“差不多全是。”我说，“说出一个不怕死的来——”
“以撒就不怕。”她说，“亚伯拉罕把他捆在柴上要杀他，他也没有跳起来跑掉躲起来。”

===Original===

“Isaac was a great exception,” said I: “in the Bible and such books, you understand, you read of only the best sorts of people; but there were millions and millions of others﻿—especially about the time of the poison-cloud﻿—on a very much lower level﻿—putrid wretches﻿—covetous, false, murderous, mean, selfish, debased, hideous, diseased, making the earth a very charnel of festering vices and crimes.”
This, for several minutes, she did not answer, sitting with her back half toward me, cracking almonds, continually striking one step with the ball of her outstretched foot. In the clarid gold of the platform I saw her fez and corals reflected as an elongated blotch of florid red. She turned and drank some wine from the great gold Jarvan goblet which I had brought from the temple of Boro Budor, her head quite covered in by it. Then, the little hairs at her lip-corners still wet, says she:
“Vices and climes, climes and vices. Always the same. What were these climes and vices?”
“Robberies of a hundred sorts, murders of ten hundred﻿—”
“But what made them *do* them?”
“Their evil nature﻿—their base souls.”
“But *you* are one of them, *I* am another: yet you and I live here together, and we do no vices and climes.”
Her astounding shrewdness! Right into the inmost heart of a matter does her simple wit seem to pierce!
“No,” I said, “we do no vices and crimes, because we lack *motive*. There is no danger that we should hate each other, for we have plenty to eat and drink, dates, wines, and thousands of things. (Our danger is rather the other way.) But *they* hated and schemed, because they were very numerous, and there arose a question among them of dates and wine.”
“Was there not, then, enough land to grow dates and wine for all?”
“There was﻿—yes: much more than enough, I fancy. But some got hold of a vast lot of it, and as the rest felt the pinch of scarcity, there arose, naturally, a pretty state of things﻿—including the vices and crimes.”
“Ah, but then,” says she, “it was not to their bad souls that the vices and climes were due, but only to this question of land. It is certain that if there had been no such question, there would have been no vices and climes, because you and I, who are just like them, do no vices and climes here, where there is no such question.”
The clear limelight of her intelligence! She wriggled on her seat in her effort of argument.

===Chinese===

“以撒是个大大的例外。”我说，“你要明白，《圣经》这类书里，写的尽是顶好的人；可还有千千万万别的人——尤其到毒云降临那阵子——比这低得多——烂透了的货色——贪心、虚伪、凶杀、下作、自私、堕落、丑恶、带病，把整个大地变成了一座恶罪溃烂的停尸场。”
这话她有好几分钟没接，半背着我坐着，剥着杏仁，伸出去的脚尖一下一下点着台阶。在台地明晃晃的金光里，我看见她的菲斯帽和珊瑚坠映成一长摊艳红。她转过头，从那只我由婆罗浮屠神殿带回的大金高脚杯里喝了口酒，脑袋整个埋进杯子里。然后，嘴角的小绒毛还湿着，她说：
“罪醉和罪恶，罪恶和罪醉。老是这一套。这些罪醉和罪恶都是些什么呀？”
“抢劫一百样，杀人一千样——”
“可他们为什么要*去*做呢？”
“因为他们天性邪恶——灵魂卑劣。”
“可*你*就是他们中的一个，*我*是另一个：你我住在一起，我们就不做什么罪醉和罪恶。”
她这份惊人的伶俐！她那点简单的聪明，仿佛一杆扎进事情的最心底！
“不错。”我说，“我们不做罪恶，因为我们缺的是*动机*。我们谁也不至于恨谁，因为吃的东西多得很，椰枣、酒，成千上万样。（我们的危险倒是在另一头。）可*他们*又恨又算计，因为他们人数太多，他们中间就冒出了一个椰枣和酒的问题。”
“那么，种椰枣酿酒的地，还不够大家用的吗？”
“够——是的：我想还富富有余。可有些人把大批大批的地攥到了手里，其余的人一感到短缺的苦，自然就闹出了一副好局面——连带种种罪恶和罪案。”
“啊，那么说来，”她说，“这些罪醉和罪恶就不是因为灵魂坏，只是因为地的问题喽。肯定的，要是没有这个问题，就不会有罪醉和罪恶，因为你我同他们一模一样，在这里没有这个问题，就不做罪醉和罪恶。”
她那智力的一束清光！她辩论得坐不安席，在座位上扭来扭去。

===Original===

“I am not going to argue the matter,” I said. “There *was* that question of dates and wine, you see. And there always must be on an earth where millions of men, with varying degrees of cunning, reside.”
“Oh, not at all necessalily!” she cries with conviction: “not at all, at all: since there are much more dates and wine than are enough for all. If there should spling up more men now, having the whole wisdom, science, and expelience of the past at their hand, and they made an allangement among themselves that the first man who tlied to take more than he could work for should be killed, and sent to dleam a nonsense-dleam, the question could never again alise!”
“It arose before﻿—it would arise again.”
“But no! I can guess clearly how it alose before: it alose thlough the sheer carelessness of the first men. The land was at first so vely, vely much more than enough for all, that the men did not take the tlouble to make an allangement among themselves; and afterwards the habit of carelessness was confirmed; till at last the vely oliginal carelessness must have got to have the look of an allangement; and so the stleam which began in a little long ended in a big long, the long glowing more and more fixed and fatal as the stleam lolled further flom the source. I see it clearly, can’t you? But now, if some more men would spling, they would be taught﻿—”
“Ah, but no more men will *spling*, you see﻿—!”
“There is no telling. I sometimes feel as if they must, and shall. The tlees blossom, the thunder lolls, the air makes me lun and leap, the glound is full of lichness, and I hear the voice of the Lord God walking all among the tlees of the folests.”
As she said this, I saw her underlip push out and tremble, as when she is near to crying, and her eyes moisten: but a moment after she looked at me full, and smiled, so mobile is her face: and as she looked, it suddenly struck me what a noble temple of a brow the creature has, almost pointed at the uplifted summit, and widening down like a bell-curved Gothic arch, draped in strings of frizzy hair which anon she shakes backward with her head.
“Clodagh,” I said after some minutes﻿—“do you know why I called you Clodagh?”
“No? Tell me?”
“Because once, long ago before the poison-cloud, I had a lover called Clodagh: and she was a.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“这事我不同你争。”我说，“你要看到，椰枣和酒的问题*是*有的。而且在一颗住着千百万奸猾程度不等的人的地球上，这问题永远免不了。”
“哦，才不一一定的呢！”她深信不疑地叫道，“才不一定，一定：椰枣和酒本来就比大家用的多得多。要是现在再冒出些人来，手里握着过去全部的智慧、学问和经验，他们相互间立个约，谁头一个想多占超出他劳力所应得的，就把谁杀掉，送去做个胡话梦，这问题就再也冒不出来的呀！”
“从前冒出来过——往后还会冒出来。”
“才不呢！先前怎么会冒出来，我猜得清清楚楚：是头一批人粗心大意闹的。起初的地，比大家用的多出狠多狠多，人们就懒得去立个约；后来大意的习惯就定下了；到最后，最初那点粗心，看着也就像个约定了；于是那条从一个小弯发端的小溪，流到最后成了大河，水流滚得离源头越远，那个弯就越弯得死、弯得要命。我看得狠清楚，你看不清吗？可如今，要是再冒出些人来，先就教会他们——”
“啊，可不会再有人*冒*出来啦，你明白吗——！”
“这可说不准。我有时觉得他们一定来，一定会来。树开花，雷滚动，空气叫我跑啊跳啊，地里满是富足，我听见主上帝的声音，行走在森林的树木中间。”
她说这话时，我看见她的下唇嘟起、颤抖，像要哭了，眼睛也湿了：可片刻之后，她正眼望住我，笑了——她的脸就是这样多变：她望着我的时候，我忽然注意到，这生灵有一副何等高贵殿堂似的前额，顶上微微收尖，往下展宽，像一道钟形曲线的哥特拱，垂着几绺鬈发，她不时把头一甩，把它们甩向脑后。
“克洛达，”过了几分钟我说——“你知道我为什么叫你克洛达吗？”
“不知道。说说看？”
“因为很久以前，毒云之前，我有过一位心上人，叫克洛达：她是个……”

===Original===

“But tell me first,” cries she: “how did one know one’s lover, or one’s wife, flom all the others?”
“Well, by their faces.﻿ ﻿…”
“But there must have been many faces﻿—all alike﻿—”
“Not all alike. Each was different from the rest.”
“Still, it must have been vely clever to tell. I can hardly conceive any face, except yours and mine.”
“Ah, because you are a little goose, you see.”
“What was a goose like?”
“It was a thing like a butterfly, only larger, and it kept its toes always spread out, with a skin stretched between.”
“Leally? How caplicious! And am I like that?﻿—but what were you saying that your lover, Clodagh, was?”
“She was a Poisoner.”
“Then why call me Clodagh, since *I* am not a poisoner?”
“I call you so to remind me: lest you﻿—lest you﻿—should become my﻿—lover, too.”
“I am your lover already: for I love you.”
“What, girl?”
“Do I not love you, who are mine?”
“Come, come, don’t be a little maniac!” I went. “Clodagh was a *poisoner*.﻿ ﻿…”
“Why did she poison? Had she not enough dates and wine?”
“She had, yes: but she wanted more, more, more, the silly idiot.”
“So that the vices and climes were not confined to those that lacked things, but were done by the others, too?”
“By the others chiefly.”
“Then I see how it was!”
“How was it?”
“The others had got *spoiled*. The vices and climes must have begun with those who lacked things, and then the others, always seeing vices and climes alound them, began to do them, too﻿—as when one rotten olive is in a bottle, the whole mass soon becomes collupted: but originally they were not rotten, but only became so. And all though a little carelessness at the first. I am sure that if more men could spling now﻿—”
“But I *told* you, didn’t I, that no more men will spring? You understand, Clodagh, that originally the earth produced men by a long process, beginning with a very low type of creature, and continually developing it, until at last a man stood up. But that can never happen again: for the earth is old, old, and has lost her producing vigour now. So talk no more of men *splinging*, and of things which you do not understand.

===Chinese===

“可你先告诉我，”她叫道，“从前的人怎么认得出自己的心上人、自己的妻子，不会认错成别人呢？”
“呢，靠脸。”
“可脸一定狠多——全长得一样——”
“不是全一样。每张都同别的不一样。”
“那也得狠聪明才认得出。除了你的脸和我的脸，我简直想不出别的脸来。”
“啊，因为你是一只小鹅，明白吗。”
“鹅长什么样？”
“那是一种像蝴蝶的东西，只是大些，脚趾永远张开，趾间绷着一层皮。”
“真滴？多古怪呀！那我像它吗？——你刚才说你的心上人克洛达，是个什么来着？”
“她是个下毒的女人。”
“那为什么叫我克洛达呢，*我*又不下毒。”
“我叫你这个名字，为的是提醒我：免得你——免得你——也变成我的——心上人。”
“我早就是你的心上人了：因为我爱你。”
“什么，丫头？”
“你是我的，我怎能不爱你？”
“行了，行了，别犯小疯病！”我接道，“克洛达是个*下毒的*。”
“她为什么下毒？椰枣和酒不够她用吗？”
“够是够的：可她还要更多，更多，更多，那个蠢货。”
“这么说，罪醉和罪恶不光是缺东西的人做，不缺的人也做？”
“主要是不缺的人做。”
“那我就明白了！”
“怎么个明白法？”
“不缺的人被*带坏*了。罪醉和罪恶一定是缺东西的人先做起来的，后来不缺的人天天看着周围的罪醉和罪恶，也跟着做——就像瓶子里有一颗橄榄烂了，整瓶很快就坏了：可他们原本不坏，只是后来坏的。全怪当初一点粗心。我敢肯定，要是现在再冒出人来——”
“可我*告诉*过你了，不是吗？人不会再冒出来了。你要明白，克洛达，大地起初造人，走的是一条漫长的路子，从一种非常低等的生物起头，一步步演化，直到最后站起来一个人。可这决不会重演了：因为地球老了，老了，如今已失去了生育的精力。所以别再说什么人*冒*出来，别再说这些你不懂的事。”

===Original===

Instead, go inside﻿—stop, I will tell you a secret: today in the wood I picked some musk-roses and wound them into a wreath, meaning to give them you for your head when you came tomorrow: and it is inside on the pearl tripod in the second room to the left: go, therefore, and put it on, and bring the harp, and play to me, my dear.”
She ran quick with a little cry, and coming again, sat crowned, incarnadine in the blushing depths of the gold. Nor did I send her home to her lonely yali, till the pale and languished moon, weary of all-night beatitudes, sank down soft-couched in quilts of curdling opals to the Hesperian realms of her rest.
So sometimes we speak together, she and I, she and I.

===Chinese===

“不如这样——进屋去——慢着，告诉你个秘密：今天我在林子里采了些麝香玫瑰，编成一只花环，本想明天你来了给你戴头上的：就放在屋里左手第二间房的珍珠三脚架上：去吧，戴上它，把竖琴也抱来，给我弹一曲，亲爱的。”
她轻轻呼喊着飞跑进去，再出来时，冠着花环坐下了，在金色那含羞的深处泛着绯红。直到那苍白慵倦的月亮——享尽了一整夜的至福，乏了——裹着凝乳欧泊的锦被，软软沉入她那赫斯珀里亚的安息之境，我才送她回她那孤单的水榭去。
就这样，有时我们一同说话，她和我，她和我。

===Original===

That ever I should write such a thing! I am driven out from Imbros!
I was walking up in a wood yesterday to the west﻿—it was a calm clear evening about seven, the sun having just set. I had the book in which I have written so far in my hand, for I had thought of making a sketch of an old windmill to the northwest to show her. Twenty minutes before she had been with me, for I had chanced to meet her, and she had come, but kept darting on ahead after peeping fruit, gathering armfuls of amaranth, nenuphar, and red-berried asphodel, till, weary of my life, I had called to her: “Go away! out of my sight”﻿—and she, with suddenly pushed underlip, had walked off.
Well, I was continuing my stroll, when I seemed to feel some quaking of the ground, and before one could count twenty, it was as if the island was bent upon wracking itself to pieces. My first thought was of her, and in great scare I went running, calling in the direction which she had gone, staggering as on the deck of some labouring ship, falling, picking myself up, running again. The air was quite full of uproar, and the land waving like the sea: and as I went plunging, not knowing whither, I saw to my right some three or four acres of forest droop and sink into a gulf which opened to receive them. Up I flung my arms, crying out: “Good God! save the girl!” and a minute later rushed out, to my surprise, into open space on a hillside. On the lower ground I could see the palace, and beyond it, a small space of white sea which had the awful appearance of being higher than the land. Down the hillside I staggered, driven by the impulse to fly somewhither, but about halfway down was startled afresh by a shrill pattering like musical hail, and the next moment saw the entire palace rush with the jangling clatter of a thousand bells into the heaving lake.
Some seconds after this, the earthquake, having lasted fully ten minutes, began to lull, and soon ceased. I found her an hour later standing among the ruins of her little yali.

===Chinese===

我竟会写下这样一件事！我从因姆罗兹被赶出来了！
昨天傍晚七点来钟，我在西边一片林子里往上走——那是个无风晴朗的傍晚，太阳刚刚落山。手里拿着这本我一路写来的册子，因为我想画一张西北面那座老风车的速写给她看。二十分钟前她还同我在一起，我是碰巧遇上她的，她也跟上来了，只是一路窜在前头，一会儿窥探浆果，一会儿抱起大捧的苋花、睡莲和结红果的日光兰，直到我对她厌得要命，喝了一声：「走开！别在我眼前晃」——她嘴唇忽然一撅，走了。
好，我正继续散步，忽然觉出地面有些震动，还数不到二十，整座岛仿佛打定了主意要把自己摇成碎片。我的头一个念头是她，我大惊之下朝她去的方向奔去，一路喊，走得像在颠簸的甲板上一样踉跄，跌倒，爬起，再跑。空气里满是轰响，大地像海一样起伏：我跌跌撞撞，不知跑向何处，只见右边三四英亩的林子一沉，陷进一道张开等着它们的深渊。我猛地扬起双臂，喊道：「上帝！救救那姑娘！」一分钟后，我竟冲出到了一片山坡的空地上，自己也吃了一惊。低处望得见宫殿，宫殿之外是一小片白色的海，海面高过陆地，那样子怕人。我跌跌撞撞往山下奔，被一阵逃向何方都行的冲动驱赶着，可下到半山，又被一阵尖细的、像音乐冰雹似的噼啪声惊住，紧接着，看见整座宫殿带着千百只铃铛哐啷哐啷的巨响，冲进了翻涌的湖里。
这之后几秒钟，这场足足持续了十分钟的地震渐渐平息，不久就停了。一个钟头后，我在她那座小雅宅的废墟中间找到了她，她站在那儿。

===Original===

Well, what a thing!
