Probably every building on the island has been destroyed; the palace-platform, all cracked, leans half-sunken askew into the lake, like a huge stranded ark, while of the palace itself no trace remains, except a mound of gold stones emerging above the lake to the south. Gone, gone﻿—sixteen years of vanity and vexation. But from a practical point of view, what is a worst calamity of all is that the *Speranza* now lies high-and-dry in the village: for she was bodily picked up from the quay by the tidal wave, and driven bow-foremost into a street not half her width, and there now lies, looking huge enough in the little village, wedged forever, smashed in at the nip like a frail matchbox, a most astonishing spectacle: her bows forty feet up the street, ten feet above the ground at the stem, rudder resting on the inner edge of the quay, foremast tilted forward, the other two masts all right, and that bottom, which has passed through seas so far, buried in every sort of green and brown seaweed, the old *Speranza*. Her steps were there, and by a slight leap I could catch them underneath and go up handover-hand, till I got foothold; this I did at ten the same night when the seawater had mostly drained back from the land, leaving everything very swampy, however; she there with me, and soon following me upon the ship. I found most things cracked into tiny fragments, twisted, disfigured out of likeness, the house-walls themselves displaced a little at the nip, the bow of the cedar skiff smashed in to her middle against the aft starboard corner of the galley; and were it not for the fact that the air-pinnace had not broken from her heavy ropings, and one of the compasses still whole, I do not know what I should have done: for the four old waterlogged boats in the cove have utterly disappeared.
I made her sleep on the cabin-floor amid the debris of berth and everything, and I myself slept high up in the wood to the west. I am writing now lying in the long-grass the morning after, the sun rising, though I cannot see him. My plan for today is to cut three or four logs with the saw, lay them on the ground by the ship, lower the pinnace upon them, so get her gradually down into the water, and by evening bid a long farewell to Imbros, which drives me out in this way.

===Chinese===

好家伙，多大的事！岛上恐怕每座建筑都毁了；宫殿的平台裂满裂纹，半沉半歪地斜插进湖里，像一只巨大的搁浅方舟，至于宫殿本身，踪迹全无，只在南面湖水里露出一堆金色的石头。去了，去了——十六年的虚荣和烦扰。可从实际一面看，最糟的灾祸是斯佩兰扎号如今高搁在村子里：它被那阵潮浪整个从码头端了起来，船头朝前塞进一条不及它一半宽的街，如今就躺在那儿，在小村子里显得硕大无朋，永远楔死了，腰部凹瘪进去，像一只单薄的火柴盒，一幅再惊人不过的景象：船头冲进街上四十英尺，船首柱离地十英尺高，船舵搭在码头内沿，前桅向前倾着，另外两根桅杆还完好，那具闯过遥远重洋的船底，埋满了各式绿褐的海草——老斯佩兰扎号。它的舷梯还在那儿，我轻轻一跃便能抓着底下，攀援而上，直到找到立足处：这是当晚十点做的，那时海水大半已从陆地退回，只是处处仍泥泞不堪；她跟到那儿，不久也随我上了船。我发现大多数东西都震成了碎片，扭着，走了形，认不出本来面目，舱房的墙壁本身也在腰部错开了一点，雪松小艇的船头撞瘪到中间，抵在厨房右舷后角上；要不是汽艇没有挣脱它沉重的缆索，还有一只罗盘仍然完好，我真不知道该怎么办：因为湾里那四条泡烂的旧船，已经无影无踪了。
我让她在铺位的残骸和一片狼藉中间，睡在舱室的地上，我自己睡在西面高处的林子里。此刻是第二天清晨，我躺在长草里写，太阳正在升起，虽然我望不见它。我今天的计划是锯三四根圆木，铺在船旁的地上，把汽艇放低到圆木上，这样让它渐渐挪下水，到晚上，向这样把我赶出去的因姆罗兹，作一个长长的道别。

===Original===

Still, I look forward with pleasure to our hour’s run to the Mainland, when I shall teach her to steer by the compass, and manipulate liquid-air, as I have taught her to dress, to talk, to cook, to write, to think, to live. For she is my creation, this creature: as it were, a “rib from my side.”
But what is the design of this expulsion? And what was it that she called it last night?﻿—“this new going out flom Halan!” “Haran,” I believe, being the place from which Abraham went out, when “called” by God.

===Chinese===

不过我仍怀着快活，盼着我们往大陆去的那一个钟头的航程，届时我要教她照罗盘掌舵，摆弄液态空气，就像我教过她穿衣、说话、做饭、写字、思考、生活一样。因为她是我的造物，这个小东西：可以说，是「从我身上取出的一根肋骨」。
可这场驱逐的用意是什么呢？还有，她昨夜把它叫作什么来着？——「这次从哈兰再出来！」「哈兰」，我想，就是亚伯拉罕蒙上帝「呼召」时离开的那个地方。

===Original===

We apparently felt only the tail of the earthquake at Imbros: for it has ravaged Turkey! And we two poor helpless creatures put down here in the theatre of all these infinite violences: it is too bad, too bad. For the rages of Nature at present are perfectly astonishing, and what it may come to I do not know. When we came to the Macedonian coast in good moonlight, we sailed along it, and up the Dardanelles, looking out for village, yali, or any habitation where we might put up: but everything has apparently been wrecked. We saw Kilid-Bahr, Chanak-Kaleh, Gallipoli, Lapsaki in ruins; at the last place I landed, leaving her in the boat, and walked a little way, but soon went back with the news that there was not even a bazaar-arch left standing whole, in most parts even the line of the streets being obliterated, for the place had fallen like a house of dice, and had then been shaken up and jumbled. Finally we slept in a forest on the other side of the strait, beyond Gallipoli, taking our few provisions, and having to wade at some points through morass a foot deep before we reached dry woodland.
Here, the next morning, I sat alone﻿—for we had slept separated by at least half a mile﻿—thinking out the question of whither I should go: my choice would have been to remain either in the region where I was, or to go Eastward: but the region where I was offered no dwelling that I could see; and to go any distance Eastward, I needed a ship. Of ships I had seen during the night only wrecks, nor did I know where to find one in all these latitudes. I was thus, like her “Ablaham,” urged Westward.
In order, then, to go Westward, I first went a little further Eastward, once more entered the Golden Horn, and once more mounted the scorched Seraglio steps. Here what the wickedness of man had spared, the wickedness of Nature had destroyed, and the few houses which I had left standing round the upper part of Pera I now saw low as the rest; also the house near the Suleimanieh, where we had lived our first days, to which I went as to a home, I found without a pillar standing; and that night she slept under the half-roof of a little funeral-kiosk in the scorched cypress-wood of Eyoub, and I a mile away, at the edge of the forest where first I saw her.

===Chinese===

在伊姆罗兹岛上，我们显然只赶上了那场地震的尾巴：它把土耳其糟蹋了！而我们这两个可怜无助的生灵，偏偏被攔在这无穷暴行的剧场正中：太糟了，太糟了。如今大自然的暴怒实在骇人，它会闹到哪一步，我不知道。月色正好时我们到了马其顿海岸，沿岸行驶，又上了达达尼尔海峡，一路留心找个村子、水榭、随便什么落脚的住处：可一切显然都毁尽了。我们看见基利德巴赫尔、恰纳卡莱、加利波利、拉普萨基尽成废墟；在最后一处我上了岸，把她留在艇里，走进去一小段，很快就带着消息回来了：连一道市场的拱门都没有完整立着的，大部分地方连街道的走向都抹平了——那地方像一座骰子搭的房子似的塌了，随后又被摇散、搅乱。最后我们睡在海峡对岸、加利波利再过去的一片森林里，带着我们那点口粮；有几段路还得躞过一英尺深的沼泽，才到得了干燥的林地。
第二天早晨，我独自坐在这里——我们睡觉的地方至少隔了半英里——琢潣我该往哪里去：按我的意愿，要么就地留下，要么往东走：可眼下的地方找不出一处能住的房子；而往东走多远都需要一条船。夜里我见到的船尽是残骸，这一带纬度里哪儿能找到船，我也不知道。就这样，像她的“亚伯拉罕”一样，我被攔着往西去了。
于是，为了往西，我先稍稍往东：再一次驶进金角湾，再一次登上烧焦的苏丹后宫台阶。人的恶毒手下留情的，大自然的恶毒给毁了；我在佩拉上段特意留下的那几所房子，如今看见也同别处一样趴了窝；苏莱曼尼耶附近那所房子——我们最初几日住过的、我当作家去投的——我去一看，一根立柱也不剩；那一夜，她睡在埃于普焦枯柏树林中一座小殡亭的半边屋顶下，我睡在一英里外，当初头一回看见她的那片林子边上。

===Original===

The next morning, having met, as agreed, at the site of the Prophet’s mosque, we traversed together the valley and cemetery of Kassim by the quagmires up to Pera, all the landscape having to me a rather twisted unfamiliar aspect. We had determined to spend the morning in searching for supplies among the earthquake-ruins of Pera; and as I had decided to collect sufficient in one day to save us further pains for some time, we passed a good many hours in this task, I confining myself to the great white house in the park overlooking Kassim, where I had once slept, losing myself in the huge obliquities of its floors, roofs and wall-fragments, she going to the old Mussulman quarter of Djianghir near, on the heights of Taxim, where were many shops, and thence round the brow of the hill to the great French Embassy-house, overlooking Foundoucli and the sea, both of us having large Persian carpetbags, and all in the air of that wilderness of ruin that morning a sweet, strong, permanent odour of maple-blossom.
We met toward evening, she quivering under such a load, that I would not let her carry it, but abandoned my day’s labour, which was lighter, and took hers, which was quite enough: we went back Westward, seeking all the while some shelter from the saturating night-dews of this place: and nothing could we find, till we came again, quite late, to her broken funeral-kiosk at the entrance to the immense cemetery-avenue of Eyoub. There without a word I left her among the shattered catafalques, for I was weary; but having gone some distance, turned back, thinking that I might take some more raisins from the bag; and after getting them, said to her, shaking her little hand where she sat under the roof-shadow on a stone:
“Good night, Clodagh.”
She did not answer promptly: and her answer, to my surprise, was a protest against her name: for a rather sulky, yet gentle, voice came from the darkness, saying:
“I am *not* a Poisoner!”
“Well,” said I, “all right: tell me whatever you like that I should call you, and henceforth I will call you that.”
“Call me Eve,” says she.
“Well, no,” said I, “not Eve, anything but that: for *my* name is Adam, and if I called you Eve, that would be simply absurd, and we do not want to be ridiculous in each other’s eyes. But I will call you anything else that you like.”
“Call me Leda,” says she.
“And why Leda?” said I.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，我们按约定在先知寺的旧址碰了头，一起蹚着泥沼穿过卡西姆谷地和墓园，上到佩拉；满眼景物在我看都有点歪扭陌生。
我们打算用一上午在佩拉的震灾废墟里搜寻补给；我打定主意一天凑足，省得日后再度劳神，于是这活儿干了好几个钟头：我专管园中那座俯瞰卡西姆的大白宅——我在那里睡过一觉——在它东倾西斜的地板、屋顶和残墙之间钻来钻去；她去近处塔克西姆高岗上旧穆斯林区姜吉尔，那边铺子多，然后绕过山嘴，到俯瞰芬杜克利和大海的大法国使馆宅邸去。我们俩都背着大波斯毯袋；那天早晨，那片废墟荒野的空气里，浮着枫树花一种甜而浓、经久不散的气味。
傍晚我们会合，她被那么一副重担压得直打颤，我不许她再背：我掠下自己那份较轻的收获，接过了她那份——也够沉了：我们往西折回，一路寻个遮蔽，好躲开此地夜里浸人的露水：什么也找不到，直到很晚，又回到了埃于普那片大墓道口她那座破殡亭。我在那些坍塌的灵柩台中间撇下她，一句话没说，因为我实在乏了；可走出一段，又折回来，想着从袋子里再抓些葡萄干；抓完，对坐在屋顶影子下一块石头上的她晃了晃她的小手，说：“晚安，克洛达。”
她没有立刻应声：而她的回答，叫我意外，是对她名字的抗议：黑暗里传来一个有点怄气、却又温柔的声音：“我*不是*下毒的女人！”
“好，”我说，“行：你愿意我叫你什么，告诉我，往后我就那么叫你。”
“叫我夏娃。”她说。
“嗯，不成，”我说，“不能叫夏娃，叫什么都行，就是这个不行：因为*我*的名字是亚当，我若叫你夏娃，那简直是荒唐，咱们犯不着在彼此眼里沦为笑柄。你要喜欢，别的我都能叫。”
“叫我莉达。”她说。
“为什么叫莉达？”我说。

===Original===

“Because Leda sounds something like Clodagh,” says she, “and you are al‑leady in the habit of calling me Clodagh; and I saw the name Leda in a book, and liked it: but Clodagh is most hollible, most bitterly hollible!”
“Well, then,” said I, “Leda it shall be, and I shan’t forget, for I like it, too, and it suits you, and you ought to have a name beginning with an *L*. Good night, my dear, sleep well, and dream, dream.”
“And to you, too, my God give dleams of peace and pleasantness,” says she; and I went.
And it was only when I had lain myself upon leaves for my bed, my head on my caftan, a rill for my lullaby, and two stars, which alone I could see out of the heavenful, for my watch-lights; and only when my eyes were already closed toward slumber, that a sudden strong thought pierced and woke me: for I remembered that Leda was the name of a Greek woman who had borne twins. In fact, I should not be surprised if this Greek word Leda is the same word etymologically as the Hebrew Eve, for I have heard of *v*’s, and *b*’s, and *d*’s interchanging about in this way, and if *Di*, meaning God, or Light, and *Bi*, meaning Life, and Io*v*e, and Iho*v*ah and Go*d*, meaning much the same, are all one, that would be nothing astonishing to me, as wi*d*ow, and veu*v*e, are one: and where it says, “truly the Light is Good (*tob*, *b*on),” this is as if it said, “truly the Di is Di.” Such, at any rate, is the fatality that attends me, even in the smallest things: for this Western Eve, or Greek Leda, had twins.

===Chinese===

“因为莉达听着有点像克洛达，”她说，“你叫惯我克洛达了，改口容易；我在一本书上见过莉达这个名字，喜欢：可克洛达狠可怕，狠狠可怕！”
“那么，”我说，“就叫莉达，我忘不了的，我也喜欢，正配你，你原就该有个*L*打头的名字。晚安，亲爱的，好好睡，做梦，做梦。”
“也祝你，愿我的上帝给你安宁又愉快的梦。”她说；我走了。
直到我躺在树叶铺的床上，头枕着长袍，一脉小溪作我的摇篮曲，满天里独独望得见的两颗星作我的守夜灯；直到我的眼睛已经合向睡乡，一个又猛又强的念头才扎进来，把我扎醒：我想起来了，莉达，是一个生过双生子的希腊女人的名字。
说真的，就算这个希腊词“莉达”在字源上同希伯来语的“夏娃”本是同一个词，我也不会惊讶：我听说过*v*、*b*、*d*这样变来变去的事；倘若意为神、为光的“*Di*”，意为生命的“*Bi*”，还有 Io*v*e、Iho*v*ah 和 Go*d*——意思都差不多——本是一体，那在我毫不稀奇，正如 wi*d*ow 同 veu*v*e 本是一词；经上说“光实在是好的（*tob*，*b*on）”，那就等于说“神实在是神”。反正，缠着我的这股宿命，连最小的事也不放过：因为这位西方的夏娃，这位希腊的莉达，生的是双生子。

===Original===

Well, the next morning we crossed by the ruins of old Greek Phanar across the triple Stamboul-wall, which still showed its deep-ivied portal, and made our way, not without climbing, along the Golden Horn to the foot of the Old Seraglio, where I soon found signs of the railway. And that minute commenced our journey across Turkey, Bulgaria, Serbia, Bosnia, Croatia, to Trieste, occupying no day or two as in old times, but four months, a long-drawn nightmare, though a nightmare of rich happiness, if one may say so, leaving on the memory a vague vast impression of monstrous ravines, ever-succeeding profundities, heights and greatnesses, jungles strange as some moonstruck poet’s fantasy, everlasting glooms, and a sound of mighty unseen rivers, cataracts, and slow cumbered rills whose bulrushes never see the sun, with largesse everywhere, secrecies, profusions, the unimaginable, the unspeakable, a savagery most lush and fierce and gaudy, and vales of Arcadie, and remote mountain-peaks, and tarns shy as old-buried treasure, and glaciers, and we two human folk pretty small and drowned and lost in all that amplitude, yet moving always through it.
We followed the lines that first day till we came to a steam train, and I found the engine fairly good, and everything necessary to move it at my hand: but the metals in such a condition of twisted, broken, vaulted, and buried confusion, due to the earthquake, that, having run some hundreds of yards to examine them, I saw that nothing could be done in that way. At first this threw me into a condition like despair, for what we were to do I did not know: but after persevering on foot for four days along the deep-rusted track, which is of that large-gauge type peculiar to Eastern Europe, I began to see that there were considerable sound stretches, and took heart.
I had with me land-charts and compass, but nothing for taking altitude-observations: for the *Speranza* instruments, except one compass, had all been broken-up by her shock. However, on getting to the town of Silivri, about thirty miles from our start, I saw in the ruins of a half-standing bazaar-shop a number of brass objects, and there found several good sextants, quadrants, and theodolites.

===Chinese===

好，第二天早晨，我们穿过希腊老法纳尔的废墟，越过三重斯坦布尔城墙——墙上还看得出爬满常春藤的幽深门洞——沿金角湾一路走去，不乏攀爬，走到老皇宫脚下，不久我就寻到了铁路的迹象。那一刻，我们横跨土耳其、保加利亚、塞尔维亚、波斯尼亚、克罗地亚直抵的里雅斯特的旅程开始了——不像旧时一两天，而是走了四个月：一场拖长了的噩梦，虽然不妨说是富于幸福的噩梦，在记忆里留下一片浩大模糊的印象：狰狞的峡谷，接踵而至的深壑，高与大的极致，怪异如梦游诗人幻境的丛林，永恒的幽暗，还有听不见的巨河、飞瀑、和那芦苇永远见不着太阳的、滞重的细流之声，处处是丰饶、隐秘、繁盛，那不可想象、不可言说的东西，一片最葱茏、最凶悍、最艳丽的蛮荒，还有阿卡狄式的山谷，渺远的山峰，羞怯如深埋宝藏的山中小湖，和冰川，而我们两个人儿，在这浩瀚里小得可怜，溺没、迷失，却总在其中穿行。
头一天我们沿着铁轨走到一列蒸汽机车跟前，我发现车头相当完好，开动它需要的一切都唾手可得：可铁轨经了地震，扭曲、断裂、拱起、掩埋，乱作一团，我沿线查看出去几百码，便看出此路不通。起初这叫我陷入近乎绝望的境地，因为我们该怎么办我不知道：可沿着那条锈迹斑斑的深轨——那是东欧特有的大轨距——徒步坚持了四天之后，我开始看出相当长的几段完好无损，便鼓起了心劲。
我随身带着地图和罗盘，却没有测高的仪器：斯佩兰扎号上的仪表，除了一只罗盘，全在那一震里毁了。然而到了离出发地约三十英里的锡利夫里镇，我在一家半立半塌的巴扎铺子的残骸里望见一些黄铜物件，在那儿寻到了几具完好的六分仪、象限仪和经纬仪。

===Original===

Two mornings later, we came upon an engine in mid-country, with coals in it, and a stream near; I had a goatskin of almond-oil in the bag, and found the machinery serviceable after an hour’s careful inspection, having examined the boiler with a candle through the manhole, and removed the autoclaves of the heaters. All was red with rust, and the shaft of the connecting-rod in particular seemed so frail, that at one moment I was very dubious: I decided, however, and, except for a slight leakage at the tubulure which led the steam to the valve-chest, all went very well; at a pressure never exceeding three-and-a-half atmospheres, we travelled nearly a hundred and twenty miles before being stopped by a head-to-head block on the line, when we had to abandon our engine; we then continued another seven miles afoot, I all the time mourning my motor, which I had had to leave at Imbros, and hoping at every townlet to find a whole one, but in vain.

===Chinese===

又过了两天早晨，我们在半路旷野上撞见一台机车，车里存着煤，近旁有一道溪流；我袋里有一羊皮囊杏仁油，仔细查验了一个钟头之后，发觉机器还能用——我举着蜡烛从人孔里检查了锅炉，卸掉了加热器的密闭罐。一切都锈得通红，连杆的轴尤其单薄，有一阵我非常狐疑：不过我下了决心，除了通往阀室的接口管有一点轻微漏汽，一切顺利：在从不超过三个半大气压的汽压下，我们走了将近一百二十英里，才被线路上迎头顶死的一处阻塞拦住，只得丢下机车；随后又徒步走了七英里——我一路哀悼着我那台不得不留在因姆罗兹的摩托车，指望在每个小镇上寻到一辆完好的，却总是落空。

===Original===

It was wonderful to see the villages and towns going back to the earth, already invaded by vegetation, and hardly any longer breaking the continuity of pure Nature, the town now as much the country as the country, and that which is not-Man becoming all in all with a certain furore of vigour. A whole day in the southern gorges of the Balkan Mountains the slow train went tearing its way through many a mile of bindweed tendrils, a continuous curtain, flaming with large flowers, but sombre as the falling shades of night, rather resembling jungles of Ceylon and the Filipinas; and she, that day, lying in the single car behind, where I had made her a little *yatak*-bed from Tatar Bazardjik, continually played the kittur, barely touching the strings, and crooning low, low, in her rich contralto, eternally the same air, over and over again, crooning, crooning, some melancholy tune of her own dreaming, just audible to me through the slow-travailing monotony of the engine; till I was drunken with so sweet a woe, my God, a woe that was sweet as life, and a dolour that lulled like nepenthe, and a grief that soothed like kisses, so sweet, so sweet, that all that world of wood and gloom lost locality and realness for me, and became nothing but a charmed and pensive Heaven for her to moan and lullaby in; and from between my fingers streamed plenteous tears that day, and all that I could keep on mourning was “O Leda, O Leda, O Leda,” till my heart was near to break.
The feed-pump eccentric-shaft of this engine, which was very poor and flaky, suddenly gave out about five in the afternoon, and I had to stop in a hurry, and that sweet invisible mechanism which had crooned and crooned about my ears in the air, and followed me whithersoever I went, stopped too. Down she jumped, calling out:
“Well, I had a plesentiment that something would happen, and I am so glad, for I was tired!”

===Chinese===

眼看村庄和城镇重归大地，实在奇妙：草木已经侵入，几乎不再打断纯粹自然界的绵延，城镇如今同乡村一样成了乡野，那「非人」的一切正带着一股狂热的劲头，日益成为一切的一切。在巴尔干山脉南麓的峡谷里，整整一天，那列慢车撕开一道又一道几英里长的旋花藤蔓——连绵不绝的一道帘幕，燃着大朵大朵的花，却暗得像垂落的夜色，倒像锡兰和菲律宾群岛的丛林；那一天，她躺在后面那节孤单的车厢里——我在塔塔尔巴扎吉克给她铺了张小小的坐褥床——不停地拨那把基图尔琴，指头几乎不碰弦，用她那丰厚的女低音低低地、低低地哼着，永远是同一支调子，一遍又一遍，哼着，哼着，某支她自己梦里的忧郁曲子，刚好透过机车缓慢吃力的单调传进我耳朵；直到我被那甜蜜的忧伤灌醉，我的上帝，那忧伤甜得像生命，那哀恸像忘忧药一样催眠，那悲苦像亲吻一样抚慰，那么甜，那么甜，那一整个木头和幽暗的世界对我失去了方位和真实，变成一座只供她呻吟和安摇篮曲的、蛊惑而愁思的天堂；那天泪水从我指缝间汩汩流淌，我能不断哀哭的只有「哦勒达，哦勒达，哦勒达」，直到我的心几乎碎掉。
这台机车的给水泵偏心轴本来单薄起皮，下午五点来钟忽然断了，我只好急忙停车；于是那支一直在我耳边的空中低吟低唱、我走到哪儿跟到哪儿的、看不见的甜美机括，也停了。她跳下车，喊道：
「哈，我早有个预感要出事，这下可高兴了，因为我累了！」

===Original===

Seeing that nothing could be done with the feed-water pump, I got down, took the bag, and parting before us the continuous screen, we went pioneering to the left between a rock-cleft, stepping over large stones that looked black with moss-growths, no sky, but hundreds of feet of impenetrable leafage overhead, and everywhere the dew-dabbled profusion of dim ferneries, dishevelled maidenhairs mixed with a large-leaved mimosa, wild vine, white briony, and a smell of cedar, and a soft rushing of perpetual waters that charmed the gloaming. The way led slightly upwards three hundred feet, and presently, after some windings, and the climbing of five huge steps almost regular, yet obviously natural, the gorge opened in a roundish space, fifty feet across, with far overhanging edges seven hundred feet high; and there, behind a curtain which fell from above, its tendrils defined and straight like a Japanese bead-hanging, we spread the store of foods, I opening the wines, fruits, vegetables and meats, she arranging them in order with the gold plate, and lighting both the spirit-lamp and the lantern: for here it was quite dark. Near us behind the curtain of tendrils was a small green cave in the rock, and at its mouth a pool two yards wide, a black and limpid water that leisurely wheeled, discharging a little rivulet from the cave: and in it I saw three owl-eyed fish, a finger long, loiter, and spur themselves, and gaze. Leda, who cannot be still in tongue or limb, chattered in her glib baby manner as we ate, and then, after smoking a cigarette, said that she would go and “lun,” and went, and left me darkling, for she is the sun and the moon and the host of the stars, I occupying myself that night in making a calendar at the end of this book in which I have written, for my almanac and many things that I prized were lost with the palace﻿—making a calendar, counting the days in my head﻿—but counting them across my thoughts of her.
She came again to tell me good night, and then went down to the train to sleep; and I put out the lantern, and stooped within the cave, and made my simple couch beside the little rivulet, and slept.

===Chinese===

眼看给水泵没法弄了，我下了车，拿起提袋，拨开面前那道连绵的帘幕，我们向左在一条岩缝间开路，跨过一块块长满黑苔的大石，头顶没有天，只有几百英尺不透光的枝叶，四下是沾满露水的、成片幽暗的蕨丛，披散的铁线蕨混着大叶含羞草、野葡萄、白泻根，一股雪松的气味，还有那不息流水的轻响，给黄昏施了魔法。路微微向上走了三百英尺，不久，转过几道弯，登上五级几乎是等高、却显然出自天然的巨大石阶，峡谷豁然张开一块圆场，宽五十英尺，四缘高悬七百英尺；就在那儿，一道从上垂落的帘幕后面——藤蔓的须子笔直齐整，像一挂日本的珠帘——我们铺开储备的吃食，我开酒、水果、蔬菜和肉，她用金盘子一样样摆好，点亮酒精灯和灯笼：因为这里已经相当黑了。近旁，藤帘后面，岩壁上有一个绿色的小洞，洞口一泓两码宽的水潭，黑而清的水悠悠打旋，从洞里放出一股细流：我在水里看见三条鸮眼的小鱼，一指长，游游停停，瞪眼张望。勒达这个人，舌头和手脚都闲不住，我们吃的时候，她用她那流利的娃娃腔絮絮地说，随后抽过一支烟，说她要去「跑一跑」，去了，把我撇在黑暗里——因为她就是太阳，是月亮，是满天的星辰；那一夜我忙着在这本写过的书末尾编一份历书，因为我的历书和许多心爱的东西同宫殿一起失掉了——编历书，在心里数着日子——却是隔着对她的思念数过去，又数过来。
她又回来跟我说了晚安，然后下到火车上睡觉；我熄了灯笼，躬身进洞，在那细流旁铺了我简单的卧处，睡了。

===Original===

But a fitful sleep, and soon again I woke; and a long time I lay so, gradually becoming conscious of a slow dripping at one spot in the cave: for at a minute’s interval it darkly splashed, regularly, very deliberately; and it seemed to grow always louder and sadder, and the splash at first was “Leesha,” but it became “Leda” to my ears, and it sobbed her name, and I pitied myself, so sad was I. And when I could no longer bear the anguished melancholy of its spasm and its sobbing, I arose and went softly, softly, lest she should hear in that sounding silence of the hushed and darksome night, going more slow, more soft, as I went nearer, a sob in my throat, my feet leading me to her, till I touched the carriage. And against it a long time I leant my clammy brow, a sob aching in my poor throat, and she all mixed up in my head with the suspended hushed night, and with the elfin things in the air that made the silence so musically a-sound to the vacant eardrum, and with the dripping splash in the cave. And softly I turned the door-handle, and heard her breathe in Asleep, her head near me; and I touched her hair with my lips, and close to her ear I said﻿—for I heard her breathe as if in sleep﻿—“Little Leda, I have come to you, for I could not help it, Leda: and oh, my heart is full of the love of you, for you are mine, and I am yours: and to live with you, till we die, and after we are dead to be near you still, Leda, with my broken heart near your heart, little Leda﻿—”
I must have sobbed, I think; for as I spoke close at her ears, with passionately dying eyes of love, I was startled by an irregularity in her breathing; and with cautious hurry I shut the door, and quite back to the cave I stole in haste.
And the next morning when we met I thought﻿—but am not now sure﻿—that she smiled singularly: I thought so. She may, she *may*, have heard﻿—But I cannot tell.

===Chinese===

却睡得断断续续，不久又醒了；我这样躺了很久，渐渐察觉洞里有一处缓慢的滴水声：每隔一分钟，暗暗地溅响一声，规规矩矩，不慌不忙；它仿佛越来越响，越来越愁，起初那溅声是「丽莎」，后来在我耳朵里变成了「勒达」，它啜泣着她的名字，我怜惜起自己来，我那么愁。等到那痉挛般痛苦的哀愁和啜泣我再受不了的时候，我起身，轻轻地、轻轻地走去，生怕她在那寂灭昏夜鸣响的静默里听见，越走近，走得越慢、越轻，喉咙里堵着一阵呜咽，脚把我领到她跟前，直到我触到车厢。我把汗津津的额头抵在车厢上，靠了很久，可怜的喉咙里一阵呜咽作痛，她在我脑子里同这悬停的静夜混作一团，同空气中那些把寂静敲出声响、敲进空落耳膜的小精灵混作一团，同洞里的滴水声混作一团。我轻轻转动门把，听见她在睡梦里呼吸，她的头离我很近；我用嘴唇碰了碰她的头发，凑在她耳边说——因为我听见她呼吸得像睡着——「小勒达，我到你这儿来了，因为我忍不住了，勒达：哦，我的心装满了对你的爱，因为你是我的，我是你的：同你一起活，活到我们死，死了以后还挨着你，勒达，让我这颗碎心挨着你的心，小勒达——」
我想我一定呜咽出了声：因为我带着热恋将熄的眼睛，贴着她耳边说话时，她呼吸里的一点不规律吓了我一跳；我慌忙轻轻带上车门，一路溜回洞里去了。
第二天早晨我们见面时，我想——如今却拿不准了——她笑得有些异样：我当时那么想。她可能，她可能听见了——可我说不好。

===Original===

Twice I was obliged to abandon engines on account of forest-tree obstructions right across the line, which, do what I might, I could not move, and these were the two bitterest incidents of the pilgrimage; and at least thirty times I changed from engine to engine, when other trains blocked. As for the extent of the earthquake, it is pretty certain that it was universal over the Peninsula, and at many points exhibited extreme violence, for up to the time that we entered upon Serbian territory, we occasionally came upon stretches of the lines so dislocated, that it was impossible to proceed upon them, and during the whole course I never saw one intact house or castle; four times, where the way was of a nature to permit of it, I left the imbedded metals and made the engine travel the ground till I came upon other metals, when I always succeeded in driving it upon them. It was all very leisurely, for not everywhere, nor every day, could I get a nautical observation, and having at all times to go at low pressures for fear of tube and boiler weakness, crawling through tunnels, and stopping when total darkness came on, we did not go fast, nor much cared to. Once, moreover, for three days, and once for four, we were overtaken by hurricanes of such vast inclemency, that no thought of travelling entered our heads, our only care being to hide our poor cowering bodies as deeply and darkly as possible. Once I passed through a city (Adrianople) doubly devastated, once by the hellish arson of my own hand, and once by the earthquake: and I made haste to leave that place behind me.
Finally, three months and twenty-seven days from the date of the earthquake, having traversed only 900 odd English miles, I let go in the Venice lagoon, in the early morning of the 10th September, the lateen sail and stone anchor of a Maltese *speronare*, which I had found, and partially cleaned, at Trieste; and thence I passed up the Canalazzo in a gondola. For I said to Leda: “In Venice will I pitch my Patriarch tent.”
But to will and to do are not the same thing, and still further Westward was I driven.

===Chinese===

有两回，横在路轨上的林木死死堵住了去路，我使尽办法也挪它不动，只得弃了机车——这是整趟旅程中最痛快的两桩苦事；别的列车堵道时，我换车头换足了有三十回。至于地震的范围，几乎可以肯定它席卷了整个半岛，许多地方还显出极度的暴烈：直到我们进入塞尔维亚地界，还不时撞见一段段铁轨扭曲错断、根本没法通行；而全程之中，我没有见过一座完好的房屋或城堡；有四次，路段条件允许，我让机车下了陷在里面的轨道，在地面上行驶，直到撞见另一副铁轨，而每回我都成功把车头开了上去。
一切都从容得很：不是处处、也不是天天能测天定方位；又怕锅炉管道年久乏力，时时只敢低压行驶；钻隧道要慢慢爬，天全黑就停车——我们走得不快，也无意走快。此外有一回三天、有一回四天，飓风追上了我们，凶寒浩大，赶路的心思根本进不了脑袋，唯一挂心的是把我们可怜哆嗦的身子藏得尽量深、尽量暗。有一回我经过一座城（阿德里安堡），它被毁了两遍：一遍毁于我亲手点的地狱之火，一遍毁于地震：我急急把它抛在了身后。
终于，地震之后三个月零二十七天，总共才走了九百多英里，9月10日清晨，我在威尼斯潟湖里放下了那条马耳他*speronare*三角帆船的斜帆和石锚——船是在的里雅斯特寻到、草草洗刷过的；随后我乘凤尾船上了大运河。因为我对莉达说过：“我要在威尼斯支起我族长的大帐。”
可想归想，做归做，我又被攔着再往西去。

===Original===

For the stagnant upper canals of this place are now mere miasmas of pestilence: and within two days I was rolling with fever in the Old Procurazie Palace, she standing in pale wonderment at my bedside, sickness quite a novel thing to her: and, indeed, this was my first serious illness since my twentieth year or thereabouts, when I had overworked my brain, and went a voyage to Constantinople. I could not move from bed for some weeks, but happily did not lose my senses, and she brought me the whole pharmacopoeia from the shops, from which to choose my medicines. I guessed the cause of this illness, though not a sign of it came near her, and as soon as my trembling knees could bear me, I again set out﻿—always Westward﻿—enjoying now a certain luxury in travelling compared with that Turkish difficulty, for here were no twisted metals, more and better engines, in the cities as many good petrol motors as I chose, and Nature markedly less savage.
I do not know why I did not stop at Verona or Brescia, or some other neighbourhood of the Italian lakes, since I was fond of water: but I had, I think, the thought in my head to return to Vauclaire in France, where I had lived, and there live: for I thought that she might like those old monks. At all events, we did not remain long in any place till we came to Turin, where we spent nine days, she in the house opposite mine, and after that, at her own suggestion, went on still, passing by train into the valley of the Isère, and then into that of the Western Rhone, till we came to the old town of Geneva among some very great mountains peaked with snow, the town seated at the head of a long lake which the earth has made in the shape of the crescent moon, and like the moon it is a thing of much beauty and many moods, suggesting a creature under the spell of charms and magics. However, with this idea of Vauclaire still in my head, we left Geneva in the motor which had brought us at four in the afternoon of the 17th May, I intending to reach the town called Bourg that night about eight, and there sleep, so to go on to Lyons the next morning by train, and so, by the Bordeaux route, make Vauclaire.

===Chinese===

因为这地方地势高的那些运河如今死水不流，纯粹成了瘟瘅的渊薮：不出两天，我便在旧总督府官邸里发着高烧打滚，她站在我床边，脸色发白，惊疑不定——生病在她完全是桩新鲜事：说真的，这也是我二十岁上下以来头一场大病，那年我用脑过度，出海跑了一趟君士坦丁堡。有几个星期我下不了床，幸好神志清明，她把药铺里整部药典都搬了来，供我挑药。
这病的根由我猜到了，虽然它一丝一毫没沾到她身上；等我的膝盖撑得住我了，我便重新上路——始终向西——比起土耳其那一段的艰难，如今旅行简直算得上奢侈：这里没有扭断的铁轨，机车又多又好，城里随我挑的好汽油车要多少有多少，大自然也明显收敛了野性。
我说不清为什么没有在维罗纳或布雷西亚停下，或在意大利湖区旁的什么地方住下——我原是爱水的：可我想，我心里装着个念头：回法国的沃克莱尔去，我在那儿住过，就在那儿住下：因为我想，她或许会喜欢那些老修士。无论如何，我们在哪一站都没久留，直到都灵，我们在那里盘桓了九天，她住在我对面的宅子里；之后，依着她自己的主意，我们继续走，乘火车进伊斯河谷，再入西罗讷河谷，终于到了日内瓦老城——它坐落在一群积雪封顶的大山中间，城踞在一泓长湖的源头，那湖是大地造的新月形，又像月亮，是件美丽而多变的东西，叫人想到一个中了魔咒与妖法的生灵。
不过，沃克莱尔的念头还盘在心头，5月17日下午四点，我们乘来时的那辆汽车离开日内瓦：我打算当晚八点左右赶到叫布尔的那座城，在那儿过夜，第二天一早坐火车去里昂，然后取道波尔多，直抵沃克莱尔。

===Original===

But by some chance for which I cannot to this hour account (unless the rain was the cause), I missed the chart-road, which should have been fairly level, and found myself on mountain tracks, unconscious of my whereabouts, while darkness fell, and a windless downpour that had a certain sullen venom in its superabundance drenched us. I stopped several times, looking about for château, chalet, or village, but none did I see, though I twice came upon railway lines; and not till midnight did we run down a rather steep pass upon the shore of a lake, which, from its apparent vastness in the moonless obscurity, I could only suppose to be the Lake of Geneva once again. About two hundred yards to the left we saw through the rain a large pile, apparently risen straight out of the lake, looking ghostly livid, for it was of white stone, not high, but an old thing of complicated white little turrets roofed with dark red candle extinguishers, and oddities of Gothic nooks, window slits, and outline, very like a fanciful picture. Round to this we went, drowned as rats, Leda sighing and bedraggled, and found a narrow spit of low land projecting into the lake, where we left the car, walked forward with the bag, crossed a small wooden drawbridge, and came upon a rocky island with a number of thick-foliaged trees about the castle. We quickly found a small open portal, and went throughout the place, quite gay at the shelter, everywhere lighting candles which we found in iron sconces in the rather queer apartments: so that, as the castle is far seen from the shores of the lake, it would have appeared to one looking thence a place suddenly possessed and haunted. We found beds, and slept: and the next day it turned out to be the antique Castle of Chillon, where we remained five long and happy months, till again, again, Fate overtook us.

===Chinese===

可不知是碰上了什么巧——到这会儿我也说不清（除非那雨就是缘故）——我错过了图上那条本该相当平缓的大路，发觉自己上了山径，辨不清身在何处；这时夜幕落下来，一场无风的豪雨，那过分的丰沛里带着一股阴沉的毒，把我们浇了个透。
我停了好几回车，四下张望，找城堡、木屋或村庄，可什么也没看见，虽然两次撞上铁路线；直到午夜，我们才顺着一条颇陡的隘口冲到一片湖岸——在无月的昏黑里，那湖显得那样浩大，我只能猜又是日内瓦湖。左边约两百码，我们隔雨望见一大堆建筑，仿佛从湖里直拔出来，泛着鬼气森森的死白：它是白石砌的，不高，却是一件古旧的物事，一座座繁复的白色小塔，顶着一顶顶暗红的烛罩似的尖顶，还有种种哥特式的怪角落、窄窗缝和轮廓，活脱一幅奇幻画。
我们绕着朝它驶去，一个个落汤之鼠，莉达叹着气，湿得狼狈不堪；我们找到一条探进湖里的低窄沙嘴，把车攔在那里，提着袋子往前走，跨过一座小木吊桥，登上一座岩岛，岛上一座城堡，四围长着一丛丛枝叶浓密的树。我们很快找到一扇敞着的小门，便把整座城堡走了一遍，得了遮蔽，兴高采烈，在那些颇为古怪的房间里，把铁壁台上找得到的蜡烛一根根点上：于是乎，这城堡在湖岸上远远可见，从岸上望来，它必定像一处骤然被鬼魂盘踞的地方。
我们寻到床，睡了：第二天才知道，这是古老的希永城堡；我们在那里住了整整五个月，长长的、快活的五个月——直到命运又一次、又一次追上我们。

===Original===

The morning after our coming, we had breakfast﻿—our last meal together﻿—on the first floor in a pentagonal room approached from a lower level by three little steps. In it is a ponderous oak table pierced with a multitude of worm eaten tunnels, also three mighty high backed chairs, an old oak desk covered still with papers, arras on the walls, and three dark religious oil paintings, and a grandfathers clock: it is at about the middle of the château, and contains two small, but deep, three faced oriels, in each face four compartments with white stone shafts between, these looking south upon shrubs and the rocky edge of the island, then upon the deep blue lake, then upon another tiny island containing four trees in a jungle of flowers, then upon the shore of the lake interrupted by the mouths of a river which turned out to be the Rhone, then upon a white town on the slopes which turned out to be Villeneuve, then upon the great mountains back of Bouveret and St. Gingolph, all having the surprised air of a resurrection just completed, everything new washed in dyes of azure, ultramarine, indigo, snow, emerald, that fresh morning: so that one had to call it the best and holiest place in the world. These five old room walls, and oak floor, and two oriels, became specially mine, though it was really common ground to us both, and there I would do many little things. The papers on the desk told that it had been the bureau of one R. E. Gaud, *Grand Bailli*, whose residence the place no doubt had been.
She asked me while eating that morning to stay here, and I said that I would see, though with misgiving: so together we went all about the house, and finding it unexpectedly spacious, I consented to stop.

===Chinese===

到达后的第二天早晨，我们在二楼吃了早餐——我们同桌的最后一顿——那是一间五角形的屋子，从下面一层上三级小台阶进去。屋里有一张笨重的橡木桌，布满虫蛀的隧道，还有三把高背大椅，一张还摊着纸的旧橡木书桌，墙上是花毯挂毯和三幅幽暗的宗教油画，一座落地大钟：屋子大约在城堡正中，开有两扇小而深的三面凸窗，每面四格，格间是白石窗棂，向南望见灌木和岛的石缘，再望是深蓝的湖，再望是另一座小小的岛，花的丛林里长着四棵树，再望是湖岸，被一条河的几处河口打断——那河原来是罗讷河，再望是坡上一座白镇——原来是维伦纽夫，再望是布弗雷和圣然戈尔夫背后的大山，全都带着一场刚刚完成的复活所特有的惊讶神情，样样都新洗过，染着蔚蓝、群青、靛蓝、雪白、翠绿，那个清新的早晨：叫人不能不称它做世上最好、最圣洁的地方。这五面老屋的墙、橡木地板和两扇凸窗，成了我特别的所在，虽然其实是我们两人共有的地方，我在那儿要做许多小小的事。书桌上的纸表明，这里从前是一位 R·E·戈的办公房，官职大总管（Grand Bailli）——这宅子想必就是他的居所。
那天早晨吃着饭，她求我住在这儿，我说再看看，心里却存着疑虑：于是一起把整座宅子走了一遍，发现它出乎意料地宽敞，我便同意留下。

===Original===

At both ends are suites, mostly small rooms, infinitely quaint and cosy, furnished with heavy Henri Quatre furniture and bed draperies; and there are separate, and as it were secret, spiral stairs for exit to each: so we decided that she should have the suite overlooking the length of the lake, the mouths of the Rhone, Bouveret and Villeneuve; and I should have that overlooking the spit of land behind and the little drawbridge, shore cliffs, and elmwood which comes down to the shore, giving at one point a glimpse of the diminutive hamlet of Chillon; and, that decided, I took her hand in mine, and I said:
“Well, then, here we stay, both under the same roof﻿—for the first time. Leda, I will not explain why to you, but it is dangerous, so much so that it *may* mean the death of one or other of us: deadly, deadly dangerous, my poor girl. You do not understand, but that is the fact, believe me, for I know it very well, and I would not tell you false. Well, then, you will easily comprehend, that this being so, you must never on any account come near my part of the house, nor will I come near yours. Lately we have been very much together, but then we have been active, full of purpose and occupation: here we shall be nothing of the kind, I can see. You do not understand at all﻿—but things are so. We must live perfectly separate lives, then. You are nothing to me, really, nor I to you, only we live on the same earth, which is nothing at all﻿—a mere chance. Your own food, clothes, and everything that you want, you will procure for yourself: it is perfectly easy: the shores are crowded with mansions, castles, towns and villages; and I will do the same for myself. The motor down there I set apart for your private use: if I want another, I will get one; and today I will set about looking you up a boat and fishing tackle, and cut a cross on the bow of yours, so that you may know yours, and never use mine. All this is very necessary: you cannot dream how much: but I know how much. Do not run any risks in climbing, now, or with the motor, or in the boat﻿ ﻿… little Leda﻿ ﻿…”
I saw her underlip push, and I turned away in haste, for I did not care whether she cried or not. In that long voyage, and in my illness at Venice, she had become too near and dear to me, my tender love, my dear darling soul; and I said in my heart: “I will be a decent being: I will turn out trumps.”

===Chinese===

两头各有一套房间，多是小小的屋子，说不出的古雅安逸，摆着笨重的亨利四世式家具和床帷；各自还有单独的、可说是隐秘的螺旋楼梯出入：于是我们商定，她住纵览长湖、罗讷河口、布弗雷和维伦纽夫的那一套；我住俯望屋后那道沙嘴和小吊桥、岸边峭壁和一直伸到水边的榆树林的那一套，从林间一点空隙可以窥见西庸那个小小的村子：这事一定，我把她的手握在我手里，说：
「好，那我们就住下，同一片屋顶底下——头一回。勒达，为什么，我不对你解释，可这很危险，危险到也许意味着我们两人中一个的死：致命地、致命地危险，我可怜的姑娘。你不懂，可事实如此，相信我，我知道得清清楚楚，我不骗你。那么你很容易明白：既然如此，你无论如何不许走近宅子我这一头，我也不走近你那一头。近来我们一直在一起，可那时我们在赶路，满是目标和营生：在这儿我们什么营生也没有，我看得出来。你全不懂——可事情就是这样。我们必须过完全分开的日子。你于我算不得什么，真的，我于你也一样，我们不过同住一个地球，那不值一提——纯属偶然。你自己的吃食、衣裳、要的一切，你自己去弄：这再容易不过：湖岸上满是宅邸、城堡、城镇和村子；我也照样自己弄。底下那辆摩托，我拨给你专用：我若还要，我再弄一辆；今天我就给你找一条船和一套钓具，在你的船头刻一个十字，好叫你认得你的，绝不用我的。这一切都非常必要：你想不到有多必要：可我知道。爬高、开摩托、坐船，都别冒一点险……小勒达……」
我看见她的下唇撅起来，急忙转过身去，因为不在乎她哭不哭。在那漫长的旅途中，在威尼斯我生病的时候，她离我太近、太亲了，我温柔的爱，我亲爱的心肝；我在心里说：「我要做个正派的人：我要出落成个像样的。」

===Original===

Under this castle is a sort of dungeon, not narrow, nor very dark, in which are seven stout dark-grey pillars, and an eighth, half-built into the wall; and one of them which has an iron ring, as well as the ground around it, is all worn away by some prisoner or prisoners once chained there; and in the pillar the word “Byron” engraved. This made me remember that a poet of that name had written something about this place, and two days afterwards I actually came upon three volumes of the poet in a room containing a great number of books, many of them English, near the Grand Bailli’s bureau: and in one I read the poem, which is called “The Prisoner of Chillon.” I found it very affecting, and the description good, only I saw no seven rings, and where he speaks of the “pale and livid light,” he should speak rather of the dun and brownish gloom, for the word “light” disconcerts the fancy, and of either pallor or blue there is there no sign. However, I was so struck by the horror of man’s cruelty to man, as depicted in this poem, that I determined that she should see it; went up straight to her rooms with the book, and, she being away, ferreted among her things to see what she was doing, finding all very neat, except in one room where were a number of prints called *La Mode*, and debris of snipped cloth, and medley. When, after two hours, she came in, and I suddenly presented myself, “Oh!” she let slip, and then fell to cooing her laugh; and I took her down through a big room stacked with every kind of rifle, with revolvers, cartridges, powder, swords, bayonets﻿—evidently some official or cantonal magazine﻿—and then showed her the worn stone in the dungeon, the ring, the narrow deep slits in the wall, and I told the tale of cruelty, while the splashing of the lake upon the rock outside was heard with a strange and tragic sound, and her mobile face was all one sorrow.
“How cruel they must have been!” cries she with tremulous lip, her face at the same time reddened with indignation.
“They were mere beastly monsters,” said I: “it is nothing surprising if monsters were cruel.”
And in the short time while I said that, she was looking up with a newborn smile.
“Some others came and set the plisoner flee!” cries she.
“Yes,” said I, “they did, but﻿—”
“That was good of them,” says she.
“Yes,” said I, “that was all right, so far as it went.”

===Chinese===

城堡底下有一处地牢，不算窄，也不很暗，里面立着七根粗壮的深灰石柱，第八根半嵌在墙里；其中一根上带着一只铁环，连同柱子四周的地面，都叫从前锁在那儿的哪个或哪几个囚徒磨得凹陷了；柱子上刻着“拜伦”这个名字。
这使我想起有位这个名字的诗人写过关于此地的东西；两天后我果真在大总管的写字台近旁一间屋里撞见了这位诗人的三卷书——那屋里藏书极多，很多是英文的：其中一卷里我读到那首诗，题作《希永的囚徒》。我读得深为动情，描写也好，只是我没见到什么七只铁环；他说到“苍白死沉的光”的地方，照我看不如说“暗褐的昏沉”，因为“光”这个字搅了想象，而苍白也罢、青蓝也罢，这里一点影踪也没有。
不过，诗里描绘的人对人的残忍，那样骇人，我打定主意也要让她看看；抱起书径直上她屋里去，她不在，我就在她的东西里翻检了一气，看她成天做些什么：只见处处收拾得干干净净，只有一间屋里堆着许多叫《时装》的画页，还有剪碎的布头，乱七八糟一大堆。两个钟头后她回来，我突然在她面前现身，“哦！”她脱口叫了一声，随即咕咕地笑起来；我领她下去，穿过一间堆满各式步枪、左轮、子弹、火药、刀剑、刺刀的大屋——显见是官家或州里的军械库——然后在地牢里指给她看磨凹的石头、铁环、墙上又窄又深的缝，对她讲那段残酷的故事；外面湖水拍打岩石的喍喍声传进来，带着一种古怪而悲惨的调子，她那张多变的脸整个化成了一团哀伤。
“那些人该有多残忍啊！”她嘴唇发颤地叫道，脸同时因义愤涨红了。
“他们不过是些畜生般的怪物。”我说，“怪物残忍，毫不奇怪。”
而在我说话的这一小会儿里，她已抬起头来，绽出一个新生的微笑。
“后来另一些人来了，把那囚图放掉了！”她叫道。
“是的。”我说，“是放了，不过——”
“那是他们好。”她说。
“嗯。”我说，“就其本身而言，那是对的。”

===Original===

“And it was a time when men had al-leady become cluel,” says she: “if those who set him flee were so good when all the lest were cluel, what would they have been at a time when all the lest were kind? They would have been just like Angels.﻿ ﻿… !”

===Chinese===

“再说，那已经是人们变得残仁的时代了。”她说，“要是大家都残仁的时候，放他走的人就那么好，那等到大家都善良的时候，他们会是什么样呢？那简直就是天使了。……！”

===Original===

At this place fishing, and long rambles, were the order of the day, both for her and for me, especially fishing, though a week rarely passed which did not find me at Bouveret, St. Gingolph, Yvoire, Messery, Nyon, Ouchy, Vevay, Montreux, Geneva, or one of the two dozen villages, townlets, or towns, that crowd the shores, all very pretty places, each with its charm, and mostly I went on foot, though the railway runs right round the forty odd miles of the lake’s length. One noonday I was walking through the main-street of Vevay going on to the Cully-road when I had a fearful shock, for in a shop just in front of me to the right I heard a sound﻿—an unmistakable indication of life﻿—as of clattering metals shaken together. My heart leapt into my mouth, I was conscious of becoming bloodlessly pale, and on tiptoe of exquisite caution I stole up to the open door﻿—peeped in﻿—and it was she standing on the counter of a jeweller’s shop, her back turned to me, with head bent low over a tray of jewels in her hands, which she was rummaging for something. I went “*Hoh!*” for I could not help it, and all that day, till sunset, we were very dear friends, for I could not part from her, we walking together by vor-alpen, wood, and shore all the way to Ouchy, she just like a creature crazy that day with the bliss of living, rolling in grasses and perilous flowery declines, stamping her foot defiantly at me, arrogant queen that she is, and then running like mad for me to catch her, with laughter, *abandon*, carolling railleries, and the levity of the wild ass’s colt on the hills, entangling her loose-flung hair with Bacchic tendril and blossom, and drinking, in the passage through Cully, more wine, I thought, than was good: and the flaming darts of lightning that shot and shocked me that day, and the inner secret gleams and revelations of Beauty which I had, and the pangs of white-hot honey that tortured my soul and body, and were too much for me, and made me sick, oh Heaven, what tongue could express all that deep world of things?

===Chinese===

在这个地方，钓鱼和远足成了她我两人每日的功课，尤其钓鱼；不过罕有一个星期不让我去一趟布弗雷、圣然戈尔夫、伊瓦尔、梅瑟里、尼永、乌希、沃韦、蒙特勒、日内瓦，或者挤满湖岸的那二十来个村镇小城中哪一个的——都是漂亮地方，各有各的风致——我多半步行，虽然铁路绕着四十多英里的湖岸整整一圈。一天正午，我正走过沃韦的大街往屈利大道去，猛地吃了一记狠惊：就在我右前方一家铺子里，我听见一个响动——一个毋庸置疑的生命的表征——像金属器件碰在一起哗啦啦地摇。我的心跳到了嗓子眼，自觉脸色刷白，踮起脚尖，小心到极点地挪到敞开的门前——往里一窥——是她，站在一家珠宝店的柜台上，背朝着我，头低低地埋在手里一盘珠宝上，正扒拉着找什么。我「嚯！」了一声，因为忍不住；那一整天直到日落，我们是非常亲爱的一对朋友，因为我离不开她了：我们一路走过山前高地、树林和湖岸，一直走到乌希，她那一天简直像为活的至福而疯了的造物，在草里打滚，滚下危险的缓花坡，傲慢地朝我跺脚——她就是那么个骄横的女王——随后又疯跑，等我追她，一路笑，一路忘情，一路唱着歌打趣，带着山野驴驹子的轻狂，散开的头发缠上酒神似的藤蔓和花，路过屈利时喝下的酒，我想，超出了对她有益的分量：还有那天射穿我、击中我的一道道火烈的闪电之矢，还有我领受的美之内在的、秘密的内光与启示，还有那折磨我灵魂与肉体的白热蜜糖般的剧痛，那对我来说太过了，叫我病了——哦，上天，哪条舌头能说出那深深的整整一个世界？

===Original===

And at Ouchy with a backward wave of my arm I silently motioned her from me, for I was dumb, and weak, and I left her there: and all that long night her power was upon me, for she is stronger than gravitation, which may be evaded, and than all the forces of life combined, and the sun and the moon and the earth are nothing compared with her; and when she was gone from me I was like a fish in the air, or like a bird in the deep, for she is my element of life, made for me to breathe in, and I drown without her: so that for many hours I lay on that grassy hill leading to the burial-ground outside Ouchy that night, like a man sore wounded, biting the grass.
What made things worse for me was her adoption of European clothes since coming to this place: I believe that, in her adroit way, she herself made some of her dresses, for one day I saw in her apartments a number of coloured fashion-plates, with a confusion like dressmaking; or she may have been only modifying finished things from the shops, for her Western dressing is not quite like what I remember of the modern female style, but is really, I should say, quite her own, rather resembling the Greek, or the eighteenth century. At any rate, the airs and graces are as natural to her as feathers to parrots; and she has changes like the moon; never twice the same, and always transcending her last phase and revelation: for I could not have conceived of anyone in whom *taste* was a faculty so separate as in her, so positive and salient, like smelling or sight﻿—more like *smelling*: for it is the faculty, half Reason, half Imagination, by which she fore-scents precisely what will suit exquisitely with what; so that every time I saw her, I received the impression of a perfectly novel, completely bewitching, work of Art: the special quality of works of Art being to produce the momentary conviction that anything else whatever could not possibly be so good.
Occasionally, from my window I would see her in the wood beyond the drawbridge, cool and white in green shade, with her Bible probably, training her skirt like a court-lady, and looking much taller than before. I believe that this new dressing produced a separation between us more complete than it might have been; and especially after that day between Vevay and Ouchy I was very careful not to meet her.

===Chinese===

到了乌希，我朝后挥了挥手臂，默默打发她走，因为我哑了，软了：我把她丢在了那儿：那一整个长夜，她的力量压着我，因为她比引力还强——引力尚可躲避——比生命的全部力量加在一起还强，太阳、月亮和地球同她相比什么都不是；她一离开我，我就像离水的鱼、入水的鸟，因为她是我生命的元素，造出来给我呼吸的，没有她我就溺死：于是那天夜里，好几个钟头，我躺在乌希城外通往墓园的那面草坡上，像一个重伤的人，咬着草。
更糟的是，她到了这地方之后穿起了欧洲衣裳：我相信，凭她那双巧手，她自己的裙子有几条就是她自己做的，因为有一天我在她屋里看见一堆彩色时装图样，还有一片裁衣般的凌乱；她也可能只是把铺子里的成衣改一改，因为她那西式装束同我记忆中的现代女装不大一样，说实在的，倒很像希腊的，或者十八世纪的。不管怎样，风姿和仪态在她就像羽毛在鹦鹉，天生地长着；她像月亮一样多变，从没有两回相同，回回都超出、压倒她上一个阶段和上一次显形：因为我再也想象不出第二个人，「品味」在她身上是这样一种独立官能，这样确凿而凸出，像嗅觉或视觉——更近乎嗅觉：因为正是这官能，一半是理性，一半是想象，凭它她预先嗅出什么同什么相配恰恰好到极处；所以我每回见到她，领受的都是一件全新、魅惑到底的艺术品的印象：艺术品的特质，正在于叫人刹那间深信，任何别的什么都不可能这么好。
偶尔，从我的窗口，我望见她在吊桥那头的林子里，清凉而雪白地待在绿荫里，多半带着她的《圣经》，像宫廷命妇那样曳着裙裾，看着比从前高了许多。我相信，这新式装束在我们之间造成了一层比原本可能更彻底的隔膜；尤其在沃韦到乌希的那一天之后，我格外小心，不与她相遇。

===Original===

The more I saw that she bejewelled herself, powdered herself, embalmed herself like sachets of sweet scents, chapleted her Greek-dressed head with gold fillets, the more I shunned her. Myself, somehow, had now resumed European dress, and, ah me, I was greatly changed, greatly changed, God knows, from the portly inflated monarch-creature that strutted and groaned four years previously in the palace at Imbros: so that my manner of life and thought might once more now have been called modern and Western.
All the more was my sense of responsibility awful: and from day to day it seemed to intensify. An arguing Voice never ceased to remonstrate within me, nor left me peace, and the curse of unborn hosts appeared to menace me. To strengthen my fixity I would often overwhelm myself, and her, with muttered opprobriums, calling myself “convict,” her “ladybird”; asking what manner of man was I that I should dare so great a thing; and as for her, what was she to be the Mother of a world?﻿—a versatile butterfly with a woman’s brow! And continually now in my fiercer moods I was meditating either my death﻿—or hers.
Ah, but the butterfly did not let me forget her brow! To the southwest of Villeneuve, between the forest and the river is a well-grown gentian field, and returning from round St. Gingolph to the Château one day in the third month after an absence of three days, I saw, as I turned a corner in the descent of the mountain, some object floating in the air above the field. Never was I more startled, and, above all, perplexed: for, beside the object soaring there like a great butterfly, I could see nothing to account for it. It was not long, however, before I came to the conclusion that she has reinvented *the kite*﻿—for she had almost certainly never seen one﻿—and I presently sighted her holding the string in the midfield. Her invention resembles the kind called “swallowtail” of old.

===Chinese===

我越见她给自己缀珠戴宝、敷粉熏香——像一袋袋香囊那样给自己熏香——给她希腊式装束的头缠上金发带，我越躲她。我自己呢，不知怎么也重新穿起了欧洲衣裳，而且，唉，我大变了，大变了，上帝知道，再不是四年前在因姆罗兹宫殿里昂首踱步、哼哼唧唧的那个臃肿虚浮的君王造物：以致我如今的生活与思想的方式，又可以说现代化、西方化了。
我的责任感因此越发可怕：而且一天强似一天。一个争辩的声音在我里面不停地规劝，不给我安宁，未生的芸芸众生的诅咒仿佛在威吓我。为坚固我的定力，我常常低声把骂名劈头盖脸压给自己，也压给她：骂自己「苦役犯」，骂她「小瓢虫」；问自己算什么人，敢来干这样一件大事；至于她，她算什么，配做一整个世界的母亲？——一只额头上安着女人前额的多才多艺的蝴蝶！于是在愈来愈凶的心绪里，我不断盘算着我的死——或者她的死。
啊，可这蝴蝶偏偏不让我忘掉她那额头！维伦纽夫西南，森林与河流之间有一片长势正好的龙胆田，离开三天之后的第三个月里，有一天我绕圣然戈尔夫回城堡，在下山转弯处，望见那片田的上空有个东西浮在空气里。我从没有那样吃惊，尤其是那样困惑：因为那东西像一只大蝴蝶翱翔在半空，四下里我却看不见任何足以解释它的东西。不过不久我就得出结论：她重新发明了风筝——因为她几乎肯定从没见过风筝——随后我就望见她站在田当中牵着线。她这发明像旧时叫作「燕尾」的那一种。

===Original===

But mostly it was on the lake that I saw her, for there we chiefly lived, and occasionally there were guilty approaches and rencontres, she in her boat, I in mine, both being slight clinker-built Montreux pleasure-boats, which I had spent some days in overhauling and varnishing, mine with jib, fore-and-aft mainsail, and spanker, hers rather smaller, one-masted, with an easy-running lugsail. It was no uncommon thing for me to sail quite to Geneva, and come back from a seven-days’ cruise with my soul filled and consoled with the lake and all its many moods of bright and darksome, serene and pensive, dolorous and despairing and tragic, at morning, at noon, at sunset, at midnight, a panorama that never for an instant ceased to unroll its transformations, I sometimes climbing the mountains as high as the goatherd region of hoch-alpen, once sleeping there. And once I was made very ill by a two-weeks’ horror which I had: for she disappeared in her skiff, I being at the Château, and she did not come back; and while she was away there was a tempest that turned the lake into an angry ocean, and, ah my good God, she did not come. At last, half-crazy at the vacant days of misery which went by and by, and she did not come, I set out upon a wild-goose quest, of her﻿—of all the hopeless things the most hopeless, for the world is great﻿—and I sought and did not find her; and after three days I turned back, recognising that I was mad to search the infinite, and coming near the Château, I saw her wave her handkerchief from the island-edge, for she divined that I had gone to seek her, and she was watching for me: and when I took her hand, what did she say to me, the Biblical simpleton?﻿—“Oh you of little Faith!” says she. And she had adventures to lisp, with all the *r*’s liquefied into *l*’s, and I was with her all that day again.

===Chinese===

可我大多是在湖上见到她，因为我们的生活主要就在湖上；偶尔也有几趟心虚的靠近和相逢，她驾她的船，我驾我的，两条都是轻巧的蒙特勒式叠层板游船，我花了好几天检修、上漆：我那条配着艏三角帆、纵帆式主帆和后桅斜桁帆；她那条小些，单桅，挂一张滑溜好使的四角斜帆。
我常常一口气驶到日内瓦，七天的巡游归来，灵魂已被这湖填满、被它抚慰——它那许许多多的性情，明丽与幽暗，安详与沉郁，悲苦、绝望与惨烈，在清晨，在正午，在日落，在午夜，一幅永不有一瞬停歇地展开变化的全景；有时我登山直上到高山牧场放羊人的地界，还在那儿睡过一夜。
有一回，一场持续两星期的恐怖把我折磨病了：她驾着小艇失踪了，我当时在城堡，她没有回来；她不在的当口，湖上起了一场风暴，把湖面翻成了一片狂怒的海洋，啊，我的上帝，她没有回来。终于，苦日子一天天空落落地过去，她还不回来，我半疯了，出发去做一场大海捞针的搜寻——天下最无望的事里数它最无望，因为世界太大——我找了，没有找到；三天后我折返回来，承认自己疯了才去搜那无穷，走近城堡时，却看见她在岛边挥她的手帕——原来她猜到我去寻她了，正望着我呢：我握住她的手时，你猜这位《圣经》里的小傻子对我说什么？——“哦，你这小信的人！”她说。于是她咿咿呀呀讲她的历险，所有的*r*都化成了*l*，而那一整天我又同她在一起了。

===Original===

Once a month perhaps she would knock at my outermost door, which I mostly kept locked when at home, bringing me a sumptuously-dressed, highly-spiced red trout or grayling, which I had not the heart to refuse, and exquisitely she does them, all hot and spiced, applying apparently to their preparation the taste which she applies to dress; and her extraordinary luck in angling did not fail to supply her with the finest specimens, though, for that matter, this lake, with its old fish-hatcheries and fish-ladders, is not miserly in that way, swarming now with the best lake trout, river trout, red trout, and with salmon, of which last I have brought in one with the landing-net of, I should say, thirty-five to forty pounds. As the bottom goes off very rapidly from the two islands to a depth of eight to nine hundred feet, we did not long confine ourselves to bottom-fishing, but gradually advanced to every variety of manoeuvre, doing middle-water spinning with three-triangle flights and sliding lip-hook for jack and trout, trailing with the sail for salmon, live-baiting with the float for pike, daping with bluebottles, casting with artificial flies, and I could not say in which she became the most carelessly adept, for all soon seemed as old and natural to her as an occupation learned from birth.

===Chinese===

大约每月一回，她会敲我最外层的门——我在家时多半锁着——给我送来一条烧得浓油赤酱、香料十足的红鳟或茴鱼，叫我狠不下心拒绝；她做这鱼的手艺精绝，滚热喷香，显然是把装扮衣裳的那份趣味用到了烹调上；她钓鱼的手气又出奇地好，最上等的货色源源不断——不过话说回来，这湖有旧日的孵鱼场和鱼梯，在这上头本不吝啬，如今满湖都是上好的湖鳟、河鳟、红鳟，还有鲑鱼；最后这一样，我就用抄网捞起过一条，我估摸有三十五到四十磅。
由于湖底从两座岛边飞快地斜下去，深到八九百英尺，我们没过多久就不满足于底钓，渐渐玩遍了各种花样：用三叉亮片组和滑动唇钩钓中层水里的狗鱼和鳟鱼，张帆拖钓鲑鱼，浮漂活饵钓白斑狗鱼，用青蝇点钓水面，用假蝇抛钓——她在哪一样上头最是从容精通，我说不上，因为样样到她手里，很快就像一门从娘胎里带来的营生，又旧又自然。

===Original===

On the 21st October I attained my forty-sixth birthday in excellent health: a day destined to end for me in bloodshed and tragedy, alas. I forget now what circumstance had caused me to mention the date long beforehand in, I think, Venice, not dreaming that she would keep any count of it, nor was I even sure that my calendar was not faulty by a day. But at ten in the morning of what I called the 21st, descending by my private spiral in flannels with some trout and par bait, and tackle﻿—I met her coming up, my God, though she had no earthly right to be there. With her cooing murmur of a laugh, yet pale, pale, and with a most guilty look, she presented me a large bouquet of wild flowers.
I was at once thrown into a state of great agitation. She was dressed in rather a frippery of mousseline de soie, all cream-laced, with wide-hanging short sleeves, a large diamond at the low open neck, the ivory-brown skin there contrasting with the powdered bluish-white of her face, where, however, the freckles were not quite whited out; on her feet little pink satin slippers, without any stockings﻿—a divinely pale pink; and well back on her hair a plain thin circlet of gold; and she smelled like heaven, God knows.
I could not speak. She broke an awkward silence, saying, very faint and pallid:
“It is the day!”
“I﻿—perhaps﻿—” I said, or some incoherency like that.
I saw the touch of enthusiasm which she had summoned up quenched by my manner.
“I have not done long again?” she asked, looking down, breaking another silence.
“No, no, oh no,” said I hurriedly: “not done wrong again. Only, I could not suppose that you would count up the days. You are﻿ ﻿… considerate. Perhaps﻿—but﻿—”
“Tell Leda?”
“Perhaps.﻿ ﻿… I was going to say﻿ ﻿… you might come fishing with me.﻿ ﻿…”
“O luck!” she went softly.
I was pierced by a sense of my base cowardice, my incredible weakness: but I could not at all help it.

===Chinese===

十月二十一日，我四十六岁生日，身体极好：唉，这一天命定要以流血和悲剧收场。如今我忘了是什么由头，让我早早在威尼斯——我想是威尼斯——提过这个日子，做梦也没想到她会记着数，甚至连我的历书是否错了一天，我都没有把握。可在我称作二十一日的那天上午十点，我穿着法兰绒衣裤，带着几条鳟鱼、山鹑饵和钓具，正顺着我那道专用螺旋梯下来——却迎面碰上她往上走，我的上帝，她根本没有半点权利待在那儿。她带着鸽鸣似的轻笑，人却苍白，苍白，一脸极心虚的神气，捧给我一大束野花。
我顿时陷入大激动。她穿一身颇花哨的双绉薄绸，通身奶油色花边，短短的宽垂袖，低开的领口一枚大钻，露出的象牙棕皮肤，衬着扑了粉的青白脸儿——脸上的雀斑却没有全盖住——脚上小小的粉缎拖鞋，没有穿袜子——一种仙气的淡粉；头发靠后箍着一圈素金的细环；她一身香气，天堂似的，上帝知道。
我说不出话。她打破尴尬的沉默，声音极轻、极弱地说：
「是那个日子！」
「我——也许——」我说，或者类似的无头话。
我看见她鼓起的那点热切，被我的态度浇熄了。
「我又做错了吗？」她低头问，又打破一阵沉默。
「不，不，哦不，」我急忙说，「没有又做错。只是，我没想到你会数着日子。你……真有心。也许——不过——」
「告诉勒达了？」
「也许。……我本来想说……你不妨同我一起去钓鱼。……」
「哦，好运气！」她轻轻地说。
一阵卑怯、一种难以置信的软弱刺穿了我：可我全然无法自主。

===Original===

I took the flowers, and we went down to the south side, where my boat lay; I threw out some of the fish from the well; arranged the tackle, and then the stern cushions for her; got up the sails; and out we went, she steering, I in the bows, with every possible inch of space between us, receiving delicious intermittent whiffs from her of ambergris, frangipane, or some blending of perfumes, the morning being bright and hot, with very little breeze on the water, which looked mottled, like colourless water imperfectly mixed with indigo-wash, we making little headway; so it was some time before I moved nearer her to get the par for fixing on the three-triangle flight, for I was going to trail for salmon or large lake-trout; and during all that time we spoke not a word together.
Afterwards I said:
“Who told you that flowers are proper to birthdays? or that birthdays are of any importance?”
“I suppose that nothing can happen so important as birth,” says she: “and perfumes must be ploper to birth, because the wise men blought spices to the young Jesus.”
This naivete was the cause of my immediate recovery: for to laugh is to be saved: and I laughed right out, saying:
“But you read the Bible too much! all your notions are biblical. You should read the quite modern books.”
“I have tlied,” says she: “but I cannot lead them long, nor often. The whole world seems to have got so collupted. It makes me shudder.”
“Ah, well now, you see, you quite come round to my point of view,” said I.
“Yes, and no,” says she: “they had got so *spoiled*, that is all. Everlybody seems to have become quite dull-witted﻿—the plainest tluths they could not see. I can imagine that those faculties which aided them in their stlain to become lich themselves, and make the lest more poor, must have been gleatly sharpened, while all the other faculties withered: as I can imagine a person with one eye seeing double thlough it, and quite blind on the other side.”
“Ah,” said I, “I do not think they even *wanted* to see on the other side. There were some few tolerably good and clear-sighted ones among them, you know: and these all agreed in pointing out how, by changing one or two of their old man-in-the-moon Bedlam arrangements, they could greatly better themselves: but they heard with listless ears: I don’t know that they ever made any considerable effort.

===Chinese===

我接过花，我们下到南边我泊船的地方；我从活水舱里捞出几条鱼丢掉；理好钓具，又给她安顿了船尾的软垫；帆升起来；我们出了湖，她掌舵，我在船头，两人之间隔着能隔的每一寸空间，她那边一阵一阵送来龙涎香、鸡蛋花或什么混香的醉人气息。早晨晴朗而热，水面上几乎没有风，湖水斑斑驳驳，像无色的水没有揽匀靛蓝洗液，我们走得极慢；所以过了一阵我才挪近她，去取山鹑饵装三叉钓组——我打算拖钓鲑鱼或大湖鳟；那一路我们彼此没说一个字。
随后我说：
「谁告诉你过生日该送花？还告诉你生日重要？」
「我想，什么事也大不过出生，」她说，「香的东西合该配出生，因为博士们给小耶稣带的正是香料。」
这份天真立刻治好了我：因为一笑就得救：我放声笑了，说：
「可你《圣经》读得太多！你满脑子都是圣经。你该读读十足现代的书。」
「我试过，」她说，「可读不长，也读不多。整个世界好像烂得太厉害了。叫我发抖。」
「啊哈，你看，这你可想通了，跟我一个看法。」我说。
「是，也不是，」她说，「他们是糟透了，仅此而已。人人好像都变得极迟钝——最明白的道理也看不见。我想得出，那帮他们拼命自富、把其余人弄穷的官能，必定磨得飞快，其余的官能却全枯了：就像我想得出一个独眼人，那只眼看出双影，另一边却全瞎。」
「啊，」我说，「我看他们压根不想看另一边。他们里头也有几个还算好、眼也算亮的，你知道：这些人都一致指出，只要改掉一两样他们那套月中人疯人院的安排，他们就能大大改好：可他们听的心不在焉：我不记得他们真下过什么大工夫。

===Original===

For they had become more or less unconscious of their misery, so miserable were they: like the man in Byron’s ‘Prisoner of Chillon,’ who, when his deliverers came, was quite indifferent, for he says:

> ‘It was at length the same to me

Fettered or fetterless to be:

I had learned to love Despair.’ ”

===Chinese===

因为他们对自身的苦楚已经差不多浑然不觉，他们苦到了那个地步：像拜伦《西庸的囚徒》里那个人，解救他的人来时，他已全无所谓，因为他说：

> 「到后来，戴不戴镣铐

> 于我已是一样：

> 我已学会爱上绝望。」

===Original===

“Oh my God,” she went, covering her face a moment, “how dleadful! And it is tlue, it seems tlue:﻿—they had learned to love Despair, to be even ploud of Despair. Yet all the time, I feel *sure* flom what I have lead, flom what I scent, that the individual man was stluggling to see, to live light, but without power, like one’s leg when it is asleep: that is so pletty of them all! that they meant well﻿—everly one. But they were too tloubled and sad, too awfully burdened: they had no chance at all. Such a queer, unnatulal feeling it gives me to lead of all that world: I can’t desclibe it; all their motives seem so tainted, their life so lopsided. Tluely, the whole head was sick, and the whole heart faint.”
“Quite so,” said I: “and observe that this was no new thing: in the very beginning of the Book we read how God saw that the wickedness of man was great on the earth, and every imagination of his heart evil.﻿ ﻿…”
“Yes,” she interrupted, “that is tlue: but there must have been some *cause*! We can be quite *sure* that it was not natulal, because you and I are men, and our hearts are not evil.”
This was her great argument which she always trotted out, because she found that I had usually no answer to give to it. But this time I said:
“Our hearts not evil? Say yours: but as to mine you know nothing, Leda.”
The semicircles under her eyes had that morning, as often, a certain moist, heavy, pensive and weary something, as of one fresh from a revel, very sweet and tender: and, looking softly at me with it, she answered:
“I know my own heart, and it is not evil: not at all: not even in the very least: and I know yours, too.”
“You know *mine*!” cried I, with a half-laugh of surprise.
“Quite well,” says she.
I was so troubled by this cool assurance, that I said not a word, but going to her, handed her the baited flight, swivel-trace, and line, which she paid out; then I got back again almost into the bows.
After a ten-minutes I spoke again:
“So this is news to me: you know all about my heart. Well, come, tell me what is in it!”
Now she was silent, pretending to be busy with the trail, till she said, speaking with low-bent face, and a voice that I could only just hear:
“I will tell you what is in it: in it is a lebellion which you think good, but is not good.

===Chinese===

「哦我的上帝，」她感叹，用手掩了一下面，「多可怕！而且是真的，听着是真的：——他们学会了爱绝望，甚至以绝望为荣。可我始终觉得，从我读过的、从我嗅到的，可以断定：每个个人都在挣扎着想看见、想活在光里，只是没有力气，像一条腿睡麻了的滋味：他们人人这一点多么好啊！他们存心都是好的——每一个人。可他们烦恼太重、悲伤太深、担子太沉：他们根本没有机会。读那整个世界，给我一种那么古怪、那么不自然的感觉：我说不出；他们一切的动机好像都沾了污，他们的生活那么偏斜。千真万确，整个头都病了，整个心都乏了。」
「正是，」我说，「而且注意，这不是什么新事：那部书一开卷我们就读到，上帝见人在地上罪恶很大，心中所思想的尽是恶。……」
「是，」她打断我，「那是真的：可总得有个原因！我们可以相当断定那不是天生的，因为你和我是人，而我们的心不恶。」
这是她的大论据，回回都搬出来，因为她发现我通常答不上来。可这一回我说：
「我们的心不恶？说你的吧：我的心怎样，你可一无所知，勒达。」
她眼睛下的那两弯青影，那天早晨照常带着一点湿漉漉、沉甸甸、愁倦的东西，像刚从一场欢宴下来，非常甜，非常软：她带着这个柔柔地望我，答道：
「我知道我自己的心，它不恶：一点也不：连一丝一毫也不：你的心，我也知道。」
「你知道我的心！」我喊，惊得半笑。
「知道得很清楚。」她说。
这冷静的笃定叫我那么不安，我一个字也说不出，只是走到她跟前，把上好饵的钓组、转环脑线和主线递给她，由她放出去；随后我几乎又缩回了船头。
过了十分钟，我又开口：
「这么说，这倒是我头回听说：我的心你全知道。好，来，说说里头有什么！」
这下她沉默了，装作忙于拖钓，直到她才开口，脸低垂着，声音我勉强听得见：
「我告诉你里头有什么：里头是一场反叛，你以为是好的，其实不好。

===Original===

If a stleam will just flow, neither tlying to climb upward, nor overflowing its banks, but lunning modestly in its fated channel just wherever it is led, then it will finally leach the sea﻿—the mighty ocean﻿—and lose itself in fullness.”
“Ah,” said I, “but that counsel is not new. It is what the philosophers used to call ‘yielding to Destiny,’ and ‘following Nature.’ And Destiny and Nature, I give you my word, often led mankind quite wrong﻿—”
“Or *seemed* to,” says she﻿—“for a time: as when a stleam flows north a little, and the sea is to the south: but it is bound for the sea all the time, and will turn again. Destiny never could, and cannot yet, be judged, for it is not finished: and our lace should follow blindly whither it points, sure that thlough many curves it leads the world to our God.”
“Our God indeed!” I cried, getting very excited: “girl! you talk speciously, but falsely! whence have you these thoughts in that head of yours? Girl! you talk of ‘our race’! But there are only two of us left? Are you talking *at* me, Leda? Do not *I* follow Destiny?”
“You?” she sighed, with down-bent face: “ah, poor me!”
“What should I do if I followed it?” said I, with a crazy curiosity.
Her face hung lower, paler, in trouble: and she said:
“You would come now and sit near me here. You would not be there where you are. You would be always and forever near me.﻿ ﻿…”
My good God! I felt my face redden.
“Oh, I could not *tell* you﻿ ﻿… !” I cried: “you talk the most disastrous﻿ ﻿… ! you lack all responsibility﻿ ﻿… ! Never, never﻿ ﻿… !”
Her face now was covered with her left hand, her right on the tiller: and bitingly she said, with a touch of venom:
“I could *make* you come﻿—*now*, if I chose: but I will not: I will wait upon my God.﻿ ﻿…”
“*Make* me!” I cried: “Leda! How make me?”
“I could cly before you, as I cly often and often﻿ ﻿… in seclet﻿ ﻿… for my childlen.﻿ ﻿…”
“*You* cry in secret? This is news﻿—”
“Yes, yes, I cly. Is not the burden of the world heavy upon me, too? and the work I have to do *vely, vely* gleat? And often and often I cly in seclet, thinking of it: and I could cly now if I chose, for you love your little girl so much, that you could not lesist me one minute.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

一条溪流如果只管流淌，不存心往上爬，也不漫过两岸，只谦卑地在命定的河道里，引到哪里流到哪里，它终究会到达海——那浩大的洋——在丰盈里失去自己。」
「啊，」我说，「这番劝可不是新道理。从前哲人们管它叫『顺从天命』，『遵循自然』。而天命和自然，我向你担保，常常把人类领上大错——」
「或者像是错了，」她说——「暂时地：就像溪流朝北流一段，而海在南边：可它终归奔着海去，还会拐回来。天命永远不能、如今也还不能论断，因为它没有完结：我们的种族应该盲目地随它所指，确信它迂回百转，终把世界领向我们的上帝。」
「『我们的』上帝！」我喊，激动起来，「姑娘！你说得头头是道，却全是假的！你那脑袋里这些念头是哪儿来的？姑娘！你口口声声『我们的种族』！可只剩下我们两个了？你这是冲着我说的吧，勒达？难道我没有遵循天命？」
「你？」她叹息，脸垂着，「唉，我多可怜！」
「我要是遵循了它，该做什么？」我说，带着发疯似的好奇。
她的脸垂得更低，更白，很为难：她说：
「你这会儿就会过来，坐到我身边。你就不会待在你待的那儿。你会永远、永远挨着我。……”
我的老天！我觉出自己脸红了。
「哦，我没法对你说……！」我喊，「你说的全是大祸……！你一点责任心也没有……！绝不，绝不……！」
此刻她左手掩着脸，右手扶着舵柄：她刻薄地说，带一点毒：
「我只要愿意，就能叫你过来——立刻：可我不：我要等候我的上帝。……”
「叫！」我喊，「勒达！怎么叫？」
「我可以在你面前哭，像我常常、常常哭的那样……偷偷地……为我的孩子们。……”
「你偷偷地哭？这可是新闻——」
「是，是，我哭。世界的担子不也压在我身上吗？我要做的工不大、不大吗？我常常、常常想着它，偷偷地哭：这会儿我只要愿意，也能哭，因为你这么爱你的小姑娘，一分钟也抗拒不了我。……”

===Original===

Now I saw the push and tortion and trembling of her poor little underlip, boding tears: and at once a flame was in me which was altogether beyond control; and crying out: “why, my poor dear,” I found myself in the act of rushing through the staggering boat to take her to me.
Midway, however, I was saved: a whisper, intense as lightning, arrested me: “Forward is no escape, nor backward, but *sideward* there may be a way!” And at a sudden impulse, before I knew what I was doing, I was in the water swimming.
The smaller of the islands was two hundred yards away, and thither I swam, rested some minutes, and thence to the Castle. I did not once look behind me.

===Chinese===

这时我看见她那可怜的小下唇又撅又扭又哆嗦，眼泪在即：我胸中立刻烧起一团全然控制不住的火；我喊了一声「哎呀，我可怜的亲爱的」，发觉自己已经在踉跄的船上冲过去，要把她搂进怀里。
然而半路上我得救了：一声耳语，亮得像闪电，把我钉住：「向前不是生路，向后也不是，往旁边或有路！」一阵突来的冲动，我还不知道自己在做什么，已经跳进水里游了起来。
小岛离我两百码，我朝它游去，歇了几分钟，再从那儿游回城堡。我一次也没有回头。

===Original===

Well, from 11 a.m. till five in the afternoon, I thought it all out, lying in the damp flannels on my face on the sofa in the recess beside my bed, where it was quite dark behind the tattered piece of arras: and what things I suffered that day, and what deeps I sounded, and what prayers I prayed, God knows. What infinitely complicated the awful problem was this thought in my head: that to kill her would be far more merciful to her than to leave her alone, having killed myself: and, Heaven knows, it was for her alone that I thought, not at all caring for myself. To kill her was better: but to kill her with my own hands﻿—that was too hard to expect of a poor devil like me, a poor common son of Adam, after all, and never any sublime self-immolator, as two or three of them were. And hours I lay there with brows convulsed in an agony, groaning only those words: “To kill her! to kill her!” thinking sometimes that I should be merciful to myself too, and die, and let her live, and not care, since, after my death, I would not see her suffer, for the dead know not anything: and to expect me to kill her with my own hand was a little too much. Yet that one or other of us must die was perfectly certain, for I knew that I was just on the brink of failing in my oath, and matters here had reached an obvious crisis: unless we could make up our minds to part﻿ ﻿… ? putting the width of the earth between us? That conception occurred to me: and in the turmoil of my thoughts it seemed a possibility. Finally, about 5 p.m., I resolved upon something: and first I leapt up, went down and across the house into the arsenal, chose a small revolver, fitted it with cartridge, took it upstairs, lubricated it with lamp-oil, went down and out across the drawbridge, walked two miles beyond the village, shot the revolver at a tree, found its action accurate, and started back. When I came to the Castle, I walked along the island to the outer end, and looked up: there were her pretty cream Valenciennes, put up by herself, waving inward before the light lake-breeze at one open oriel; and I knew that she was in the Castle, for I felt it: and always, always, when she was within, I knew, for I felt her with me; and always when she was away, I knew, I felt, for the air had a dreadful drought, and a barrenness, in it.

===Chinese===

就这样，从上午十一点到下午五点，我把一切想了个透：我趴在我床边凹室里的沙发上，穿着湿漉漉的法兰绒衣，凹室在那幅破旧的挂毯后面，相当暗：那一天我受了什么罪，探了多深的渊，做了怎样的祷告，只有上帝知道。
把这可怕的难题搅得无比复杂的，是我脑中的这个念头：杀了她，远比我自己先死、把她独自留下，对她仁慈得多：上天知道，我心里想的只有她，自己倒全不在乎。杀了她更好：可要我用这双手去杀——这对我这样一个可怜虫，终究只是亚当一个平平常常的子孙、从来不是什么崇高的自我牺牲者——未免指望太高了，他们里头倒有那么两三位是的。
一连几个钟头我躺在那儿，眉头拧成一团苦痛，嘴里只呻吟这几个字：“杀了她！杀了她！”有时又想，不如也饶了我自己，我去死，让她活，什么都不管——反正我死后也看不见她受苦，因为死人无所知：再说，指望我亲手杀她，实在有点过分。
然而我们两人必死一个，这一点确凿无疑：因为我知道我正站在背誓的崖边上，这里的事已到了明摆着的关口：除非……我们横下心分开……？让整个地球的宽度横在我们中间？这个念头冒出来过：在思绪的翻腾里，它似乎是一线可能。
最后，下午五点来钟，我拿定了主意：我先跳起来，下楼穿过宅子进军械库，挑了一支小号左轮，装上子弹，拿到楼上，用灯油润了滑，再下楼出吊桥，走到村子外面两英里，朝一棵树开了枪，见它机件精准，便动身回来。
到了城堡，我沿岛走到尽头，抬头仰望：她那幅漂亮的乳白瓦朗西纳花边窗帘，她自己挂上去的，正在一扇敞开的凸窗前，随着轻轻的湖风向里飘动；我知道她在城堡里，因为我感觉得到：每回、每回她在里面，我都知道，因为我感到她在我身边；而每回她不在，我也知道，也感觉得到，因为那时的空气里有一种可怕的干涸，一种荒芜。

===Original===

And I looked up for a time to see if she would come to the window, and then I called, and she appeared. And I said to her: “Come down here.”

===Chinese===

我仰头望了一阵，看她会否来到窗前，然后我唤了一声，她出现了。我对她说：“下来。”

===Original===

Just here there is a little rock-path to the south, going down to the water between rocks mixed with shrub-like little trees, three yards long: a path, or a lane, one might call it, for at the lower end the rocks and trees reach well over a tall man’s head. There she had tied my boat to a slender linden-trunk: and sadder now than Gethsemane that familiar boat seemed to my eyes, for I knew very well that I should never enter it more. I walked up and down the path, awaiting her: and from the jacket-pocket in which lay the revolver I drew a box of Swedish matches, from it took two matches, and broke off a bit from the plain end of one; and the two I held between my left thumb and forefinger joint, the phosphorus ends level and visible, the other ends invisible: and I awaited her, pacing fast, and my brow was as stern as Azrael and Rhadamanthus.
She came, very pale, poor thing, and flurried, breathing fast. And “Leda,” I said, meeting her in the middle of the lane, and going straight to the point, “we are to part, as you guess﻿—forever, as you guess﻿—for I see very well by your face that you guess. I, too, am very sorry, my little child, and heavy is my heart. To leave you﻿ ﻿… alone﻿ ﻿… in the world﻿ ﻿… is﻿—death for me. But it must, ah it must, be done.”
Her face suddenly turned as sallow as the dead were, when the shroud was already on, and the coffin had become a stale added piece of room-furniture by the bedside; but in recording that fact, I record also this other: that, accompanying this mortal sallowness, which painfully showed up her poor freckles, was a steady smile, a little turned-down: a smile of steady, of slightly disdainful﻿—Confidence.
She did not say anything: so I went on.
“I have thought long,” said I, “and I have made a plan﻿—a plan which cannot be effective without *your* consent and cooperation: and the plan is this: we go from this place together﻿—this same night﻿—to some unknown spot, some town, say a hundred miles hence﻿—by train. There I get two motors, and I in one, and you in the other, we separate, going different ways. We shall thus never be able, however much we may want to, to rediscover each other in all this wide world. That is my plan.”
She looked me in the face, smiling her smile: and the answer was not long in coming.

===Chinese===

就在这里，南边有一条小小的石径，夹在乱石和灌木似的小树之间通到水边，长三码：说它是路径，不如说是一条巷子，因为下端的石头和树，高过一个高个子男人的头顶。她把我的船系在一根细细的椴树干上：那条熟悉的船，此刻在我眼里比客西马尼还凄凉，因为我知道得很清楚，我再也不会踏上它了。
我在小径上来回踱步，等她：从装着左轮的上衣口袋里，我摸出一盒瑞典火柴，取出两根，把其中一根的平头掐短一截；两根都夹在我左手拇指和食指的关节间，磷头齐平、露着可见，另一头藏得看不见：我等她，走得很快，眉宇冷峻如亚兹拉尔和拉达曼提斯。
她来了，脸色惨白，可怜的人儿，慌乱着，喘得急。“莉达，”我在巷子中段迎住她，开门见山地说，“我们要分别了，你猜到了——永远分别，你也猜到了——因为从你脸上我看得很清楚，你猜到了。我也很难过，我的小孩子，我的心沉重。把你……撇下……孤零零在世上……对我就是——死。可这事必须，啊必须，办成。”
她的脸霎时黄了下来，黄得像死人——寿衣已经穿好、棺材已在床边摆成一件旧家具时的那种死黄；可在记下这一点时，我也得记下另一点：伴着这要命的蜡黄——它把她那可怜的雀斑衬得刺眼——的是一个稳稳的微笑，嘴角微微向下：一种坚定的、略带轻蔑的——信。
她什么也没说：我便接着讲。
“我想了很久。”我说，“我定了一个计划——一个没有*你的*同意与合作便无法生效的计划：计划是这样：今夜——就在今夜——我们一起离开此地，坐火车去某个没人知道的地方，某座小城，比如说一百英里外。在那里我弄两辆汽车，我开一辆，你开一辆，我们分道扬镳，各走各路。这样一来，任我们如何想，在这茫茫大千世界，也永远休想再寻见彼此。这就是我的计划。”
她望着我的脸，含着她的那种笑：回答没有让我等多久。

===Original===

“I will go in the tlain with you,” says she with slow decisiveness: “but where you leave me, there I will stay, till I die; and I will patiently wait till my God convert you, and send you back to me.”
“That means that you refuse to do what I say?”
“Yes,” said she, bowing the head with great dignity.
“Well, you speak, not like a girl, Leda,” said I, “but like a full woman now. But still, reflect a minute.﻿ ﻿… O reflect! If you stayed where I left you, I *should* go back to you, and pretty soon, too: I know that I should. Tell me, then﻿—reflect well, and tell me﻿—do you definitely refuse to part with me?”
The answer was pretty prompt, cool, and firm:
“Yes; I lefuse.”
I left her then, took a turn down the path, and came back.
“Then,” said I, “here are two matches in my grasp: be good enough to draw one.”
*Now* she was hit to the heart: I saw her eyes widen to the width of horror, with a glassy stare: she had read of the drawing of lots in the Bible: she knew that it meant death for me, or for her.
But she obeyed without a word, after one backward start and then a brief hovering in decision of thumb and forefinger over my held-out hand. I had fixed it in my mind that if she drew the shorter of the matches, then she should die; if the longer, then I should die.
She drew the shorter.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“我跟你的火擦去。”她慢慢地说，斩钉截铁，“可在你撇下我的地方，我就待下去，到死为止；我要耐心等我的上帝把你感化回来，把你送回我身边。”
“这就是说，你不肯照我说的做？”
“是的。”她说，极有尊严地低下头。
“好啊，你这话说得不像个姑娘了，莉达，”我说，“倒像个十足的女人。可是再想想。……哦，想想！你若留在我撇下你的地方，我*一定*会回到你身边去，而且用不了多久：这我知道。那么告诉我——好好想，告诉我——你是断然拒绝同我分开吗？”
回答来得干脆、冷静、坚决：
“是的；我拒缝。”
我便撇下她，沿小径踱了个来回，又回来了。
“那么，”我说，“我手里攒着两根火柴：请抽一根。”
这一下她被击中了心脏：我看见她的眼睛猛地睜大，睜到恐怖的宽度，目光发直：《圣经》上她读到过抽签的事：她知道，那意味着我死，或者她死。
可她一声不吭地服从了：先向后一缩，随后拇指和食指在我摊开的手掌上方悬了一瞬，定夺下来。我早已在心里定死：她若抽着短的那根，就她死；抽着长的，就我死。
她抽着了短的那根。……

===Original===

This was only what I should have expected: for I knew that God loved her, and hated me.
But instantly upon the first shock of the enormity that I should be her executioner, I made my resolve: to drop shot, too, at the moment after she dropped shot, so disposing my body, that it would fall half upon her, and half by her, so that we might be close always: and that would not be so bad, after all.
With a sudden movement I snatched the revolver from my pocket: she did not move, except her white lips, which, I think, whispered:
“*Not yet.*﻿ ﻿…”
I stood with hanging arm, forefinger on trigger, looking at her. I saw her glance once at the weapon, and then she fixed her eyes upwards upon my face: and now that same smile, which had disappeared, was on her lips again, meaning confidence, meaning disdain.
I waited for her to open her mouth to say something﻿—to stop that smile﻿—that I might shoot her quick and sudden: and she would not, knowing that I could not kill her while she was smiling; and suddenly, all my pity and love for her changed into a strange resentment and rage against her, for she was purposely making hard for me what I was doing for her sake: and the bitter thought was in my mind: “You are nothing to me: if you want to die, you do your own killing; and I will do my own killing.” And without one word to her, I strode away, and left her there.
I see now that this whole drawing of lots was nothing more than a farce: I never could have killed her, smiling, or no smiling: for to each thing and man is given a certain strength: and a thing cannot be stronger than its strength, strive as it may: it is so strong, and no stronger, and there is an end of the matter.
I walked up to the Grand Bailli’s bureau, a room about twenty-five feet from the ground. By this time it was getting pretty dark, but I could see, by peering, the face of a grandfather’s-clock which I had long since set going, and kept wound. It is on the north side of the room, over the writing-desk opposite the oriels. It then pointed to half-past six, and in order to fix some definite moment for the bitter effort of the mortal act, I said: “At Seven.” I then locked the door which opens upon three little steps near the desk, and also the stair-door; and I began to pace the chamber.

===Chinese===

这本在我意料之中：因为我知道上帝爱她，恨我。
可在「我将是她的行刑者」这桩骇人之事的第一记震骇之后，我立刻拿定主意：在她吞铅之后，我也吞铅，把身子安置得一半倒在她身上，一半倒在她旁边，好教我们永远挨着：那样到底也不算太坏。
我一个急促的动作，从口袋里拔出左轮枪：她没有动，只动了动惨白的嘴唇，我想那是在低语：
「还不到时候。……”
我垂着胳膊站着，食指扣在扳机上，望着她。我看见她的目光在枪上掠过一次，随后便抬起来，直盯住我的脸：此刻那消失过的微笑又回到她唇上，含着信，含着轻蔑。
我等她张口说话——好叫那微笑止住——那我便可以又快又突然地开枪：她偏不，因为她知道她微笑着我便杀不了她；忽然，我对她的全部怜爱，翻成一股对她的古怪怨愤和狂怒，因为她故意把我为她才做的事，弄得难上加难：我心里怀着那个苦毒的念头：「你于我什么也不是：你想死，你自己动手；我自己死我自己的。」我没对她说一个字，大步走了，把她丢在那里。
我如今明白，这整场抽签不过一场闹剧：我本来就杀不了她，笑也罢，不笑也罢：因为每样东西、每个人都有一定的强度：一物无论怎样挣扎，也强不过它的强度：它就这么强，强不更多，事情到此为止。
我走上大总管的书房，一间离地约二十五英尺的屋子。这时天已相当黑，但眯眼还看得见一座落地大钟的钟面——我早已上好它，一直给它上弦。它在屋子北面，在凸窗对面的写字桌上方。钟面指着六点半，为了给这桩致命之举的苦斗定一个确切的时刻，我说：「七点。」随后我锁上书桌旁那扇通三级小台阶的门，又锁了楼梯门；开始在屋里踱步。

===Original===

There was not a breath of air here, and I was hot; I seemed to be stifling, tore open my shirt at the throat, and opened the lower half of the central mullion-space of one oriel. Some minutes later, at twenty-five to seven, I lit two candles on the desk, and sat to write to her, the pistol at my right hand; but I had hardly begun, when I thought that I heard a sound at the three-step door, which was only four feet to my left: a sound which resembled a scraping of her slipper; I stole to the door, and crouched, listening: but I could hear nothing further. I then returned to the desk, and set to writing, giving her some last directions for her life, telling her why I died, how I loved her, much better than my own soul, begging her to love me always, and to live on to please me, but if she *would* die, then to be sure to die near me. Tears were pouring down my face, when, turning, I saw her standing in a terrified pose hardly two feet behind me. The absolute stealth which had brought and put her there, unknown to me, was like miracle: for the ladder, whose top I saw intruding into the open oriel, I knew well, having often seen it in a room below, and its length was quite thirty feet, nor could its weight be trifling: yet I had heard not one hint of its impact upon the window. But there, at all events, she was, wan as a ghost.
Immediately, as my consciousness realised her, my hand instinctively went out to secure the weapon: but she darted upon it, and was an instant before me. I flew after her to wrench it away, but she flew, too: and before I caught her, had thrown it cleanly through two rungs of the ladder and the window. I dashed to the window, and after a hurried peer thought that I saw it below at the foot of a rock; away I flew to the stair-door, wrung open the lock, and down the stairs, three at a time, I ran to recover it. I remember being rather surprised that she did not follow, forgetting all about the ladder.
But with a horrid shock I was reminded of it the moment I reached the bottom, before ever I had passed from the house: for I heard the report of the weapon﻿—that crack, my God! and crying out: “Well, Lord, she has died for me, then!” I tottered forward, and tumbled upon her, where she lay under the incline of the ladder in her blood.

===Chinese===

这里没有一丝风，我热；简直透不过气，我扯开喉咙口的衬衫，推开了凸窗中央窗棂格的下半扇。几分钟后，六点三十五分，我点亮桌上的两支蜡烛，坐下给她写信，手枪放在右手边；可刚起头，我仿佛听见三级台阶那扇门上有动静——那门离我左边只有四英尺：一种像她的拖鞋刮擦的声音；我蹑到门边，蹲下听：再听不见什么。我回到桌前，动笔写信，给她一生留下最后的叮嘱，告诉她我为什么死，我怎样爱她，远胜过我自己的灵魂，求她永远爱我，活下去讨我欢喜，可她倘若定要死，那务必死在我近旁。泪水正从我脸上倾泻，我一回头，看见她站在我身后不到两英尺的地方，一副吓坏了的姿势。那把我不知不觉引她到、放她在那儿的绝对无声无息，像个奇迹：那架梯子——梯头正探进敞开的凸窗，我看得分明——我认得，常在楼下一间屋里见到，梯长足有三十英尺，分量也决非轻巧：可它搭上窗户，我竟没有听见一丝声息。可无论如何，她就在那儿，苍白得像个鬼。
我的知觉一认出她，手便本能地伸出去抢枪：可她扑上去，快了我一瞬。我飞身去夺，她也飞跑：还没等我抓着她，她已经把枪干干净净地从梯子两档和窗口扔了出去。我扑到窗前，匆匆一望，仿佛看见它落在下面一块岩石脚边；我直奔楼梯门，拧开锁，三级并作一步冲下楼梯去捡。我记得当时还相当惊讶她没有跟下来，全忘了那架梯子。
